# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).



## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **Stale local-library bind at boot now raises a warning notification instead of only an hourly INFO line**: when docker starts the container before the host's network share (e.g. an NFS `_netdev` mount) is up, the local-library bind captures the bare underlying directory and never sees the real share — bind mounts don't follow later host remounts. Diagnosed live: the host rebooted and docker won the race by 26 seconds, so the library page silently showed debrid-only content (a show with 28 local episodes displayed a single debrid grab) while the log printed `local library appears empty (network mount may not be ready)` hourly at INFO. The existing "Local Library Empty" alert only fires on a present→empty *transition*, which can never happen when the bind is stale from the very first scan. Now, when local library paths are configured but no local content has been seen since startup for 3 consecutive scans, a one-shot `health_error` warning notification ("Local Library Never Seen") names the likely stale-bind cause and both fixes: restart the container, or add `:rslave` propagation to the local-library binds so host remounts propagate in automatically. A scan that finds local content resets the counter and re-arms the alert. New TROUBLESHOOTING entry covers the symptom; the blackhole/symlink guide's compose example now uses `:rslave` on the local-library and blackhole/completed binds.
- **Library UI: per-provider filtering and storage-by-source chips**: the source filter now gains "On RD" / "On TB" (/ "On AD") options, added dynamically for every debrid provider actually present in the library — an item counts as "on" a provider if its primary or alternate copy lives there, so dual-provider items appear under both. Next to the filter bar, a new chip row shows how much content each source holds (e.g. `Local 1.2 TB · RD 8.4 TB · TB 3.1 TB`), computed across movies + shows regardless of the active tab or filters. Attribution is per-episode for shows (a mixed show's local episodes count toward Local, its cloud episodes toward its provider); content that exists in two places counts toward each side that holds it. Clicking a chip applies the matching filter (click again to clear). `_formatBytes` gained a TB tier so multi-terabyte totals don't render as thousands of GB.
- **Mount self-heal now also recovers mounts that were skipped at startup**: if a WebDAV endpoint isn't reachable within the startup probe window (the classic case: host crash-reboot, container starts before the host's network/DNS is ready — hit twice in production, leaving the RD or TorBox mount absent for 12–17 hours until a manual restart), `setup()` used to log "Skipping rclone setup" and never try again, and the existing dead-FUSE self-heal couldn't help because no rclone process was ever registered for the mount. Skipped mounts (WebDAV timeout or any per-mount configure error) are now kept in a pending registry, and the `mount_liveness` probe retries the full per-mount setup on its ~70s cadence — first retry immediate, then at most once per 10 minutes per mount, with a short 30s WebDAV probe so a still-down endpoint can't stall the scheduler. A successful deferred start logs a `repair` event (`mount_deferred_start`) to the Activity feed; repeat failures of the same outage don't re-send the failure notification. Gated by the existing `MOUNT_SELFHEAL_ENABLED` (default `true`).

### Changed

- **Library UI: media-location vocabulary renamed "Debrid" → "Cloud"**: the poster location badge (now `Cloud` / mini `C`, alongside `Local`), the `Cloud Only` source filter, `Prefer Cloud` preference options, `Switch to Cloud` buttons/dialogs, the `Cloud N/A` unavailable badge, `Migrating to Cloud`, and all related toasts/confirmations/notifications now say "cloud" when describing *where media lives*. Rationale: TorBox is not strictly a debrid service, so labeling a TB-backed item "Debrid" was inaccurate — "Cloud" is a provider-neutral umbrella. "Debrid" remains the term for the *provider category* everywhere it names accounts, torrents, mounts, or settings (RD/AD/TB provider badges, "your debrid account", `Primary Debrid`, Debrid Health, env vars). Display strings only — no API fields, CSS classes, env vars, or stored values changed.

### Changed

- **Status page "Recent Events" now shows meaningful activity instead of scheduler heartbeats**: the feed used to be flooded by an `info`-level event on *every* scheduler run — the ~70s `mount_liveness` monitor and no-op cleanups alone filled the entire 100-entry buffer, evicting anything worth seeing (`Task 'mount_liveness' success — 7 entries…` repeated forever). The scheduler now emits a Recent Event only when a task *fails*. The feed is instead fed from the user-facing history activity log — media grabbed, symlinks created, mounts repaired, searches/grabs failed — merged with in-memory lifecycle events (process crashes, config reloads, scheduler errors, dashboard startup), newest-first. Routine `task_completed` summaries stay off the feed (they remain on the Activity page and the scheduler card). Blackhole failures (`failed`/`blocklisted`/uncached/`debrid_add_failed`/etc.) surface as *warnings* — visible, but they don't flip the Status card's health indicator to amber, which stays reserved for genuine system errors and keeps the card in agreement with the "Errors" count. Genuine errors are priority-retained so a burst of routine activity can never crowd a crash off the feed. A short cache keeps the per-poll cost off the history file.

### Fixed

- **Library toolbar: duplicate "Scanning…" indicator removed**: during a scan the Refresh button already swaps to a spinner + "Scanning…" and disables itself, but the adjacent status line *also* showed a pulsing dot + "Scanning…" — two identical indicators side by side. That status line now stays on its "Last scanned: Nm ago" timestamp (still useful mid-scan) and no longer repeats the label; the Refresh button is the single scanning indicator.
- **Status page System card: missing Disk label and misaligned CPU Time row**: the Disk usage row had its label/value assignments inverted relative to Memory — the byte figures (`240 GB / 2 TB`) were written into the name slot, overwriting the "Disk" label so it never appeared, while the bare percent sat in the value slot. Disk now matches Memory: `Disk (12%)` as the label, bytes as the value. Separately, in a container the CPU stat is a cumulative cgroup counter (`cpu_usage_usec`) with no meaningful 0–100 bar, so the bar is hidden and the label reads "CPU Time" — but the row's head kept its reserved 5px track margin, leaving a stray gap that made the row look out of place next to Memory. A `no-track` class now collapses that margin so the CPU Time row sits flush; the bar returns automatically in environments that expose a real `cpu_percent`.
- **Cross-name show merges no longer lose their debrid-provider identity or mislabel local episodes as cloud**: when the IMDb-based dedup collapsed a local entry with a debrid entry whose parsed names differ (e.g. local "Andor" + RD "Star Wars Andor"), the local sibling wins the merge and carried no `source_debrid` — so the merged item rendered no RD/TB provider badge, was invisible to the new provider filters, and its bytes vanished from the storage chips. Additionally, a single group-wide episode-source default tagged the local sibling's episodes as cloud-sourced ("Switch to Local" offered on files already local). The merge now inherits provider identity from whichever sibling has it (a second distinct provider becomes the alt, matching the alt-mount merge semantics), and stamps each sibling's episodes with that sibling's own source — an episode held on both sides comes out `both`. Regression-tested.

- **Library detail Activity panel: accessibility and UX pass** (from an `/impeccable audit`): episode chips failed WCAG AA contrast (3.6–4.0:1 at ~10px) — now full-contrast text on a lifted type scale (row 0.85em; timestamps/chips no longer compound below 11px); the redundant red side-stripe on failure rows is gone (the timeline dot already carries the state); decorative event icons are hidden from screen readers; relative times ("3h ago") are real `<time>` elements with the absolute local timestamp on hover; the panel's loading/empty/error states announce via `role="status"`, the error state gained a Retry button, and the empty state explains what will appear; when the 30-event cap is hit, a "View all in Activity →" link deep-links to the Activity page pre-filtered to the title (new `?q=` URL param, mirroring the existing `?type=` deep link).
- **Fixed disabled features running anyway**: `DUPLICATE_CLEANUP=false`, `ZURG_UPDATE=false`, and `PD_UPDATE=false` were treated as enabled (string truthiness on env booleans). Worst case: with `DUPLICATE_CLEANUP_KEEP=zurg`, the explicitly-disabled cleanup still deleted local media on its 24h schedule.
- **Fixed supervision loss after config reload**: saving settings (SIGHUP) or using the UI Restart button cleared a process's restart policy without re-arming it, leaving Zurg/rclone/plex_debrid without auto-restart until the container was recreated. A process that exhausted its restart budget re-notified every 10 seconds instead of alerting once. `ZURG_LOG_LEVEL=off`/`RCLONE_LOG_LEVEL=off` created stdout/stderr pipes nobody drained, freezing the child once it wrote 64KB.
- **Fixed subprocess exit codes always reading 0**: SIGCHLD was set to SIG_IGN, which makes CPython report returncode 0 for every child — shutdown unmount fallbacks never ran (the log claimed success on failure), a failed `rclone obscure` silently produced a credential-less rclone.config, and crashes logged as clean exits. Now SIG_DFL with an explicit orphan drain in the process monitor, and both failure paths log loudly.
- **Dashboard state-changing endpoints now reject cross-origin browser requests (CSRF)**: `do_POST`/`do_DELETE` on the Status UI trusted a valid Basic-auth header alone — but browsers attach saved Basic auth automatically, and a `<form enctype="text/plain">` submission produces a body that parses as valid JSON, so any page the operator's browser visits could silently fire `/api/library/delete`, `/api/settings/env`, or a restart against the dashboard. Both handlers now check the request's `Origin` (falling back to `Referer`) against the `Host` header before doing anything else, rejecting mismatches (and the sentinel `null` origin) with a 403 naming the cause. Reverse-proxied or hostname-rewritten deployments allow-list their public origin via the new `STATUS_UI_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` env var (comma-separated, picked up on Settings → Save & Reload or SIGHUP like the rest of the Status UI group); requests with neither header (curl, scripts, existing test harnesses) are unaffected. New TROUBLESHOOTING entry covers the symptom. Final-review hardening: a malformed Origin/Referer (e.g. unbalanced `[`) no longer crashes the request with an unhandled `ValueError`, and the guard now also accepts a matching `X-Forwarded-Host` so a proxy that doesn't rewrite `Host` can't lock the operator out of every POST/DELETE (including the Settings save that would otherwise fix it).
- **`atomic_write` now actually fsyncs, matching its "crash-safe" docstring**: the helper renamed a temp file into place via `os.replace` but never called `fsync` on the file or its containing directory — durable against concurrent readers, but a crash or power loss between the write and the next `fsync`-by-something-else could still lose or corrupt state files (`.env`, `settings.json`, `library_prefs.json`, `blocklist.json`, history/blocklist stores) that this helper is relied on to protect. `atomic_write` gained an `fsync=True` kwarg (default on for every existing caller): it flushes and fsyncs the temp file before the rename and fsyncs the containing directory after, so the rename itself survives a crash. The one exception is the TMDB metadata cache (`utils/tmdb.py`), a rebuildable hot-path cache that now passes `fsync=False` to skip the extra syscalls.
- **`/api/logs?lines=` no longer 500s on a non-numeric or negative value**: the query param was parsed with a bare `int()` and only clamped on the high end (`min(lines, 1000)`), so a malformed or negative `lines` value raised an uncaught `ValueError` or requested a negative slice. It now falls back to the default of 100 on a parse failure and clamps to `[1, 1000]`.
- **Manual library actions now log history events and send notifications; the advertised `arr_deleted` notification actually fires**: the manual UI endpoints for removing local files, switching a show to debrid symlinks, and confirming a debrid-torrent removal performed the same destructive operations as the scheduled source-preference enforcement path but emitted no Activity Log entry and no notification — a user who removed local episodes or switched sources from the Library UI had no record of it. `/api/library/remove-local` (all three success paths: Sonarr episode removal, Radarr movie removal, and the direct-file-deletion fallback), `/api/library/switch-to-debrid`, and `/api/library/remove-debrid/confirm` now each log a `switched_source` history event (`cause: preference_source_switch`) and send a `library_refresh` notification, mirroring `enforce_source_preferences`'s own emissions. Separately, `arr_deleted` was documented as a notification event in Settings but had no sender anywhere — `/api/library/delete` now sends it (`warning` level) alongside its existing history log entry. Overseerr fallback requests (`ensure_and_request_tv`/`ensure_and_request_movie`) now log a `search_triggered` history event like the Sonarr/Radarr request paths already did, so a watchlist item routed to Overseerr is no longer invisible in Activity.
- **Scoped prefer-local debrid deletion — no longer deletes sole copies of debrid-only episodes (CRITICAL data loss)**: the TV "mixed" prefer-local toggle (episodes both debrid-only and both-source in the same show) deleted *every* debrid torrent matching title+year seconds after triggering an async Sonarr search that can take minutes to hours — including the sole debrid copy of episodes that had no local file yet (and unrelated debrid-only seasons matched by the same title+year), which could leave those episodes with no copy anywhere until Sonarr's search happened to re-find them. `/api/library/remove-debrid` now derives the debrid-only episode set from the library scanner (`LibraryScanner.debrid_only_episodes`) and scopes the returned torrent list via a new `filter_safe_torrent_deletes()` helper, which parses each torrent's `SxxEyy` / season-pack / whole-show claim (`_torrent_episode_claim()`) and keeps any torrent that could be backing an unsafe episode — unparseable names fail closed (kept). `/api/library/remove-debrid/confirm` now requires a `type` (`show`/`movie`) and, for shows, re-derives the same safety check server-side against a *fresh* provider listing before deleting anything, refusing (`skipped`, with a reason) any client-supplied item that isn't in the fresh deletable set — closing the staleness window between the two requests. The confirmation dialog now lists kept torrents and why; a fully-blocked deletion tells the user to retry once downloads finish instead of silently deleting nothing.
- **Plex now auto-refreshes for library-scanner / TorBox-delivered content**: content the library scanner symlinks into the arr's media folder is discovered by a Sonarr/Radarr *disk rescan* (`RescanSeries`/`RescanMovie`), which is not an import event — so the arr's own "update Plex library" connection never fires for it. Zurg's `on_library_update` hook only covers RealDebrid, not the separate TorBox rclone mount. The net effect: TorBox/scanner-delivered titles reached disk with nothing telling Plex to scan, staying invisible until a manual Plex library scan (Plex's own change-detection scan doesn't fire on rclone/FUSE mounts). The library scanner now triggers a Plex section refresh after it creates debrid symlinks — full refresh of the show sections when shows were symlinked, movie sections when movies were (Plex's scanner is incremental, so it only processes changed files). Fires once per scan cycle, only when new symlinks were created, and is best-effort (a Plex outage can never break a scan). Gated by the existing `PLEX_REFRESH` toggle (the same switch as Zurg's RD hook), which now also governs this scanner-side refresh; requires `PLEX_ADDRESS` and `PLEX_TOKEN`. New TROUBLESHOOTING entry covers the symptom.
- **Library scan no longer walks Zurg's `__downloads__` virtual directory (or any future dunder-named internal dir)**: Zurg v1.0.0 (the new `zurg-public` stable line) advertises a `__downloads__` virtual directory at the mount root but returns a 500 (`cannot find directory __downloads__`) when it is actually listed. The library scanner's internal-directory skip list was a hardcoded pair (`__all__`, `__unplayable__`), so every scan tried to list `__downloads__`, producing an rclone `IO error` + Zurg router error pair in the logs each cycle *and* — worse — tripping the per-category listing-error handler, which flags the entire scan as truncated; a permanently-truncated scan routes downstream consumers (stale-content cleanup, vanished-from-debrid detection) into their last-good-fallback mode indefinitely. The skip list is replaced by a naming-convention check: any dunder-named directory (`__*__`) is treated as a Zurg virtual dir and skipped in both the FUSE and WebDAV scan paths (the `__all__`-only fallback for category-less mounts is unchanged), so new virtual dirs in future Zurg releases can't reintroduce the problem. A second trigger got the same treatment: the RC `vfs/refresh` issued ahead of every FUSE scan was a *recursive root* refresh, so rclone itself walked into `__downloads__` and logged the same error pair once per scan even with the scanner skip in place — the pre-scan refresh now re-lists the root non-recursively (virtual-dir *contents* are never listed) and recurses only into the real categories, batched into a single RC call via new multi-dir support in `refresh_dir()` (rclone's `dir`/`dir2`/`dir3` … convention).
- **Library "Remove from debrid" now searches every configured provider, not just the priority one**: in a multi-debrid setup (e.g. RD + TorBox) the remove flow called `get_debrid_client()` with no argument, which returns only the single priority provider (RD before AD before TB). A torrent that lived on a *secondary* account — the common case for a TorBox-served item when RD is primary — returned "No debrid torrents found", leaving a direct provider API call the only way to remove it. Discovery (`/api/library/remove-debrid`) now fans out across all configured providers via a new `find_torrents_by_title_multi()` helper, tagging each match with the `service` it was found on and isolating a single provider's API failure so an RD outage can't hide a TB item (surfaced as a per-provider `errors` map). Deletion (`/api/library/remove-debrid/confirm`) now takes per-torrent `{id, service}` items and routes each delete to its own account via `delete_torrents_multi()`, replacing the old single-provider `torrent_ids` + mismatch-guard contract — so a TorBox id can never be misdirected to an RD torrent of the same id shape. The confirmation dialog shows each torrent's provider when the results span more than one account.
- **`wait_for_url` timeout is now a wall-clock cap**: the readiness probe only checked its deadline between attempts, so the per-request 10s socket timeout and the exponential backoff sleep (up to 60s) could push a probe well past its budget. The per-request timeout and the sleep now shrink to the remaining window — important for the new deferred mount retry, which probes on the scheduler thread with a 30s budget and must not stall other scheduled tasks.
- **Mount-failure notification no longer hardcodes "Zurg WebDAV timeout"**: a TorBox WebDAV probe timeout used to send a notification blaming Zurg; the message now names the actual probe target. Additionally, a mount whose WebDAV probe succeeded but whose rclone process failed to spawn no longer sends a false "Mount is ready" success notification — it stays in the deferred-retry registry instead.
- **Blackhole: a TorBox cross-probe "cached" verdict is now acted on instead of discarded**: under `BLACKHOLE_REQUIRE_CACHED`, when the routed debrid's cache verdict is unknown (always the case on RD/AD — their cache endpoints are dead) the gate cross-probes TorBox; a `False` verdict already deleted the drop with arr feedback, but a `True` verdict fell through to the defer path, throwing away a confirmed cache hit. Normally `cache_aware` routing picks TorBox before the gate ever runs, but the router's 60s per-instance probe cache can hold a stale `None` (recorded while the TB probe budget was exhausted) that keeps the grab on the primary even after a fresh `True` lands at the gate — each such cycle deferred the file and burned duplicate probe budget, slowing backlog drain under the new 10-req/min TB throttle. The gate now re-routes the add to TorBox on a `True` cross-probe verdict (after a TB-account dedup check, mirroring the router-picked path) and refreshes the router's probe cache so the next routing decision agrees.
- **TorBox checkcached probe storm — batched requests, verdict cache, auth circuit breaker, and a rate-limit backstop**: the TB cache probe (`utils/search.py:_check_cache_tb`) issued one HTTP request *per hash*, fanned out across an 8-thread pool, with no result caching and no auth-failure handling — live, the periodic search/recovery passes sustained ~175 requests/minute (10,445/hour measured) against `/v1/api/torrents/checkcached`, which earned the account an automated TorBox abuse warning and an API-key rotation on 2026-08-16; the rotated key then turned the storm into a hot 403-retry loop, since errors returned `None` (unknown) and every pass re-probed the same hashes. Four layers now stand between callers and the endpoint: (1) all hashes in a probe ride **one comma-joined batched request** (`format=object`, capped at `_TORBOX_MAX_PROBES`) — the endpoint has always accepted multi-hash queries (the vendored plex_debrid TB client already used them), so per-hash fan-out was pure request-volume waste, and the now-unused per-hash helper and thread pool are deleted; (2) a **15-minute TTL verdict cache** serves repeat probes of the same hash from memory, so periodic passes over a stable backlog no longer re-ask TB the same question every cycle; (3) an **auth circuit breaker** — any HTTP 401/403 halts *all* TB probes for 30 minutes and logs a single ERROR naming the rejected key as the cause, because retrying a dead/rotated key is pure abuse-signal (a fixed key recovers without a restart once the cooldown lapses); and (4) a **hard rate-limit backstop** of at most `_TB_PROBE_MAX_PER_MIN` (10) batch requests per sliding minute, so no upstream caller loop can ever push TB past ~10 req/min regardless of future bugs. Batch failures now leave every probed hash as `None` (unknown) rather than isolating per-hash — request-volume control outweighs per-hash isolation under the I4 contract, since `None` already means "caller decides".

### Changed

- **Docs: README audit fixes** — removed the dead GitHub Actions build badge (workflows are intentionally disabled; there is no published image, so the badge only ever showed red or nothing), corrected the Licensing sections in `README.md` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` that claimed the Zurg binary is downloaded "at image build time" (it is fetched at container startup; the image only bakes Zurg's config template), and fixed the pd_zurg migration note in `TROUBLESHOOTING.md` that said "new pushes go to `fjmerc/zurgarr`" — Zurgarr is currently build-from-source only, with no prebuilt image to pull.
- **Zurg source re-pinned to the new `zurg-public` repo (v1.0.0 stable line)**: upstream renamed its public distribution from `zurg-testing` to `zurg-public` ("the name going forward"; the old repo now only mirrors stable releases). The container's baked `config.yml` and `plex_update.sh` were pinned to a two-year-old `zurg-testing` commit whose config template still carried keys upstream has since removed (`concurrent_workers`, `auto_delete_rar_torrents`, `enable_download_mount`, `serve_from_rclone`, `network_buffer_size`, `verify_download_link`, `rate_limit_sleep_secs`) and lacked newer ones (`mount_path`, `rclone_enabled`, `.strm` support, `delete_error_torrents`, `force_select_playable_files`, `addl_playable_extensions`). The `Dockerfile` ADD lines now pin those two files to `zurg-public@ae54761`, and the runtime binary fetch/update paths (`zurg/download.py`, `zurg/update.py`) point `releases/latest` at `zurg-public` instead of `zurg-testing`. The sponsor **nightly** path (the private `zurg` repo, used when `GITHUB_TOKEN` is set, which carries the TorBox/AllDebrid/Usenet backends) is unchanged. pd_zurg still runs rclone as its own managed process, so zurg's new self-managed mount (`rclone_enabled`) stays off by default — and a guard in `zurg/setup.py` now force-disables (comments out, with a warning) any `rclone_enabled: true` that arrives via a user config override, since zurg spawning its own rclone would collide with pd_zurg's managed mount. Doc links in `README.md` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` updated to match. Requires a rebuild to take effect.
- **UI: filled accent buttons now pass WCAG contrast in dark mode**: an `/impeccable` pass on the Settings page found that every white-text-on-accent control failed 4.5:1 in the (default) dark theme — the bright `--blue`/`--green`/`--red` tokens read fine as text/borders on the dark surface but only hit ~2.5:1 behind white button labels. Measured failures: the primary **Save & Apply** button (2.54:1), the shared confirm-dialog primary button (2.53:1), filled danger buttons and the sidebar Wanted badge (3.35:1), plus the sibling-page `.step-num`, library source-filter, and card select-mode active buttons (all white-on-`--blue`, 2.53:1). Rather than darken the semantic accents (which would dull the toggles, success banners, and status text that read them *as* color), three new theme-aware fill tokens (`--blue-solid`/`--green-solid`/`--red-solid`) carry the same hue at a depth where white text clears AA (dark ≥4.6:1); every filled button/badge points at the `-solid` variant while the bright accents stay for text, borders, and toggles. Light theme was already passing and is visually unchanged. The deeper Save-button green also reads as a more deliberate CTA than the old bright fill.
- **Status: accessibility, alerting, and polish pass on the dashboard**: an `/impeccable` critique + technical audit of the Status page surfaced a batch of gaps, now closed. **Alerts** — the expiry/rate-limit banner used a single `#banner` slot with a `break` that hid every fault after the first, so a second live problem (e.g. an expired provider *and* a rate-limited one) was invisible; it now collects *every* active alert (per-service expiry + per-provider rate-limit) into a stacked, `aria-live="polite"` region that sorts critical-first, shows up to three with an "+N more" overflow, links straight to the affected service, and has a keyboard-operable dismiss that stays dismissed until the underlying state actually changes; the region only rewrites its DOM when the alert set changes (a rendered-signature guard) so the poll loop no longer re-announces the same alerts to a screen reader every few seconds, and the alert links are passed through an `https?:`-only `_safeUrl()` allowlist that percent-encodes attribute-breakout characters. **Contrast** — the muted `--text3` token failed WCAG 4.5:1 on card backgrounds in both themes (dark 3.33:1, light 3.08:1) and the inactive theme-switch label failed on its pill; `--text3` is bumped to AA (dark `#7d8792` 4.74:1, light `#6a737d` 4.82:1) and the inactive theme label moved to `--text2`. **Semantics/ARIA** — added an `<h1>`, `scope` on every table header (incl. `scope="row"` on the mount role cells), a visually-hidden text label beside every color-only status dot, a polite live region that announces overall-health transitions, `aria-pressed` on the library source filter (was a broken `role="tab"` set), and a real labelled `<label>` on the refresh selector. **Library bars** — the in-bar white count labels sat on segment colors that only cleared AA in light mode; both source colors are now single AA-passing tokens (`--lib-local`/`--lib-cloud`) shared by bars and legend swatches, so the labels pass in dark mode too. **Touch** — coarse-pointer min-heights on the small buttons, theme switch, hamburger, and library filter (desktop density untouched). **Usage viz** — the three System resource rings (memory/CPU/disk) were three separate SVG donut gauges reading as decoration rather than comparison; they're replaced by a single stacked-bar idiom (`.usage-list`) that reuses the library-bar visual grammar, each a real `role="progressbar"` with live `aria-valuenow`, threshold-colored (green <60% / yellow <85% / red above) on both the fill and the value readout, and honoring `prefers-reduced-motion` on the fill transition. The CPU-time fallback (cumulative counter, not a 0–100 ratio) hides its track instead of drawing a meaningless bar. **Services disclosure** — each service tile had exposed up to ten data atoms (API count, error count, avg latency, rate-limit mini-bar, last-error) with no severity ordering; the routine metrics now collapse behind a per-tile native `<details>` "Metrics" disclosure (open-state keyed by service name so it survives the poll rebuilds, correlated by index rather than injected into markup) while the actionable **last error** stays pinned outside the disclosure in red. **Polish** — the stat readouts default to `--text` instead of always-blue, and the refresh interval persists across reloads.
- **Library: detail-view polish — recoverable clipped cast names and a non-color failure cue**: two final accessibility gaps from the detail-view critique. The cast-and-crew names/roles are clamped to two lines (`-webkit-line-clamp:2`, `max-width:92px`), so a long actor or character name was clipped with no way to recover the full text; each `.cast-name`/`.cast-role` now carries a `title` attribute with the full string, so hovering reveals it. Separately, the history-sidebar load-failure message conveyed its error state through red text alone (`color:var(--red)` on "Failed to load") — it now leads with a "⚠ " glyph so the failure reads without relying on color perception.
- **Library: detail-view polish — reduced-motion for the cast rail and an Esc affordance on Back**: final pass over the detail view. The cast-and-crew rail scrolled smoothly (CSS `scroll-behavior:smooth` plus a `behavior:'smooth'` on the arrow-button `scrollBy`) with no `prefers-reduced-motion` guard, unlike the alphabet-jump scroller which already honored the preference — the rail now falls back to instant scrolling under reduced-motion in both the CSS and the JS path. The "Back to Library" button gained a `title="Back to Library (Esc)"` hint surfacing the already-wired Escape shortcut (the keydown handler routes Esc to `hideDetail()`), so the keyboard exit is discoverable instead of hidden.
- **Library: detail-view layout pass — title-size bug, long-title overflow guard, file-only season pills, and pref-block cleanup**: a layout review of the detail view surfaced four structural issues. (1) The intended `.detail-header h2` title styling (1.3em / weight 600 / 6px bottom margin) never applied because the renderer wraps the title in `.detail-info`, not `.detail-header` — so the title had been rendering at the oversized browser-default h2 size and `.detail-header` was dead CSS; the rule is retargeted to `.detail-info h2`, reviving the intended size. (2) That same `<h2>` had no wrap guard, so a long unbroken title token could overflow horizontally on narrow screens — it now carries `overflow-wrap:anywhere` and `text-wrap:balance`. (3) A season with no known episode total (file-only / no TMDB data) rendered *no* progress pill at all, while a season with a known total but nothing on disk rendered a red "0 / N" pill — visually unparallel; file-only seasons now show a neutral grey "N file(s)" pill using the previously-dead `.progress-empty` class, so "unknown total" and "nothing acquired" read as sibling states. (4) The "Prefer Local / Prefer Debrid" explainer was always-on visual noise directly above the action buttons — it's now a collapsed-by-default native `<details>` disclosure ("What do these do?"). Alongside, the pref block's ~10 scattered inline `style=` attributes (the source-preference label, the explainer, the two "configure in Settings" hints, and the three action-button rows) were pulled into shared classes (`.pref-label`, `.pref-help`, `.pref-config-hint`, `.detail-action-row`) applied identically to the movie and show renderers, which also normalizes a stray `margin-top:2px` up to the 6px rhythm used elsewhere in the info column.
- **Library: the detail-view synopsis now says "Show more" instead of hiding its expandability behind a fade**: the plot synopsis was clamped to ~7.5em with a soft bottom fade as the *only* hint it expanded — no label, no cue that clicking did anything — and it clamped even short synopses that had nothing hidden, so a one-line plot still showed a faded bottom edge and toggled to no visible effect. The synopsis now renders as plain static text and is measured after render: only when it actually overflows the clamp does it gain a visible **Show more** / **Show less** affordance (and the fade), plus the button semantics (`role="button"`, `tabindex`, `aria-expanded`, and a "Show/Hide full synopsis" `aria-label` that flips with state). A synopsis that fits drops the clamp and fade entirely and carries no misleading button role. The measure-and-mark runs synchronously in the same frame as the render, so the unmarked state never paints. Two icon-button accessible-name gaps closed alongside: the movie "Block this release" button had only a `title` (no `aria-label`, unlike its per-episode counterpart which already had one — parity), and the season "Collapse" footer's accessible name was the raw `▲ Collapse` glyph string; both now carry explicit `aria-label`s.
- **Library: touch devices get >=44px hit areas on the detail view without bloating the desktop layout**: the detail-view controls were sized for a mouse — the "Back" link (`padding:0`, ~18px tall), the round icon buttons (28x28), the per-episode action buttons (which the mobile width query *shrank* further to `padding:2px 5px`), the season collapse footer (~20px), and the cast-rail scroll arrows (32x32) all fell short of the 44x44 minimum touch target, and the mobile episode-action row could force horizontal scroll. A new `@media(pointer:coarse)` block (keyed on *input type*, not viewport width, so the dense `pointer:fine` desktop stays compact) raises all of these to >=44px — icon buttons to 44x44, the back link and season footer to a 44px min-height, the episode buttons back up to `.8em`/`8px 12px`/44px-min (overriding the width query's shrink since it sits later in the cascade), and cast arrows to 44x44 — and lets the episode-action row wrap instead of scrolling off-screen. Global `box-sizing:border-box` makes the pixel values exact.
- **Library: the "Debrid N/A" state badge now visually dominates, plus detail-view contrast fixes**: on the detail view (and the card grid, which shares the badge) the "Debrid N/A" alert — the one badge that signals the operator must act — rendered as a 6%-alpha red wash indistinguishable in weight from the passive quality/size pills beside it, so during an incident the actionable state didn't stand out. It's now a solid-filled red chip (white text, ~6.5:1 dark / ~5.4:1 light), and the solid fill is itself a non-hue cue so it reads as "different" even for red/green-colorblind users. Three measured sub-4.5:1 contrast spots were also corrected: the show-status chip (`.detail-status`, ~4.0:1 grey-on-grey → `--text`), episode relative-air-date text, and the whole missing-episode row (both `--text3` ~3.8:1 → `--text2`). The benign "Downloading Locally" state and the descriptive quality/size pills were deliberately left as muted tints so only the act-now state shouts.
- **Library: detail view no longer reflows (or hangs blank) while poster/synopsis load**: opening a movie/show detail rendered the title+badges immediately but omitted the entire poster column and synopsis until a second `/api/library/metadata` fetch resolved — so the info column visibly jumped rightward when art landed, and a title TMDB has no poster for showed *nothing* in the poster slot. Worse, if that fetch returned non-OK or errored, the view was never re-rendered, so the missing pieces stayed missing indefinitely. The poster column is now always present at its fixed 150px width: a shimmer skeleton (reusing the grid's `.skeleton-line`) while the fetch is in flight, the real art when it arrives, or a stable lettered placeholder when the fetch finishes with no artwork — so the layout never shifts. A matching multi-line synopsis skeleton fills the overview area during the load. The metadata fetch now re-renders on *both* its resolve and reject paths (and on a non-OK response), so a failed or artless lookup drops the skeletons instead of shimmering forever. Applied identically to the movie and show renderers.
- **Activity: accessibility, contrast, polling, and error-feedback pass on the History/Blocklist/Stuck tabs**: an `/impeccable` critique of the Activity section surfaced a batch of gaps, now all closed. **Tabs** were plain `<button>`s with no ARIA — they now implement the full WAI-ARIA tabs pattern (`role="tablist"`/`tab`/`tabpanel`, `aria-selected`, `aria-controls`/`aria-labelledby`, roving `tabindex`, and Arrow/Home/End keyboard navigation with focus-follows-selection); panels are labelled by their tab and focusable. **Contrast**: the several `--text3` spots that failed WCAG 4.5:1 on body text (event time/source cells, the three empty-state messages, blocklist/stuck dates, the stuck legend prose, and the `(unknown)`-title placeholder) moved to `--text2`, which passes in both themes; `--text3` is kept only for the non-text pager ellipsis. **Polling** now pauses on a backgrounded tab and skips the History auto-refresh while it's off-screen, paginated past page 1, or while the user is mid-search (search box focused *and* non-empty — a gate that can't wedge on a native `<select>` retaining focus); Blocklist/Stuck refreshers gate on visibility so their tab-count badges stay live. **Errors**: each loader now raises a single throttled toast on fetch failure (deduped per-tab so a persistent outage doesn't spam), and the blocklist-remove/stuck actions surface success/failure toasts. The blocklist short-hash cell became a real `<button>` (copies the full hash to the clipboard with a toast, keyboard-operable, focus-visible) instead of a bare styled `<td>`, and the 13-entry type filter is grouped into Lifecycle/Triggers/Blocklist/Health `<optgroup>`s. **Reviewer-flagged fix folded in**: `stuckRetry` toasted "Retry failed" on a non-ok `/api/stuck/retry` response but never re-ran `loadStuck()`, leaving the table stale after a failed explicit retry — the refresh now runs on both the success and failure paths.
- **Library: persistent collapsible color key, tokenized status colors, and contrast fixes**: the poster progress-bar color legend only rendered when TMDB data was present and was appended *below* the entire 600+ card grid — visible exactly when recall was impossible and it couldn't help. It's now a persistent, collapsible "Color key" (`<details>`/`<summary>`, keyboard-operable, focus-visible) that sits above the grid and shows the swatches for the active tab (Movies: Available/Switching; Shows: Continuing/Ended/Missing/Switching), hidden only when the view has no cards to explain. The four status-strip colors were hardcoded hex literals duplicated across the movie progress-bar path, the show path, the async enrichment repaint, the ended-corner badge, and the legend itself — a fourth color system off the design palette that could silently drift; they're now four page-scoped CSS custom properties (`--status-continuing/complete/missing/switching`) referenced from every site, so the bars and their key share one source of truth. Several low-contrast spots (~2.8:1 on `--card`) were darkened: episode filenames, cast character names, and the scan-info line move from `--text3` to `--text2`, and the pending-callout body text moves off `--orange`-on-orange-tint to `--text2` while keeping the orange left-border as the semantic accent.
- **Library: Refresh button shows an in-button spinner and "Scanning…" label**: while a manual refresh or a picked-up background scan was in flight the button just went disabled with its "Refresh" label unchanged — the only progress signal was the small pulsing dot in the scan-info line beside it. It now swaps to a spinner + "Scanning…" (the shared `.spinner`) and restores "Refresh" when the scan settles; both the manual-refresh path and the background-poll path route through the same `_setRefreshButtonDisabled` helper so the state stays consistent, and a button min-width keeps the label swap from reflowing the toolbar.
- **Library: the three bulk/Wanted select-mode confirmations moved onto the shared confirm dialog**: the select-mode "apply preference to N items" and "search selected on debrid" prompts, and the Wanted view's "search/download all" prompt, were the last native browser `confirm()` calls on the page — the follow-up explicitly noted when the detail-view confirmations were consolidated. All three now use the themed, focus-trapped `showConfirm` modal with an explicit confirm-button label, preserving each call's existing in-flight guard and cancel-cleanup (the Wanted path still re-enables its button and clears its progress line on cancel).
- **Library: predictable toolbar wrapping on narrow screens**: the seven-control toolbar (search + source/status/year/sort selects + Select/Refresh) wrapped raggedly as the viewport narrowed, breaking mid-row unpredictably. Below 560px the search field now takes its own full-width row so the filter/sort controls wrap beneath it as one predictable group.
- **Library: accessibility pass on tabs, synopsis, and the blocklist/search modals**: four keyboard/screen-reader gaps on the library page are closed. The Movies/Shows tabs pointed `aria-controls` at `tab-movies`/`tab-shows` IDs that never existed (the real results panel is `#content-area`) — the reference is now correct, `#content-area` is marked `role="tabpanel"`, and the internal active-tab bookkeeping moved to a dedicated `data-tab` attribute so it no longer piggybacks on `aria-controls`. The expandable detail-view synopsis was a bare `onclick` `<div>` — unreachable and unoperable by keyboard — and is now a `role="button"` element with `tabindex`, `aria-expanded` state, and Enter/Space handling. The blocklist modal and the debrid search modal were plain overlays with no dialog semantics: both now carry `role="dialog"`/`aria-modal="true"`/`aria-labelledby`, move focus into the dialog on open, trap Tab within the dialog, and restore focus to the triggering control on close; the search modal additionally gained an Escape-to-close handler it previously lacked (the blocklist modal already had one). The focus-trap/restore wiring is a single shared helper used by both modals.
- **Library: destructive actions now use the shared confirm dialog, and detail-view results survive navigation**: the library detail page had grown its own feedback/confirm layer separate from the app's shared primitives, and the **Delete** button (which permanently removes the debrid torrent(s) and symlink folders) was gated by the *same* generic two-click "Are you sure?" as reversible actions like "Switch to Local" — no disclosure of what actually gets destroyed. Delete now opens the shared `showConfirm` modal (focus-trapped, keyboard-accessible) with an explicit scope line ("Removes X from Radarr/Sonarr, and permanently deletes its debrid torrent(s) and symlink folder(s). This cannot be undone.") and a red **Delete** confirm button. The five detail-view preference-switch confirmations (switch-to-local, switch-to-debrid, and the mixed cases — the destructive switch-to-debrid variants that remove local files now get a red **Switch** button) moved off the browser-native `confirm()` onto the same themed modal. The inline transfer-message helper now falls back to a shared toast whenever the detail view has already closed (e.g. after a successful delete hides the panel), so success/error messages are never silently dropped — previously they wrote to a DOM element that no longer existed. `showConfirm` gained an optional `{confirmLabel, danger}` argument (backward-compatible) so destructive confirmations render a distinct danger button, and its message paragraph now honors line breaks. (The three bulk/Wanted select-mode confirmations still use native `confirm()` — a separate subsystem left for a follow-up.)


## Version [2.21.0] - 2026-08-09

### Added

- **Status page: TorBox service card**: TorBox now appears in the Services section alongside Real-Debrid and AllDebrid, showing connectivity status, account email, plan (Premium/Free badge), days remaining until expiration, and the same per-provider API call/error/response-time health metrics that RD and AD already surface (the provider key was already wired in the frontend; only the backend `check_services()` probe was missing).

- **Manual search: TorBox cache badges + per-provider Add buttons**: the library detail page's search modal was hardwired to the RD-first auto-detected provider — every "Add" landed on Real-Debrid even on a dual RD+TorBox deployment, and results carried no cache information (the endpoint never opted into annotation, and the auto-detected RD probe is a permanent no-op since RD deprecated instantAvailability in Nov 2024). The search results table now shows a sortable **Cached** column (`TB ✓` / `✗` / `–` for unknown) populated by the only working pre-add probe (TorBox `checkcached`), and when more than one provider is configured the Add button splits into one button per provider (`+ RD`, `+ TB`) so the user picks where the torrent lands; `/api/search/add` accepts an optional `service` field (400 when the named service isn't configured), and `/api/search` returns the configured-provider list alongside results. The probe-service selection is **opt-in per caller** (`cache_service='auto_probe'`): the quality-compromise engine keeps annotating against the primary provider, because its grabs land there and a TB "cached" verdict says nothing about RD cache state. Two probe-path improvements ride along for all callers: results are quality-ranked *before* the capped 25-hash probe batch is built (previously raw Torrentio order, so top releases could go unprobed), and the TorBox per-hash probe fan-out now runs on a **shared module-level** 8-worker thread pool (worst case with TB timing out drops from ~4 min to ~30 s; typical searches annotate in ~1-2 s; sharing one pool means stacked concurrent searches + the compromise engine + the recovery pass split a single 8-probe budget instead of spawning 8 threads each). **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded into the same change:** (a) `/api/search/add`'s `service` field is length-capped like its sibling fields, so a giant payload can't be echoed back in the 400 error body; (b) `info_hash` is format-validated at the endpoint (rejected, not truncated — slicing a longer hex-looking string to 40 chars could silently add a *different* valid torrent); (c) pre-existing gap amplified by the change: `add_to_debrid` redacted credential patterns from provider error strings for history.jsonl but returned the raw string to the browser and the notification body — redaction now happens once, in place, before any consumer sees it; (d) a single-provider deployment now also gets a labelled `+ RD`-style button instead of a bare "Add", so the user always sees where the torrent will land. New tests: configured-services helpers, probe-service preference and the compromise-engine default guard, quality-ranked probe ordering, returned-error redaction, and endpoint tests for the providers payload plus explicit/omitted/unconfigured/non-string/oversized `service` and malformed `info_hash` handling.

- **Wanted season-pack recovery for partially-present shows** (`WANTED_SEASON_RECOVERY_ENABLED`, default ON): the Wanted→debrid recovery pass previously probed only fully-absent "ghost" titles, so once those drained, the recovery metric plateaued — on the reference deployment 93% of the remaining wanted units were missing episodes *inside* partially-present shows, unreachable by both the ghost-only pass and the arr's indexer pool (the settled acquisition-side gap). The pass now also builds a target per season that still has missing aired episodes: it searches Torrentio at the season's first missing episode, keeps only releases that actually cover the target (a season pack marking that season — `S03`, `S01-S04`, `Season 3`, `S22.COMPLETE` — or an exact-episode release; wrong-season/wrong-episode/unmarked junk is dropped), prefers cached packs over cached singles, and adds the best TorBox-cached one. One pack add fills every gap in the season — the symlink phase already skips episodes present on disk. Season targets are TorBox-only (probing a whole pack through RD's add-poll-delete cycle is expensive at RD's measured ~8% hit rate), share the existing `WANTED_TB_RECOVERY_MAX_PER_SCAN` trickle budget with whole-title targets (which keep first claim), sort biggest-gap-first, and reuse the existing per-title cooldown/no-results memos under a distinct `imdb:season:pack` key namespace. Unmonitored seasons and unaired episodes are excluded for free by the existing missing-episode computation, and a TMDB "don't know" answer is never probed blind. Gap-fill arr searches keep running for the same seasons — a deliberate dual-path overlap that self-corrects at import time.

- **Settings: modified-from-default indicators + "Show only modified" filter**: with 133 env vars across 21 sections, there was no way to see which settings actually differ from the application defaults — auditing a deployment's config meant eyeballing every field against `CONFIGURATION.md`. Each field whose current value differs from its default now carries a teal "modified" chip, a toolbar checkbox filters the page down to just those fields (composing with the existing text search), and a live counter shows "N of 133 modified" — chips and count update as you type, before saving. Defaults come from the existing `POST /api/settings/reset/env` payload (a pure read despite the name — the same call the reset flow already used), so no new endpoint. Comparison semantics avoid unset-value false positives: an empty field runs at the application default so it never counts as modified, booleans normalize unset→`false` on both sides (an unchecked toggle equals a blank default), and secrets compare set-vs-unset only (masked values can't be compared literally). **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded into the same change:** (a) a failed defaults fetch now yields NO chips and a blank count instead of false-positively flagging the ~14 server-pre-filled true-default booleans/numerics as modified (which would also have made "Show only modified" hide nearly everything); (b) the per-field undo button now refreshes the chip and re-applies the active filter — programmatic value assignment fires no input event, so the reset row previously kept lying about being modified; (c) the render-time and live-update chip paths now share the same field-type vocabulary so a future type-specific comparison branch can't silently diverge between initial render and edits; (d) pre-existing gap amplified by the chips: `FORCE_GRAB_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (12), `BLACKHOLE_TB_ALT_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (12), and `BLACKHOLE_ARR_FEEDBACK_MAX_STRIKES` (8) had runtime defaults their help text documented but `_ENV_DEFAULTS` didn't declare, so the UI rendered them as empty fields (and the chip could never fire) — now declared, with a new source-scan drift-guard test pinning each declared default to the `os.environ.get` fallback literal at every call site (the existing Config drift guard can't cover these live-env knobs). Browser-verified end-to-end against a live server: exactly the deliberately-set env vars flagged, filter/search composition, live chip updates, undo-refresh, and the no-defaults degrade path all confirmed.

- **Notifications: `retry_giveup` event — the system tells you when it stops trying**: both terminal give-up paths were silent (log lines only): the Wanted-recovery pass giving up on a title confirmed filter-blocked on RealDebrid AND uncached on TorBox across 3 passes, and the force-grab loop exhausting its `FORCE_GRAB_MAX_ATTEMPTS` re-grab budget. An operator only discovered either by happening to open the Stuck tab. A new `retry_giveup` notification event (level `warning`) now fires exactly once at each crossing — for force-grabs the notify hangs off the strike-bump site rather than the every-scan cap-skip branch, so a capped title can't re-notify each pass — with parity across shows (per-season) and movies, and the body points at the Stuck tab's Retry action. Registered in `ALL_EVENTS` and the `NOTIFICATION_EVENTS` settings help text; a new registry-parity test fails any future event added to one but not the other.

- **Stuck tab: dismissed items are now visible and reversible**: "Dismiss 7d" wrote a `stuckdismiss:` attempt-ledger entry with no UI to inspect or undo it — the only trace was a grey "N dismissed" footnote, and un-snoozing early meant waiting out the 7-day TTL (or a Retry, which also resets the give-up caps). The footnote is now a click-toggle that expands the dismissed records as greyed-out rows (full reason chips and last-event context, capped at 50 in the payload with the exact count still shown), each with an **Undismiss** button backed by a new auth-gated `POST /api/stuck/undismiss` endpoint (`stuck.undismiss()` drops just the dismissal key, leaving retry caps untouched; safe no-op for unknown or already-expired keys). The `/api/stuck` payload gains a `dismissed_items` list alongside the existing `dismissed` count — additive, back-compat safe.

- **Vendored plex_debrid: TorBox createtorrent pacing guard**: the vendored watchlist engine (`plex_debrid/debrid/services/torbox.py`) fired `createtorrent` calls back-to-back — a burst of watchlist adds could overrun TorBox's documented 60-creates/hour account limit and arm the abuse system's 24-hour account freeze, taking down every TorBox consumer (blackhole, Wanted recovery, mounts) along with it. A self-contained module-level pacer (no project imports — the vendored tree runs as a separate subprocess) now enforces a minimum 61 s interval between creates across both `createtorrent` call sites, with concurrent callers queuing under a lock so each gets its own full interval, and a 429 response from any TorBox POST pushes the next allowed create out by 5 minutes. **Reviewer-flagged fix folded in:** the 429 penalty hook uses a non-blocking lock acquire — it runs inside `post()` on arbitrary threads, and blocking there while a creator sleeps out its interval under the same lock would freeze an unrelated request for minutes (a skipped penalty self-corrects: persistent 429s re-trigger it on the next response). Deliberately does NOT gate on the `/user/me` `cooldown_until` flag — that flag is advisory on paid plans, and gating on it starves adds that would have succeeded (the 2026-07-06 lesson from the Wanted→TB leg). Local modification documented in `plex_debrid/ATTRIBUTION.md`.

### Changed

- **Backups now include the operator-state stores — a restore brings back retry/give-up state, health verdicts, recovery metrics, and activity history, not just config**: the backup archive covered only `.env`, `settings.json`, `library_prefs.json`, and `blocklist.json`, so restoring onto a fresh `/config` silently reset every hard-won operational store: the attempt ledger (`grab_attempts.json` — force-grab/TB-alt caps, `wantedblock:` give-up strikes, arr-feedback strikes, persisted `rdblock:` filter verdicts), debrid-health probe state (`debrid_health.json`), the recovery-metric snapshots feeding the TB-viability verdict (`recovery_snapshots.json`), the Wanted-recovery pacing memos (`wanted_memos.json`), pending library migrations (`library_pending.json`), and the 30-day activity log (`history.jsonl`). Losing the ledger alone re-arms every capped retry loop the caps exist to stop — a restore that resurrects month-old config while zeroing the strike counts is worse than no restore. All six are now archived (each optional — absent files are skipped, as before) and restored through the same snapshot/rollback machinery. `history.jsonl` gets a dedicated 32 MiB per-member cap (production runs 7+ MB, over the 5 MiB default that still applies to every other member) plus per-line JSON validation, and the decompressed-archive bomb cap grew 50→96 MiB so a legitimately maximal archive (82 MiB of members at their caps) still restores. Stateful members restore through **owner-side restore functions** — each store's own module (attempt ledger, blocklist, debrid health, prefs, pending, recovery snapshots, history) performs the atomic file write *and* the in-memory refresh under the module's own lock, so an in-flight read-modify-write (a sweep persist, a `bump()`, a blocklist `add()`) can't land pre-restore memory over the freshly-restored file; the backup-level `_restore_lock` alone couldn't serialize against those. Debrid-health's `restore_state_bytes()` additionally mutates the live singleton **in place** (an in-flight sweep holds a reference from `_get_state()` — reassigning would leave it persisting an orphaned copy over the restored file), and the scanner merges restored Wanted memos via a new `reload_wanted_memos()` (the loader is additive and TTL-aware, so calling it on a running scanner is safe — memos are pacing hints, so a concurrent scanner persist racing the merge is an accepted, self-correcting loss). Env-file validation errors now cite only the offending line *number*, not its content, so a mistyped secret can't leak into the error response or logs. `pending_monitors.json` is deliberately excluded — it lives in the blackhole watch/completed dir (not `/config`) and self-heals via monitor resume + timeout. New tests: operator-state build/restore round-trip, the raised history cap (over-5M history archived while an over-5M member without an override is skipped, over-32M rejected on restore), per-line history validation, non-object and unparseable-JSON rejection for every JSON-object member, owner-applier dispatch (fires for `/config`, bypassed for other dirs), the memo-merge hook (and missing-scanner tolerance), `restore_state_bytes` in-place semantics, and env-error content non-leakage.
- **Debrid Health: `run_sweep` gained an in-module re-entry guard**: the task scheduler already serializes scheduled and manual (`run_now`) invocations via its per-task `running` flag, but `run_sweep` clears the sweep stop-signal at entry — any future caller bypassing the scheduler would unmuzzle a stopping sweep and interleave probes and state persists. A non-blocking lock now makes the no-overlap guarantee local to the function: a second concurrent call returns immediately with `sweep already running`. Also documented the `_search_cooldown` no-lock invariant in the library scanner (only touched from `_search_for_missing_episodes`, whose sole caller `_scan_effects` is serialized by `_effects_running`) so the missing lock isn't re-flagged as a race.

- **Settings: the plex_debrid tab is now labelled "Watchlist (plex_debrid)"**: the bare internal name didn't tell a user what the tab configures (watchlist automation — monitor Plex/Trakt/Overseerr, scrape, hand to debrid). The descriptive-first/technical-in-parens label matches the Title Case of the sibling "Zurgarr" tab while keeping the recognized upstream name discoverable. Label-only change.
- **Settings: sections reordered by workflow and a new "Recovery & Reconciliation" section extracted**: the section order was accretion-ordered (newest features appended), so the settings an operator touches most sat scattered. Sections now follow the pipeline: core services (Zurg → rclone → TorBox → Media Services → Blackhole → Multi-Debrid Routing → Debrid Search) → recovery (new **Recovery & Reconciliation** section collecting the 8 knobs that govern the Wanted-recovery legs, force-grab caps, unavailable thresholds, gap-fill, and rescan pacing: `WANTED_TB_RECOVERY_*`, `WANTED_RD_RECOVERY_*`, `FORCE_GRAB_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, `DEBRID_UNAVAILABLE_THRESHOLD_DAYS`, `GAP_FILL_ENABLED`, `LIBRARY_RESCAN_NFS_DELAY`) → quality/health → metadata/integrations → operational (Notifications → Monitoring → Status UI → Logging → Backups → General → Advanced). Pure schema reorder — no keys added, removed, or renamed (pinned by a new flatten-invariant test against `_ALL_KEYS`).

### Fixed

- **Library: `refresh()` no longer wedges `_scanning=True` when the worker thread fails to start**: a `Thread.start()` failure (e.g. `OSError` under resource exhaustion, `RuntimeError` at interpreter shutdown) left `_scanning` stuck `True` indefinitely — with `get_data()`'s synchronous fallback removed, every subsequent request served the stale/empty snapshot and every future `refresh()` call short-circuited; only a process restart recovered. `refresh()` now clears `_scanning` and re-raises if `Thread.start()` throws.

- **Housekeeping now prunes old Zurg daily logs (14-day retention)**: Zurg rotates its log by date (`logs/zurg-YYYY-MM-DD.log`) but never deletes anything, so the logs accumulated forever — a 2026-07-14 disk audit found 8.3 GB of them (one noisy day alone produced an 8 GB file, plus 200+ files going back years). The daily housekeeping task now removes `zurg-*.log` files older than 14 days from both the RD and AD log dirs, keyed on mtime so the file Zurg is currently writing is never touched; non-log files in the dir and missing dirs (RD-only deployments) are ignored. New tests: old-removed/fresh-kept, non-matching filenames preserved, missing AD dir tolerated.

- **Stale non-FUSE mount layers at `/data/<mount>` are now cleared before rclone starts — a leftover bind mount can no longer burn the whole restart budget**: rclone refuses to mount while *any* mountinfo entry remains at the target path ("directory already mounted"), but every stale-mount defense was FUSE-specific (the `fusermount`-based unmount ladder, the dead-FUSE corpse probe, the host-side rebuild script) and the setup-time plain `umount` ran exactly once — so a *stacked* leftover (observed live 2026-07-14: the docker override ext4 bind surviving beneath a stripped FUSE layer after a container recreate) popped one layer, left the other, and torbox rclone crashlooped through all 5 restart attempts while the healthcheck reported "not a mount point". The single umount + dead-corpse probe are replaced by a layer-peeling loop (`_clear_leftover_mounts`): re-check mountinfo each pass, route dead FUSE layers through the existing escalate-and-verify ladder, plain-umount anything else, bounded at 10 layers — and stop without forcing if a plain umount fails (EBUSY means something is actually serving). The same function is now the per-mount `pre_restart` hook, so even a layer that appears *after* setup self-heals on the next restart attempt instead of exhausting the budget. **Reviewer-flagged fix folded in:** setup no longer ignores a failed peel — rclone keys off the same mountinfo, so launching after a failed peel is a *guaranteed* refusal; the mount is now skipped through the existing per-mount error/notify path (matching the stale-corpse failure branch) instead. New tests: bare-path no-op, ext4-bind variant, stacked binds, FUSE-corpse-over-bind, busy-stop without force, failed-ladder stop, the max-layers bound, and the setup-skips-on-failed-peel path.

- **Clearing a library preference now cancels its in-flight pending transition**: setting a title's preference back to "None" popped the preference entry but left an active `to-debrid`/`to-local` pending transition running — the scanner kept searching, force-grabbing, and burning attempt budget for a move the user had just revoked, and the UI kept showing the pending badge with no way to stop it short of deleting the title. The preference handler now purges the pending entry (and any scanner-known alias norms, mirroring the delete-path purge) whenever a preference is cleared, via a new `clear_pending_with_aliases()` helper; the API response gains an additive `pending_cleared` count, and a purge failure logs a warning without failing the preference save. New tests: primary norm cleared, alias sweep, scanner-unavailable fallback still clears the primary, and no-pending no-op.

- **TB-alt give-up strikes are cleared when a title resolves — a re-request after resolution starts with a fresh attempt budget**: when a pending title resolved (episodes appeared on debrid, or the debrid-unavailable path closed it out), `_clear_resolved_pending` reset the force-grab attempt-ledger keys (`fg:{title}` and per-season variants) but left the blackhole TB-alternative keys (`tbalt:{imdb}:s{N}`) holding their strikes — so a title that had burned its TB-alt attempts before eventually resolving would, if it ever went missing and got re-grabbed later, start at the give-up cap instead of zero and silently skip the TB-alternative fallback. Resolution now also resets the `tbalt:` keys for each resolved season, including the `sNone` key blackhole writes for movies (`parse_release_name` yields no season for movie releases). Titles without an IMDb ID are unaffected (their `tbalt:` keys never existed — blackhole builds the key from the arr-provided IMDb ID). **Reviewer-flagged fix folded in:** IMDb-ID registration is collision-safe — reboot-family siblings share the bare title norm the pending entry is keyed by but carry distinct IMDb IDs, so a first-iterated-wins binding could reset the WRONG sibling's strikes while leaving the resolved show capped; a conflicting registration now marks the key ambiguous and resolution skips the `tbalt:` reset entirely (fail-safe — the `fg:` reset still fires). New tests: show resolution clears the resolved season's `tbalt:` strike while an unresolved season keeps its own; movie resolution clears both the `sNone` and `s0` variants; a reboot-family bare-key collision clears the pending entry but touches neither sibling's strikes. (E2E-audit finding 10.)

- **Library page never blocks on scans**: `/api/library` now always serves the last-known snapshot instantly and refreshes in the background — previously the first request after cache expiry blocked on a full scan (~53s on a ~700-item library) for up to 50 minutes of every hour, and a duplicate scan could fire during cold start. The persisted warm-start cache also survives version bumps, so the library renders immediately after container rebuilds instead of starting empty.

- **Reboot families no longer aggregate into one show — same-title siblings with distinct TMDB IDs (iCarly 2007 vs 2021) keep separate identities end-to-end**: the whole scan/merge pipeline keyed shows by bare normalized title, so a reboot and its original collapsed into a single library item — episode counts mixed both runs, and the symlink phase could link one sibling's release into the other's Sonarr folder (observed live: an iCarly 2007 release symlinked under iCarly 2021). The TMDB cache now exposes the *collision bases* — bare titles claimed by 2+ fresh year-qualified entries with different TMDB IDs (`get_yearless_collision_bases`) — and every show scan path (FUSE mount, WebDAV, TorBox API, local library) switches those titles to **year-qualified grouping keys** (`icarly (2007)`), stamped on the item as `_norm_key` so the downstream merge/dedup/path-index/effects stages (all rekeyed via a single `_show_norm` helper) never re-aggregate the siblings. A **yearless release in a collision family is attributed by episode shape** (`resolve_show_year_by_episodes`): the sibling whose cached episode list covers every `(season, episode)` the release carries wins, but only when exactly one fits — 2007's S01 has 25 episodes, 2021's has 13, so S01E20 attributes cleanly while S01E05 stays ambiguous. Unattributable items are marked `_ambiguous_reboot` and treated as **identity-unknown, fail-safe**: excluded from symlink creation, from alias/prefix merge hops, from post-enrichment IMDb-ID dedup (their imdb_id is whichever sibling the bare cache key resolved to), from gap-fill arr searches, and from TB season-pack probes — shown in the library but never acted on, rather than guessed into the wrong show. Non-collision titles (the entire rest of the library) keep the plain bare key — zero behavior change. Title-level preferences saved under the bare title still apply to every sibling via a year-strip fallback in the pref lookup. Collision detection is fail-open: any error loading the cache restores the old bare-key grouping. Movies keep the pre-existing same-title collapse (known limitation, unchanged). **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded into the same change:** (a) the ambiguous-item merge guards are now **symmetric** — an attributed sibling can no longer alias- or prefix-hop *into* an identity-unknown item's bare key (the bare TMDB cache entry shares its tmdb_id with one sibling, making them mutual aliases) in either the debrid TMDB dedup or the debrid↔local merge; (b) **pending/search state stays keyed by the bare title norm** the UI writes — the gap-fill search loop, resolved-pending clearing, and local-fallback rerouting all bridge the year-qualified item key back to the bare pending key, so reboot items neither orphan their pending rows nor split them per sibling; (c) `/api/library/switch-to-debrid` now resolves the year-qualified path-index keys reboot items are indexed under — deriving the qualified key from a trailing `(YYYY)` in the display title, else falling back to a *unique* qualified sibling; two-plus siblings stay ambiguous and fail safe with "no matching episodes"; (d) episode-shape attribution ignores **announced-but-unaired** episodes in a sibling's cache entry (UTC date cutoff, consistent with the rest of the TMDB module), so a future-scheduled season can't fake structural coverage of a release; (e) the pending source-map registration **merges instead of overwriting** — when an attributed sibling and an ambiguous item (whose norm IS the bare key) coexist, iteration order can no longer clobber the attributed sibling's episodes out of the bare-key entry. New tests (`test_reboot_families.py`): collision-base detection (distinct/same IDs, staleness, movies section), episode-shape attribution (unique/both/neither/empty fits, unaired exclusion, cold-cache fallback), group-key + identity-stamp semantics, sibling separation through the TB-union / alt-debrid / TMDB-dedup / IMDb-dedup merge stages (both guard directions, including a full `_scan_read` debrid↔local merge), local-scan identity round-trip, symlink-phase exclusion of ambiguous items plus correct `_norm_key`-keyed linking of attributed ones, bare-key pending resolution, and the switch-to-debrid endpoint's year-aware lookup.

- **Stuck tab: history-only failure streaks are reconciled against the live library, so titles the library already shows satisfied no longer linger as "stuck"**: the Stuck collector reconstructs per-title failure streaks from the 30-day history event log, and that reconstruction has no view of current library state — a title eventually filled by a path that logged no progress-cause event (a quality upgrade, a different release, an out-of-window grab) kept showing its stale streak until it aged out of the window. Observed live: *Dexter* surfaced as stuck while the library reported it 100% complete, and the LEGO movies surfaced while their arr entries had `hasFile=true`. The collector now cross-checks each history-**only** streak against the scanner's merged library (the same `peek_data` snapshot the Wanted-ghost collector already reads) and drops the record when the library confirms that title satisfied — a movie present on disk, or a show with zero missing episodes. Because history events carry no year or imdb_id, the join is by normalized title, which is weak (the trailing `(YYYY)` is stripped, so a remake collapses onto its original); to make suppression only ever run in the safe direction, a match must be unambiguous on the two axes the streak carries: **media_type** (a movie streak is only cleared by a satisfied movie, a show streak by a satisfied show) and **single identity** (a normalized title mapping to more than one library entry, or to a lone entry whose year is unknown, is never suppressed). Live in-flight/wanted-backed records are never touched, and a show whose missing-episode count is unknown (`None`) is treated as unsatisfied so a genuine gap can't be hidden. **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded into the same change:** (a) a satisfied library entry with `year=None` — or two entries collapsing to the same identity — can no longer confirm a streak, closing the same-name/unknown-year collision where a satisfied remake could have hidden a genuinely-stuck original of the same name; (b) the streak's media_type is now derived from **any** episode-bearing event in it, not just the last, so a season-pack failure (whose event carries no per-episode marker) can't mistype a stuck show as a movie and let a satisfied movie of the same name clear it. New tests: `TestReconcileSatisfied` (satisfied movie/show suppressed across `both`/`debrid`/`local` sources, show-with-missing-episodes kept, unknown missing-count kept, wanted-source kept, live-kind kept, media-type mismatch kept, ambiguous same-name/different-year kept, unknown-year not confirmable, same-year duplicate not confirmed, season-pack streak typed as show, no-scanner no-op, end-to-end movie + show suppression through `collect`, and reconcile-raises-safe).

- **Library: season-aware merge veto stops different shows sharing a title word from being aggregated into one entry — killing a phantom-season symlink loop**: the TMDB title cache maps each parsed title to exactly ONE show, so when two distinct shows normalize near each other — observed live: a *Marvel's Daredevil* (2015) S03E08 release parses to bare `daredevil`, which the cache resolves to *Daredevil: Born Again* (2025) — the alias map groups them and two pre-enrichment merge sites (`_dedup_by_tmdb`'s canon loop and the `_scan_read` debrid↔local alias hop / prefix fallback) merged them blind. Enrichment's season-aware rename guard can never recover a wrong merge after the fact (the merged item's title words no longer subset-match the true show), so Born Again carried a phantom Season 03 whose source path never exists on the mount — producing a per-scan skip warning forever and, before the phantom-source guard shipped, a create→cleanup symlink churn loop. All three merge sites now consult a season-aware veto before merging: if the item's max season resolves (via the same season-aware TMDB lookup enrichment uses) to a **different** tmdb_id than the bare title does, the merge is refused and the items stay separate — enrichment then renames the unmerged item to its true show and the external-id dedup folds it correctly. The veto is deliberately **fail-open** (no season data, no direct cache entry, no alternate found, or any lookup error → merge proceeds as before), so same-show merges — including a cache that merely lags a season — are untouched. **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded into the same change:** (a) the prefix-fallback site checked only the parsed-key side, which fail-opens whenever the parsed release title has no direct cache entry (`Daredevil Blood in the Streets` → no entry → blind merge) — the canonical-key side is now checked too, with the item's own seasons; (b) shows whose episode filenames didn't parse (`_episodes` empty but a seasons count set — the local-scan fallback shape) reported max season 0 and skipped the veto entirely — the seasons count now serves as a fallback proxy (count ≤ true max, so proxy error stays fail-open). New tests: `TestSeasonAwareMergeVeto` (16 — veto unit matrix incl. fail-open paths, dedup separation + order independence + same-show/no-data controls + movies never load the cache, local-merge integration both directions, prefix-fallback veto + agree control, seasons-count fallback).

- **Library: bare `S01`-style season subdirs (and `S01 Bluray`-style unbracketed qualifiers) are now collected — three more owned season packs materialize**: the day-earlier season-dir relaxation covered the `Season N (…)` dialect, and its new owned-but-never-materialized warning immediately surfaced the next one on its first live pass: packs whose per-season subdirs are named bare `S01`/`S02` (observed live: Broadchurch S01-S03, Barry S01-S04, Bad Sisters S01-S02) or `S01 Bluray`/`S04 WEB-DL` (Arrested Development S01-S04) were still invisible to both collectors. The pattern now accepts a bare `SNN` prefix (season number stays in `group(1)` for every consumer; the end anchor still rejects `S01E01` episode tags, `S01-S03` ranges, and `S01.COMPLETE` release-name shrapnel), and an *unbracketed* qualifier only when it starts with an allowlisted source/quality keyword — arbitrary bare words (`S01 Bloopers`) stay rejected, per the reviewer-recommended allowlist approach. **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded into the same change:** (a) the junk denylist moved from a first-token lookahead into a new single gate `_match_season_dir()` that searches the *entire* qualifier tail — a leading legit keyword is no longer a permission slip for trailing junk (`S01 Bluray Extras`, `Season 1 Complete Extras`, `Season 1 (BluRay Extras)` were all accepted by the lookahead form and would have injected phantom episodes); all four pattern consumers now go through the helper; (b) `scenes`/`bloopers`/`making of`/`plex versions` added to the junk vocabulary (the `_SKIP_FOLDERS` mirror had gaps); (c) season digits capped at 4 (`S99999` shrapnel rejected, year-based `Season 2023` daily-show dirs preserved); (d) `_count_show_content` now dedupes by season *number*, so a show folder holding both `Season 01` and a legacy `S01 Bluray` sibling counts one season, not two; (e) the blocklist climb fallback aborts when the grandparent is empty or itself season-shaped (flat release named like a season dir under an unmapped mount). New tests: bare-SNN/allowlist accepts incl. `S03 WEB` and `Season 2023`, junk-anywhere-in-tail reject matrix, S-prefix bracketed-junk siblings, mixed-dialect season dedupe, and a bare-SNN `_collect_episodes` case.

- **Library: episodes inside bracket-qualified season subdirs — `Season 1 (BluRay)`, `Season 2 (AMZN WEB-DL)`, `Season 3 [1080p x265]` — are now collected**: the season-subdir pattern was anchored to the bare `Season N` form, so a season pack that qualifies its per-season folders with a source/quality tag had every episode silently skipped by both collectors (FUSE walk and TB-mylist/WebDAV).
