Observed live: The Americans S01-S06 mega-pack sat fully materialized on the TorBox mount while all its episodes stayed "wanted" — and the Wanted-recovery pass re-selected the same owned pack every scan forever (add skipped as "already in account", per-season 6h memos rotating so the cycle never ended). The qualifier must be **bracketed** — bare-word suffixes (`Season 1 Extras`) stay excluded so junk files can't ride the no-`SxxEyy` sequential-key fallback into `season_data` as phantom episodes. The relaxation flows through all consumers of the single shared pattern, which also fixes the blocklist folder-climb (a `Season 1 (BluRay)` parent was mistaken for the release folder) and `_count_show_content` (its divergent inline copy of the regex is removed). And the loop's silent signature is now loud: when a Wanted-recovery add is skipped because a covering release is **already in the TB account** yet the target is still missing, the pass logs a warning naming the target and release — owned-but-never-materialized content is a parsing gap to investigate, not a supply problem to re-probe hourly. **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded into the same change:** (a) bracketed *junk* qualifiers — `Season 1 (Extras)`, `(Featurettes)`, `(Bonus)`, `(Behind the Scenes)`, `(Deleted Scenes)`, `(Specials)`, `(Sample)`, `(Subs)`, `(Trailers)`, `(Interviews)` — are denylisted in the pattern (vocabulary mirrors `_SKIP_FOLDERS`), so their no-`SxxEyy` contents can't ride the sequential-key fallback into `season_data` as phantom episodes that Plex would surface as unmatchable videos; source/quality tags like `(Complete)` stay allowed; (b) the TV blocklist check now derives the release folder as the **first path component under the debrid mount** (via the existing per-debrid mount pairs) instead of climbing past Season-dir names — a flat torrent literally named `Season 3 (BluRay)` at mount root made the climb overshoot to the mount root and silently no-op the block; the climb survives only as a fallback for paths under no known mount. New tests: `TestSeasonDirPattern` (accept/reject matrix incl. the junk-qualifier denylist + the exact prod WebDAV-bucket regression), a qualified-season-dir case in `TestCollectEpisodes`, and `TestCreateDebridSymlinksBlocklistReleaseFolder` (flat `Season N`-named release blocked, pack blocked by top folder, unblocked control linked).

- **Library: TV debrid symlinks are no longer created to source paths that don't exist on the mount — killing a create→delete churn loop**: the enumeration layer (TorBox mylist API, Zurg WebDAV) can report folder names the FUSE layer renames on disk (observed live: TorBox strips `&` from a release folder, so the API said `…Upstairs&Downstairs…` while the mount had `…UpstairsDownstairs…`), so the episode path index can carry paths that never materialize. The TV symlink branch linked them blind — the symlink was born broken, the cleanup pass (whose job is deleting symlinks whose target vanished from the mount) removed it on the next scan, and creation re-made it on the scan after, forever: a permanent per-episode churn cycle logging a create + a cleanup every scan. The movie branch was never affected (it picks its source by actually scanning the mount directory). The TV branch now verifies the source exists before linking — the mount is ground truth — skipping phantoms with a bounded per-episode warning plus a total count. New tests: `TestCreateDebridSymlinksPhantomSource` (phantom source → nothing linked; control: real source → linked).

- **Rclone: each mount now gets its OWN ProcessHandler — the registry finally matches reality, and the mount self-heal can actually fire**: `setup()` created a single `ProcessHandler` shared by every mount; `start_process()` overwrites the handler's stored command/key_type and `register_process()` dedups by handler identity, so a dual-mount deployment ended up with exactly ONE registry entry — named after the *first* mount while the handler's internals tracked the *last*-started one. Every per-mount consumer was broken by it: shutdown terminated only one rclone (the other got orphaned → SIGKILLed on container stop → left a dead FUSE mount behind), the process monitor auto-restarted only one pid, `restart_service('rclone', key_type=…)` couldn't reach the second mount, and — the failure that surfaced it in production (2026-07-11): the scheduler's mount self-heal refused to heal a dead `/data/torbox` because `service_registered('rclone', 'torbox')` truthfully reported no such registration ("no rclone process is registered for 'torbox' — cannot self-heal"), while the auto-restart loop burned all 5 attempts relaunching into "directory already mounted". Each mount now creates its own handler (registering separately under its mount name), and the dependency check treats a multiply-registered name as alive when *any* instance is alive — one dead mount must not wedge plex_debrid restarts (or, worse, mark plex_debrid permanently dead when only that mount exhausts its restart budget).
- **Rclone: dead-FUSE corpses are cleared before *every* rclone launch, not just when `makedirs` happens to fail**: the startup stale-mount guard fired only when `os.makedirs(exist_ok=True)` raised — but a corpse re-imported from the host through the `:shared` bind (the recurring `--force-recreate` failure) usually still `stat`s fine, so the guard never saw it and rclone crashlooped on "directory already mounted". A new fstype-aware probe (`_dead_fuse_mount_at`) inspects `/proc/self/mountinfo` for a `fuse.*` entry at the mount path (`/data/<mn>` may itself be a docker bind mount, so mere mountpoint-ness proves nothing) and probes deadness via `listdir` (a corpse fails ENOTCONN; a healthy mount lists and is never touched). It runs at setup before the first launch, and — closing the incident's second gap — as a per-mount `pre_restart` hook on the process handler, so every auto-restart / `restart_service` / SIGHUP relaunch clears the corpse the crashed instance left behind instead of spending the whole restart budget refusing to mount over it. Hook failures log and never block the relaunch. **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded into the same change:** (a) the monitor's `_handle_restart` runs the hook BEFORE taking `_registry_lock` (via a new `run_pre_restart()` + `restart_process(run_pre_restart=False)` split) — the hook can sleep and fork for seconds, and holding the registry lock through that would stall the status UI, `service_registered()`, and every other registry consumer exactly when an operator is watching a crash; ordering stays correct since the process is already dead when the monitor gets there; (b) the mount-probe timeout error now logs the actual probe label and endpoint instead of unconditionally claiming "Zurg WebDav URL …/dav" for a TorBox WebDAV timeout. New tests: `tests/test_rclone.py` (`TestDeadFuseMountAt` — fuse-vs-bind fstype discrimination, healthy-mount no-touch, corpse-stacked-over-bind, octal-escaped paths; `TestClearDeadMount`; `TestPerMountProcessHandler` — two mounts get distinct handlers registered under their own names, each hook clearing its own path) and `tests/test_processes.py` (`TestPreRestartHook` — hook runs before relaunch, a raising hook doesn't block it, `run_pre_restart=False` skips it, and `_handle_restart` provably runs it with the registry lock free; `TestDependencyCheckMultiEntry` — any-alive dependency semantics, all-instances-exhausted marks the dependent permanently dead, an intentionally-stopped instance keeps it deferring).
- **`.dockerignore` now excludes `.git`, `.env`, `.venv`, `tests/`, `log/`, `__pycache__`, and local tooling dirs**: the Dockerfile's `ADD . / ./` copied the entire build context into the image, so a repo-root `.env` (every API key and password) would have been baked into any image built from a configured working tree — readable by anyone with image access, and surviving even if the file were later deleted (it lives in the layer). A local `log/` directory (runtime logs, 38M, potentially credential-bearing) was likewise baked in AND broke the build outright — the copied dir made the Dockerfile's `mkdir /log` fail, so an image couldn't be built from a tree that had ever run the app locally. `.git`, `tests/`, `.venv`, `.pytest_cache/`, and editor/agent tooling dirs (`.claude/`, `.plans/`, `.playwright-mcp/`) round out the list — nothing in the container imports from any of them (runtime config loads `/config/.env`, not a baked copy). Verified with a full local image build from a working tree that previously could not build.
- **`.env.example` caught up with the settings schema — 11 missing vars added, and a drift guard pins it**: `BLOCKLIST_AUTO_ADD`, `BLOCKLIST_EXPIRY_DAYS`, `GAP_FILL_ENABLED`, `HISTORY_RETENTION_DAYS`, `NOTIFICATION_DIGEST_ENABLED`, `NOTIFICATION_DIGEST_TIME`, `PD_LOGFILE`, `PD_REPO`, and the three `RCLONE_VFS_CACHE_*` knobs had settings-schema entries (and `CONFIGURATION.md` docs) but no commented template line in `.env.example`, so a user configuring by template couldn't discover them. All 133 schema keys are now templated, each placed in its matching section with the schema's help text and default. New test `test_env_example_covers_all_schema_keys` fails the suite if a future env var lands in the schema without a template line (the CLAUDE.md new-env-var checklist item, now enforced).
- **System → Config: the read-only config dump now masks `NOTIFICATION_URL` and no longer silently drops ~34 settings**: the `/system` Config tab renders `get_sanitized_config()`, whose masking keyed only on name patterns (`KEY`/`TOKEN`/`PASS`/`SECRET`/`AUTH`) — so `NOTIFICATION_URL`, whose Apprise value routinely embeds a credential (`discord://token@id`, `slack://tok@…`), was printed **in the clear**. Separately, the key set was built by walking `os.environ` and keeping only vars matching a hand-maintained `_CONFIG_PREFIXES` tuple; that tuple had drifted behind the 133-key settings schema, so whole feature families (recovery, quality-compromise, debrid-health, and more — ~34 keys) were structurally absent from the dump regardless of value. **Fix:** the key set is now the union of the authoritative `settings_api._ALL_KEYS` and any prefix-matched live env var, so every schema key always appears (unset ones as `(not set)`) while non-schema knobs like `ZURG_INSTANCES_CONFIG` still surface; and a new `_EMBEDDED_CREDENTIAL_KEYS` set (currently `NOTIFICATION_URL`) is masked alongside the name-pattern matches. **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded into the same change:** (a) masking now also consults the schema's `secret`-typed fields (new `settings_api._SECRET_KEYS`), so a future secret field whose name lacks a `KEY`/`TOKEN`/`PASS`/`SECRET`/`AUTH` token can't leak — the same drift class this whole change closes (all 13 current secret fields happen to match by name, so this is preventive); (b) the partial-mask threshold was raised from `len > 8` to `len > 12` — at 9 chars the old rule showed first-4 + last-4, i.e. 8 of 9 characters of a short credential. Deliberately left readable: base-address/url fields (`PLEX_ADDRESS`, `SEERR_ADDRESS`, `SONARR_URL`, the base `TORRENTIO_URL`, …) hold no embedded credential (the paired key/token is always a separate field), so masking them would gut the viewer's purpose. New tests in `tests/test_status_ui_enhancements.py::TestGetSanitizedConfig`: `NOTIFICATION_URL` with an embedded token is masked (and still identifiable, not over-masked), every schema `secret` key is masked, an `_EMBEDDED_CREDENTIAL_KEYS` entry with a clean name is masked, every `_ALL_KEYS` key is present (anti-drift guard), the formerly-missing feature keys show up, and a prefix-only non-schema var still appears.

- **Library: Wanted-recovery memos now survive container restarts — a restart no longer re-probes the whole cooled-down backlog against the TB create budget**: the three per-title memo stores that pace the Wanted recovery pass (`no_results` 6h, `rd_miss` 7d, `tb_cooldown` 6h) lived only in scanner memory as `time.monotonic()` stamps, so every deploy/restart forgot them — the next scan re-walked every recently-probed title, re-searching Torrentio and re-probing the TorBox cache for titles known-uncached hours earlier, burning exactly the create/probe volume the memos exist to protect (and each of the last several deploys was followed by one of these redundant full-backlog passes). **Fix:** the scanner snapshots the memos to `/config/wanted_memos.json` (atomic write, one write per recovery pass — piggybacking the existing `wanted_recovery_snapshot()` age-in-seconds wire format) and reloads them in `__init__`, converting stored ages back to fresh monotonic stamps with container downtime (`wall_now - saved_at`) counted against each TTL, so a memo can't outlive its window just because the container was down — entries already past TTL are dropped at load. Corrupt/missing/foreign-shaped files load nothing (fresh-start semantics, never raises), and the operator Stuck-tab **Retry** path (`clear_wanted_memos`) rewrites the snapshot immediately so a cleared memo can't be resurrected by a later restart. New tests: `tests/test_library.py::TestWantedMemoPersistence` (8 tests — round-trip, past-TTL entries dropped at load, downtime counted against TTL, restored ages honored by the pass-start expiry sweep, corrupt/missing file no-ops, Retry rewrites the file, recovery pass persists).

- **Scheduler: dead FUSE mounts now self-heal — the ENOTCONN corpse no longer needs a human with `fusermount -uz`**: the canonical failure after a container recreate is rclone getting SIGKILLed while its mount-table entry survives; every traversal then returns `ENOTCONN` ("Transport endpoint is not connected") and the supervisor's rclone restarts crashloop on "directory already mounted" until an operator manually lazy-unmounts. The mount-liveness probe has *detected* this for a while (System page + healthcheck) — this closes the detection→remediation gap. **Fix:** after 2 consecutive unresponsive probes (~2 min at the default 60s interval), `_maybe_selfheal_mount` lazy-unmounts the corpse by reusing the existing startup ladder (`rclone.rclone._force_clear_stale_mount`: fusermount3 -uz → fusermount -uz → umount -l, with verify+retry) and restarts the owning rclone via the process registry — `restart_service()` gained an optional `key_type=` filter so the RD and TB mounts (both registered as `rclone`, distinguished by mount name) heal independently, and a new `service_registered()` pre-check guarantees the corpse is never unmounted unless there's a registered rclone to remount it. Deliberately narrow: heals ONLY the 'Mount unresponsive' (dead-FUSE) signature — 'Mount hung' (alive-but-slow rclone) and 'absent' are left alone, since killing a live rclone mid-operation risks worse. Per-mount 600s cooldown between attempts prevents unmount/remount thrash when the restart doesn't take; a successful heal resets the failure streak, a failed one leaves it standing. Each attempt logs a history event (new cause `mount_selfheal`, type `repair` on success / `failed` when the rclone restart doesn't come back — Python formatter + JS mirror, golden-tested). Kill-switch env var `MOUNT_SELFHEAL_ENABLED` (default ON) wired through `base/__init__.py`, `settings_api.py`, `CONFIGURATION.md`, and `.env.example`. **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded into the same change:** (a) a refused heal (no matching rclone registered — e.g. startup ordering) no longer latches the 600s cooldown, since nothing was attempted — the moment an rclone registers, the very next probe can heal; the operator-attention error log is streak-throttled instead; (b) `restart_service()` with no `key_type` now restarts EVERY registered instance of the name instead of first-match-only — pre-existing debt surfaced by the review: in a dual-mount setup the UI's "restart rclone" button only ever reached the first-registered mount, so the TorBox rclone was unrestartable from the UI. New tests: `tests/test_mount_liveness.py::TestMountSelfheal` (11 tests — 2-strike threshold, key_type routed by mount basename, success resets streak, env kill-switch, cooldown suppression + expiry re-attempt, unregistered-process refusal without cooldown latch, hung-signature exclusion, failed-restart event) plus two formatter goldens and two `restart_service` multi-instance tests.

- **Healthcheck: worker-thread liveness — a dead scheduler/watcher/monitor thread now fails the container instead of stopping all periodic work silently**: the Docker healthcheck runs as a separate short-lived process, so it can see OS processes and FUSE mounts but not the long-lived worker threads inside the main process. A dead task-scheduler loop silently stops ALL periodic work (health sweeps, scheduled scans, backups, ledger prunes), a dead process monitor means no crashed service ever auto-restarts again, and a dead blackhole watcher means grabs pile up in the watch dir — in every case the container stayed "healthy" forever. **Fix:** a new registry (`utils/heartbeat.py`) lets each worker loop register a per-thread staleness ceiling and beat once per iteration; beats land in memory and flush to `/healthcheck/heartbeats.json` throttled (5s) and atomically. `healthcheck.py` evaluates the snapshot: any entry whose last beat is older than its ceiling fails the check — dead or permanently wedged, a Docker restart is the correct remedy for both. Ceilings are deliberately generous so long legitimate work never flaps the container (scheduler 5 min against a 10s tick; process monitor and blackhole watcher 15 min, each beating inside their long-work loops — per restart-entry around the ≤300s backoff waits, per watch-file so a serial mass drop can't stack past the ceiling). Every heartbeat call is exception-safe (plumbing must never become a NEW kill path for the loop it protects), a missing/corrupt snapshot degrades to the pre-heartbeat checks, a future `last_beat` (wall-clock step) reads as age 0, and `main.py` resets the file at startup so a worker legitimately absent this boot (feature disabled, setup validation failure) can't inherit a stale ghost entry from before a `docker restart`. Workers unregister on purposeful stops (orderly shutdown, SIGHUP blackhole restart) so a disabled feature doesn't read as a dead thread. **The two verified kill paths are fixed in the same change:** the scheduler loop body was completely unguarded — a single `Thread.start()` failure (e.g. `RuntimeError: can't start new thread` under resource pressure) killed all periodic work permanently, and the just-set `task.running` flag stayed `True`, locking that task out of ever being scheduled again even if the loop had survived; both are now handled (spawn failures log and clear the flag, plus a belt-and-suspenders guard around the whole tick). Likewise the process monitor's `_handle_restart` call was unguarded — a raise from `restart_process()` killed the monitor, after which no process ever auto-restarted; it now logs and keeps the loop alive. **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded into the same change:** (a) the snapshot write happens *under* the registry lock — two concurrent flushes writing outside it race on `os.replace` ordering, and a beat's snapshot landing after an unregister's empty snapshot would have resurrected the removed entry on disk as a stale ghost; (b) timestamps are `time.monotonic()` (system-wide on Linux), not wall clock — an NTP step correcting a drifted RTC would have instantly aged every live worker past its ceiling; cross-boot leftovers read as age 0 (fail-safe) until the startup reset clears them; (c) `heartbeat.reset()` moved *before* `run_validation()` so a wedge anywhere in startup can't leave the previous run's entries aging toward a restart storm; (d) the watcher's beats are gated on its own stop event — the SIGHUP reload path stops the old watcher without joining it, and an old thread still mid-scan would otherwise keep beating the name the new watcher just registered, masking a wedge in the new thread; (e) `_cleanup_symlinks` beats per release dir — it resolves every symlink against the FUSE mount, and a slow-but-alive mount could stretch a large library's sweep past the ceiling. New tests: `tests/test_heartbeat.py` (registry semantics, throttled/exception-safe flush, `stale_entries` degradation matrix, scheduler-loop spawn-failure survival + running-flag reset, monitor-loop restart-failure survival, blackhole watcher register/unregister round-trip, post-stop beat suppression) and a `TestMainHeartbeatWiring` class in `tests/test_healthcheck.py` (stale → exit 1, fresh/missing snapshot → pass).

- **Blackhole: uncached-rejected grabs are now reported back to Sonarr/Radarr as failed downloads — the silent delete → identical re-grab loop is broken**: when the blackhole rejects a grab as uncached (and the TB cached-alternative recovery also declines), it deleted the watch-dir file silently — the arr was never told anything went wrong, so its next RSS/search pass re-grabbed the *identical* release, forever (observed live: the same hash re-dropped 25×/week). **Fix:** a new shared `mark_download_failed(info_hash)` on the arr client base (Sonarr/Radarr parity by inheritance) reports the failure via the arr's own failed-download machinery — exact queue lookup by `downloadId` → `DELETE /api/v3/queue/{id}?blocklist=true&skipRedownload=false`, falling back to `POST /api/v3/history/failed/{id}` on the grab-history record when the arr already pruned the queue item — so the arr blocklists THAT release and immediately searches for the next candidate. Called at both uncached-rejection points in `_process_file` just before the delete. A persistent per-title strike cap (`arrfail:` attempt-ledger keys, per-episode for TV, keyed by the arr's IMDb id with a parsed-title fallback) bounds the escalation chain: each report makes the arr walk to its next candidate, so an entirely-uncached title would otherwise burn feedback on its whole release list — past `BLACKHOLE_ARR_FEEDBACK_MAX_STRIKES` (default 8) the reject degrades to the status-quo silent delete and the Wanted recovery pass owns the title. Blackhole label subdirs are authoritative for arr selection: `_resolve_arr_identity` (shared with the TB-alt path) now accepts the drop's label, so a movie-shaped release name under `sonarr/` can't be looked up in the wrong arr. Every accepted report logs a history event (new cause `arr_feedback_blocklisted`, Python formatter + JS mirror, golden-tested) carrying the strike count. Feature-gated by `BLACKHOLE_ARR_FAILED_FEEDBACK_ENABLED` (default ON, wired through `base/__init__.py`, `settings_api.py`, `CONFIGURATION.md`, `.env.example`); the strike cap follows the live-`os.environ` numeric-knob precedent (`BLACKHOLE_TB_ALT_MAX_ATTEMPTS`). Never raises — any failure degrades to the silent-delete status quo. **Reviewer-flagged fix folded in:** the history fallback now tries ALL 'grabbed' records for the hash instead of aborting on the first failed POST (a re-grabbed release leaves several grabbed records for the same `downloadId`). New tests: `tests/test_arr_client.py::TestMarkDownloadFailed` (queue delete with blocklist+research params, history fallback, second-grabbed-record retry after a failed POST, uppercase-hash normalization, Radarr-inherits-parity pin) and `tests/test_blackhole.py::TestArrFailedFeedback` (feedback fires before delete at both rejection points, strike cap stops reports, disabled flag / no-hash / unconfigured-arr no-ops, label override picks the right arr).

- **Library: Wanted recovery now terminally gives up on titles filter-blocked on RealDebrid *and* uncached on TorBox — the endless recovery loop finally has a floor**: a Wanted ghost whose top release is rejected by RealDebrid's May-2026 keyword filter (deterministic and permanent for that hash) and is simultaneously uncached on TorBox can never be recovered by either leg, yet `_recover_wanted_via_debrid` re-probed it on every scan forever — the RD leg conflated a permanent filter-block with a retry-worthy cache miss into one 7-day in-memory memo (lost on every restart), so nothing ever accumulated toward a terminal verdict. The new Stuck tab surfaced these titles but couldn't stop the churn. **Fix:** the RD leg now distinguishes a filter-block (new `'filter_blocked'` probe outcome) from a genuine cache miss, and when a single pass *confirms both* RD-filter-block and TorBox-uncached, it accrues a persistent, restart-surviving strike in the attempt ledger (`wantedblock:<imdb>`). After `WANTED_FILTER_GIVEUP_STRIKES` (3) confirming passes a top-of-loop guard skips the title entirely and one terminal history event fires (new cause `wanted_filter_giveup`, Python formatter + JS mirror, golden-tested). The strike is deliberately *not* accompanied by a cooldown memo — leaving both legs clear lets each re-confirm the doom on successive scans so the count can actually climb to threshold (an earlier draft stalled at 1 strike because the TB cooldown suppressed the TB leg after the first pass). Ambiguous passes never strike: a TorBox probe error falls back to the old cooldown + RD-miss memo, and a plain TB miss with no RD filter-block just cools down as before. The give-up is fully reversible — the Stuck tab's **Retry** button clears the `wantedblock:` key (alongside the existing `fg:`/`tbalt:` caps and recovery memos) and the ledger's time-decay prune sweep eventually expires it, so a title that becomes cacheable later gets another chance. Surfaced on the Stuck tab as a new `filter_giveup` reason chip ("Filter-blocked everywhere — recovery gave up"). Sonarr and Radarr both covered (the recovery pass is arr-agnostic — movies and shows flow through the same `targets` loop). New tests: `tests/test_library.py::TestWantedFilterGiveup` (5 tests — strike accrues only when both providers confirm doom in the same pass, three strikes log the terminal event exactly once then the guard skips further probing, TB-unavailable and TB-probe-error paths fall back without striking) plus two `tests/test_stuck.py` collector tests (give-up surfaces at threshold, stays quiet below it) and a `wantedblock:` reset assertion added to the clear-retry-state test, and a golden test for the new cause.

- **Activity: new "Stuck" tab — titles caught in retry loops finally have an operator surface (`/api/stuck`)**: the retry/give-up state that decides whether a title will ever be retried was scattered across four stores with no UI: `pending_monitors.json` (in-flight grabs that never completed), the persistent attempt ledger (`fg:`/`tbalt:` give-up caps), the scanner's in-memory Wanted-recovery memos (Torrentio-empty / RD-miss / TB-cooldown, invisible and lost on restart), and the history JSONL (failure streaks). An operator watching a title sit in Wanted for a week had no way to see *why* it's stuck or to un-stick it short of shell archaeology. **Fix:** a new read-only aggregator `utils/stuck.py` joins the four stores into title-level records — stale in-flight grabs older than `PENDING_WARNING_HOURS`, Wanted ghosts with active recovery memos or exhausted give-up caps, and ≥24h/≥3-event history failure streaks with no progress event after them (grab submissions count as progress, so an actively-worked title isn't flagged) — annotated with blocklist/RD-filter flags and human-readable reason chips, cached 60s (the history join walks the 30-day retention window and must not run per page-poll). Served by `GET /api/stuck` and rendered as a third Activity tab with a badge count and per-row actions (auth-gated): **Retry** clears the give-up caps, recovery memos, and any dismissal so the next scan retries — and for movies also chains the existing `/api/library/download` Radarr search; **Blocklist** feeds the existing manual-blocklist endpoint (hash when known, title-synthetic otherwise); **Dismiss** snoozes the row for 7 days, persisted as `stuckdismiss:` attempt-ledger keys so no new store (and no new env var) is needed and dismissals survive restarts. Supporting plumbing, each independently useful: the scanner's three Wanted-memo dicts are now guarded by a class-level lock with `wanted_recovery_snapshot()` / `clear_wanted_memos()` accessors (previously the HTTP thread couldn't safely read them at all), `LibraryScanner.peek_data()` returns the cached scan payload without ever triggering a scan, `attempt_ledger.snapshot()` gives a race-free cross-thread copy of the ledger, and `history.events_since()` reads events from a start timestamp. Sonarr and Radarr both covered (title-level join is arr-agnostic). New tests: `tests/test_stuck.py` (26 tests — per-collector qualification/exclusion rules, dismissal TTL round-trip, retry-state clearing incl. sibling-key survival, cache behavior, collector-failure isolation, plus guards that every emitted reason slug has a UI label and every referenced cause slug exists in the history `CAUSE_*` vocabulary).

- **Library: Wanted recovery gained a RealDebrid leg — the add is the probe, RD drains the backlog without TorBox's cooldown**: the Wanted→TorBox recovery pass (below) is throughput-capped by TorBox Essential's ~24h create-volume abuse cooldown — measured net drain ~3 titles/day against a 240-unit backlog. RealDebrid has no create-volume cooldown, but its cache-query endpoint is dead (deprecated Nov 2024), so it couldn't participate in the "probe cache, add if cached" flow. **Fix:** the pass (renamed `_recover_wanted_via_debrid`) now runs an RD leg before the TB leg for each Wanted ghost, using the add itself as the cache probe: the top Torrentio release is added to RD (`RealDebridClient.add_magnet`), polled briefly for `downloaded` (instantly ready = cached, ~20s ceiling), verified against the May-2026 keyword filter via `probe_file` so a filter-blocked add is never counted as a recovery, and kept on success — otherwise the probe add is deleted and the title falls through to the existing TB trickle. Every attempt doubles as an RD cache-hit measurement: new history causes `wanted_rd_recovered` / `wanted_rd_uncached` (Python formatters + JS mirrors, golden-tested) make the RD hit rate on the backlog queryable from history. A TorBox account cooldown now disables only the TB leg instead of aborting the whole pass. RD misses are memoized per-title for 7 days (vs the 6h TB cooldown) so uncached titles aren't add/delete-churned against RD's API; transient add failures are not memoized. Torrentio candidates are now filtered against the blocklist in this pass (previously missing — a hash blocklisted after a prior filter block could have been re-added). Shared plumbing: `RealDebridClient` gained `torrent_status()` + `select_files()` (re-issues a selection that fired during magnet conversion and didn't stick), `debrid_client` exports `RD_READY_STATES`/`RD_FAIL_STATES`, and `debrid_routing.attempt_add_rescue` gained an opt-in `fail_states=` short-circuit so terminal RD states (`magnet_error`, `dead`, ...) fail fast instead of burning the full ready-timeout. The pass got its own 120s time budget (`_WANTED_RECOVERY_BUDGET_SECONDS`) — the previous 30s search budget would have capped it at ~1 RD attempt per scan. New env vars `WANTED_RD_RECOVERY_ENABLED` (default ON) + `WANTED_RD_RECOVERY_MAX_PER_SCAN` (default 4, counts attempts not successes) wired through `base/__init__.py`, `settings_api.py`, `CONFIGURATION.md`, and `.env.example`. Sonarr and Radarr both covered (single shared path). **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded into the same change:** (a) the per-title cooldown gate is now per-leg — previously a 6h TB cooldown (set on any TB miss) also suppressed the RD leg, contradicting the two-legs-are-independent design; empty-Torrentio results moved to their own leg-independent `_wanted_no_results` memo (short 6h TTL, so newly-released content isn't held back by the 7-day RD miss window); (b) if the RD account listing used for dedup is unavailable, the probe now bails *before* adding — RD returns the pre-existing torrent's id for a duplicate add, so an undeduplicated probe-miss delete could have destroyed a user's in-flight download; (c) local skips (dedup hit, listing unavailable) no longer burn the per-scan attempt budget — only real RD adds count; (d) an RD probe only starts when a full poll window fits inside the pass's 120s budget, so a clamped-short window can't misclassify cached titles as 7-day misses; (e) the blocklist filter guard widened from `ImportError` to any exception (a blocklist-internal failure now degrades to unfiltered results with a warning instead of aborting the whole pass), with the import hoisted out of the per-title loop; (f) the `wanted_rd_recovered` formatter default display-cases `RealDebrid` to match the TB formatter's `TorBox`, and a failed RD client init now logs a warning instead of silently disabling the leg.

- **Library: proactive Wanted → TorBox recovery — cached titles the arr never grabbed are now added directly**: The blackhole's cached-alternative recovery (below) can only fire when a grab actually *reaches* the blackhole. But a discriminating probe of the live "Wanted" backlog (titles Radarr/Sonarr monitor with no file) found ~93% of them already cached on TorBox at full resolution, yet stuck in Wanted forever — because the arr searches its own Prowlarr/Torznab indexer pool, a *different* population than the Torrentio feed zurgarr queries directly, so a grabbable release never surfaces and nothing reaches the blackhole to be rejected/recovered. The gap to 100% recovery on this branch is this acquisition path, not TorBox supply. **Fix:** a new effects-phase pass `LibraryScanner._recover_wanted_via_torbox` (runs right after the gap-fill missing-episode search in `_scan_effects`) walks every Wanted ghost — released movies and shows (probing the first still-missing episode, defaulting to S01E01) — searches Torrentio by `imdb_id`, ranks candidates by quality score then seeds, probes the top `_WANTED_TB_MAX_PROBES=12` against TorBox's cache in one batched `check_debrid_cache(service='torbox')` call, and adds the best cached release straight to TorBox via the now-parametrized `search.add_to_debrid(service='torbox', cause='wanted_tb_recovered', source='library')`. The scanner's own symlink phase links it on a subsequent scan and the arr imports it from the mount. Bounded per scan (`WANTED_TB_RECOVERY_MAX_PER_SCAN`, default 10) to respect TorBox's 60-creates/hour cap, gated on TorBox's account-level cooldown (reuses `blackhole._check_torbox_cooldown`, cached), and per-title cooldowned in memory for 6h so a deep backlog isn't re-probed every scan and a probed-but-uncached title isn't re-walked. Complete no-op without TorBox + `TORRENTIO_URL` configured, or when `WANTED_TB_RECOVERY_ENABLED=false`. `add_to_debrid` gained behavior-preserving keyword-only params (`service`/`api_key`/`cause`/`source`) so it can target a specific provider instead of the RD-first auto-detect; the interactive-search UI caller is unchanged. New history cause `wanted_tb_recovered` (Python formatter + JS mirror in `activity_format.py`, golden-tested). Gated env vars `WANTED_TB_RECOVERY_ENABLED` (default ON) + `WANTED_TB_RECOVERY_MAX_PER_SCAN` (default 10) wired through `base/__init__.py`, `settings_api.py`, `CONFIGURATION.md`, and `.env.example`. Sonarr and Radarr both covered. New tests: `tests/test_library.py::TestRecoverWantedViaTorbox` (cached movie recovered with the new cause/title; disabled flag / no-Torrentio-URL / TB-cooldown / per-title-cooldown all no-op; budget caps adds; uncached title not added but cooled down; unreleased / no-imdb / non-wanted skipped; show ghost recovered with an `S01E01` episode string), five `add_to_debrid` override tests in `tests/test_search.py`, and two golden tests in `tests/test_activity_format.py`. Full suite: 2,406 tests pass.
- **Blackhole: TorBox cached-alternative recovery — uncached grabs no longer drop abundantly-cached titles to "Wanted"**: Root-caused from a "Sing 2 stuck in Wanted" trace. The blackhole's cache-aware routing rejects a grab when *that exact hash* is uncached on its routed debrid (and, on the strict cross-confirm path, also uncached on TorBox), then deletes the watch-dir file. But Sonarr/Radarr only ever hand the blackhole the single release their RSS/search happened to pick — so a title with a dozen TorBox-cached releases sits in "Wanted" forever just because the one hash the arr selected isn't cached. Live trace confirmed *Sing 2*: Radarr grabbed the `CYBER` 1080p release (genuinely uncached on both RD and TB), the blackhole rejected + deleted it, yet 6 of the 12 other approved 1080p releases of the same movie were cached on TorBox the whole time. This is a pipeline gap, not a TorBox supply problem (the Wanted backlog is ~97% TB-cached). **Fix:** at both uncached-rejection points in `blackhole.py::_process_file` (provider-confirmed-uncached and the TB-cross-confirmed branch), before deleting, `_try_torbox_cached_alternative` now resolves the title's IMDb id via Sonarr/Radarr (`_resolve_arr_identity`), searches Torrentio for same-title releases, probes TorBox in a single batched `check_debrid_cache(service='torbox')` call (RD/AD cache endpoints are dead post-Nov-2024, so TB must be probed explicitly — `search_torrents` would annotate via the primary RD and return all-None), keeps only releases cached on TB **at the same quality tier the arr already approved** (`_filter_candidates` + `_rank_within_tier` from `quality_compromise.py`, `min_seeders=0` since a TB-cached release streams from TB storage regardless of seeders), grabs the best via a throwaway `.magnet` on TorBox, removes the original watch-dir file, and starts a TB-routed symlink monitor (`_start_monitor(..., debrid='torbox')`). Excludes the rejected hash from candidates, requires a parseable tier (declines on `Unknown` rather than risk a below-profile grab), and is a complete no-op without TorBox configured. New history cause `tb_cached_alt_grabbed` (formatter + JS mirror in `activity_format.py`). Gated by new env var `BLACKHOLE_TB_ALT_RECOVERY_ENABLED` (default ON), wired through `base/__init__.py`, `settings_api.py`, `CONFIGURATION.md`, and `.env.example`. Sonarr and Radarr both covered (single debrid/label-agnostic path). **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded into the same change:** (a) the candidate list is now pre-filtered to the approved tier and ranked by seeders *before* the TorBox probe, and only a bounded top-K (`_TB_ALT_MAX_PROBES=12`) is probed — TorBox's cache endpoint is per-hash and capped at 25, so probing the full unranked Torrentio result set (often 40-60+ streams across all tiers) would have left genuinely-cached same-tier releases past the cap looking uncached, silently defeating the recovery on exactly the popular titles it targets; the pre-rank also bounds worst-case added latency on the serial blackhole thread; (b) in symlink mode, if the TorBox torrent id can't be extracted or `_start_monitor` errors, the method now declines (leaves the original for normal rejection handling) instead of deleting the original and returning success — previously that path orphaned the torrent on TorBox with no monitor and no symlink, losing the title exactly like the "Wanted" failure this feature prevents; the monitor is started *before* the original is removed; (c) the post-recovery history/notify/metrics tail is wrapped so a bookkeeping failure can't raise out of the `_process_file` worker after state was already mutated; (d) the hash-validation regex accepts mixed case to match the established `_submit_compromise_candidate` pattern, and `tempfile` moved to module-level imports. Thirteen new tests in `tests/test_blackhole.py::TestTorboxCachedAlternative` (disabled-via-env / no-hash / no-TB-key / unparseable-tier / no-IMDb all decline and keep the file; no-cached-alt and wrong-tier-alt decline; rejected-hash excluded from candidates; happy-path grabs the best-seeded cached alt, removes the original, logs `tb_cached_alt_grabbed`; TorBox-add failure keeps the file; symlink-mode starts the TB monitor + removes the file; symlink-mode missing-torrent-id declines + keeps the file) plus two golden tests in `tests/test_activity_format.py`. Full suite: 2,333 tests pass.

### Changed

- **Library: Wanted recovery goes TorBox-first — the RealDebrid leg is demoted to a fallback that only fires on titles TorBox doesn't have cached**: live measurement settled the leg-value question — the RD "add is the probe" leg ran first on every Wanted ghost and hit ~8% cumulative (12 recoveries / ~145 attempts, 0-for-37 over the final 24h) while the TB leg delivered 75 recoveries against a backlog that's 93-97% TB-cached; RD-probing a TB-cached title is pure add/delete churn plus up to ~20s of ready-polling for a copy TB serves anyway. **Fix:** the per-title loop in `_recover_wanted_via_debrid` now runs the TorBox cache probe first — a TB-cached title is claimed by the TB leg exclusively (the RD probe never fires on it, even when the TB add fails transiently: the cached TB copy is the cheaper retry once the cooldown memo expires) — and the RD leg fires only when TB confirms uncached or can't answer (probe error, leg disabled, budget spent, per-title cooldown), so RD keeps draining whatever TB genuinely lacks and keeps working when TB is unavailable. Terminal give-up semantics are unchanged: the `wantedblock:` strike still requires RD-filter-blocked AND TB-uncached confirmed in the same pass, ambiguous passes (TB probe error / TB leg not run) still never strike, and persisted `rdblock:` verdicts still count as the filter-block half without burning RD budget. The RD probe's full-poll-window headroom check moved from loop-top to RD-leg time, since the TB leg now consumes pass budget before RD starts. Sonarr and Radarr both covered (the pass is arr-agnostic). `CONFIGURATION.md` + docstring updated to describe the new leg order. Tests: three new ordering tests (TB-cached title never probes RD; failed TB add on a cached title still doesn't fall through to RD; TB probe error lets RD fire) and the existing RD-leg suite re-pinned to the TB-uncached precondition it now requires.

- **System page: Debrid Health cards decluttered — one headline number per card, details collapsed**: the RD and TorBox cards showed raw counts, config pills, explanatory paragraphs, and buttons at equal visual weight, burying the two numbers that matter. Each card now leads with a single large headline — RD: the filter-blocked count (green 0 / red when >0, with "N of T probed healthy · last sweep age" as the secondary line, or a muted dash before the first sweep); TorBox: the existing rescue-rate % (falling back to the rescued count when no RD filter-blocks are recorded yet) — and the counts breakdown, remediated-24h, auto-remediate/cross-rescue config state, and explanatory notes moved into a collapsed "Details" disclosure. The card header keeps only the provider pill, plus a "Disabled" pill when the sweep is off. Purely presentational — no API or data changes. **Follow-up**: both cards carried the same generic "Debrid Health" title with only a small provider pill telling them apart — cards are now titled by what they measure ("RD Filter Blocks" / "TorBox Rescues") under a single "Debrid Health" section heading, and the now-redundant provider pill is gone (the "Disabled" sweep pill remains).
- **Wanted→TorBox recovery now trickles creates instead of bursting (`WANTED_TB_RECOVERY_MAX_PER_SCAN` default 10 → 2)**: live telemetry showed a 5-creates-in-one-minute recovery burst arming TorBox Essential's ~24h account-cooldown minutes later, capping effective recovery throughput at ~5 adds/day — the cooldown the burst triggers costs far more than the smaller per-scan cap does. At 2 per scan (~11 min apart) the same backlog drains at up to ~250/day without presenting as a create-volume burst. Explicitly-set env values are unaffected; docstring, settings-UI help text, `CONFIGURATION.md`, and `.env.example` updated with the rationale.

- **rclone config generation deduplicated between `setup()` and `regenerate_config()`**: both functions carried character-identical copies of the RD/AD/TorBox remote-stanza writing block — a drift trap that already bit once (TorBox had to be retrofitted into `regenerate_config` after landing only in `setup`). Both now call a shared `_write_rclone_config()` / `_write_zurg_remote()`; as a side effect, SIGHUP regeneration now also emits the incomplete-TorBox-config warning and the config-backup log line. The dead `key_type` parameter was dropped from `get_port_from_config()`. `rd_port`/`ad_port` are now always initialized (None when the provider is unconfigured) instead of relying on the mount-name invariant to avoid a `NameError`.
- **Silent `except: pass` blocks in the library scanner now log at debug**: the pre-FUSE-scan RC refresh and the two post-rescan `retry_counter.reset` calls swallowed all failures invisibly; each now emits a debug line so "RC not up yet" is distinguishable from "import broken" when diagnosing cold-cache scans or stale retry annotations.
- **`MEDIA_EXTENSIONS` parity is now enforced by a test**: CLAUDE.md documents that the three definitions (library.py / blackhole.py / scheduled_tasks.py) must stay identical, but nothing enforced it — a divergence would make files visible to symlink creation but invisible to verification. New `tests/test_media_extensions.py` fails on any drift.
- **Library: arr title-match cascade consolidated into one helper**: the exact → `_norm_for_matching` → TMDB-ID → prefix-match cascade existed as four hand-synced inline copies (movie/show symlink dir selection, movie/show post-symlink rescan trigger) that CLAUDE.md could only protect with a "keep all four symmetric" warning. All four sites now call a single `_match_arr_entry()`, with the TV-only season-aware sub-step gated on `is_tv`. As a hardening side effect, a malformed `pending_monitors.json` episode entry (missing season/episode key) can no longer abort the whole missing-episode search loop.
- **Library: pending-retry staleness logic deduplicated**: the shows and movies paths of `_search_for_missing_episodes` carried copy-pasted (and already drifting) blocks deciding give-up vs retry vs wait for pending entries; both now call a shared `_check_pending_freshness()` returning an explicit verdict.

### Fixed

- **Library: Wanted→TorBox recovery no longer self-starves on TorBox's advisory cooldown flag — and RD filter-block verdicts now survive restarts**: two recovery-throughput bugs closed in one pass. **(1) Advisory cooldown treated as enforcement:** the Wanted→TB leg skipped the entire pass whenever `/user/me` reported a `cooldown_until`, but on Pro plans that flag is ADVISORY — organic creates succeed while it's set, and organic traffic keeps re-arming it, so the leg logged "skipped — cooldown for 83693s" on every scan indefinitely while 181 blackhole creates/week sailed through on the same account. The leg now always attempts its first add (the pre-check is logged as informational only); ENFORCEMENT is detected from an add actually *failing* while the cooldown flag is set — the leg then backs off for the rest of the pass instead of burning the remaining TB budget against a genuinely-cooling account. The enforcement re-probe passes a new `force_refresh=True` through `_check_torbox_cooldown` to bypass its 30s success cache (the advisory pre-check may have cached 0 seconds before this add armed the cooldown — a cached read would mask the just-armed flag and skip the back-off), and duplicate-add results are exempt (a dedup hit proves nothing about enforcement). **(2) RD filter-block verdicts died with the process:** RD's keyword filter rejects the same hash deterministically forever, but the Wanted pass's RD-miss memo is in-memory — observed live as 6-7 redundant probe adds of the same blocked hash per title across a multi-deploy weekend, each burning an RD add + delete and a per-scan budget slot. A confirmed filter block now persists a `rdblock:{hash}` attempt-ledger verdict; the recovery loop checks it *before* the probe, treating the hash as filter-blocked with no API call and **no RD budget spent**, while the TB leg still runs so `wantedblock:` give-up strikes keep accruing toward the terminal verdict. Cleared by the ledger's 30-idle-day decay prune — the safety valve if RD ever unblocks a hash. **Reviewer-flagged fix folded in:** only a genuine `infringing_file` probe verdict persists — `probe_file` also returns `status='blocked'` for a bare HTTP 404 (`not_found`: transient hoster-indexing lag on a just-added torrent), and persisting THAT would lock a possibly-cached hash out of the RD leg for 30 days on a transient error. Sonarr and Radarr both covered (the pass is arr-agnostic). New tests: `tests/test_library.py` advisory/enforcement matrix (cooldown-flag-set proceeds; add-failure-with-flag disables the leg; add-failure-without-flag keeps it alive; duplicate exempt) and 7 rdblock cases (persist on `infringing_file`, no persist on `not_found`, persisted verdict skips the probe / burns no budget / still accrues the give-up strike) with an autouse ledger-isolation fixture, plus a `force_refresh` cache-bypass test in `tests/test_blackhole.py`.

- **Cross-rescue: two data-loss classes in the RD→TorBox rescue closed — a mislabeled symlink retarget and a hash-dedup delete could each destroy user data**: the debrid cross-rescue (re-hosts an RD-filter-blocked torrent on TorBox and retargets the arr library symlinks to the TB mount) carried two latent data-loss bugs. **(1) Symlink retarget over-match:** the health sweep's `filename` for a multi-file RD torrent can be an INNER file path (`Show/S01/ep.mkv`), not the torrent's top-level folder — anchoring the retarget on that basename stem could match and rewrite an UNRELATED torrent's symlinks (e.g. a single-episode release of an episode a season pack also contains) to a mount that lacks its content, silently breaking playback of the innocent title. **Fix:** `_release_anchor_candidates()` now derives authoritative folder names from RD's `/torrents/info` (`filename` / `original_filename`), adding per-file `files[].path` first-components only when no authoritative name exists *and* every file path shares one unanimous first component (a wrapper-less torrent's loose `Season 01/`, `Sample/`, `CD1/` dirs are never trusted individually); `.`/`..` components are rejected. `_retarget_symlinks_to_alt()` matches whole directory components (not substrings) against that candidate set and substitutes TorBox's real on-disk folder (the mylist gotcha — first component of `files[].name`) for the matched component so the rewritten link resolves. When no anchor is derivable the retarget is *declined* (new `anchor_underivable` outcome) rather than guessed — the content is still reachable via TorBox, existing symlinks are left untouched. **(2) Pre-existing-torrent delete:** RD and TorBox add endpoints hash-dedup — adding a magnet the account already holds returns the USER'S pre-existing torrent id, so a rescue that failed to reach ready and cleaned up its "own" add would delete the user's content. **Fix:** a shared `make_preexisting_check()` factory (`debrid_routing.py`) builds a guard that reads the alt entry's creation timestamp (`added` for RD, `created_at` for TorBox) and skips the cleanup delete when it predates the probe (minus a 30s clock-skew grace); it is conservative on *every* uncertainty — no `torrent_info` method, non-dict response, missing/unparseable/naive-timezone timestamp all classify as pre-existing (an orphaned probe entry is a tolerable leak; a wrong delete is not). Wired into all three `attempt_add_rescue` cleanup-delete sites (library Wanted-RD probe, health-sweep cross-rescue, blackhole add-time rescue) plus the library filter-block probe delete; the Wanted-RD dedup listing now force-refreshes past the 30s TTL cache (the staleness window is *correlated* with a user's manual add of a popular release — exactly when the probe grabs the same hash). Sonarr and Radarr both covered (all rescue paths are arr-agnostic). Also folded in: `RealDebridClient._pick_smallest_media_link` now reuses the new `torrent_info()` (gaining `_SAFE_ID` validation + key-scrubbed logging), the second alt-key lookup in `attempt_add_rescue` mirrors the `REALDEBRID`→`RD_API_KEY` env-name special-case (a bare `REALDEBRID_API_KEY` lookup would have handed the RD-as-alt direction an empty key), and both `torrent_info` methods now debug-log non-200 responses so a conservative skip-delete leaves a trace. New tests: `TestReleaseAnchorCandidates` (authoritative-name derivation, unanimous vs ambiguous `files[].path` fallback, no-extension-stripping, inner-path first-component-only, `.`/`..` rejection), `TestMakePreexistingCheck` (field precedence, grace-window arithmetic, naive-tz + unparseable + missing-method conservatism), the three `TestCrossRescue` retarget/decline cases, and the four `TestWantedRdRecovery` pre-existing-guard cases. Full suite: 2,651 tests pass.
- **Recovery metric: a scan with failed Sonarr/Radarr enrichment can no longer poison the daily snapshot**: observed live on 2026-07-05 — the first post-rebuild library scan hit transient DNS failures querying Sonarr (`GET /api/v3/series: [Errno -3] Try again`), so `_apply_sonarr_monitored_filter` silently fell back to the inflated TMDB-only missing math (unmonitored/unaired episodes counted as wanted) and the snapshot writer upserted `wanted` 194 → 585, dropping the System-page availability headline from 96.0% → 88.9% with zero real library change (on_disk actually rose by 1). The inverse failure also existed: a failed Radarr movie-list or Sonarr series-list fetch skips ghost injection entirely, *deflating* wanted. **Fix:** the three arr-list consumers (`_apply_sonarr_monitored_filter`, `_apply_sonarr_wanted_shows`, `_apply_radarr_wanted_movies`) now distinguish "fetch failed" (`None`) from "empty library" (`[]`) and report failures into a per-scan `arr_degraded` set stamped on the scan payload; `recovery.record_snapshot` refuses to write when the flag is non-empty (warning logged), so a same-day healthy snapshot survives and the per-day upsert lets the next healthy scan record the point — better a gap than a fiction. An unconfigured arr is still the normal TMDB-only state, not a degradation. Library UI/scan behavior is unchanged — only the daily metric write is gated. **Reviewer-flagged hardening folded in:** the warm-start cache loader (`_deserialize_cache_state`) strips `arr_degraded` from a persisted payload so a previous run's flag can never be replayed into a future snapshot write, and the skip-path log message stringifies tokens defensively. New tests: degraded-set flagging across all three helpers (fetch failure flags; empty-library and not-configured don't — full Sonarr/Radarr parity), recovery-side guards (degraded scan skips, preserves an earlier same-day healthy snapshot, empty `arr_degraded` records normally), and the warm-start strip.
- **Blackhole: TB-alt recovery no longer re-grabs the same failed alternative every ~6 hours — the retry loop that kept poking TorBox's abuse cooldown is closed**: when a cached alternative grabbed by `_try_torbox_cached_alternative` failed to import (mislabeled upload delivering the wrong episode, torrent ready on TB but no symlink ever produced, scanner-handoff limbo), nothing remembered the failure — so the arr's next search cycle re-entered the recovery just as the 6h sibling-dedup TTL expired and grabbed the *exact same* release again, burning a TorBox create and re-arming the Essential-plan abuse cooldown on every cycle until the 12-attempt give-up cap finally tripped (~3 days). Observed live on Arrested Development S04E01 (grabs at 19:50, 02:05, 08:12) and High Potential S01E07. Three defects fixed: (a) the candidate filter never consulted the blocklist — unlike the legacy alt-retry path and the main `_process_file` gate — so even a correctly-blocklisted alternative stayed selectable; (b) the post-import completeness audit *did* detect the mislabeled-episode case and tried to blocklist, but extracted the hash without the monitor's `debrid`, defaulting to the instance provider (RD) against a TorBox status payload — the RD shape lookup returned `''` and the blocklist add silently skipped, so no failure memory ever formed (`_audit_release_completeness` now takes `debrid` and threads it into `_extract_hash_from_info`); (c) the load-bearing memoization: every alt grab is now recorded in the attempt ledger under `tbalttried:<imdb>:s<season>:<hash>` at grab-commit time, and already-tried hashes are excluded from the candidate set *before* the probe slice (so dead candidates can't occupy cache-probe budget either). Recording at grab time rather than at import-failure time covers every failure mode without per-path report-back hooks: a successful import means the arr stops hunting, so the same (imdb, season) re-entering the recovery is itself proof the previous alt failed. Net effect: each cycle picks a genuinely different release or declines immediately (title falls back to Wanted), instead of 12 identical grabs. Tried-entries ride the ledger's existing 30-idle-day prune, so long-abandoned titles get a fresh chance. New tests: `TestTorboxAltRetryLoopBreaker` in `test_blackhole.py` (blocklisted candidate excluded; grab records the tried-key; second cycle picks the other candidate and third cycle declines with no TorBox create; season-scoping doesn't cross-bar S03 after S02) and a TorBox-payload audit test in `test_blackhole_completeness.py` proving the real hash now reaches the blocklist.
- **Anti-DMCA obfuscated releases (hex-named EZTV payloads) no longer break the import pipeline**: some indexers (EZTV notably) ship torrents whose folder and media files are a random 32-hex string plus a tracker tag (`1f9da83faaf847949e043d0dae9684aa[eztv.re].mkv`); the real release name lives only in the indexer listing / `.magnet` filename. Three pipeline steps that key off the on-disk name all mishandled them: the blackhole named completed-dir symlinks after the hex mount folder (so Sonarr/Radarr couldn't parse the import), the post-grab completeness audit ran `_parse_episodes` on the hex file name, got an empty episode set, and falsely flagged a correctly-delivered release as `release_incomplete` (blocklisting the hash + re-searching in a loop), and the library scanner imported the hex mount dirs as junk "movies"/"shows". **Fix:** a shared `is_obfuscated_name()` helper in `utils/blackhole.py` (strip a known media/`.magnet`/`.torrent` extension, strip `[tracker.tag]` bracket groups, and match a bare 16–64 char hex remainder). `_create_symlinks` gained a `display_name` param — when the mount folder is obfuscated it names the completed dir (and, for a single-media-file payload, the link basename) after the `.magnet`-derived real release name while keeping symlink *targets* on the real mount folder so they still resolve. `_audit_release_completeness` skips blocklist/history/re-search when every payload media file name is obfuscated. The library `_create_debrid_symlinks` movie and show loops skip obfuscated titles/mount dirs (lazy import to avoid the library↔blackhole cycle). **Reviewer-flagged hardening folded in:** (a) the hex floor was raised from 16 to 32 chars (real payloads are md5/sha1-length) to shrink the false-positive surface against short hex-lookalike release names; (b) the library show-loop guard now checks the mount folder name in addition to the parsed title, matching the movie loop (Sonarr/Radarr parity); (c) the multi-season split path now threads the display name through `_create_split_season_symlinks` so an obfuscated multi-season pack builds per-season dirs from the real release name (targets still resolve against the hex mount folder). New tests: `TestIsObfuscatedName`, `TestCreateSymlinksObfuscated` (incl. the multi-season case), `TestAuditReleaseCompleteness` in `test_blackhole.py`, and `TestCreateDebridSymlinksSkipsObfuscated` in `test_library.py`. **Follow-up:** the first round only guarded the library's *effects* phase (`_create_debrid_symlinks`), so obfuscated mount dirs still surfaced as hex "movies" in the Library UI via the read phase — all read-phase enumeration paths now skip obfuscated folder names at listing time: `_scan_mount` (FUSE, incl. flat layout), `_webdav_scan_mount` (Zurg PROPFIND), `_scan_torbox_via_api` (the actual TB enumeration path in dual-debrid setups — this was the one rendering the hex "Debrid TB" cards), and `_scan_local_movies`/`_scan_local_shows` (hides pre-fix hex leftovers in the local library). Tests cover each path, including the only-obfuscated-category WebDAV case (must not poison the recursive-PROPFIND-unsupported memoization).
- **Recovery auto-add paths now sanity-check release names against the target title — mislabeled Torrentio uploads can't land in the wrong slot**: Torrentio's stream lists are keyed by IMDb id but polluted with mislabeled uploads, and both auto-add recovery paths trusted them blindly. The first live RD-leg pass proved it: The Fountain's `tt0414993` list ranked a mislabeled "Fight Club (1999) AI UHD" 2160p junk entry (3 seeds) above the real "The Fountain 2006 1080p BluRay" (57 seeds) because ranking is (quality score, seeds) — so the RD leg added Fight Club into The Fountain's slot and logged it as a recovery. **Fix:** a shared `release_matches_title()` predicate in `utils/library.py` — parses the release name (`parse_release_name`), strips a trailing "(YYYY)" from the media title, normalizes both via `norm_for_matching` (transliteration + punctuation-insensitive), and accepts an exact match or the media title as a token-aligned prefix of the release name ("F1" → "F1 The Movie") but never the reverse, so a short junk name can't claim a longer title; empty normalized forms never match. Applied at both auto-add sites: the Wanted recovery pass filters Torrentio results before either leg runs (an all-mismatched result set lands in the no-results memo), and the blackhole's TorBox cached-alternative recovery requires candidates to match the arr-approved release name parsed from the grabbed file. Filter failures fail open in the Wanted pass (unfiltered results + warning) and fail closed in the blackhole path (decline — its existing behavior). Interactive-search UI adds are untouched — a human picks those. **Reviewer-flagged hardening folded in:** (a) a leading article is stripped on both sides before comparing — scene names routinely omit the "The" the arr keeps (`Big.Bang.Theory` vs `The Big Bang Theory`), which would otherwise soft-starve those titles through the no-results memo every scan; (b) a year cross-check closes the sequel/remake hole the prefix rule opens — when the media year is known (Wanted pass forwards the ghost's `year`; the blackhole extracts it from the grabbed filename) a release whose embedded years all sit more than ±1 away is rejected, so `Dune Part Two 2024` can't ride the prefix rule into `Dune` (2021)'s slot and `It 2017` can't claim `It` (1990); years that are the title itself ("1917") don't count as release-year evidence, and a release with no year at all skips the check; (c) a ghost with no title now logs a warning and fails open instead of silently skipping the filter. New tests: `TestReleaseMatchesTitle` golden cases (mislabel rejection, transliteration, prefix direction, article-strip, sequel/remake year rejection, year-in-title, ±1 tolerance, TV/year forms, empty-collapse), two Wanted-pass filter tests (mislabeled top result skipped → real release probed; all-mislabeled → memoized), and a TB-alt mislabel-exclusion test.
- **Wanted→RD recovery: an HTTP 451/403 at addMagnet time is now classified as filter-blocked, not transient — blocked titles stop burning the RD budget every scan**: RD's keyword filter can reject a magnet at `addMagnet` time (observed live: Maharaja, No Other Choice, Sentimental Value all got HTTP 451 on the first post-filter pass), but `RealDebridClient.add_magnet` returned `None` without the status, `attempt_add_rescue` mapped that to a generic `add_failed`, and `_wanted_rd_probe_add` treated it as transient — no 7-day miss memo, no history event. Consequence: every RD-filter-blocked title was retried on *every* scan forever (that first pass burned all 4 per-scan RD budget slots on titles that can never succeed), churning RD's API, and the silent failures were invisible to the `wanted_rd` hit-rate measurement (undercounted denominator). **Fix:** `add_magnet` now records the failing HTTP status on the client (`last_add_status`, reset on each call), `attempt_add_rescue` surfaces it to callers as `http_status` in its add-failure results (part of the return contract, not a client-internals side channel — reviewer-preferred shape), and the Wanted probe's not-rescued branch checks it — a 451 or 403 (the same codes `probe_file` classifies as blocked) memoizes the 7-day `_wanted_rd_miss` and logs `wanted_rd_uncached` with the new reason `infringing_add`, distinct from the post-add `infringing_file` so the two block points stay distinguishable in history while both count under the same cause for the hit-rate measurement. Genuinely transient failures (network error, 5xx, 429) keep the no-memo retry behavior. The activity formatter (Python + JS mirror) renders `infringing_add` with the same "filter-blocked on RealDebrid" wording. New tests: add-time 451 memoizes + logs `infringing_add` with the TB fallback still running, 403 via `add_error` also memoizes, 5xx stays transient, plus a formatter golden test.
- **Debrid Health: deleted torrents no longer haunt the RD card as permanently-blocked ghosts**: the sweep's probe loop only iterates `list_torrents()`, so once auto-remediation deleted a filter-blocked torrent from RD its state entry was never revisited — and never removed. Production showed the symptom at full scale: the card claimed 34 blocked of 165 probed while RD actually held 120 torrents with zero currently blocked (all 34 were remediated-then-deleted ghosts, plus 11 more stale healthy/unknown entries). The ghosts also polluted `get_blocked_hashes()` (library enrichment flags) and skewed the TorBox card's rescue-rate denominator. Each sweep now prunes state entries whose hash is absent from the freshly-fetched RD torrent list — no extra API call, and an interrupted sweep still gets the cleanup because the prune persists before the probe loop starts. **Reviewer-flagged hardening folded in:** the prune refuses to act on a possibly-incomplete list — it skips when the response is empty (indistinguishable from a soft failure, and a truly empty account has nothing worth pruning) or sits at the client's 2500-item page limit (`list_torrents` doesn't paginate, so an at-limit response may be truncated and page-2 torrents would read as deleted); and `RealDebridClient.list_torrents()` now raises on a non-list payload (RD can return HTTP 200 with an error dict) instead of silently returning `[]`, which consumers would have read as "account is empty".
- **System page: TorBox rescue counter no longer shows 0 while real rescues exist — counts derived from history, not the sweep-state flag**: the TB card's `rescued` count read the sweep-state `rescued=True` flags, which only the sweep's own cross-rescue path sets (never fired in production) and which a re-probe wipes anyway because it rebuilds the state entry from scratch — so a live system with 318 actual rescues (247 blackhole cached-alternative grabs + 71 Wanted-backlog recoveries) displayed "rescued: 0" and no rescue rate. The counter (and the summary's `rescued_24h`) is now derived from history events across all three rescue causes (`debrid_rescued`, `tb_cached_alt_grabbed`, `wanted_tb_recovered`), bounded by the 30-day history retention window; UI labels/tooltips now say "in 30d" and describe the rate denominator accurately (rescues over 30d vs. titles the *latest sweep* still reports blocked). One shared single-pass counter (`history.count_by_cause_windows`) feeds rescued-total, rescued-24h, and remediated-24h so the 30-second summary poll reads the history file once instead of three times. History reads also gained `errors='replace'` so a torn multibyte write can't poison every history consumer with a `UnicodeDecodeError`.
- **Blackhole: local-library dedup no longer misses punctuation-bearing titles (duplicate imports)**: `_check_local_library` compared the parsed release name (punctuation already stripped by `parse_release_name` — dots/hyphens become spaces) against raw on-disk arr folder names (punctuation preserved) using only lowercase + year-strip normalization, so `Whats.Eating.Gilbert.Grape.1993…` never matched the local `What's Eating Gilbert Grape (1993)` folder and the release was imported to debrid a second time. Same class of miss for any apostrophe/colon/ampersand title (`Schitt's Creek`, `Marvel's The Avengers`). The folder loops now fall back to a punctuation-insensitive compare (`norm_for_matching`) when the strict compare misses, with the year stripped *before* fuzzing (fuzzy normalization keeps digits, so `(1993)` would otherwise survive as a token on the folder side only) and empty fuzzy forms (non-ASCII titles that collapse under transliteration) never matching each other.
- **Blackhole: TB-alt sibling-grab suppression now survives container restarts**: the 6h dedup window that stops sibling episodes of the same season from each grabbing another season pack lived only in an in-memory dict — a restart mid-backfill wiped it, re-enabling redundant TorBox creates (exactly the volume events that arm TB Essential's abuse cooldown) and burning the `tbalt:` give-up ledger. Grabs are now mirrored into the persistent `attempt_ledger` under a separate `tbaltdedup:{imdb}:s{season}` key family (new `attempt_ledger.last_seen_epoch()` helper); the in-memory dict remains the fast path and the ledger answers after a restart, with the same TTL applied on read.

- **Blackhole: alt-release and quality-compromise grabs now bind torrent-ID extraction and the symlink monitor to the routed provider**: when a file was routed to TorBox (RD primary) and the add was rejected, the alt-release/compromise chain submitted the alternative via the routed handler but extracted the torrent ID with the *primary* provider's schema — for RD that's `str(result)`, i.e. the raw TorBox response dict stringified — and started the monitor against the primary's API. The monitor's status poll returned nothing and timed out: content landed on TorBox but was never symlinked, never imported, and the torrent sat orphaned. The routed service name is now threaded through `_try_alternative_release` → `_try_releases` / `_try_compromise` → `_submit_compromise_candidate` into `_extract_torrent_id(debrid=...)` and `_start_monitor(debrid=...)`.
- **Blackhole: rescue orphans recovered at startup are no longer marked alt-exhausted (permanent dead-end)**: `_recover_alt_pending` flagged *every* stranded `.alt_pending/` file `alt_exhausted`, including `.rescue-<uuid>-` cross-rescue orphans — which never ran an alt-release search. Since `_retry_failed` skips alt-exhausted files unconditionally, a container restart mid-rescue permanently stranded the release in `failed/` with retries remaining. Rescue orphans now recover without the flag so the normal retry schedule re-submits them; alt-release orphans (no prefix) keep the exhausted marker as before.
- **Library: the pre-FUSE-scan recursive `vfs/refresh` no longer walks the TorBox mount (WebDAV listing 429s)**: when the WebDAV scan falls back to FUSE, the scanner warms rclone's dir cache with a recursive `vfs/refresh` across *all* registered mounts — but the TorBox mount is enumerated via the `mylist` API, so a recursive PROPFIND walk over it is pure collateral that trips TorBox's WebDAV listing rate-limit and starves real reads. `refresh_dir()` gained an `exclude_mounts` parameter (backed by a new `get_rc_urls_excluding()` in the RC registry) and the library scanner now excludes `TORBOX_MOUNT_NAME` from that recursive refresh. Targeted single-dir refreshes (grab-time `refresh_dir('')`) still cover every mount.
- **Security: plaintext debrid/Zurg password no longer exposed via `/proc/*/cmdline` during `rclone obscure`**: `obscure_password()` passed the plaintext password (`ZURGPASS` / `TORBOX_WEBDAV_PASS`) as an argv element to `rclone obscure`, making it readable in `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` for the child's lifetime — and `zurg/update.py`'s process sweep logs joined cmdlines at debug level, so the password could land in the shared log file. The error branch compounded it: `str(CalledProcessError)` includes the full argv, so a failed obscure (e.g. missing rclone binary) logged the password outright. **Fix:** the password is now piped via stdin (`rclone obscure -`, documented rclone behavior for exactly this purpose) and the error log reports only the exit code, never the command line.
- **Security/robustness: Zurg WebDAV credentials are now YAML-quoted in config.yml**: `update_creds` wrote `password: {value}` verbatim — a password containing YAML-significant characters (`:`, `#`, `%`, leading `!`, embedded quotes, leading/trailing spaces) either broke Zurg's config parse (startup crash-loop) or silently parsed to a *different* value than the user set. Credentials are now written as single-quoted YAML scalars with embedded quotes escaped, so any password round-trips exactly.
- **Debrid API keys can no longer leak into logs via urlencoded request URLs**: `debrid_client._sanitize_error` only did an exact-string replace of the API key, but `requests` exceptions echo the full request URL — and AllDebrid carries the key as an `apikey=` query param, where percent-encoding of any byte defeats exact matching. Error messages are now additionally scrubbed by regex (`apikey|api_key|token|key|bearer` query params + `Authorization: Bearer` headers), matching the redaction `search.py` already applies to history events.
- **Shutdown no longer aborts (and hangs until SIGKILL) when a dead FUSE mount is present**: the SIGTERM handler's unmount loop called `os.path.ismount()` bare, which raises `OSError` (ENOTCONN) on a FUSE corpse — the exception escaped the loop, skipped remaining unmounts and the clean `sys.exit(0)`, and left Docker to escalate to SIGKILL while the shutdown notification claimed success. The check is now per-mount fault-tolerant (an ENOTCONN mountpoint is treated as "needs unmounting") and a failed plain `umount` escalates to lazy `umount -l`.
- **Second "Save & Apply" during an in-flight config reload is no longer silently lost**: `_do_reload` skipped entirely when the reload lock was contended, but the running reload had already snapshotted `os.environ` — so env changes written just before the second SIGHUP were never applied until container restart. A contended trigger now queues a follow-up pass that runs as soon as the current reload finishes.
- **Trakt `.env` rewrite on config reload is now atomic**: the SIGHUP path rewrote plex_debrid's `.env` with truncate-then-write (`open(..., 'w')`); a crash or SIGTERM between the two left an empty file and silently broke Trakt integration. Now uses `file_utils.atomic_write` like every other config write.
- **Activity/history reads no longer stall event writers**: `/api/history` parsed the entire 30-day JSONL (potentially 100k+ events) while holding the same lock `log_event()` needs, so opening the Activity tab could block blackhole/library threads mid-scan on their history writes. The lock is now held only for the raw file read; JSON parsing happens outside it.
- **Port availability probe no longer false-negatives on TIME_WAIT sockets**: `is_port_available` now sets `SO_REUSEADDR`, matching how the Go services (zurg/rclone) actually bind — a recently-closed listener in TIME_WAIT no longer burns one of the 50 random-port attempts.
- **System → Logs tab no longer shows stale logs frozen at the last rollover**: The tab read the *lexicographically greatest* `ZURGARR-*.log` filename, but `CustomRotatingFileHandler` keeps the **active** file as `ZURGARR-<date>.log` and renames rolled-over content to `ZURGARR-<date>_1.log` (`_2`, …). Because `_` (0x5F) sorts after `.` (0x2E), `max(log_files)` returned the frozen `_1` backup, so after any size-based rollover the tab displayed old entries that stopped at the rollover instant (observed live: tab stuck at 09:34 while the container was actively logging at 20:59). **Fix:** `read_log_lines` now selects the file by mtime (`max(log_files, key=os.path.getmtime)`) — the file still being written always wins, across both same-day rotation backups and day boundaries. New test `tests/test_status_ui_enhancements.py::TestReadLogLines::test_prefers_active_file_over_rotation_backup`.
- **Blackhole readiness probe no longer fires an N-way root-relist burst that trips TorBox's WebDAV listing rate-limit (HTTP 429 storm)**: Diagnosed from a live 429 storm on `webdav.torbox.app` (30–40 "rate limit exceeded" responses per 60s window) that was distinct from the abuse-cooldown (HTTP 400 on createtorrent) and the add rate-limit (429 on createtorrent) — this one is TorBox's **WebDAV listing** rate-limit on PROPFIND. Phase 2 of `blackhole.py::_process_torrent` (wait-for-mount) kicked rclone to re-list the mount root via RC `vfs/refresh` once per monitor so a freshly-cached torrent surfaces without waiting out the 2h TorBox dir-cache (TorBox WebDAV has no working ChangeNotify polling, so the cache won't refresh on its own). On the FLAT TorBox mount that refresh is a full-root PROPFIND. When a season pack lands, its N episode monitors all reach Phase 2 within the same second and each fired its own full-root re-list — an N-way PROPFIND burst that trips the listing ceiling. **Fix:** a module-level coalescing guard `blackhole._coalesced_root_refresh` (lock-protected, claims the window *before* the best-effort refresh runs so concurrent callers can't double-fire) collapses the burst to at most one re-list per `_ROOT_REFRESH_COALESCE_S` (15s) window across all monitor threads — and because one root re-list surfaces every sibling episode at once, the suppressed monitors still find their content on the very next poll. Provider-agnostic and behavior-preserving for an isolated monitor (still refreshes once at start), and the window uses a monotonic clock (it's a pure elapsed-time measurement, so a backward NTP/VM-resume step can't over-suppress refreshes). Paired with lowering `TORBOX_RCLONE_TPSLIMIT_BURST` from 10→3 to cap the instantaneous transaction burst. New tests: `tests/test_blackhole.py::TestCoalescedRootRefresh` (5 cases — first call fires, an 8-monitor burst collapses to one, a call past the window fires again, 8 *real* threads racing the unseamed path collapse to one refresh, and a refresh failure is swallowed but still claims the window). Full suite: 2,449 tests pass. **Note:** this removes the self-inflicted listing burst; it does not raise TorBox's Essential-tier listing ceiling, which is plan-side.
- **Force-grab and TB-alt recovery loops no longer re-poke debrid forever on a stuck title (TorBox abuse-cooldown re-arming)**: Diagnosed from the recovery-metric plateau on this branch — the last ~6–7% of unrecovered media wasn't a TorBox *supply* gap (the Wanted backlog measured ~93–97% cached) but a self-inflicted *acquisition* throttle. Two independent loops re-acted on the same uncached / never-completing titles on every scan: (1) the library scanner's prefer-debrid **force-grab** path (`library.py::_search_for_missing_episodes` → `arr_client.ensure_and_search` interactive-grab) re-grabbed an already-present local file to push it to debrid, and (2) the blackhole's **TorBox cached-alternative recovery** (`blackhole.py::_try_torbox_cached_alternative`) re-grabbed a cached alternative every time the arr re-dropped the title's `.magnet`.
