Each add re-arms TorBox's per-tier abuse cooldown (a single add can arm a ~24h window), and with ~27–52 stuck titles cycling every ~6h the cooldown never decayed — starving the genuine Wanted→TorBox recovery (only ~15 real adds over 27 days). The loops *appeared* to have a give-up gate, but it keyed on the wall-clock age of a `created` timestamp that flapping (direction changes, momentary debrid resolution, clear+recreate) silently reset, so the attempt count never actually climbed. **Fix:** a new persistent attempt counter `utils/attempt_ledger.py` (disk-backed JSON at `/config/grab_attempts.json` via `file_utils.atomic_write`, restart-survivable since the loops span days, lock-protected, safe-when-uninitialized) gives each loop a real give-up cap keyed independently of any flap-prone state. (a) **Force-grab** (`library.py`): keyed `fg:{norm}:s{sn}` per show season / `fg:{norm}` per movie, bumped only on an *actual* force-grab (`ensure_and_search` now returns a `grabbed` count, so a plain search doesn't inflate the counter); at `FORCE_GRAB_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (default 12) the season/movie is skipped and the title is escalated to the existing `debrid-unavailable` give-up state (reuses its UI, cleanup, notify, and auto-resolution machinery — when the content later appears on debrid the counter resets and the entry self-heals). (b) **TB-alt** (`blackhole.py`): keyed `tbalt:{imdb_id}:s{season}`, bumped on each committed recovery; at `BLACKHOLE_TB_ALT_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (default 12) the recovery declines and the title falls back to "Wanted" instead of re-grabbing on every re-drop. Both counters reset when a title lands on debrid and time-decay after 30 idle days (pruned once per scan in `_clear_resolved_pending`). Two new env vars (`FORCE_GRAB_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, `BLACKHOLE_TB_ALT_MAX_ATTEMPTS`) wired through `settings_api.py`, `CONFIGURATION.md`, and `.env.example` (read directly from `os.environ`, mirroring `DEBRID_UNAVAILABLE_THRESHOLD_DAYS`). Sonarr and Radarr both covered. New tests: `tests/test_attempt_ledger.py` (8 unit cases — bump/get/reset/prune/persist/reload/corrupt-file/uninitialized-safe), `tests/test_gap_fill.py::TestForceGrabCap` (7 — bump only on force-grab, no bump on plain search, show + movie cap-and-escalate, movie bump, resolution resets the ledger, and a multi-season show with only one capped season is *not* escalated when another season errors transiently), and `tests/test_blackhole.py::TestTorboxAltGiveUpCap` (cap declines + keeps file, success bumps). A show is escalated to `debrid-unavailable` only when *every* actionable season hit the cap, so a transient per-season error can't prematurely give up on the whole title. Full suite: 2,444 tests pass. **Note:** this stops the *self-inflicted* cooldown re-arming; it does not change the underlying TorBox plan-side abuse policy.
- **Symlink cleanup no longer mass-deletes (and thrashes) TorBox symlinks when only the TB mount is throttled**: Live observation on prod — every library scan logged `Created 16 debrid symlink(s)` immediately followed by `Cleaned up 16 broken debrid symlink(s)`, looping each cycle, while TorBox was under a 429 read-throttle (Essential-plan abuse cooldown). Root cause is an RD/TB parity gap in the two symlink-cleanup passes' safety guards. Both `library.LibraryScanner._cleanup_broken_debrid_symlinks` and `scheduled_tasks.verify_symlinks` have a guard whose explicit purpose is "a stalled mount makes `os.path.exists()` return False for everything, which would cause mass deletion" — but each guard only confirms *some* mount is healthy (the categorized RD/Zurg mount), then proceeds to delete per-symlink based on `os.path.exists()`. When RD is healthy but the TorBox mount is throttled/stalled, the guard passes and every TB symlink — whose target the throttled mount momentarily can't serve — is deleted as "broken." The next scan finds the content readable again (`_create_debrid_symlinks` verifies a *resolving* video via `_find_largest_movie_video`, so the create side is correct) and re-lays the same links, which the following cleanup deletes again: a create/delete thrash that also fires a spurious arr re-search on each disappearance. **The create side decides presence correctly (resolving video); the verify/cleanup side decided absence by a weak proxy (one global mount-health check standing in for per-mount health).** **Fix:** both passes now compute mount health **per mount** and skip deletion for any symlink routed to a mount that isn't confirmed populated — so a throttled TB mount can no longer trigger deletion of TB symlinks, while broken symlinks on a healthy mount are still cleaned. New module-level helper `library._mount_has_content(mount_real, flat=False)` (categorized mounts need a non-empty `movies/`/`shows/`/`anime/` category; the flat TorBox mount needs a non-empty top-level listing). In `_cleanup_broken_debrid_symlinks` the old RD-only early-return guard is replaced by a per-mount health map over the (prefix, mount) pairs (aborting only when *all* mounts are dead) plus a per-symlink gate before the `os.path.exists` check — this also fixes a latent accumulation bug where an RD-down/TB-up state previously aborted the whole pass and let broken TB symlinks pile up. `verify_symlinks` reuses its already-computed `zurg_has_content`/`tb_has_content` booleans as the health map and gates each symlink before queuing it for deletion. The gate biases to safety (skip when uncertain): worst case a genuinely-broken link survives one extra cycle. New tests: `tests/test_library.py::TestMountHasContent` (4 unit cases) + `test_cleanup_keeps_tb_symlinks_when_tb_mount_unhealthy` (TB link survives an empty/throttled TB mount), `test_cleanup_removes_broken_tb_symlinks` updated to use a populated TB mount, and `tests/test_verify_symlinks.py::test_tb_symlink_survives_when_tb_mount_throttled`. Full suite: 2,412 tests pass. **Note:** this fixes the symptom (wasteful thrash + transient link teardown during throttle windows); it does not address the underlying TorBox Essential-plan abuse cooldown that causes the throttle, which is account/plan-side.
- **Library: nested-folder movie torrents now get a debrid symlink (detected-as-available but never linkable)**: Found by auditing the movie/Radarr side for the same White Lotus discrepancy class — a movie zurgarr shows as available while Radarr reports it missing. Live trace of the whole library (589 movies) found exactly one genuine offender, *The Artist (2011)*: the TorBox grab packs the 9 GB feature one folder deep (`The.Artist…/The.Artist…/The.Artist….mkv`) with only `.nfo`/`.txt` at the top level. `LibraryScanner._create_debrid_symlinks`' movie loop discovered the video with a **flat top-level `os.listdir(mount_dir)`** and, finding no media file, hit `if not media_file: continue` — so the movie was never symlinked. But scan-time *detection* (`_scan_read`) classifies a movie as on-debrid by checking the top level **and one level of subdirs** (the disc-rip reclassification path), so the movie showed "available" in zurgarr yet stayed `hasFile=false` in Radarr forever. The TV path has no equivalent gap: it symlinks the full episode path discovered during the scan (via `path_index`), not a re-`listdir`. **Fix:** new module helper `_find_largest_movie_video(mount_dir)` searches the top level first and only descends one level when the top level holds no video, mirroring detection's depth. The movie loop now uses the helper's relative path for the symlink *target* (so it resolves on the arr side) while keeping a flat basename for the local filename (the arr re-imports by content). **Reviewer-flagged hardening folded in:** the one-level descent skips `_SKIP_FOLDERS` (sample/extras/featurettes/subs/...) — the same set scan-time detection skips at the disc-rip reclassification path — so a large featurette in an `Extras/` subdir can't be mistaken for the feature when the top level is junk-only; top-level-first selection is unchanged from the prior flat code (no regression for standard releases). A reviewer also noted the existing basename-keyed idempotency guard (`if os.path.islink(local_path) or os.path.exists(local_path): continue`) could skip replacing a *stale* same-basename symlink pointing at a swapped-out release — left unchanged here because it self-heals: `_cleanup_broken_debrid_symlinks` translates each symlink target back to the rclone mount and removes the ones that no longer resolve, so the next scan recreates them (and the audit of all 589 production movies surfaced zero instances). Four new tests in `tests/test_debrid_symlinks.py` (nested-folder video creates a flat symlink with the nested target; top-level video preferred over a larger nested decoy; nested descent skips an `Extras/` featurette; existing flat behavior unchanged). Full suite: 2,389 tests pass.
- **Library: a dangling video symlink no longer counts as local content (recovery-metric integrity)**: Found by auditing the same bug class as the blackhole subtitle fix below. `LibraryScanner._has_media_files` (used by `_scan_local_movies` to decide a folder holds genuine local content) counted any media-extension entry where `f.is_file() or f.is_symlink()` — and `is_symlink()` is True for a *dangling* symlink. So a movie folder whose only video is a broken symlink (e.g. after a `BLACKHOLE_SYMLINK_TARGET_BASE` rename leaves old-prefix symlinks, or a non-debrid symlink whose target vanished) was classified `source='local'`, which **inflated `available_local`/`on_disk`/`pct_on_disk`** in the recovery snapshot and could hide the movie from "Wanted" — directly biasing the TorBox-viability metric this branch exists to measure. The upstream `_is_debrid_symlink_dir` guard already neutralizes folders of *debrid* symlinks (it matches the target string, so even broken debrid links are skipped); the residual leak was symlinks pointing outside every configured debrid base. The function's own docstring says its job is to *skip* dirs "whose symlinks were deleted" so the library can recreate them — a dangling symlink is morally identical to a deleted one, so counting it contradicted the function's intent. **Fix:** a symlink now counts only if it resolves (`f.is_symlink() and os.path.exists(f.path)`); real files still short-circuit via `is_file(follow_symlinks=False)` before any deref so a live mount isn't stat'd unnecessarily. The TV path (`_collect_episodes`, `is_file(follow_symlinks=False)` + extension filter) was already immune — this brings the movie path to parity. Two new tests in `tests/test_library.py` (dangling-symlink-only dir is skipped; resolving-symlink dir is still local) and one updated (`test_non_debrid_symlink_classified_local` now uses a resolving NAS target). Full suite: 2,386 tests pass. **Known pre-existing parity gap (not changed here):** the TV scan (`_collect_episodes`/`_count_show_content`) counts only real files (`is_file(follow_symlinks=False)`), so a personal TV library kept as symlinks to external storage is invisible to the local scan — the movie path supports such symlink farms but TV does not. Deferred deliberately: closing it would add a per-episode `os.path.exists` stat across the TV library each scan, reintroducing the FUSE-mount stat load this branch exists to eliminate.
- **Blackhole: a subtitle-only (or broken-symlink-only) local folder no longer permanently blocks a grab as "exists locally"**: User report — "I tried searching for Adolescence, but it says it exists locally already, although it's showing as missing in Sonarr." Root cause: `BlackholeHandler._check_local_library` decided "content exists locally" with bare `os.listdir(dir)` truthiness checks — *any* directory entry counted. In production `/local_media/tv/Adolescence/Season 01` held only 8 orphan `.srt` subtitle files (no video), so every Sonarr grab logged `[blackhole] Skipping Adolescence.S01…: 'Adolescence' Season 1 exists locally` and was deleted as a local duplicate, leaving the show permanently "Wanted" in Sonarr with no way to acquire it. The companion `_local_episodes` had the same flaw — it scraped `SxxEyy` episode numbers from *any* filename, so a stray `Show.S01E03.srt` made episode 3 look present. **Fix:** dedup now requires a *resolving video* file. New module helpers `_is_resolving_video(path)` (extension in `MEDIA_EXTENSIONS` **and** `os.path.exists` — so a dangling debrid symlink doesn't count) and `_dir_has_video(path, recursive=False)` replace the three `os.listdir` truthiness checks (season-pack, show-level recursive, movie); `_local_episodes` now only counts episode numbers from resolving video files. The check fails *safe*: a folder with no real video yields a (harmless, idempotent) duplicate grab rather than a permanent skip, and orphan subtitles are left untouched (no destructive cleanup — once the video imports they're inert). **Reviewer-flagged hardening folded in:** the recursive show-level scan descends exactly one level (`show/Season NN/episode.mkv` is the deepest real arr/Plex layout) instead of an unbounded `os.walk`, so a no-video show folder full of subtitle/metadata files can't trigger a full-subtree `stat` storm against a throttled FUSE mount; the `os.path.exists` dereference is retained deliberately (a dangling debrid symlink is the same permanent-block class this fix targets, so `lexists` would reintroduce it). New tests in `tests/test_blackhole.py`: five in `TestCheckLocalLibrary` (subtitle-only season pack / episode / movie all allow the grab; broken video symlink doesn't count; mixed video+subtitle still matches) plus eleven unit tests in `TestDirHasVideo` covering `_is_resolving_video`, `_dir_has_video` (flat/recursive/depth-bound/missing-path), and `_local_episodes` subtitle skipping. Full suite: 2,384 tests pass.
- **Library: TorBox enumeration now uses the `mylist` API instead of walking the FUSE mount — eliminates the recurring 429 storm at the source**: The hourly library scan enumerated TorBox content by walking the rclone mount (`_scan_mount(flat_layout=True)`): one `os.scandir` + `stat` per file across ~450 release folders, all over a mount throttled to `--tpslimit=5`. That walk contended with real content downloads on the shared 5-tps budget and produced 429 storms — in production it abandoned an in-flight download (Bad Sisters S02E07 hit rclone's "too many errors 10" ceiling and gave up). The earlier 429 fixes (cached-alternative dedup, slow-but-alive probe tolerance) treated symptoms; this removes the cause. **Fix:** new `search.list_torbox_torrents()` pulls the entire account (folder names + per-file relative paths/sizes) in ONE `mylist` HTTP call, and new `library.LibraryScanner._scan_torbox_via_api()` builds the same `(movies, shows)` output the FUSE walk produced — reusing `_collect_episodes_from_webdav`, `_parse_folder_name`, `_detect_tv_marker`, `_merge_show_group`, and `_get_movie_quality_from_webdav` — with **zero FUSE ops**. Each item's file `path` is synthesized as `<tb_mount>/<files[].name>` to match the rclone layout exactly, so `_create_debrid_symlinks`/`_resolve_symlink_target` and symlink verification resolve identically to a walked scan. **The on-disk folder is the FIRST path component of each file's `mylist` `name` (the original mount-relative torrent path) — NOT the torrent-level `name` field.** Live production validation found the entry-level `name` is a *sanitized display string* (dots→spaces, truncated, `&`→`and`) that matches the real rclone folder for only 102 of 490 torrents; deriving the folder from `files[].name` instead matched 485/490 entries (1717/1749 files). Keying paths off the display `name` would have synthesized dead symlink targets for ~80% of TB content. The FUSE mount is still discovered and retained — but only for symlink *targets* (real file access), not enumeration. The `_scan_read` TB block now calls the API scan; the truncation/last-good-baseline machinery is unchanged (an API *failure* — `None` — sets `_last_scan_mount_truncated` so the last-good TB set is unioned rather than dropping titles to "Wanted"; an empty account `[]` is a complete authoritative zero-item scan). `date_added` is now populated from the torrent's `created_at` (more meaningful than the old FUSE folder mtime). The `TORBOX_SCAN_TIMEOUT` env var — previously a FUSE-walk deadline — now caps the single `mylist` HTTP call (floored at 10s). `_scan_mount` is retained unchanged for the RD/AD categorized path. Sixteen new tests (`tests/test_search.py` mylist parse/failure/malformed/timeout; `tests/test_library.py::TestScanTorboxViaApi` movie/show split, synthesized-path-under-mount, season-subdir, no-prefix file, TV-marker fallback, API-failure-incomplete, empty-complete, no-key-incomplete, date_added; `TestParseTbTimestamp`); the four TB truncation-fallback tests were repointed from the removed `_scan_mount` TB call to `_scan_torbox_via_api`. **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded in:** (a) the `mylist` call now passes `bypass_cache=true` (mirroring `debrid_client.list_torrents`) so a server-side-cached stale page can't be promoted as the authoritative TB baseline and drop since-added titles to "Wanted"; (b) synthesized paths are built from cleaned path components (empty components dropped so a `//` can't reintroduce an absolute root, `..` rejected, bare root-level files skipped) so a crafted/odd API path can't make `os.path.join` discard the mount prefix and escape `<tb_mount>`; (c) malformed `mylist` elements (non-dict entry, `None`/non-list `files`, non-dict file) degrade to a per-entry skip rather than raising and aborting the whole scan into the last-good fallback. Full suite: 2,368 tests pass.
- **Startup: stale `/data/torbox` mount no longer survives a rebuild and leaves the container `unhealthy`**: After `docker compose up --force-recreate`, the TorBox rclone mount kept failing with `failed to mount FUSE fs: directory already mounted: /data/torbox`, exhausting its 5 restart attempts and pinning the container `unhealthy` (only torbox; the zurgarr mount came up clean). Root cause: the TorBox mount at container path `/data/torbox` is nested *inside* the `:shared` bind of host `/mnt/remote/realdebrid` → `/data`. A `:shared` bind is bidirectional, so when the prior container's rclone is SIGKILLed mid-shutdown (didn't unmount within the 30s stop timeout) it leaves a stale FUSE corpse on the host at `/mnt/remote/realdebrid/torbox`, and `--force-recreate` re-imports that corpse *into* the fresh container via the shared bind before rclone starts. The existing startup escalation (commit b43557a) ran a single lazy unmount (`fusermount3 -uz`, which detaches asynchronously) then *immediately* remounted — racing the deferred kernel teardown and losing, so rclone saw the path still mounted. The manual recovery (`docker exec … fusermount3 -uz /data/torbox` then `docker restart`) worked only because the restart interposed a full namespace recycle after the detach. **Fix:** the `except OSError` escalation branch in `rclone.py::_configure_mount` now calls a new `_force_clear_stale_mount` helper that escalates *and verifies* — it runs the unmount ladder, then polls `/proc/self/mountinfo` (via `_is_mount_point`, with octal-escape decoding for paths) until the mountpoint is genuinely gone and the dir is usable, retrying a bounded number of times (10 × 0.5s) before remounting. This closes the lazy-unmount race from inside the container, so the mount self-heals on *every* start regardless of how the container was (re)started. If the corpse truly can't be cleared, it now raises a descriptive error (caught by the per-mount isolation `try/except`, which logs + fires a `health_error` notification) instead of letting a generic `makedirs` `OSError` bury the diagnostic. The escalation remains reachable only after a healthy-mount `makedirs(exist_ok=True)` would have succeeded, so an actively-streaming mount is never force-detached (SIGHUP-reload safe). The host-side `rebuild.sh` was hardened in parallel (switch fuse2 `fusermount` → `fusermount3` matching the fuse3 mounts, plus a verify-gone poll before `--force-recreate`). Six new tests in `tests/test_rclone.py` (`TestIsMountPoint`, `TestForceClearStaleMount`). Full suite: 2,347 tests pass.
- **TorBox cached-alternative recovery no longer grabs multiple season packs for one season (429-storm fix)**: Follow-up to the cached-alternative recovery feature above. In production the feature worked but produced a TorBox `429 Too Many Requests` storm (~450 vfs errors in 30 min, with recovered titles never surfacing on the mount inside the 300s window → "not on mount after 302s — handing off to library scanner"). Root cause: when a whole season is uncached-but-Wanted, Sonarr drops every episode's `.magnet` at once; each episode independently ran `_try_torbox_cached_alternative`, and because a season pack takes ~300s to land and get symlinked, sibling episodes processed in that window each picked a *different* best-seeded cached release — so one season grabbed 3+ distinct full-season packs (observed: Yellowjackets S02 pulled the `edge2020`, AMZN-x265, and `Gh` packs). Re-adding the *same* hash to TorBox is idempotent, but three *different* complete-season packs triple the rclone vfs download load, overrunning the existing `--tpslimit=5` throttle. **Fix:** an in-memory per-`(imdb_id, season)` dedup guard (`_tb_alt_recent_grabs` + lock, mirroring the existing `_audit_retrigger` pattern). The first *committed* grab for a season records the key; sibling episodes within `_TB_ALT_DEDUP_TTL` (6h, sized to cover the grab→land→hourly-scan→symlink window) are skipped *before* the Torrentio search, sparing the TorBox cache-probe calls too. One cached pack recovers the whole season, so the first grab wins and the rest defer. The guard records only on success — a failed attempt (no cached alt, missing torrent id, monitor-start error) does not poison the season, so a later sibling can still recover; suppressed episodes keep their `.magnet` on disk and self-heal via the arr re-drop after TTL (delayed, never data-loss). Movies key on `(imdb_id, None)`, which never collides across titles. The existing `--tpslimit=5`/`--tpslimit-burst=10` on the TorBox mount was already deployed and left unchanged — the burst volume, not the per-request rate, was the problem. Four new tests in `tests/test_blackhole.py::TestTorboxCachedAlternative` (same-season suppression, different-season independence, failed-grab-doesn't-poison, movie None-season key). Full suite: 2,337 tests pass.
- **Container no longer flaps `unhealthy` (and the System page no longer flips the TB mount red) on every TorBox FUSE walk**: Follow-up to the TB scan-resilience fixes below. Once those landed, the library counts held through a TorBox `429` storm, but two *liveness probes* still treated a slow-but-alive mount as dead. (1) The Docker healthcheck (`healthcheck.py`) probed mount liveness with a bare, unbounded `os.listdir('/data/torbox')`; during a 429 storm (cold-start cache fill, or Plex walking the same mount) that listdir blocks, so Docker killed the whole probe at its 10s timeout (`Health check exceeded timeout (10s)`) and the container went `unhealthy` every 60s even though all processes were up and the mount was merely rate-limited. (2) The scheduler's `mount_liveness` probe (`scheduled_tasks._probe_mount`) already bounded its listdir via `_listdir_with_timeout`, but mapped the resulting `TimeoutError` to `status='error'` — so the System page mount tile flipped red on every TB walk, indistinguishable from a genuinely dead mount. **Root insight:** a *dead* FUSE mount (rclone process gone) raises `ENOTCONN` *immediately*; only a *slow / rate-limited* one blocks. So a timeout means "slow, alive" and an exception means "dead" — they must be handled differently. **Fix (both probes, symmetric):** (a) `healthcheck.py` now bounds each mount's listdir with a daemon-thread + bounded-join helper (`_listdir_with_timeout`, mirroring the scheduler's, safe to leak the worker because the healthcheck is a short-lived process) capped at `_MOUNT_PROBE_TIMEOUT_SEC=3`; a `TimeoutError` is treated as alive-and-slow (logs a stderr warning, does **not** add to `error_messages`), while a hard exception (ENOTCONN etc.) still fails the check. **Shared probe budget (reviewer-flagged):** the per-mount 3s cap alone wasn't enough — a 3-mount install (RD+AD+TB) all slow at once could stack 3×3s = 9s of mount probing plus a 3s status check = 12s, re-introducing the flap above Docker's 10s cap at higher mount counts. The 3 mount probes now share a single wall-clock budget (`_MOUNT_PROBE_BUDGET_SEC=6`): each call passes `min(per-mount cap, budget remaining)` so their SUM is bounded at 6s (the per-mount cap still applies so one slow mount can't starve a later, possibly-dead one of its turn, with a 0.5s floor so a dead mount can always raise ENOTCONN instantly). With the status-server probe timeout tightened 5s→`_STATUS_PROBE_TIMEOUT_SEC=3`, worst case is 6s mounts + 3s status = 9s, safely under Docker's 10s cap regardless of mount count. healthcheck.py's orchestration was moved under `def main()` + `if __name__ == '__main__'` so the helpers are importable/testable (it previously ran the live check on import). (b) `scheduled_tasks._probe_mount` grows a `tolerate_timeout=False` parameter; `mount_liveness_probe` passes `tolerate_timeout=True` for the TorBox mount only, so a TB listing that exceeds the 15s scheduler budget reports `success` ("Mount responsive but rate-limited") instead of `error`. RD/primary keeps strict semantics (a timeout there is still an error). A genuinely dead TB mount still raises ENOTCONN → `error`, so the Plan-39 "silently-dead TB mount" regression stays caught. **Not addressed here:** the 429 load itself — these probes just stop misreporting a rate-limited mount as dead. **Also (same probe):** the status-server check no longer logs a spurious "Status server is not responding on port 8080" warning on every run. The UI is auth-gated (`STATUS_UI_AUTH`), so the unauthenticated probe gets an HTTP 401 — which *is* a response (server up and serving), not a connection failure. The probe was extracted into a `_status_server_alive()` helper that treats any `HTTPError` (401 etc.) as alive and only warns on a connection-level failure (refused / reset / timeout). New tests: 12 in `tests/test_healthcheck.py` (listdir success/oserror/timeout; `_mount_alive` for not-a-mount / alive / ENOTCONN-dead / slow-timeout-treated-as-alive) and 2 in `tests/test_mount_liveness.py` (timeout is error by default; timeout tolerated returns success).
- **TorBox titles still dropped to "Wanted" on rate-limited scans — incomplete TB walks no longer overwrite the good library view**: Follow-up to the scan-budget fix below. After giving the TB walk its own 180s deadline, prod still logged `[library] Timeout during mount scan` every hour and the debrid count kept sliding overnight (movies 152→123, episodes 662→498, `pct_debrid` 17.6→13.8). Root cause was deeper than walk duration: TorBox returns `429 Too Many Requests` *during* the directory walk (`IO error: couldn't list files: rate limit exceeded` — triggered when a concurrent vfs downloader is pulling file content, e.g. Plex playback, which competes with the listing for TB's API budget). The walk grinds against retries until it trips the deadline, and crucially `_scan_mount` **discarded its `timed_out` flag** — it returned the partial result as if complete, so the caller fed a truncated TB set into the merge and every missing title flipped to "Wanted" again next scan. **Fix:** `_scan_mount` now records truncation on a new `self._last_scan_mount_truncated` instance flag (set on the deadline branch, on a mid-walk listing `OSError`/429, and on the cannot-list-mount early return) — without changing its `(movies, shows)` return signature, since ~10 tests unpack exactly two values. `_scan_read` reads the flag right after the (synchronous, single-threaded) TB scan: a COMPLETE scan is promoted to an in-memory last-known-good baseline (`_last_tb_movies`/`_last_tb_shows`); an INCOMPLETE scan unions the partial *over* that baseline via the new `_union_tb_items` helper (partial wins per normalized title so freshly-grabbed content still appears, last-good carries the rest), so a rate-limited walk can no longer drop titles. The exception path (`_scan_mount` raising) is also treated as incomplete and uses the same fallback. **Tradeoffs (documented in code):** the baseline is in-memory only (lost on restart — the first post-restart scan with no baseline degrades to today's behavior, then self-heals once one complete scan lands), and a title genuinely deleted from TB during a run of truncated scans lingers as "available" until the next complete scan re-promotes a fresh baseline — cheap next to flipping the whole TB library to "Wanted" hourly. **Not addressed here:** the 429s themselves (the external vfs downloader contending for TB's rate budget) — this change makes the scan *resilient* to them rather than eliminating them. RD is unaffected: it scans via WebDAV (the FUSE fallback that shares `_scan_mount` doesn't consult the flag). **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded in:** (a) the baseline is now `copy.deepcopy`'d on promotion — a shallow `list()` copy shared dict references with the items fed into `_merge_alt_debrid_items` (which sets `has_alt_source`/`alt_source_debrid` in place) and the downstream dedup/enrichment stages, so the "last-good" snapshot was being silently mutated by the current scan and a later truncated scan would carry over polluted entries; (b) `_union_tb_items` coerces `title` via `or ''` so a `None` title can't `AttributeError` in `_normalize_title`; (c) the union's normalized-title keying (same key `_scan_mount`/`_merge_alt_debrid_items` already use) is documented as introducing no new same-year-collapse beyond the pre-existing pipeline limitation; (d) the mid-walk `OSError` branch deliberately continues (best-effort across categories for RD) rather than breaking, now documented. Nine new tests in `tests/test_library.py::TestTbScanTruncationFallback` (flag set on deadline, flag set on a listing OSError/429, flag cleared on clean scan, union partial-wins + carry-over, union empty/None inputs, complete-scan promotes baseline, baseline isolated from downstream mutation, truncated-scan falls back to baseline, first-scan-truncated uses partial without promoting). Full suite: 2,305 tests pass.
- **TorBox titles intermittently flip to "Wanted" because the cold FUSE walk can't finish inside the shared 30s scan deadline**: User report — "Dungeons & Dragons: Honor Among Thieves (2023) shows as Wanted but I have it on TorBox." The acute cause that day was a dead `/data/torbox` mount (fixed separately in the startup-resilience commit), but the underlying systematic bug remained: `_scan_read` walked the TorBox FUSE mount under a single shared `deadline = start + 30s` covering the RD WebDAV scan, the TB walk, and the local scans. The TB mount is deliberately throttled (`rclone --tpslimit=5 --tpslimit-burst=10`, load-bearing to avoid TorBox's "too many errors 11/10" 429 cascade), so a cold walk of a large library (~450 release folders at 5 tps ≈ 90s) far exceeds the remaining budget. `_scan_mount` would hit the deadline, set `timed_out=True`, and **return partial results** — every TB title past the cutoff vanished from the scan, and because each such movie is Radarr-monitored + `hasFile=false`, it got re-injected as a `source='wanted'` ghost. The cache was cold on *every* scheduled scan because `LIBRARY_SCAN_INTERVAL` (1h) outlived the previous shared `dir-cache-time` default, so the dir cache always expired between scans. **Two-part fix:** (a) `_scan_read` now gives the TorBox walk its own deadline, `time.monotonic() + max(TORBOX_SCAN_TIMEOUT, 30)` (default 180s), measured fresh so the time already spent on the RD/WebDAV scan doesn't eat into it — TB gets the budget a throttled mount actually needs without loosening the 30s ceiling on the fast RD path; (b) the TorBox rclone mount now uses a longer `--dir-cache-time` (`TORBOX_RCLONE_DIR_CACHE_TIME`, default `2h`) so the dir cache outlives the hourly scan and most scans hit a warm cache instead of re-listing every folder over the throttled mount. New content still surfaces promptly because grabs call `refresh_dir('')` on every mount (TB included). **Reviewer-flagged hardening folded in:** the TB dir-cache branch in `rclone.py` is gated on `torbox_remote_written` (not just `mn == TORBOX_MOUNT_NAME`), so an RD/AD Zurg mount a user happens to name `torbox` (via `RCLONE_MOUNT_NAME` with TorBox unconfigured) doesn't wrongly inherit the 2h cache — matching the existing tpslimit gating. Two new env vars (`TORBOX_SCAN_TIMEOUT`, `TORBOX_RCLONE_DIR_CACHE_TIME`) wired through `base/__init__.py`, `settings_api.py`, `CONFIGURATION.md`, and `.env.example`; `library.py` reads `TORBOX_SCAN_TIMEOUT` live from `os.environ` so a UI settings change applies on the next scan without a restart. Full suite: 2,296 tests pass.
- **Startup: a failed secondary rclone mount no longer silently kills the task scheduler (zombie container)**: User report — "how come only the duplicate-cleanup task shows up in the Tasks list?" Root cause was a startup-abort cascade triggered by a stale FUSE mountpoint. On reboot, `/data/torbox` survived from the previous container as a dead mountpoint; `rclone.py`'s per-mount `umount` was `check=False` so it silently failed to clear it, then `os.makedirs("/data/torbox", exist_ok=True)` raised `FileExistsError: [Errno 17] File exists` (a stale mountpoint exists but isn't a usable directory). That exception hit `rclone.setup()`'s blanket `except Exception: logger.error(e); exit(1)`. `exit(1)` raises `SystemExit`, which is `BaseException` — **not** `Exception` — so `main.py`'s `except Exception` around the rclone setup call never caught it, and `main()` aborted at the rclone step, never reaching `register_all()` + `scheduler.start()`. Only `duplicate_cleanup` (registered one line earlier in `main()`) survived in the Tasks list; the always-on `housekeeping`, `library_scan`, `verify_symlinks`, `debrid_health_reconcile`, etc. were never registered and the scheduler never started. The container didn't even die cleanly: the Zurg auto-update thread (`auto_update.py`) was non-daemon and runs `while True`, so the interpreter's shutdown blocked forever joining it — leaving a half-alive container that passed Docker health checks (status server up, library scanner still running on its own thread, rclone/zurg subprocesses still up) while the scheduler was dead. **Three-part fix:** (a) **per-mount isolation** — the mount loop body in `rclone.setup()` is wrapped in `try/except` that logs, fires a `health_error` notification, and `continue`s, so one mount failing (stale mountpoint, unreachable WebDAV) no longer aborts the other mounts or the rest of startup; (b) **robust stale-mount cleanup** — the plain `umount` + `os.makedirs` is kept for the normal path (plain umount no-ops on a healthy busy mount via EBUSY, which matters because `setup()` also runs on SIGHUP reload), and only when that `makedirs` raises `OSError` (proving the path is a dead mountpoint) does it escalate to lazy unmounts (`fusermount3 -uz` → `fusermount -uz` → `umount -l`, each wrapped to tolerate a missing binary since the fuse3-based image ships only `fusermount3` — without that tolerance `subprocess.run` raises `FileNotFoundError` on the absent `fusermount` and aborts the escalation before `umount -l` can run, which is exactly what was observed on the first prod deploy: the stale `/data/torbox` was isolated but never actually cleared) and retry — so a healthy actively-streaming mount is never force-detached, but a corpse left by a prior run is cleared; (c) the blanket `exit(1)` on pre-loop config failure is replaced with `raise`, so `main.py`'s existing handler logs it (with traceback) and startup continues to register the scheduler instead of producing a `SystemExit` zombie — and the Zurg auto-update thread is now `daemon=True` so any future fatal startup error actually exits the process and lets Docker restart it, rather than wedging interpreter shutdown (this also removes a ~10s `docker stop` hang, since the previously-non-daemon `while True` thread blocked interpreter shutdown until the SIGKILL grace period elapsed). **Reviewer-flagged fixes folded into the same change:** (a) the per-mount body was extracted into a `_configure_mount()` helper so the new isolation `try/except` reads cleanly instead of straddling the loop indentation; (b) `_rc_urls[mn]` (the RC-refresh URL registry) is now populated only after the mount process actually starts — previously it was registered unconditionally before the readiness probe, so a skipped or failed mount (now reachable via the per-mount `continue`) left a dead `localhost` URL that the RC-refresh consumers would fruitlessly poll; (c) the live per-instance Zurg binary copy in `zurg/update.py` now uses `utils/file_utils.atomic_write` (stage-to-temp + `os.replace`, preserving the existing `+x` mode) instead of a bare `shutil.copy`, so a SIGTERM that kills the now-daemon auto-update thread mid-write can never leave a truncated, unexecutable zurg binary on disk; (d) the pre-loop config-failure path now also fires a `health_error` notification before re-raising, matching the per-mount failure path.
- **System page: "View activity" buttons on the Debrid Health cards were dead links**: Both the Real-Debrid and TorBox Debrid Health cards linked to `/activity?type=debrid`, but the activity route was matched with an exact `self.path == '/activity'` test in `status_server.py`, so the query-string variant fell through to a 404 — the button did nothing. Even had it routed, the Activity page's type-filter `<select>` had no "debrid" option and never read the `type` URL parameter, so the link couldn't pre-filter to the debrid-health events (`type='debrid'`) the reconciler actually emits (`CAUSE_DEBRID_FILTERED` / `CAUSE_DEBRID_RESCUED`). **Fix (UI + route):** (a) `/activity` now also matches `/activity?...` (mirrors the existing `/library` handling); (b) the Activity page gains a "Debrid Health" option in the type dropdown and an on-load IIFE that reads `?type=` from the URL and pre-selects the matching filter, so the deep link lands on a debrid-only view; (c) added a `.type-debrid` badge style so those events render with a distinct color like every other type. The Media Recovery card's "View activity" link (plain `/activity`, all types) was already valid and is unchanged. Full suite unchanged at 2,296 tests.

- **Recovery metric: count wanted TV from fully-absent monitored shows (Sonarr ghost-show injection)**: The `/api/recovery` denominator summed `missing_episodes` only across shows the disk scanner had already discovered, so a Sonarr-monitored series you'd downloaded *zero* episodes of never reached `data['shows']` and its wanted episodes were invisible — the TV side of `pct_on_disk`/`pct_debrid` read systematically high. Radarr already had `_apply_radarr_wanted_movies` injecting monitored-but-absent movies as `source='wanted'` ghost entries; Sonarr had no equivalent (a gap the earlier TMDB-poisoning changelog entry explicitly called out as "no parity fix needed" — now closed). **Fix:** new `_apply_sonarr_wanted_shows()` in `utils/library.py` injects a ghost show entry for every monitored Sonarr series not already matched to a real library card, carrying `missing_episodes` from Sonarr's aired-monitored season statistics (so unaired episodes stay excluded, matching the on-card rebase). `_apply_sonarr_monitored_filter` now returns the set of matched series ids so the injector skips already-represented series (no double-count), and the shared per-series math is factored into `_sonarr_monitored_missing()` used by both. Ghost shows carry `source='wanted'` with empty `season_data`/`_episodes`, so every effect loop (symlink creation, missing-episode search, preference enforcement, path-index build) naturally no-ops on them — verified by audit. A series is injected only when its monitored-missing count is > 0 (series Sonarr considers satisfied never appear), and in-flight downloads are suppressed via the same pending snapshot as the movie path. **Required guard:** `compute_library_stats` skipped `source='wanted'` for movies but not shows — a ghost show would have been miscounted as a `debrid` show and inflated the Library Composition card; added the matching skip. `imdb_id`/`tmdb_id` are carried from Sonarr so a later `_dedup_shows_by_external_id` pass collapses any ghost that collides with a real show the title-match cascade happened to miss. **Reviewer-flagged bugs fixed in the same commit:** (a) `matched_ids` alone wasn't enough to prevent double-counting — a *partially*-on-disk show whose title-match cascade the monitored filter happened to miss never lands in `matched_ids`, so both its real entry (TMDB-based `missing_episodes`) and a Sonarr-based ghost would have been summed into the recovery denominator; `_apply_sonarr_wanted_shows` now also dedups candidates against the on-disk `shows` list by `tmdb_id`, `imdb_id`, AND `(norm_title, year)` (mirroring `_apply_radarr_wanted_movies`), because the post-injection `_dedup_shows_by_external_id` keys on `imdb_id` only and TVDB-only series / cache misses frequently lack it. This dedup also closes a downstream `_clear_resolved_pending` regression where a colliding ghost overwrote a real show's source-map entry with `{}`, blocking a recovered pending entry from clearing. (b) `_search_for_missing_episodes`' shows loop now skips `source='wanted'` ghosts before consulting `_compute_missing_episodes` — the ghost's empty `season_data` is irrelevant because that method derives candidates from the TMDB episode cache, so without the guard a ghost fired redundant Sonarr searches and wrote a `set_pending` entry that self-erased the ghost on the next scan (the same regression the movie path already guarded against). **Impact:** the recovery snapshot's wanted-TV denominator is now honest, which matters because this branch exists specifically to measure TorBox viability against RD's filter-gate — an inflated `pct_on_disk` would have biased that verdict. Seventeen new tests across `tests/test_library.py` (`_sonarr_monitored_missing` helper, filter matched-id return, the injector's inject/skip/pending/error paths, the three on-disk dedup paths by tmdb/imdb/(norm,year), the composition-stats guard, and the ghost-show search-loop skip) and `tests/test_recovery.py` (ghost show adds to `wanted` without inflating `on_disk`). Full suite: 2,296 tests pass.

- **Blackhole: mount-wait timeout on a confirmed-ready debrid add no longer fails the item — it's handed off to the library scanner**: When the blackhole adds a torrent and the debrid confirms it ready (`_is_torrent_ready` passes, disc-rip check passes, `cached` history event fires), the worker then waits for the file to surface on the rclone mount, bounded by `BLACKHOLE_MOUNT_POLL_TIMEOUT` (default 300s). Under TorBox 429 rate-limiting the file frequently doesn't surface inside 5 minutes, so the Phase-2 timeout fired and the item was dropped — the canonical local-media folder left empty, recoverable only if Sonarr/Radarr re-searched. Confirmed live: *Inside Out 2 (2024)* reported "Torrent ready" at 11:00:11, timed out on the mount at 11:05:16 (305s), and never materialised even though it was permanently in TorBox. **Fix:** reaching the Phase-2 mount-timeout branch in `_monitor_and_symlink` structurally guarantees the add was confirmed-ready (it's only reachable after Phase 1 breaks out via `_is_torrent_ready` and passes the disc-rip check), so a slow mount is no longer treated as a hard failure. The new `_register_scanner_handoff` records a `to-debrid` library pending entry keyed by the canonical normalized title (movie → `(season 0, episode 0)`; TV with parseable episodes → those episodes), and the existing library-scanner machinery takes over: `_create_debrid_symlinks` (which runs unconditionally every scan) creates the symlink once the content surfaces, `_clear_resolved_pending` clears the entry, and `_escalate_stuck_pending` marks it `debrid-unavailable` after `DEBRID_UNAVAILABLE_THRESHOLD_DAYS` (default 3) if it never does. The misleading `download_error` notification is dropped (the downstream `pending_warning`/`debrid_unavailable` notifications cover stalls) and a `cached`-type history event with the new `blackhole_mount_handoff` cause records the hand-off for the activity feed. The watch-dir `.torrent`/`.magnet` file is already `os.remove`'d at add time and the dedup cache is primed, so there's no re-add loop; the blackhole monitor entry is removed (ownership handed to the scanner — a resumed monitor would just re-time-out). `_monitor_and_symlink` is a single debrid/label-agnostic path, so Sonarr and Radarr grabs both take the hand-off. **Reviewer-flagged hardening landed in the same commit:** (a) multi-season and cross-season packs (`S01-S05`, `S01E01-S02E10`, `Complete Series`) are detected via the existing `_is_multi_season_pack` and skip pending registration — a multi-season pack with no single-season marker parses as `is_tv=False` and would otherwise have registered a bogus movie `(0,0)` entry under the show's title that never resolves and falsely escalates; a cross-season range would otherwise register only a single partial episode; the check runs on the de-extensioned filename before the movie branch since the season range is stripped out of the parsed show name; (b) the timeout-branch history block is wrapped in `try/except` so a parse/log failure can't kill the monitor thread before `_remove_pending` runs (a leaked `_active_monitors` entry would block a later re-add of the same id); (c) `debug` log lines added at each hand-off-refusal path for operator diagnosability. **Known gap:** TV packs whose episode list can't be represented as a single `(season, episodes)` entry (true single-season packs, multi/cross-season packs) don't get a pending entry — content still recovers via the scanner's unconditional symlinking, just without the escalation safety-net. No new env var (reuses `BLACKHOLE_MOUNT_POLL_TIMEOUT` + `DEBRID_UNAVAILABLE_THRESHOLD_DAYS`). Eight new `tests/test_blackhole.py::TestScannerHandoff` tests (movie/single-ep/episode-range registration, single-season/multi-season/cross-season pack skips, plus movie + show timeout-branch integration asserting both the library pending entry and blackhole-monitor removal) and one `test_activity_format.py` golden test for the new cause.

- **TMDB cache poisoning via /api/library/metadata?type=undefined**: User reported "wrong posters in library — Alien shows Resident Alien, Aliens shows Ancient Aliens, Almost Famous shows some Korean movie, 65 shows a Japanese music special." Root cause was a four-step cascade: (1) `_apply_radarr_wanted_movies` injected Radarr-monitored-but-no-file movies as ghost entries into the movies list, but the ghost dict lacked a `type` field; (2) the Library page detail-view JS at `library_page.py:2341` built the metadata fetch URL as `'&type=' + encodeURIComponent(item.type)` — for ghost movies, `item.type` was `undefined`, so the URL serialised as `&type=undefined`; (3) the server endpoint at `status_server.py:1558` did `media_type = qs.get('type', ['show'])[0]` — defaulted to `'show'` when missing, and the string `"undefined"` also fell through to the show branch because it wasn't `'movie'`; (4) `get_show_info("Alien", 1979)` ran → TMDB TV search returned zero results (no show called "Alien" premiered 1979) → `fallback_no_year=True` retried without the year filter → TMDB returned "Resident Alien" (loose fuzzy match) → cached under the movie-style key `'alien (1979)'` in the **shows** section. Each click on a ghost movie poisoned another cache entry. **Four-layer fix:** (a) `_apply_radarr_wanted_movies` ghost dict now carries `'type': 'movie'` so the JS never serialises `undefined`; (b) `/api/library/metadata` returns HTTP 400 when `type` is missing, `"undefined"`, or anything other than `'show'`/`'movie'` — silent default removed; (c) `search_show` and `search_movie` reject `fallback_no_year=True` results whose returned year drifts more than `_YEAR_FALLBACK_MAX_DRIFT` (10) years from the requested year — admits legitimate cases (anime dub vs original air year, shelved films like *The Other Side of the Wind*, miniseries adapted years after a source novel year) while still blocking the Alien (1979) → Resident Alien (2021) class of cross-decade mismatch; (d) the Library page JS at both the detail-view fetch and the IntersectionObserver card-hover fetch now guards `if (item.type && item.type !== 'undefined')` before issuing the request. **Reviewer-flagged hardening landed in the same commit:** (e) `_cache_lookup` got an on-read year-drift sanity check — entries whose stored `release_date` (movies) or `first_air_date` (shows) year drifts more than the same window from the requested year are treated as a miss, so any pre-fix poisoned cache entries left over from before operators run `rm /config/tmdb_cache.json` will be transparently refetched on next lookup instead of serving stale wrong data forever; (f) `search_show` / `search_movie` coerce string-typed `year` arguments via `int(year)` so a caller passing `"2020"` doesn't TypeError in the drift arithmetic — preserves the pre-fix tolerance for caller type quirks; (g) the IntersectionObserver path's card-render fetch (separate code path from the detail-view fetch) got the same JS guard so card-hover background fetches can't bypass the type check; (h) the `_YEAR_FALLBACK_MAX_DRIFT` constant carries a comment warning maintainers not to widen beyond ~10 without re-verifying the Resident-Alien regression test. **Operators:** the existing poisoned `/config/tmdb_cache.json` should be cleared once to flush the stale entries — the on-read sanity check will reject them transparently afterwards, but a clear forces immediate clean refetch. Symmetric Sonarr-side ghost-injection does NOT exist (Sonarr's `_apply_sonarr_monitored_filter` rebases counts on existing scanner-discovered show entries rather than injecting ghosts), so no parity fix needed. Nine new tests in `tests/test_tmdb.py::TestSearch` and a new `TestCacheLookupOnReadSanity` class cover: far-year-drift rejection for shows and movies (the Alien and 65 regression cases), boundary-drift acceptance (10-year window), missing-result-year fail-open for both shows and movies, string-year coercion, garbage-year silent drop, on-read drift rejection, on-read drift acceptance, yearless lookup bypass, no-date legacy pass-through, and release_date-vs-first_air_date forward-compat. One new test in `tests/test_library.py::TestApplyRadarrWantedMovies` asserts the ghost dict carries `type='movie'`. Two existing tests in `TestSearch` were updated — they used 1981 release dates with year=2020 (39-year drift) to test "fallback retry returns SOMETHING", now use within-drift release dates to test the same logic without triggering the new guard. Full suite: 2,253 tests pass.

- **Library: collapse duplicate show cards that share an IMDb ID after enrichment**: User report — "why am I seeing three posters for Your Friends and Neighbors on Zurgarr?" Three different debrid folder names for the same series (`Your Friends And Neighbors S01E09`, `Your Friends Neighbors S02E08`, `Your Friends and Neighbours S01E03` — note the American/British/no-and spelling variants) survived as three library cards. All three parsed to distinct normalized titles, the TMDB alias map didn't carry all three variants as known aliases for the same show, so `_dedup_by_tmdb` (which runs pre-enrichment and keys by normalized parsed-folder titles) left them as three separate groups. `_enrich_with_tmdb_cache` then stamped each with the canonical TMDB title (`Your Friends & Neighbors`) and the same `imdb_id` (`tt30459041`), producing three UI cards that displayed identically. The existing `_strip_ghost_duplicates(movies)` post-enrichment pass handled debrid+local duplicate movies via `(normalized_title, year)` keying, but had no equivalent for debrid+debrid show duplication via external-ID equality. **Fix:** new `_dedup_shows_by_external_id(shows)` runs after `_strip_ghost_duplicates(movies)` in `_scan_read`'s post-enrichment block. Keys by `imdb_id` only (enrichment never stamps `tmdb_id` on shows so a fallback would be dead code). For each multi-entry group: picks a survivor via `_rank`, unions `_episodes` with per-episode size-based collision compare (larger size wins, equal size keeps first-seen to preserve folder/blocklist tracking on the survivor's release name), rebuilds `season_data` via `_build_season_data` so downstream consumers (composition card, prefs enforcer, gap-fill, search loops) see the full episode set, recomputes `size_bytes` from merged per-episode sizes (with show-level max fallback when legacy scanner paths omit per-episode sizes), promotes `source` to `'both'` when any input is local-or-mixed, takes earliest non-zero `date_added`. **Reviewer-flagged hardening landed in the same commit:** (a) survivor selection now prefers entries that `_apply_sonarr_monitored_filter` matched (entries with `monitored_episodes` set), via a new first-tier rank key — prevents the Grey's-Anatomy regression where a non-Sonarr-matched rank-winner would inherit TMDB-all missing math when a Sonarr-matched sibling existed; (b) the season_data rebuild uses a non-promoted `default_source` so debrid-only sibling episodes whose info dicts lack an explicit `source` key (FUSE/WebDAV scanner paths don't write one) stay tagged `'debrid'` rather than inheriting the show-level promoted `'both'` and surfacing false source badges in the UI; (c) episodes lacking a `'file'` key (legacy `_normalize_episodes_for_merge` empty shells from list-of-tuples shapes) are skipped during the union rather than propagated into `_build_season_data` (which would raise `KeyError: 'file'` mid-scan); (d) `size_bytes` recompute falls back to `max(sibling show-level size_bytes)` when the per-episode sum is 0, so legacy-scanner shows don't silently zero-out the composition-card footprint. **What this fixes for the user's setup:** after deploy + library rescan, the three Your-Friends-&-Neighbors cards collapse to one; the 5 episodes across 2 seasons unify into a single card with merged season_data, deduplicated sizes, and Sonarr-aware missing counts preserved. Eighteen new tests in `tests/test_library.py::TestDedupShowsByExternalId` covering the three-folder regression repro, per-episode quality compare, size_bytes correctness and zero-fallback, distinct-IDs-not-merged, no-imdb-pass-through, tmdb-alone-does-not-merge, source promotion, `_rank` tie-breaking, season_data rebuild, Sonarr-aware missing_episodes preservation, plus reviewer-flagged edge cases: Sonarr-filtered sibling wins survivor pick, debrid-only sibling not tagged `'both'`, file-less episodes skipped without crash, equal-size collision keeps first-seen, single/empty/mixed no-ops. Full suite: 2,241 tests pass.

- **Library: TorBox-routed symlinks no longer misclassified as local content (shows appearing as movies in Recently Added)**: User report — "why are some shows showing up as movies in recently added? Grey's Anatomy, Tulsa King, Pagan Peak". Diagnosis surfaced three concrete show folders sitting under `/local_media/movies/` (e.g. `/local_media/movies/Greys Anatomy/Greys.Anatomy.S19E09....mkv`) each containing a single symlink targeting `/mnt/debrid_torbox/...` — the auto-derived TorBox symlink-target base. `_scan_local_movies::_is_debrid_symlink_dir` and `_scan_local_shows::_is_debrid_symlink_only` were checking only against `BLACKHOLE_SYMLINK_TARGET_BASE` (the Real-Debrid prefix, e.g. `/mnt/debrid/`). The TB symlink target `/mnt/debrid_torbox/...` does NOT start with `/mnt/debrid/` (the `_torbox` suffix follows `debrid`, not a path separator), so the dir-skip check failed and the scanner classified those TB-only folders as genuine local movie content. Once a folder appeared in the movies list, `_create_debrid_symlinks` happily kept the misclassification alive and the library UI rendered show episodes as movie cards. Plan 39 (dual-debrid) introduced the per-debrid target-base concept via `utils/debrid_routing.py::symlink_target_base_for_debrid(TORBOX)` — which returns the explicit `BLACKHOLE_SYMLINK_TARGET_BASE_TORBOX` env var or auto-derives `<RD>_torbox` when unset — but the two local scanners were never updated to consult it. **Fix:** new module-level `_all_debrid_symlink_prefixes()` helper iterates `VALID_DEBRIDS` from `utils/debrid_routing.py` and returns every configured per-debrid prefix as a tuple of `os.path.normpath`-normalized, trailing-`os.sep`-terminated strings, with deduplication and order preservation. Both `_is_debrid_symlink_dir` (movie) and `_is_debrid_symlink_only` (show, Sonarr parity) now take this tuple instead of a single `symlink_base` string and check each symlink's target against `any(target.startswith(p) for p in prefixes)`. Both callers in `_scan_local_movies` and `_scan_local_shows` updated to source the tuple from the new helper. Real local content (a non-symlink file alongside a TB symlink, or a symlink pointing at a non-debrid path like a NAS mount) still correctly classifies as local — the new check only disqualifies a folder when EVERY media file is a symlink AND every symlink targets a known debrid prefix. Adding a third debrid in the future requires no helper changes — `VALID_DEBRIDS` already covers AD and `symlink_target_base_for_debrid` is the single source of truth for the per-provider base. **Hardening surfaced in review and fixed in the same commit:** (a) `_cleanup_broken_debrid_symlinks` carried the same single-prefix bug as the scanners — broken TB symlinks under the auto-derived TB base were silently skipped by the prefix-check and stayed on disk forever. Now builds `(prefix, mount)` pairs for every configured debrid (RD pair + TB pair when `_discover_torbox_mount` resolves), iterates the symlink's target against each, and uses the matching pair to translate to the per-debrid rclone mount for the existence check. `_extract_release_info` already accepted a prefix-list, so the arr-research path needed only the call-site update to pass `debrid_prefixes_only`. Two new tests cover broken-TB-symlink removal and intact-RD-symlink preservation. (b) The helper had hardcoded `TORBOX` knowledge — a future AD-specific `BLACKHOLE_SYMLINK_TARGET_BASE_ALLDEBRID` env var would have silently bypassed it. Now iterates `VALID_DEBRIDS = (REALDEBRID, ALLDEBRID, TORBOX)` and consults `symlink_target_base_for_debrid(svc)` for each, with a per-service try/except so one provider's resolution failure doesn't break the others. (c) The original `except Exception: pass` on the `debrid_routing` import silently swallowed any error and degraded to RD-only — re-introducing the exact bug the helper exists to prevent. Now logs at debug level when `ImportError` fires, so a misconfigured install leaves a trace. (d) `os.path.normpath` added to the prefix-build path so consecutive separators (`/mnt//debrid`) and relative segments collapse to canonical form before the `+ os.sep` suffix is appended — without this, a raw env value with `//` would produce a prefix no real symlink target starts with. (e) `BLACKHOLE_SYMLINK_TARGET_BASE_TORBOX` added to `tests/conftest.py::clean_env`'s `pd_vars` list so a developer with that env var exported can't have shell state leak into tests claiming a clean slate. (f) Replaced the weak `test_all_debrid_symlink_prefixes_single_debrid_no_dup` self-assertion (`len(prefixes) == len(set(prefixes))`, trivially true for any tuple) with a meaningful test that sets RD and explicit-TB to the SAME path and asserts the result collapses to one entry. Plus four new tests: TB-only-install regression coverage (RD unset, TB explicit), path normalisation (`/mnt//debrid` → `/mnt/debrid/`), broken-TB-symlink removal via `_cleanup_broken_debrid_symlinks`, intact-RD-symlink preservation as a false-positive guard. Full suite: 2,223 tests pass (vs 2,210 pre-change). **Out of scope but flagged for follow-up:** `_enforce_library_preferences` at `library.py:2774` reads `BLACKHOLE_SYMLINK_TARGET_BASE` directly to drive prefer-debrid symlink replacement; for `source='both'` episodes that live on the TB mount, the prefer-debrid path translation would produce the wrong target. Fixing this requires API changes to `replace_local_with_symlinks` and is left to a focused multi-debrid-preferences commit. **What this fixes for the user's setup:** after the next library scan with this code deployed, the misclassified TB-routed entries drop out of the movies bucket and the corresponding show entries (already in the shows bucket via the debrid scanner) become the sole library cards. Note: existing `/local_media/movies/<show-name>/` folders remain on disk after the fix — a separate cleanup pass (manual `rm -rf` after verification, or a future opt-in remediation flag) is required to fully reclaim the disk state. Folders containing real local files are correctly untouched.

- **Blackhole strict mode: defer-loop on dual-debrid setups closed via TB cross-probe**: When `BLACKHOLE_REQUIRE_CACHED=true` and the routed debrid is RD (the common dual-debrid setup with RD as primary), the strict-mode cache probe at `blackhole.py:3917` would return `cached=None` for every file because RD's `/torrents/instantAvailability` endpoint was deprecated November 2024 and now returns empty payloads. The existing `cached is None` branch (transient-outage protection) then deferred the file — leaving it in the watch_dir for the next poll, which would re-route to RD, re-probe (still None), re-defer, forever. Files that were uncached on TB would loop indefinitely in the watch dir instead of cleanly skipping. **Fix:** before falling through to the defer path, when the chosen debrid is NOT TorBox AND TB is configured, query TB explicitly via `check_debrid_cache(service='torbox')`. If TB returns `cached=False` (definitively uncached — TB's `/api/torrents/checkcached` is the only working cache probe across debrids as of 2026-05), treat as uncached and run the same skip-and-delete path the chosen-debrid-False branch already uses: `os.remove(file_path)`, emit `uncached_rejected` history event with a `cross_confirmed_via: 'torbox'` meta marker so audit trails can distinguish single-probe from cross-probe skips, increment `blackhole_processed` metric with `status='skipped_uncached_cross_confirmed'`. When TB probe also returns `None` (TB rate-limited / 429 / cooldown) or TB isn't configured, preserve the existing defer behaviour — the transient-outage protection rule "an AD/TB blip must not silently eat every in-flight drop" remains intact. Net behaviour: in dual-debrid (RD+TB) setups, files Sonarr/Radarr drop are now either added (cached on either debrid), skipped (uncached on TB → drop won't be useful on either), or deferred (TB legitimately unreachable). The forever-loop case is eliminated. **TB-only and TB-as-primary setups unaffected** — when the chosen debrid is `torbox` (case-insensitive via `.lower()` normalisation), the cross-probe block skips because the existing single-probe path already covers it. **Single-debrid RD setups unaffected** — `_api_key_for('torbox')` returns None when TB isn't configured, so the cross-probe block skips to the existing defer path. **Hardening surfaced in review and fixed in the same change:** (a) the original ternary `debrid != 'torbox'` was case-sensitive — if `BLACKHOLE_DEBRID_PRIMARY` were set to mixed-case `TorBox` (env-var values are user-supplied), the chosen debrid would self-cross-probe, doubling TB's rate-limit consumption on every uncached hit; now normalised via `(debrid or '').lower() != 'torbox'`; (b) the cross-probe `check_debrid_cache` call was unguarded — `_check_cache_tb` catches expected network/JSON errors and returns None, but an unexpected TB API schema change surfacing as KeyError would have propagated out of `_process_file` and orphaned the file (no history event, no metric, no defer log); now wrapped in `try/except Exception` with a debug log on raise so the file falls through to defer cleanly; (c) the formatter at `utils/activity_format.py:350` (`_fmt_uncached_rejected`) and its JS mirror at `:670` were dropping the `cross_confirmed_via` meta field, so the UI couldn't distinguish single-probe rejections from cross-confirmed ones — exactly the operationally interesting case. Both now append ` (cross-confirmed via {provider})` when the meta key is present; (d) the pre-existing `test_unknown_blocked_when_gate_on` test had a now-stale docstring (claimed "treat None as not cached") and lacked the `assert os.path.exists(path)` that would have caught a regression in the defer-vs-delete semantics — updated to reflect the post-fix single-debrid behaviour with the missing assertion. Seven new tests across `tests/test_blackhole_debrid_dedup.py::TestBlackholeRequireCachedCrossProbe` (4 cross-probe paths) and `tests/test_activity_format.py` (formatter cross-confirmation surface) plus 1 test docstring/assertion update; 2,210 tests total pass (was 2,207 pre-change).

  **Known follow-up flagged but not fixed in this commit:** the `_process_file` strict-mode block has no per-file in-flight lock, so two concurrent invocations (poller + inotify trigger) on the same path could race to `os.remove` and double-log the history event. The cached=False branch (line 3917) has had this race shape since strict mode shipped; the new cross-probe slightly widens the window with an extra synchronous HTTPS round-trip. Production impact is bounded (`os.remove` raises `FileNotFoundError` on the second call, caught and logged WARN; history dedup at the consumer side absorbs the duplicate event). Recommend deferring the in-flight set refactor (the pattern `search.py:_inflight_adds` already uses) to a focused follow-up.

- **Blackhole: TorBox `.torrent` uploads no longer 400 with `BOZO_TORRENT`**: `_add_to_torbox` was passing a bare file handle to `requests.post(..., files={'file': f})`, which emits the multipart file part with `Content-Type: application/octet-stream` (requests' default when MIME isn't supplied via the 3-tuple form). TorBox's `/api/torrents/createtorrent` rejects that with `{"success":false,"error":"BOZO_TORRENT","detail":"Torrent file is not valid"}` even though the bencode bytes are 100% valid — verified against a known-cached release where the same bytes uploaded via `curl -F file=@…` (which auto-detects `application/x-bittorrent` from the `.torrent` extension) return `success:true`. Real-world impact: every `.torrent` Sonarr/Radarr dropped into the blackhole for the entire TB-enabled lifetime of the install failed the add step and rotated into `failed/`, where the alt-release retry loop pounded `createtorrent` indefinitely with the same broken request. The 7286 files currently sitting in `/watch/failed/sonarr/` are the visible fallout. Only `.magnet` adds succeeded because the magnet code path uses `data={'magnet': …}` form-encoded — no file-part MIME involvement — so the bug was invisible to the magnet-heavy workflow. Likely contributor (not sole cause) to the 2026-05-27 abuse-system cooldown that capped mylist at 270: every retry tick burned a `createtorrent` call against TB's 60/hour rate limit while yielding zero successful adds, which is exactly the volume signature that triggers the abuse system. Fix is the 3-tuple form `files={'file': (basename, f, 'application/x-bittorrent')}` so requests emits the correct Content-Type on the file part; verified live against the same Alien Earth S01E08 release that had been bouncing all weekend and now returns `success:true, "Found Cached Torrent. Using Cached Torrent."` with the torrent_id. RD's `.torrent` path is unaffected because it uses raw `requests.put(..., data=f.read(), headers={'Content-Type': 'application/x-bittorrent'})` — different mechanism, explicit header. AD's `.torrent` path has the same shape as the TB bug but AD is being abandoned post-trial so no fix needed. The phase D cooldown gating (`0f701f0`) stays valuable because the 7286-file drain at TB's 60/hour rate limit takes ~5 days and could otherwise re-trigger the abuse system. No new tests — `_add_to_torbox` has no direct unit-test coverage, and the one test that touches it (`tests/test_blackhole_debrid_dedup.py:32`) wholesale-mocks the method. Existing blackhole tests still pass.

### Changed

- **System page: clearer Debrid Health tiles + removed duplicated filter-gate block**: The Tasks-tab tiles showed the same filter-gate numbers twice under different names — the **Media Recovery** card carried a "Filter-gate (Real-Debrid)" subsection (Blocked / Filter-blocked 24h / Rescued to alt 24h) that was a relabeled, *frozen* copy of the live **Debrid Health** card metrics (those same values are `counts.blocked`, `remediated_24h`, `rescued_24h`). Worse, the recovery copy was baked into the once-per-day snapshot, so it read stale under an "as of <date>" pill while the Debrid Health card next to it was live — the same metric could show two different values. **Changes (UI only — `utils/system_page.py`):** (a) dropped the entire "Filter-gate (Real-Debrid)" subsection from the Media Recovery card; that tile is now purely about library availability (`pct_on_disk` headline + recovery breakdown). (b) The TorBox Debrid Health card now leads with a **rescue-rate headline** — `rescued ÷ (rescued + still-blocked)`, the share of RD filter-blocks TorBox could re-serve, which is the core TorBox-viability signal this branch exists to measure — plus a "Not rescued (blocked)" row so the denominator is legible (red below 50%). (c) The Real-Debrid card relabels "Last probe" → "Last sweep", colors the healthy count green, and gains an inline note defining *blocked* (RD refuses to serve — the filter-gate) and *remediated* (blocked item removed & re-queued). (d) Removed the now-unused `.rec-sep` CSS rule. No API or backend change — `recovery.py` still records the `filter_gate` block into each snapshot for the longitudinal series; it's just no longer rendered as a duplicate tile. The rescue rate is honest because rescued entries flip to `status='unknown'` while unrescued filter-blocks stay `status='blocked'`, so the two sets are disjoint. Full suite unchanged at 2,296 tests.

### Added

- **Media recovery tracking (`/api/recovery`)**: records a once-per-day snapshot of how much of your library is playable on the debrid mount versus on-disk-only (local fallback) versus not-yet-acquired (wanted), measured in media units (each movie = 1, each episode = 1). Two framings live in every snapshot: a trustworthy **library-health** headline (`pct_debrid` = debrid-playable units / total wanted units) and a decision-relevant **filter-gate** block (daily counts of RD filter-blocks, alt-rescues, and the currently-blocked hash population) for evaluating TorBox viability against Real-Debrid's filter-gate. Snapshots are upserted by UTC day into `/config/recovery_snapshots.json` (capped by `RECOVERY_SNAPSHOT_RETENTION_DAYS`, default 365) off the existing hourly library scan, building a longitudinal series. Surfaced as a **Media Recovery** tile on the System page (Tasks tab) whose headline is the **available / ready-to-play** percentage (`pct_on_disk` = debrid + local + both) with a sparkline of that trend; the debrid-recovery percentage (`pct_debrid`) and filter-gate counts are shown as secondary stats for the TorBox-viability experiment. The "wanted" denominator counts only *releasable* media — monitored Radarr movies that haven't reached their minimum-availability date (`isAvailable=false`) are excluded, and missing-episode counts inherit Sonarr's aired-only view — so the percentage isn't diluted by announced/unaired future content.
- **Dual-debrid System-page mini-dashboard + Routing settings section (plan 39 phase 5)**: The Debrid Health card now renders side-by-side Real-Debrid + TorBox cards when both providers are configured, collapses to a single card when only one is, and is hidden when neither is — same visual pattern as the existing single-card layout but with per-provider labels, color-coded provider pills (RD yellow, TB blue), and per-card metrics (RD: last probe, healthy/blocked/unknown breakdown, 24h remediation count; TB: WebDAV mount status, total rescued-from-RD count, 24h rescue count). The RD card retains the "Run sweep now" action; the TB card surfaces a "View activity" link. New `Multi-Debrid Routing` accordion section in the settings page exposes `BLACKHOLE_DEBRID_ROUTING` (cache_aware / primary_only), `BLACKHOLE_DEBRID_PRIMARY` (RD/AD/TB), `BLACKHOLE_SYMLINK_TARGET_BASE_TORBOX`, and `DEBRID_HEALTH_CROSS_RESCUE` (auto/true/false), so dual-debrid operators can adjust routing without editing `.env`. **API change:** `/api/debrid_health/summary` now returns a `providers[]` list of per-provider card dicts alongside the legacy top-level fields (`rd_configured`, `enabled`, `auto_remediate`, `counts`, `remediated_24h`) and adds `rescued_24h`, `cross_rescue` so the UI can render the new pills. Back-compat preserved: when `providers[]` is empty (older server build), the JS synthesizes a single RD card from the legacy fields so a hot-reloaded browser doesn't crash on a stale endpoint. **Plan-39 bug-fix bundle (this commit) closes all CRITICAL + HIGH + MEDIUM findings from the prior reviewer pass** before Phase 5 lands: CRITICAL-1 (`_retarget_symlinks_to_alt` mass-retargets — now filtered by `/<release_name>/` path segment so a single rescue scopes to one torrent, not the whole RD library); CRITICAL-2 (`_merge_alt_debrid_items` dict+dict TypeError — new `_normalize_episodes_for_merge` helper accepts dict shape from `_scan_mount` AND legacy list-of-tuples test inputs); HIGH-3 (`_cleanup_symlinks` only knew RD base — now iterates per-debrid `(target_base, mount)` translation pairs so TB-routed completed symlinks don't accumulate as ghosts); HIGH-4 (`_mount_for('torbox')` returned wrong path on tortured layouts — basename-equals-RCLONE_MOUNT_NAME heuristic + parent fallback); HIGH-5 (`TorBoxClient.add_magnet` AttributeError on non-dict response — isinstance guards); HIGH-6 (`source_debrid` hard-coded to `'realdebrid'` in primary scan — uses `resolve_primary()` so AD-only and TB-only setups get correct badge); HIGH-7 (rescue blocked sweep, ignored `_stop_event` — new module-level `_stop_event` + `request_stop()` wired into `main.py:shutdown()` so SIGTERM during a 60s rescue poll aborts cleanly within scheduler.stop's 15s window); HIGH-8 (no Plex active-session guard during retarget — new `_plex_session_active_for_release` defers rescue and cleans up the TB add when Plex is mid-stream); HIGH-9 (TB-only + symlinks-enabled + unset target base would create relative-path symlinks — startup validation now requires `symlink_target_base_for_debrid(svc)` non-empty for every configured debrid); MEDIUM-10 (RD `add_magnet` selectFiles failure leaked half-added torrent — moved outside addMagnet try, cleans up + logs on cleanup failure); MEDIUM-11 (`DEBRID_HEALTH_CROSS_RESCUE` only accepted literal `true`/`false` — now accepts `1`/`yes`/`0`/`no` matching `_is_truthy` vocabulary); MEDIUM-12 (cache-probe coalescing — new instance-level `_probe_cache` with 60s TTL on `(svc, hash)` keys); MEDIUM-13 (reserved `tag`/`round_robin` routing modes silently fell through — one-shot WARNING per mode); LOW (`torrent_status` now `.strip().lower()` for whitespace tolerance). Reviewer-feedback fixes: `_normalize_eps` hoisted to staticmethod (was redefined per loop iter), `dict.keys() or ()` dead idiom removed, rescue poll honours stop signal between `add_magnet` and first `torrent_status` call, `_probe_cache` made instance-scope (test isolation), selectFiles cleanup-of-cleanup-failure surfaces a WARNING. **17 new tests** covering each fix; full suite: 2042 passes.

- **Unified dual-debrid library view (plan 39 phase 4)**: the library scanner now enumerates BOTH the Real-Debrid (Zurg) mount and the TorBox mount in the same scan pass, items get a `source_debrid` field stamped at discovery (`realdebrid`/`torbox`/`alldebrid`), and the library UI renders a small RD/TB provider badge next to each card so users can see at a glance which side is hosting their content. Items present on **both** mounts (e.g. the same movie cached on RD and TB simultaneously) collapse into a single library card with `has_alt_source=true` + `alt_source_debrid='torbox'` meta, surfaced in the UI as an additional `+TB` (or `+RD`) badge — "available on both" instead of two duplicate cards. **Scanner changes:** `LibraryScanner._scan_mount()` takes a new `source_debrid='realdebrid'` parameter that gets stamped on every returned movie/show dict. The WebDAV scan path (`_webdav_scan_mount`) doesn't go through `_scan_mount`, so its items get tagged via a small `setdefault('source_debrid', 'realdebrid')` loop in `_scan_read` — same end-state, different code path. **New helpers on the LibraryScanner class:** `_discover_torbox_mount()` returns the TB rclone mount path (defers to `utils.debrid_routing.mount_for_debrid` so the per-debrid path contract is in one place) when `TORBOX_API_KEY` is set AND the mount is up; returns `None` otherwise so the second-pass scan is a no-op for users who don't have TB configured. `_merge_alt_debrid_items(primary_movies, primary_shows, alt_movies, alt_shows)` does the cross-mount dedup: matches by `_normalize_title`, flags duplicates with `has_alt_source=True`, **unions episode sets** for shows on both mounts (so a TV series with S01 on RD and S02 on TB shows the correct total `seasons`+`episodes` counts, deduplicated by `(season, episode)` tuple), and appends alt-only items as new entries. **Backward compat:** users with only RD configured see zero behaviour change — `_discover_torbox_mount` returns `None`, the second-pass scan is skipped, and every item gets `source_debrid='realdebrid'` (which the UI renders as a single RD badge that's the same color across all items — a small visual cue, not informational noise). **UI changes:** `buildBadges(source, item)` now takes the item dict and appends per-debrid provider badges after the existing Local/Debrid/Both/Wanted badge. New CSS classes `.badge-provider-rd` (green, mirrors Local's color scheme), `.badge-provider-tb` (blue, distinct from RD), `.badge-provider-ad` (orange) with full-text on desktop and 2-letter mini variants on mobile (≤640px). The `+RD`/`+TB`/`+AD` styling for the alt-source badge uses the same color palette so users can spot pair-availability without reading text. **What this unlocks:** once TorBox-routed content lands (either via Phase 2 cache-aware blackhole routing or via Phase 3 cross-debrid rescue), the library page surfaces it inline with RD content — no separate Plex library required to see "what's on TB". The `_episodes` union semantics mean a partial-coverage show (some seasons on RD, others on TB) renders accurately rather than under-counting. **Known limitations** (deferred to Phase 5 or later): no year-aware title tie-break in the alt-merge — `Dune (1984)` and `Dune (2021)` would collapse if both mounts had both (rare in practice, the cache rarely holds two films of the same title on the same provider); no source filter in the library page sidebar yet (use the existing search + read the badge for now); single-debrid users with only TB configured will see the TB scan as the *primary* (no `realdebrid` items at all) — the current code does the second pass against TB only when RD's primary scan ran, so a TB-only setup currently sees an empty library page.
