The TB-only edge case is rare enough that I'd rather fix it when someone reports it than carry the abstraction cost now. **Tests:** 8 new tests across `tests/test_library.py::TestPhase4DualDebridMerge` and `::TestPhase4DiscoverTorboxMount` — alt-only items appended with provider tag, same-title-both-mounts gets has_alt_source flag, episode union math for shows on both, empty alt is no-op, missing-title doesn't crash the merge, mount discovery returns None without API key, mount discovery returns None when path doesn't exist, mount discovery returns the path when both conditions met (via monkeypatched `mount_for_debrid` since `/data/torbox` requires the live container). Full suite: 2024 passes (up from 2016).

- **Cross-debrid rescue when Real-Debrid filter-blocks content (plan 39 phase 3)**: completes the dual-debrid story. Before plan 39, when the debrid-health reconciler detected an RD filter-block (May 2026 `infringing_file` / error-35 / HTTP 451), the only remediation was to blocklist the hash, delete the torrent from RD, and trigger an arr re-search — which typically just re-grabbed another release of the same content that *also* hit the filter, looping forever and leaving the user without the file. Phase 3 closes that loop: when both RD and TB are configured, every confirmed-blocked torrent first runs through `_attempt_cross_rescue` before any of today's blocklist/delete/research steps. The rescue cache-probes TB for the same hash via `utils.search.check_debrid_cache`; on a hit it calls `TorBoxClient.add_magnet(hash)` (new method — uses TB's existing `/torrents/createtorrent` endpoint with `magnet:?xt=urn:btih:<HASH>`), polls `TorBoxClient.torrent_status` (also new) up to `_RESCUE_READY_TIMEOUT` (60 s) for `completed`, then scans `BLACKHOLE_LOCAL_LIBRARY_TV` and `BLACKHOLE_LOCAL_LIBRARY_MOVIES` for arr-library symlinks pointing at the RD `BLACKHOLE_SYMLINK_TARGET_BASE` prefix and atomic-retargets each one (via `os.symlink + os.replace`, so there's no window where Plex sees a missing file) to the equivalent path under `BLACKHOLE_SYMLINK_TARGET_BASE_TORBOX`. On success: emit `CAUSE_DEBRID_RESCUED` history with `from`/`to`/`retargeted` count/`rescue_outcome` meta + `debrid_rescued` notification + mark the probe-cache entry so the next sweep doesn't immediately re-rescue, then `continue` to the next torrent — the blocklist/delete/research pipeline is **bypassed entirely** for rescued items. The hash is NOT blocklisted (it's good content again via TB), the RD entry is NOT deleted (user can decide later; leaving it inert is harmless), and the arr is NOT asked to re-search (we have the file). **Symlink retargeting strategy:** prefix-match on `os.readlink()` against the RD base, swap the prefix to the TB base, write to a `.rescue-tmp` sibling, then `os.replace` over the original — atomic at the inode level so Plex mid-stat sees either the old or new symlink, never nothing. Zero symlinks found is a soft warning, not a failure (the content can have been freshly grabbed but not yet imported by the arr — the file is still accessible via TB going forward), and the rescue still records a `debrid_rescued` event with `rescue_outcome: 'no_symlinks_found'` so the activity feed tells the story. **Symmetric provider API:** `RealDebridClient.add_magnet` lands alongside the TB counterpart even though today's only rescue direction is RD→TB; the symmetry makes a future TB→RD rescue (e.g. when TB has an outage) a one-line config change rather than another full implementation. **Failure modes all fall through to today's behaviour:** TB not cached, TB add returns None, TB never reaches `completed` within budget (with cleanup `delete_torrent(tb_id)` so the stale entry doesn't linger), cache-probe raises, alt client not configured — each path leaves the existing `_remediate` to run unchanged so users without TB configured see zero behaviour change. **New env var `DEBRID_HEALTH_CROSS_RESCUE`** with auto-default semantics: ON when both `RD_API_KEY` and `TORBOX_API_KEY` are set, OFF otherwise; explicit `true`/`false` overrides the default. **New history cause `CAUSE_DEBRID_RESCUED`** plus Python formatter (`_fmt_debrid_rescued`) and JS mirror (`FORMATTER_JS.debrid_rescued`) that render the recovery as `Filter-blocked on RD — rescued via TORBOX — N symlink(s) retargeted`. **New notification event `debrid_rescued`** added to `ALL_EVENTS` and the help text in `settings_api.py`. **Testability:** the rescue poll timeout + interval live as module constants (`_RESCUE_READY_TIMEOUT`, `_RESCUE_POLL_INTERVAL`) so tests can monkeypatch them to sub-second values without touching `time.time` globally (which would break pytest's own timing infrastructure — a real footgun discovered during development of these tests, with one runaway 99% CPU spin to prove it). **Tests:** 7 new in `tests/test_debrid_health.py::TestCrossRescue` plus a new `_patch_clients(rd, tb)` helper for service-dispatching `get_debrid_client` mock — happy path (RD blocked + TB cached + symlink retargeted, asserts blocklist/delete/research NOT called and history event has the rescue cause), TB not cached falls through to remediation, TB add returns None falls through, TB never reaches completed cleans up the TB entry and falls through, rescue toggle OFF skips the rescue path entirely (still runs existing remediation), zero-symlinks-to-retarget still counts as rescue (with `rescue_outcome: 'no_symlinks_found'` meta), unrelated symlinks pointing outside the RD base are NOT touched (boundary test for the prefix-match). Full suite: 2016 passes (up from 2009). **Operational note:** the rescue ships ON by default the moment both API keys are present — pairs naturally with phase 1 + 2 to make the dual-debrid story end-to-end without an extra opt-in flip. Users who want to test rescue in isolation set `DEBRID_HEALTH_CROSS_RESCUE=false` first, observe a baseline sweep, then enable.

- **Per-grab dual-debrid routing for the blackhole (plan 39 phase 2)**: with both `RD_API_KEY` and `TORBOX_API_KEY` set, each `.torrent`/`.magnet` drop now routes to whichever debrid has the hash cached — Real-Debrid and TorBox operate as co-equal peers rather than a single primary + fallback. Default mode is `cache_aware`: for every new grab, `_route_grab(info_hash)` calls `utils.search.check_debrid_cache` against both providers in parallel, prefers whichever returns a cache hit, breaks ties with the primary (`BLACKHOLE_DEBRID_PRIMARY`, default RD), and falls back to primary when both are uncached or both probes fail. `primary_only` keeps the pre-plan-39 single-debrid behaviour for users who'd rather pin everything to one provider. Single-debrid setups (only `RD_API_KEY` set) see zero behaviour change — routing collapses to `primary_only` automatically and the watcher binds to the one configured debrid for the lifetime of every grab, exactly as before. **The big-picture refactor:** every blackhole method that previously branched on `self.debrid_service` / `self.debrid_api_key` now takes optional `debrid=` / `api_key=` parameters with default-to-instance semantics — `_add_to_realdebrid`/`_add_to_alldebrid`/`_add_to_torbox` (api_key), `_check_realdebrid_status`/`_check_alldebrid_status`/`_check_torbox_status` (api_key), `_extract_torrent_id`/`_is_torrent_ready`/`_is_terminal_error`/`_extract_release_name`/`_extract_hash_from_info`/`_extract_filenames_from_info`/`_has_usable_media_files` (debrid), `_find_on_mount` (debrid → picks the right rclone mount via `_mount_for`), `_create_symlinks`/`_create_split_season_symlinks` (debrid → picks the right symlink target base via `_symlink_target_base_for`). The per-grab lifecycle threads the chosen debrid from `_process_file` → `_start_monitor` → `_monitor_and_symlink` so a single `BlackholeWatcher` instance hosts concurrent grabs against different debrids without any thread-local state — every parameter is local to the call stack. New helpers `_api_key_for(debrid)`, `_mount_for(debrid)`, `_symlink_target_base_for(debrid)` and `_route_grab(hash)` are the single source of truth and are also consumed by phase 3's cross-debrid rescue and phase 4's dual-mount library view. **Schema migration:** `pending_monitors.json` entries gain a `debrid` field stamped at write time; `_resume_pending_monitors` reads it back (defaulting missing → `realdebrid` for pre-plan-39 entries, which had no other possible origin) and binds the resumed monitor thread to the recorded debrid so an RD-routed grab in flight before a container restart keeps polling RD after restart, not TorBox. Tampered/unknown debrid values fall back to the instance default with a warning log line — `pending_monitors.json` is trust-boundary state and a hostile entry must never redirect API calls to an arbitrary URL. **Backward compat:** the legacy `__init__` signature still works (single `debrid_api_key`+`debrid_service` pair, no `debrid_api_keys` dict) — when the new dict isn't supplied, the watcher synthesizes `{debrid_service: debrid_api_key}` internally so all the per-grab routing code paths still resolve a valid api key. Existing tests that bypass `__init__` via `__new__` and set only the legacy attributes continue to pass because `_api_key_for` uses `getattr(self, 'debrid_api_keys', None)` defensively. Two pre-existing tests (`test_resume_pending_validates_label`, `test_resume_pending_skips_bad_entries`) had their `monkeypatch.setattr` lambdas updated to accept the new `debrid` kwarg via `**_` — minimal churn, contract preserved. **New env vars:** `BLACKHOLE_DEBRID_ROUTING` (mode), `BLACKHOLE_DEBRID_PRIMARY` (fallback/tiebreak), `BLACKHOLE_SYMLINK_TARGET_BASE_TORBOX` (host-side path for TorBox-routed symlinks — distinct from the RD base so Plex sees them as separate libraries per plan 39 Q1). All three default sensibly when unset: routing → `cache_aware` with 2+ debrids and `primary_only` otherwise; primary → first-configured of RD/AD/TB (honouring legacy `BLACKHOLE_DEBRID` as an override); TB symlink base → `<RD base>_torbox` suffix derived from the existing setting. **Tests:** 60 new tests across `tests/test_debrid_routing.py` (42 pure-function tests of the routing helpers — configured_debrids, resolve_primary, resolve_routing_mode, mount_for_debrid, symlink_target_base_for_debrid, pick_debrid_for_grab covering single-debrid, primary_only, cache_aware tiebreak/fallback/probe-failure, explicit primary override, empty-hash short-circuit) and `tests/test_blackhole_routing.py` (18 watcher-integration tests — api key resolution including legacy `__new__` fixture compat, `_route_grab` end-to-end with mocked cache probe, pending_monitors.json schema migration including legacy-entry resume and tampered-debrid sanitisation, per-debrid mount + symlink-base resolution). Full suite: 2009 passes (up from 1949).

- **TorBox WebDAV co-debrid mount (plan 39 phase 1)**: Zurg only supports Real-Debrid and AllDebrid, so the existing `TORBOX_API_KEY` integration in `utils/search.py`, `utils/blackhole.py`, and `utils/debrid_client.py` lacked a corresponding mount — TorBox content was reachable by the API code paths but invisible to the symlink machinery and the library scanner. Phase 1 closes that gap by adding a second rclone webdav remote pointed directly at `https://webdav.torbox.app/` (bypassing Zurg, since Zurg can't proxy TorBox). The mount comes up at `/data/$TORBOX_MOUNT_NAME/` (default `/data/torbox/`) with its own RC port from the existing `_RC_BASE_PORT + idx` allocator and its own `wait_for_url` health probe. **TorBox WebDAV authenticates differently from the API.** Empirically verified against `webdav.torbox.app` on 2026-05-25: the API key alone returns 401; WebDAV requires HTTP Basic with the account email and a separately configured WebDAV-only password set in the TorBox dashboard → Settings → Integrations → WebDAV. Three new env vars expose this: `TORBOX_WEBDAV_USER` (email), `TORBOX_WEBDAV_PASS` (the dashboard-generated password — obscured via `rclone obscure` before writing to `/config/rclone.config`), and `TORBOX_MOUNT_NAME` (default `torbox`, validated against `RCLONE_MOUNT_NAME` for collision at both rclone-setup time and config-validation time). **Partial configuration is tolerated.** Setting `TORBOX_API_KEY` without the WebDAV credentials skips the mount but keeps the non-mount features (cache probes, search-add, blackhole routing) fully working — config validator warns once at startup. Setting all three brings the mount up. **Backward compat:** users with only `RD_API_KEY` see zero behavior change. The existing RD/AD setup path in `rclone/rclone.py` is untouched aside from the additive TB write at the end of `rclone.config` generation and the mount-launch loop's new branch for `mn == TORBOXMOUNTNAME` (probes TorBox's WebDAV directly with the WebDAV creds instead of `http://localhost:<zurg_port>/dav/`). New helper `_torbox_mount_configured()` is the single predicate gating all of this. New helper `_write_torbox_remote(f, name)` emits the rclone.config stanza. Settings UI exposes the three new vars under a new "TorBox" accordion section with tooltip pointing at the WebDAV-password setup. New TROUBLESHOOTING entry "TorBox mount fails to authenticate / 401 Invalid credentials" walks through the dashboard step. Smoke-test script `scripts/torbox_rescue_probe.py` (read-only, run-once tool used during plan 39 design) measured 82.4% RD-blocked-hash cache hits on TorBox against the live blocklist, validating that the mount unlocks recoverable content. Phase 2 (per-grab debrid routing) and phase 3 (cross-debrid rescue in debrid_health) build on this; this commit ships only the mount + plumbing so phase 1 is independently shippable per the plan's "each phase shippable on its own" rule. No tests for rclone.py (network/process-startup heavy, covered by the live smoke test once the mount is deployed); config_validator tests `TORBOX_WEBDAV_USER`/`PASS` missing-warning behavior and mount-name collision detection.

### Fixed

- **TB-scan-heuristics bundle: "my freshly-grabbed TB content isn't showing up" (plan 41 phase B — three correlated friction fixes)**: Three independent bugs in the TB-content discovery path that all surface as the same user report ("Sonarr says hasFile=false right after my grab landed, but the file is on TB and the symlink exists"). All ship in one commit because they share the user story and the library/blackhole code region. **(B.1) Season packs and multi-season packs misclassified as movies on TB's flat layout.** `LibraryScanner._scan_mount` with `flat_layout=True` (the TB-mount path) relied on `_collect_episodes` to recognise TV content via `SxxExx` patterns. Folders like `Greys.Anatomy.S22.COMPLETE.1080p.WEB.H264-AMB3R` (season-only marker, no E##) or `For.All.Mankind.S01-S04.COMPLETE` (multi-season range) returned empty episode dicts and bucketed as movies. Cascading wasted work per scan cycle: failed Radarr lookups (`Could not match 'Greys Anatomy' to a Radarr movie — rescan skipped`), failed gap-fill searches against Radarr (`Search failed for movie Grey's Anatomy: Movie not found`), and the TV content never got its Sonarr-side rescan trigger — falling back to next library_scan cycle and compounding with the NFS-rescan race (B.2 below). New `_detect_tv_marker(folder_name)` helper in `utils/library.py` recognises four TV-marker patterns beyond `SxxExx`: `Sxx` season-only (`S22.COMPLETE`, `S03.1080p`), `Sxx-Syy` / `Sxx-yy` multi-season ranges including en-dash form (`S01–S04`), `Season N` / `Seasons N` word form (`Season.3.`, `Seasons 1`). The regexes use word boundaries and a negative lookahead `(?![Ee\d])` to prevent the season-only matcher from consuming digits that belong to an episode tag. In `_scan_mount`'s classification cascade, after `_collect_episodes` returns empty, the folder name goes through `_detect_tv_marker`; on a match, `is_show=True` is set WITHOUT injecting synthetic episode entries (which would surface as "Episode 0" placeholders in the library UI). The show enters `show_groups` with empty episodes; the next scan cycle, after TB finishes caching the actual files, populates real `SxxExx` entries. Matches the existing Zurg-shows-no-media fallback pattern in the same method. **(B.2) NFS attribute-cache race on immediate arr rescan.** When Sonarr/Radarr lives on a different host from the symlink target and reaches it via an NFS share, the arr-side kernel attribute cache (default 30-60s TTL on most NFS mounts) hides freshly-created symlinks for ~30-60 seconds. The library scanner's `_create_debrid_symlinks` triggers `rescan_series`/`rescan_movie` immediately after symlink creation; the arr walks the directory before the cache refreshes, sees nothing new, and completes successfully — the file only lands on the next 1h `library_scan` cycle (which re-triggers and this time succeeds). User-visible symptom: "I see the file on the TB mount and the symlink in the local library, but Sonarr says hasFile=false even after Refresh & Scan." Concrete case observed 2026-05-25: Pagan Peak S02E06 — symlink created at 21:38, library scanner triggered RescanSeries(198) at 21:38, Sonarr scan completed at 21:38 with zero imports (no `Found N possible extra files` line). A manually-triggered RescanSeries(198) ~20min later imported all 14 symlinks cleanly. New env var `LIBRARY_RESCAN_NFS_DELAY` (integer seconds, default `0` to preserve local-FS behaviour, recommended `30` for NFS users, clamped `[0, 300]` so a typo can't stall the scan loop indefinitely). New module-level helper `_resolve_nfs_rescan_delay()` does the parse-and-clamp in one place; `_create_debrid_symlinks` sleeps that many seconds between symlink creation and the rescan-trigger loop when `symlinked_shows or symlinked_movies` is non-empty. Sleep happens in the effects phase (post-cache-refresh), so the UI shows the updated library snapshot during the wait. Single sleep covers both Sonarr and Radarr rescan loops — Sonarr/Radarr parity preserved. Boilerplate ships with the env var: `base/__init__.py` Config attr + module global + `__all__`, `utils/settings_api.py` UI field under "Media Services" section as `number:0-300`, `CONFIGURATION.md` table row in the Scheduled Tasks section, `.env.example` commented line, and a new TROUBLESHOOTING entry keyed by *"Sonarr says hasFile=false right after a scan but imports correctly a minute later"* walking through symptom → cause → fix. The `task_scheduler.py` extension for one-shot delayed tasks (the architectural fix per the followup memo) is explicitly deferred to plan 40 era — this is the cheap mitigation. **(B.3) Cyrillic / non-English title fuzzy match in `_find_on_torbox_mount`.** TB's API `data.name` field returns the indexer's *display* title. For non-English trackers (Russian/Italian/etc.) that title is in the native language, while TB stores the folder on its WebDAV mount under the `.torrent`'s `info.name` (English release-group form). Concrete case observed 2026-05-25 (For All Mankind S03 from a Russian tracker): API name `Ради всего человечества  For All Mankind  Сезон 3  Серии 1-10 из 10 (Сара Бойд) [2022, США, фантастика, драма, WEB-DL 1080p] Dub (Невафильм)`; TB folder `For.All.Mankind.S03.1080p.ATVP.WEB-DL.DDP5.1.H.264-EniaHD`. Pre-fix, the existing `_strip_indexer_prefix` + `norm_for_matching` fuzzy match couldn't bridge that gap — TB-routed grabs from non-English trackers hit the 300s mount-poll timeout and disappeared into `.alt_pending`. Library scanner's flat-layout scan eventually picked up the file on its next 1h cycle (friction, not data loss), but Sonarr's blackhole-import client never saw the immediate symlink in `/completed/sonarr/<release>/`. New `build_tb_lookup_candidates(release_name, file_names=None)` helper in `utils/debrid_routing.py` is the single source of truth for TB candidate folder names. It builds an ordered, deduped list: (1) the API name, (2) extension-stripped variant for single-file releases, (3) `[indexer.to]`-prefix-stripped variant, (4) (3) with extension stripped, and (5) — the new and load-bearing addition — the first path segment of each entry in `file_names`. TB's `data.files[].name` is the actual on-WebDAV path, so the segment before the first `/` (or `\` for Windows-packed torrents) is the folder name TB used — derived from the .torrent's `info.name`, which gives us the English release form even when the API name is Cyrillic. `BlackholeWatcher._find_on_mount` now takes a `file_names=None` keyword arg threaded from `_monitor_and_symlink`, where it's extracted via the existing `_extract_filenames_from_info(info, debrid=debrid)` ONCE before the mount-poll loop (TB's file list is stable post-cache-hit; no need to re-extract every poll iteration). The TB branch of `_find_on_mount` now delegates to the new helper, replacing the inline candidate-building. The robust info-hash fallback via TB `/torrents/mylist` correlation suggested in the followup memo is explicitly deferred — the `info.files`-derived candidate covers the dominant case and arrives with the existing API response, no extra round-trip needed. **Tests:** 32 new tests — 9 in `TestBuildTbLookupCandidates` (test_debrid_routing.py) covering the Cyrillic bridge with the live FAM S03 case, Windows path separators, dedup, invalid file entries, no-files fallback; 8 in `TestResolveNfsRescanDelay` (test_library.py) covering unset/empty/honoured/at-max/clamped-above-max/negative/non-integer/float-string; 11 in `TestDetectTvMarker` (test_library.py) covering canonical `SxxExx`, season-only pack, season-with-quality-tag, multi-season range plus alt forms (`S01-04`, en-dash), word forms (`Season 3`, `Seasons.1`), real-movie negative case, year-only negative (`2024` must not match as season), embedded-marker positive cases; 3 in `TestScanMountTvMarkerFallback` (test_library.py) — the integration test that drops a `Greys.Anatomy.S22.COMPLETE` folder on a tmp dir and asserts it lands in shows (not movies), the `For.All.Mankind.S01-S04` multi-season case, and a regression guard that a real movie folder (`Dune.Part.Two.2024`) still buckets as a movie. **Reviewer-feedback fixes (code-reviewer + bug-hunter pass):** (BUG) Cross-module private-name dependency — `utils.debrid_routing.build_tb_lookup_candidates` was lazy-importing `_strip_indexer_prefix` (private, leading underscore) from `utils.blackhole`. Plan 40's eventual `DebridProvider` consolidation would have silently broken the TB Cyrillic path on a rename. Moved the helper into `utils.debrid_routing` as the public `strip_indexer_prefix` (with the regex constant alongside); `utils.blackhole._strip_indexer_prefix` becomes a thin delegating alias so existing in-module callers don't grow a top-level import that would re-form the `blackhole ↔ debrid_routing` cycle. The `MEDIA_EXTENSIONS` lazy-import keeps an explanatory comment because the constant is intentionally duplicated across `utils.{library,blackhole,scheduled_tasks}` per the 3-file rule in CLAUDE.md. (LOW) B.2 sleep gate tightened — `_create_debrid_symlinks` was sleeping whenever `symlinked_shows or symlinked_movies` was truthy, even when no matching arr was configured (e.g. Radarr-only user with show symlinks but no `SONARR_URL`). The arr-loop's existing warn-and-skip block would then fire with no rescan to follow, so the user would just see an unexplained 30s stall. Sleep now requires `(symlinked_shows and SONARR_URL) or (symlinked_movies and RADARR_URL)`. (LOW) `LIBRARY_RESCAN_NFS_DELAY` added to `settings_api.numeric_ranges` so a user setting `=500` in `.env` (or POSTing through the API) gets a clear "outside recommended range [0-300]" warning rather than silent clamping behind their back — parity with every other `number:MIN-MAX` schema field. (LOW) Show-group `path` swap on heavier folder — pre-existing bug surfaced by B.1: when the same show key is encountered first as an empty-marker entry (TV-marker fallback for season packs whose files haven't cached yet) and later in the same scan as a populated entry, the show's `path` field stayed pinned to the first-seen empty folder. Downstream `date_added` and symlink-target consumers would stat an empty dir while a populated sibling existed in the same scan. The merge branch in `_scan_mount` now captures `existing_count_before` BEFORE merging the new folder's episodes, then swaps `path` when the new folder carries more episodes than the previously-stored one. Bias toward populated source; equal counts keep the first-seen path for stability. **Additional tests:** 7 in `TestStripIndexerPrefix` (public-API contract pin), 1 in `TestBlackholeStripIndexerPrefixAlias` (delegation pin so future refactors can't drift), 1 in `TestScanMountPathSwapOnHeavierFolder` (the empty-marker-then-populated scenario from the bug-hunter LOW #3 finding). **Second-pass reviewer findings (code-reviewer + bug-hunter pass-2 on the fix-up diffs themselves):** (HIGH) Path-swap heuristic was applied to `_scan_mount` (FUSE branch) but missed in `_webdav_scan_mount` — and WebDAV PROPFIND is the HOT path that runs BEFORE the FUSE fallback for any Zurg version that supports recursive PROPFIND, so the original B.1 bug would still have manifested in production despite the FUSE-path fix. Fixed by extracting `_merge_show_group(show_groups, key, title, year, episodes, path)` as the single source of truth for show-group merging; both `_scan_mount` and `_webdav_scan_mount` now call this helper instead of carrying structurally-identical inline blocks that drifted out of lockstep. (HIGH) `_recover_alt_pending` was preserving the `.rescue-<uuid8>-` prefix when moving rescue-orphan files to `failed/` on container restart — Sonarr/Radarr's blackhole import doesn't recognise the mangled name, and the file would rot silently in `failed/` with a `.rescue-` sidecar that `RetryMeta.mark_alt_exhausted` keyed on the wrong basename. New `_restore_rescue_basename(name)` helper + `_RESCUE_STAGED_PREFIX_RE` regex (anchored exactly on 8 hex digits) strip the prefix before computing `dest`. Pre-plan-41 alt-release-staging files (no prefix) pass through unchanged — regex is a no-op. (HIGH) Pathological release names exceeding POSIX `NAME_MAX` (255 bytes) on the rescue staging path — a long multi-byte UTF-8 filename from a non-English tracker plus the 17-byte `.rescue-<8hex>-` prefix exceeds the limit and `os.rename` raises `ENAMETOOLONG`, which the outer `except OSError` catches with a misleading "permissions"-shaped log line. Filename truncated to `_RESCUE_STAGED_FILENAME_MAX=220` bytes before prefix application so the full basename stays under 255 bytes. (HIGH) TOCTOU race in the rescue-failure restore: `if not os.path.exists(file_path): os.rename(staged_path, file_path)` is not atomic, so a fresh Sonarr re-grab landing at `file_path` between the check and the rename would be silently overwritten by the older staged file (POSIX rename clobbers). Switched to `os.link(staged_path, file_path) + os.unlink(staged_path)` — `os.link` is atomic on same-filesystem and raises `FileExistsError` when the destination exists, so the fresh drop is preserved and the rescue's staged file remains under its unique name for manual recovery. (LOW deferred) Bug-hunter flagged `time.sleep(nfs_delay)` as non-interruptible on SIGTERM; documented as a known limitation rather than fixed. The scanner runs in a daemon thread, so on shutdown the daemon-thread auto-kill terminates the sleep without graceful cleanup; the worst-case side-effect is the rescan trigger never fires for the symlinks just created, which the next container startup's `library_scan` cycle re-discovers and triggers cleanly. Adding cooperative shutdown (a `_stop_event` on `LibraryScanner`) is disproportionate to the impact and is deferred to plan 40's broader scanner-threading refactor. **Additional tests (16 in this second pass):** 7 in `TestMergeShowGroup` (fresh insert, episode union, path swap on heavier folder, path stays on equal count, year propagation, per-season `_folder_ep_count` preference), 1 in `TestRescueRestoreAtomicity::test_fresh_drop_during_rescue_wait_preserves_both_files` (simulates a Sonarr re-grab landing during the alt-add call and asserts both the fresh drop AND the rescue's staged file survive — neither is silently overwritten), plus 4 in `TestRescueOrphanRecovery` covering the prefix-strip regex edge cases (8-hex-exact match, no-prefix passthrough, 7-char too-short, 9-char too-long, non-hex slot rejection) and 3 integration tests covering recovery to `failed/` for flat layout, labeled layout, and the legacy-non-prefixed-orphan case, plus 1 in `TestRescueStagingFilenameLength::test_long_filename_staged_under_name_max` (240-char filename rescue path — pre-fix would have raised ENAMETOOLONG and silently dropped the rescue). Full suite: 2199 passes (up from 2142).

- **Filter-block cross-rescue at blackhole add time (plan 41 phase A — data-loss fix, observed live 2026-05-26)**: When Sonarr/Radarr handed a magnet to the blackhole and Real-Debrid returned `{"error":"infringing_file","error_code":35}` (the May-2026 keyword filter), the existing pipeline went straight to alt-release search (a *different* release of the same media) — never trying the *same* hash on TorBox. Result: popular Disney/HBO/Apple titles silently vanished into `/watch/.alt_pending/` or `/watch/failed/` while TorBox had the exact hash cached and ready to play. Concrete loss observed 2026-05-26: Yellowjackets S02E09 and Star Wars Andor S02E01, both grabbed at 07:33, both filter-blocked, both lost — Sonarr's next search-cycle re-grabbed them and the loop repeated indefinitely. Plan-39 phase-3 cross-rescue handled the same defect for previously-grabbed-then-blocked torrents (sweep-driven, symlink retarget), but was structurally unable to fire at magnet-add time because it operated on `/torrents/info` entries that don't exist for a magnet RD has refused to accept. Phase A closes the gap: when `classify_add_failure(result) == 'filter_block'` AND `info_hash` is parseable AND an alt debrid is configured, `BlackholeWatcher._process_file` calls the new `_attempt_add_time_rescue` method BEFORE the existing `_try_alternative_release` fallback. The rescue cache-probes the alt for the same hash via `utils.search.check_debrid_cache`, on a hit re-submits the original `.magnet`/`.torrent` via the alt's `_add_to_<provider>` method (so `.torrent` bencoded files are handled correctly, not just hash-only magnets), waits for a ready state via `attempt_add_rescue`'s shared cache-probe+add+wait-ready core, extracts the alt torrent id, starts the symlink monitor on the alt provider, removes the watch-dir file, and logs a `debrid_rescued` history event with `meta['rescue_stage']='add_time'` to distinguish from sweep-driven rescues. **Shared rescue core extracted into `utils/debrid_routing.py::attempt_add_rescue`** — same cache-probe+add+wait-ready code path is now used by both `debrid_health._attempt_cross_rescue` (sweep, retargets existing symlinks) and the new add-time path (no symlinks yet, alt monitor takes over). The shared core uses dependency injection (`cache_probe`, `alt_client`, `alt_add_fn`, `status_fn`, `ready_states`, `stop_event`) to avoid an import cycle with `utils.blackhole` and to keep tests mockable without touching the network. Plan-40 will absorb it as `DebridProvider.attempt_rescue()`. **Both-providers-filter-block annotation:** when RD and TB BOTH return `infringing_file` for the same hash, the blacklist gets a `filter_blocked_everywhere (rd+tb)` entry so future re-grabs of the same hash short-circuit at the pre-submit gate instead of burning two API calls + one indexer hit per re-grab cycle. **Filter-block vocabulary centralised:** new `classify_add_failure(result_text)` returns `'filter_block'` / `'invalid_torrent'` / `None` — replaces the inline `_REJECTION_CODES`/`_REJECTION_KEYWORDS` constants previously duplicated in `blackhole.py` and the inline `(reason == 'infringing_file')` check in `debrid_health.py`. Companion `is_filter_block_reason(reason)` operates on the probe-result reason field; both share the `_FILTER_BLOCK_KEYWORDS` set so a future RD response-string change updates both call sites at once. **Cross-rescue direction generalised:** new `pick_alt_debrid(source_debrid)` codifies the RD ⇄ TB rescue graph (no AD rescue partner today — AD-only and AD+TB users fall through to existing failure paths). Today's RD→TB direction can flip to TB→RD without further code changes if a user sets `BLACKHOLE_DEBRID_PRIMARY=torbox`. **Activity formatter branching:** `_fmt_debrid_rescued` and its JS mirror `FORMATTER_JS.debrid_rescued` now branch on `meta['rescue_stage']` — `'add_time'` renders as `"Filter-blocked on RD — rescued via TORBOX — routed to alt at add time, alt monitor took over"`; `'sweep'` keeps the existing `"— N symlink(s) retargeted"` shape. Back-compat default = sweep so events still inside the 30-day retention window render correctly without the new field. Sweep-side emission tagged with `rescue_stage='sweep'` going forward. **No new env vars:** rescue is ON whenever the alt debrid is configured, mirroring the sweep-driven `DEBRID_HEALTH_CROSS_RESCUE=auto` default — turning it off requires unsetting one of the API keys. **Sonarr/Radarr parity:** the rescue path is debrid-agnostic and label-preserving; the alt monitor starts with the same `label` so per-arr routing (sonarr/radarr completed-dir layout) survives the rescue. **Tests:** 4 new `TestAddTimeFilterBlockRescue` integration tests (RD-blocked → TB rescue succeeds end-to-end; RD-only setup falls through to existing alt-release search; both providers block → blocklist annotated; unsupported source short-circuits without network) plus 30 new pure-function tests across `TestClassifyAddFailure`, `TestIsDebridRejection`, `TestIsFilterBlockReason`, `TestPickAltDebrid`, `TestAttemptAddRescue` (covering cache-probe-error, not-cached, add-error, add-failed, never-ready cleanup, stop-event cancellation, custom add-fn injection, ready-state case-normalisation, alt-debrid override for TB→RD direction). **Reviewer-feedback fixes (code-reviewer + bug-hunter pass):** (HIGH) `classify_add_failure` substring matching was prefix-greedy — `'"error_code": 35'` matched `350`/`35099` and would have falsely cross-rescued a future RD code in the 35X range; replaced with digit-boundary-anchored regex (`_ERROR_CODE_RE = re.compile(r'"error_code"\s*:\s*(\d+)\b')`). (HIGH) Blackhole rescue did not pass `stop_event=self._stop_event` to `attempt_add_rescue` so a SIGTERM during the 60s wait_ready loop ignored the shutdown signal and could `os.remove(file_path)` AFTER `pending_monitors.json` had been persisted — orphan TB entry + lost watch-dir file; now plumbed through. (HIGH) File-overwrite race during the up-to-60s rescue wait — Sonarr/Radarr re-grab landing a new file at the same `file_path` would silently overwrite (POSIX rename). Rescue now stages the file to `.alt_pending/.rescue-<uuid8>-<filename>` BEFORE entering the wait_ready loop (unique name prevents collision with the alt-release path's same-directory staging); on rescue success the staged file is removed; on failure it's moved back to `file_path` unless a fresh drop has landed (in which case the original is preserved under its unique staged name for manual recovery). (MEDIUM) `_extract_torrent_id` returning empty for a SUCCESSFUL alt add (TB shipping a response variant where `data.torrent_id` becomes a differently-named field) was conflated with the legitimate `add_failed` reason — would have permanently blocklisted a hash TB actually had. Closure now sets a distinct `extract_failed` flag; the `filter_blocked_everywhere` blocklist gate requires `not extract_failed` so schema drift doesn't poison the blocklist. (MEDIUM) Filename sanitization — the watch-dir filename comes from an uploader-controlled torrent name and can carry ASCII control characters; the blocklist title now goes through `re.sub(r'[\x00-\x1f\x7f]', ' ', filename)[:200]` before persistence so logs and notifications don't render raw control sequences. **Documented known limitation (BH-3):** if `_add_to_torbox` raises mid-`.json()` (TB serving malformed JSON during an incident), the TB torrent may exist server-side but no id is available for cleanup — pre-existing in `_add_to_torbox` regardless of rescue; closing the orphan window for ALL TB callers is a follow-up wired through `_add_to_torbox`'s exception handling rather than added to the rescue path. **Additional tests:** 5 new in `TestClassifyAddFailure` (error_code 350/300/35099 regression, trailing-field termination) and 2 new in `TestAddTimeFilterBlockRescue` (invalid_torrent code 30 must NOT trigger rescue — falls through to alt-release; `.torrent` file flow preserves the extension through the staged path so `_add_to_torbox`'s ext-branch picks the correct code path). Full suite: 2142 passes (up from 2098).

- **Healthcheck + `mount_liveness_probe` now cover the TB rclone mount (plan 39 dual-debrid follow-up — surfaced live)**: `healthcheck.py` only probed the RD/AD mounts via `os.path.ismount`, and `utils/scheduled_tasks.py::mount_liveness_probe` only probed `BLACKHOLE_RCLONE_MOUNT`. A half-stuck TB FUSE (mount table entry persists in `/proc/self/mountinfo` so `ismount` returns True, but the rclone process behind it has died → every traversal returns `OSError(ENOTCONN)`) was invisible to both — the Docker `HEALTHCHECK` reported `healthy` and the System page showed `mount_liveness: success` while every TB grab silently timed out at the 300 s mount-poll boundary and got auto-blocklisted. Fix in `healthcheck.py`: new `rclonemn_torbox` entry in the process-check table (gated on `TORBOXAPIKEY + TORBOXWEBDAVUSER + TORBOXWEBDAVPASS`, matching `rclone/rclone.py::_torbox_mount_configured`) plus a `_mount_alive(path)` helper that pairs `os.path.ismount` with an `os.listdir` probe — the listdir is what catches half-stuck FUSE. Exception catch is widened from `OSError` to `Exception` because libfuse bindings can surface decode/parse errors on corrupted dentry blocks too. The TB-mount block is intentionally NOT gated by `ZURG=true` (TorBox uses its own WebDAV endpoint, no Zurg dependency) — that asymmetry is documented inline. Companion fix in `scheduled_tasks.py`: extracted single-mount logic into `_probe_mount(path)` returning `(status, message, items)` with three states (`success`/`error`/`absent` — last distinguishes "TB not configured" from "TB dead"). The TB-side probe is gated on the same three env vars as `_torbox_mount_configured` rather than on whether `mount_for_debrid` returns a path (the latter always synthesises a path for TORBOX, so a pure-discovery gate would false-trigger on every RD-only install). The slow-mount threshold is hoisted to a module constant `_SLOW_MOUNT_THRESHOLD_SEC=5`. **New `_listdir_with_timeout(path, timeout)` helper** runs `os.listdir` in a daemon thread with `_LISTDIR_TIMEOUT_SEC=15` join — addresses the bug-hunter MEDIUM that a wedged-but-not-erroring FUSE (rclone busy with vfs-refresh, blocked on a debrid 5xx) would otherwise hang the scheduler thread forever, taking down `verify_symlinks`, `library_scan`, and `debrid_health` sweeps with it. New `tb_status`/`tb_items`/`tb_mount` fields in the result dict so the System page can break out per-mount state. **13 new tests in `tests/test_mount_liveness.py`** covering alive/absent/dead/half-stuck/hung/slow states across RD-only, TB-only, dual-debrid, and TB-discovery-failure configurations.
- **`debrid_health` no longer triggers destructive remediation or speculative TB rescue on HTTP 404 from `/unrestrict/link` (plan 38 + plan 39 follow-up)**: `RealDebridClient.probe_file` maps HTTP 404 to `{status:'blocked', reason:'not_found'}` — a transient RD CDN miss, file being re-processed, or stale link from `/torrents/info`. The cross-rescue gate (`utils/debrid_health.py:890`) and remediation gate (line 958) both checked only `status == 'blocked'`, so a transient 404 triggered the full destructive pipeline (blocklist + RD delete + Sonarr/Radarr re-search) AND a speculative TB add with symlink retarget — silently breaking playback when RD recovered a sweep later, and accumulating ghost entries on the TB account. Both gates now compute a single `is_filter_block` boolean (`status == 'blocked' AND reason == 'infringing_file'`) so only the confirmed RD May-2026 keyword-filter return triggers action. 404 entries still persist to the state file (visible on the dashboard) so operators can see the count, but no destructive action runs — the entry will re-probe on next sweep and resolve to `healthy` if the underlying transient cleared. Future RD response-shape drift (e.g. a new `dmca_takedown` reason) fails closed (no action) but surfaces a throttled per-reason-per-sweep WARN so operators see drift before counts grow silently. **3 new tests** in `tests/test_debrid_health.py`: `test_blocked_not_found_does_not_trigger_remediation` in `TestRemediation` confirms a 404 + a real block in the same sweep result in only the real block being touched, `test_blocked_unknown_reason_warns_but_takes_no_action` covers the response-drift WARN and per-sweep throttling, and `test_blocked_not_found_does_not_trigger_rescue` in `TestCrossRescue` confirms the rescue gate doesn't fire `add_magnet` on `not_found`. Full suite: 2098 (was 2082).

- **TorBox end-to-end was silently broken — eight compounded defects from plan 39, all surfaced and fixed in one live debugging session**: Plan 39 shipped TB as a co-equal debrid alongside RD, but the integration had no live cache-side traffic until a user reported "TB grabs reach 'Torrent ready' then nothing happens — no symlink, no Sonarr import, nothing in Plex." Eight independent defects compounded into that single user-visible symptom; the diagnosis surfaced each as the previous one was unblocked. All eight ship in this bundle. **(1) TB cache state not recognised as ready (`utils/blackhole.py`)**: `_is_torrent_ready` only matched TorBox's `completed` state. Under plan 39's cache-aware routing every TB-routed grab is cache-positive by construction, so TB reports `cached` (instant-cache hit) or `uploading` (post-download seed phase) — never `completed` — and the blackhole poll loop timed out at `mount_poll_timeout` (default 300 s) on every grab, then auto-blocklisted the hash as "uncached timeout". New module-level `TB_READY_STATES = {'completed', 'cached', 'uploading'}` is the single source of truth, consumed by both `_is_torrent_ready` and the debrid_health cross-rescue poll (`utils/debrid_health.py`). **(2) `TorBoxClient.delete_torrent` sent capitalised `"Delete"` (`utils/debrid_client.py:511`)**: TB's API rejects mixed-case operations with `INVALID_OPTION` and the client swallowed the error silently — every cleanup-on-failure path (terminal error, disc rip, mount timeout, cross-rescue failure) thought it succeeded while the orphaned torrent stayed in the TB account forever. The companion script `scripts/cleanup_tb_false_blocklist.py` removed 70 spuriously-blocklisted hashes accumulated from bug #1; the lowercase-`"delete"` payload fix prevents future accumulation. **(2b) Delete failure log crashed on `{"detail": null}` (`utils/debrid_client.py:521` — bug-hunter HIGH)**: `data.get('detail', '')[:80]` returns `None` (not the default) when the key is present with a null value, and `None[:80]` raises `TypeError`.  `TypeError` was NOT in the except tuple so the crash escaped, breaking every cleanup-on-failure caller — precisely on the unhappy path the diagnostic log was supposed to illuminate. Fix: `(data.get('detail') or data.get('error') or '')[:80]` plus widened except tuple. Two new tests cover null-detail and the `error` field fallback. **(3) `_find_on_mount` couldn't see the TB WebDAV layout (`utils/blackhole.py`)**: TB's WebDAV mount is **flat** — release folders sit directly under the mount root with no `shows/movies/anime` or `__all__` subdivision the Zurg layout uses. `_find_on_mount`'s category-loop probed only those four subdirs, so for TB it returned `None` on every grab no matter what was on the mount. Compounding it, TB's API `data.name` field sometimes carries a leading `[indexer.to] ` prefix added by the scraper (e.g. `[bitsearch.to] www.UIndex.org    -    Landman S02E01 ...`) while the actual folder TB wrote to WebDAV has the bare torrent name. Fix: `_find_on_mount` is now debrid-aware. TB grabs route to a new `_find_on_torbox_mount` helper that (a) probes the flat mount root with exact + ext-stripped + leading-`[indexer.to]`-stripped candidates, then (b) walks `os.listdir(mount)` and fuzzy-matches via `norm_for_matching(_strip_indexer_prefix(entry))` to catch additional sanitisation drift. The fuzzy fallback returns a hit only when exactly one folder matches — refuses to guess between duplicates. The category result for TB is the empty string (no subdivision), passed through to `_create_symlinks`. Zurg-backed mounts (RD/AD) keep the existing categorized search unchanged. **(3b) Path-traversal hole via attacker-controlled release_name (`utils/blackhole.py` — bug-hunter CRITICAL)**: `os.path.join('/mnt/tb', '/etc')` returns `'/etc'` (absolute right-arg resets the join). The `release_name` candidates flow from TB's `data.name` field which is uploader-controlled at torrent-submission time. An adversarial name like `/etc` or `../../etc` would have made `_find_on_torbox_mount` return that path as the matched mount path → passed straight to `_create_symlinks` as the `os.walk` root for symlink target construction → arbitrary directory tree exposure + arbitrary symlink writes. Same surface existed in the pre-existing RD/AD branch with one extra category-segment to climb. Fix: new `_is_safe_mount_name(name)` helper rejects empty/`.`/`..`/path-separator/NUL/absolute-path names, applied to BOTH the API-derived candidates AND the listdir-walk entries (defense-in-depth against a buggy/adversarial FUSE driver). Also added a `normpath`+`startswith` guard in `_create_symlinks` so even if a candidate slipped through, the constructed `completed_release_dir` can't escape `completed_base`. The split-season branch already had this guard; the single-dir branch did not. 13 new tests (parametrised over `/etc`, `/etc/passwd`, `../../etc`, `..`, `.`, `foo/../bar`, `a/b`, empty, NUL-byte) for both TB and RD branches. **(4) `scheduled_tasks._extract_release_info` rejected TB symlinks (`utils/scheduled_tasks.py:237` — code-reviewer HIGH)**: required `len(parts) >= 3` and unpacked as `[category, release, rel_file]`. TB symlinks with `category=''` collapse via `os.path.join` to a 2-part path — caused `verify_symlinks` to delete every TB symlink during any mount flap rather than repair it. Fix: accept 2-part (TB-flat) layout as `(release, rel_file, '')`. Companion `_find_release_on_mount` (line 251) now falls through to the bare mount root for TB after the Zurg category probes miss. `verify_symlinks` reworked to route through per-debrid `(target_base, mount)` pairs so TB symlinks translate to the TB mount for existence-checks (not the RD mount where they'd never resolve → mass deletion), and the mount-health gate now accepts EITHER RD or TB having content (was Zurg-categories-only, would have aborted TB-only setups). 6 new TB-specific tests including the load-bearing "symlink stays put when only TB mount has the file" regression. **(5) Library scanner under-counted TB content by 30× (`utils/library.py` — Phase 4 follow-up)**: `_scan_mount` assumed 2-level structure `<mount>/<category>/<release>` unconditionally. For TB it treated each release folder AS a category and looked for sub-folders inside (finding only media files) — every TB show and movie except the rare ones with internal subdirs got silently dropped. Live observed: 30+ TB folders on mount, scanner reported 1 movie + 1 show. Fix: new `flat_layout=True` parameter to `_scan_mount`, passed by the TB-pass caller in `_scan_read`. Sentinel `scan_dirs=['']` so the join evaluates to `mount_path` itself; `category_is_shows` is False (no category hint, rely on per-folder SxxExx detection). 4 new tests covering flat-layout with show episodes, with movies, regression-guard for categorized layout, and a realistic-noisy-name case using the live Landman/FAM folder name patterns. **(6) Library scanner couldn't symlink TB content into local arr libraries (`utils/library.py:3984` — Phase 4 follow-up)**: `_create_debrid_symlinks` hard-coded `real_mount = realpath(BLACKHOLE_RCLONE_MOUNT)` and skipped any episode whose `realpath` didn't start with that single mount. TB-source episodes (correctly detected by Phase 4 + the `flat_layout` fix) couldn't pass that gate. Result: even with TB content fully detected in the scan output, ZERO TB symlinks ever landed in `/local_media/tv` — the load-bearing final step of Phase 4 that wasn't wired up. Fix: new closure `_resolve_symlink_target(real_debrid_path)` iterates a list of per-debrid `(mount, target_base)` pairs and returns `<base> + <suffix-under-that-mount>` for the first match. Both the movie and show branches use it. RD-only setups behave identically (single pair). Live verification after deploy: scanner created 70 TB symlinks in one library_scan, Sonarr's `RescanSeries` then imported them — FAM alone went from 2/40 episodes present to 26/40 in one rescan. **(7) Rate-limit detector + per-provider backoff (`utils/blackhole.py`)**: Live-observed during the search-storm validation: Sonarr's `MissingEpisodeSearch` fires dozens of magnet drops within the same second, the blackhole submits all of them to the same debrid provider in rapid succession, the provider returns `rate limit exceeded` on every one after the first few, the blackhole returns False for each, the files land in the 5-min retry queue, the next retry hits the same rate limit. Self-DoS loop. Fix: new module-level `_check_rate_limit(provider)` + `_mark_rate_limited(provider)` + `_is_rate_limit_response(response)` helpers. Each `_add_to_*` calls `_check_rate_limit` first (sleeps until any active window expires) and on HTTP 429 or "rate limit" body text marks the provider as rate-limited for 60s. Per-provider isolation: a 429 on RD does NOT pause TB calls — cache_aware routing keeps moving even when one debrid is under pressure. 12 new tests including parametrised body+status detection, sleep-on-active-window, no-op-when-no-window, per-provider isolation, and expiry. **(8) Status server log spam from client-disconnect tracebacks (`utils/status_server.py`)**: BaseHTTPRequestHandler.handle_one_request raises `BrokenPipeError` / `ConnectionResetError` when polling clients (traefik health checks, watchtower, browser-closed-tabs) disconnect mid-response. socketserver's default `handle_error` prints a ~10-line traceback per disconnect into zurgarr's logs. Override `StatusHandler.handle_one_request` to swallow those two specific exceptions and log at DEBUG; other exceptions propagate unchanged. 4 new tests including a regression-guard that ValueError (a real bug) still raises. **Operational sidecars shipped alongside the code fixes:** `scripts/sweep_tb_orphans.py` — one-shot symlink reconciler that walks the TB mount, looks each release up in Sonarr or Radarr by parsed title (via `library.parse_folder_name`, which already strips `www.<indexer>.<tld>` prefixes via `_SITE_PREFIX_PATTERN`), and creates the symlink `BlackholeWatcher._create_symlinks` would have created on a successful grab. Idempotent (skips already-symlinked entries), dry-run by default, `--apply` to commit. Used live during this session to recover 71 orphans from pre-fix sessions, 0 errors. `scripts/cleanup_tb_false_blocklist.py` — removes spuriously-blocklisted hashes accumulated under bug #1 (uncached-timeout auto-blocklist firing on every TB grab); ran live and removed 70 entries. **Tests:** 41 new tests across `tests/test_blackhole.py`, `tests/test_debrid_client.py`, `tests/test_debrid_health.py`, `tests/test_library.py`, `tests/test_status_ui_enhancements.py`, `tests/test_verify_symlinks.py`. Full suite: 2082 passes (up from 2041 pre-bundle). **Live verification end-to-end:** with all eight fixes deployed and the new ansible playbook (separate repo) bind-mounting `/mnt/debrid_torbox` into Sonarr/Radarr/Bazarr on dockers, TB-source episodes flow correctly through the chain: TB cache → rclone TB mount on plex (zurgarr container) → zurgarr library scanner detects via Phase 4 flat-layout → local-arr-library symlinks via debrid-pair resolution → dockers rclone TB mount → Sonarr follows the symlink → Sonarr DB `hasFile=True` → Plex (snap on plex-host, sees its own `/mnt/debrid_torbox` symlink to `/mnt/remote/realdebrid/torbox`) plays the file.

- **Wanted view: both Movies and Shows now accessible with per-tab counts (user-reported UX regression from 071ba5d/8625e42)**: After the Radarr-wanted-movies feature shipped, the Wanted view started showing ONLY movies for users who had movies-with-ghosts AND shows-with-missing — the opposite of the pre-fix experience that showed only shows. Root cause was twofold: (1) the auto-switch logic at `library_page.py:1909` only fires when the current tab has zero matches, and post-fix the Movies tab always has matches (Radarr ghosts), so it never switches to Shows — user lands on Movies forever; (2) `switchTab` cleared `_activeWantedPreset` and stripped `?filter=missing` from the URL on every tab click, meaning the user couldn't simply click the Shows tab to see wanted shows — they'd exit the Wanted view entirely and have to re-enter via the sidebar. Three fixes land together in this commit. **(a) First-land heavier-tab routing.** New `_wantedTabAutoLanded` one-shot guard: on the first library fetch after entering Wanted mode, count matches in each tab and set `_activeTab` to whichever has more. Ties keep the current tab. After this first decision (or on any manual tab click), the flag flips true so subsequent fetches never override the user. The existing "switch if current is empty" logic stays as a safety net for users whose only wanted items are on one side. **(b) Tab badges show per-tab wanted counts.** `updateBadges` gained a wanted-mode branch that computes each tab's wanted-match count and renders it on the badge regardless of which tab is active. So the user sees `Movies (5)` / `Shows (12)` at a glance and knows there are 12 shows waiting on the other tab. Wanted-mode badge math runs over both `_allMovies` and `_allShows` once per render — O(n) per dataset, fine even for 5000-item libraries. **(c) Tab clicks preserve the Wanted preset.** `switchTab` no longer clears `_activeWantedPreset` or removes `?filter=missing` from the URL — it just toggles the active tab and re-renders. So in Wanted mode, clicking Shows shows you wanted shows; clicking Movies shows wanted movies; the wanted-pills bar stays visible the whole time. To exit Wanted entirely the user clicks the currently-active wanted pill (existing toggle behavior at `library_page.py:1758`) or navigates away. This also sets `_wantedTabAutoLanded = true` so the next fetch's auto-land doesn't bounce the user back to the "heavier" tab against their explicit click. No new tests — pure JS UX surface, validated by suite-green Python (1944 passes) plus reasoning. Pure additive JS; no library-data shape changes.

- **Radarr-wanted-movies: reviewer-flagged fixes for commit 071ba5d**: Code-reviewer + bug-hunter pass on the Option-B ghost-movies commit surfaced five real bugs. **CRITICAL (Wanted view self-erases after one scan cycle):** ghost entries with `source='wanted'` were being processed by `LibraryScanner._search_for_missing_episodes` because its source filter only excluded `('debrid', 'local', 'both')` — `'wanted'` fell through. The function called `ensure_and_search` on Radarr (which is already searching for these movies via its own RSS sync), then wrote `set_pending(...)`. On the *next* scan, `_apply_radarr_wanted_movies`'s pending-suppression block saw that entry and skipped the ghost. Net effect: Wanted view appeared for one scan, then emptied silently — every monitored-no-file movie acquired a zombie pending entry that suppressed it for the 35-day pending TTL. Fix: explicit `if movie.get('source') == 'wanted': continue` at the top of the movie loop in `_search_for_missing_episodes`. Radarr owns the search for these movies; pd_zurg shouldn't issue redundant `/command/MoviesSearch` calls. **HIGH (Ghost-vs-real duplication after TMDB rename):** the pre-injection dedup in `_apply_radarr_wanted_movies` uses parsed-folder norms (e.g. `"F1 The Movie"`), but `_enrich_with_tmdb_cache._maybe_rename` runs later and rewrites a real entry's `title` to its canonical TMDB spelling (`"F1"`), which can now collide with a ghost that was injected before enrichment ran. Without intervention, the library renders two cards for the same movie — one real (green, Available) and one ghost (red, Wanted). Fix: new `_strip_ghost_duplicates(movies)` post-enrichment dedup pass that walks the movie list once after `_apply_sonarr_monitored_filter` and drops any ghost whose post-rename `(normalized_title, year)` matches a real on-disk entry. Real entries always win — the ghost was a synthetic placeholder. Subsumes the MEDIUM finding about alias-map ignorance (post-enrichment titles already reflect the alias graph) and the year-mismatch concern when enrichment fills in a previously-None year on the real entry. **HIGH (JS bulk-search silently skips ghost movies):** both `_resolveMissingTasks` (line 1752ish) and the selection-mode task builder (line 940ish) in `library_page.py` gate movie tasks on `item.missing_episodes > 0` — a shows-only field that's never set on movies. Result: when the user selects ghost movies in the Wanted view and clicks "Search for missing", the bulk search silently builds an empty task list for them, defeating one of the primary use-cases for the feature. Fix: both sites now check `item.missing === true || item.missing_episodes > 0` (the same dual-field pattern already established in `_countAiredMissing` and `computeProgress`). **LOW (duplicate Radarr API call per scan):** `LibraryScanner._create_debrid_symlinks` called `client.get_all_movies()` directly at line 3660 instead of routing through the new `_get_radarr_movies_list(client)` cache helper introduced in 071ba5d. Mirrors how the function already uses `_get_sonarr_series_list` for Sonarr at line 3634. With this fix the same scan cycle shares one HTTP round-trip for the full Radarr movie list across `_apply_radarr_wanted_movies` and `_create_debrid_symlinks` instead of paying for two. **LOW (Delete-from-Radarr broken for ghosts):** `library_page.deleteItem` required `_detailMeta.tmdb_id`, but ghost entries whose TMDB cache lookup hasn't populated yet (recently-monitored movie not in the local cache) have a null `_detailMeta`. The ghost dict already carries `_radarr_tmdb_id` (same TMDB ID, set at injection time), so the fix is a one-line JS fallback: prefer `_detailMeta.tmdb_id`, fall back to `_detailItem._radarr_tmdb_id`, only error if both are null. **Test coverage:** 7 new `TestStripGhostDuplicates` tests (collision dropped, unique ghost preserved, year mismatch keeps both, no-real / only-real edge cases, multiple-ghost partial-collision behaviour, empty no-op) plus 1 new `TestSearchForMissingEpisodesSkipGhosts` test that monkeypatches `RadarrClient.ensure_and_search` and asserts it is NOT called when the movies list contains only a ghost entry — the critical regression reproducer for the self-erase bug. Full suite: 1944 passes.

- **Library page: movies now appear in the Wanted view (architectural gap, not just a typo)**: User report — "Wanted only shows shows, no movies, is that correct?" Root cause was twofold. **(1) Dead-code bug in `get_wanted_counts`**: the movie loop checked `movie.get('missing_episodes')`, a field that's only set on shows (assigned at `library.py:581-582, 722, 728`). Movies never had this field, so `me is not None and me > 0` was always False and movies were silently excluded from the 'missing' bucket entirely. The JS mirror in `library_page.py:_countAiredMissing` had the same bug. **(2) The bigger issue: the scanner had no data source for monitored-but-not-downloaded movies.** Library scanner reads from disk (debrid mount + local); a movie you've requested via Radarr but haven't downloaded yet is invisible to the scanner, so even fixing the field-name bug would have left the Wanted view empty for movies in practice. **Option B fix per the user's call:** integrate Radarr's monitored movie list and inject "ghost" entries into the library data. New `_apply_radarr_wanted_movies(movies, pending)` walks `RadarrClient.get_all_movies()` and for each movie with `monitored=True` AND `hasFile=False` injects a ghost entry: `{title, year, source: 'wanted', size_bytes: 0, path: '', missing: True, _radarr_id, _radarr_tmdb_id}`. Wired into `_scan_read` immediately after the debrid+local movie merge step and before TMDB enrichment, so ghost entries also get poster URLs / IMDb IDs / canonical titles from the cache. **Dedup cascade mirroring `_apply_sonarr_monitored_filter`'s approach**: TMDB ID is authoritative (Radarr-supplied `tmdbId` matches existing library `tmdb_id` → suppress ghost), `(normalized_title, year)` is the fallback for hand-imported libraries without `tmdb_id`. Year-mismatch (Dune 1984 vs Dune 2021) correctly does NOT dedup — they're different movies. **Pending suppression**: a movie currently being downloaded already counts under the 'pending' bucket via the existing `pending_monitors` mechanism, so the ghost is suppressed to avoid double-counting. **Source bucketing safety**: the new `'wanted'` source label is intentionally outside the `('local', 'debrid', 'both')` set used by every other code path — preference enforcement, symlink workflows, mount-driven actions all use `src in ('local', 'debrid', 'both')` checks that naturally skip ghosts without needing defensive code at each site. **`compute_library_stats` updated** to skip wanted-source movies explicitly — otherwise the unknown-source fallback at line 5031 would have bucketed them into `'debrid'`, inflating the Library Composition card's debrid count by the user's entire Radarr wanted list. **Performance**: new `_radarr_movies_cache` (120 s TTL, threading.Lock) mirrors the existing Sonarr series cache so a `find_movie_in_library` call sharing the same scan window doesn't double-hit the API. **UI**: `buildBadges('wanted')` returns the existing red `.badge-missing` styling (the same one used for episode-level missing markers), and `computeProgress` for ghost movies returns a 0% red bar with tooltip "Wanted — not yet downloaded" instead of the default 100% green "Available" that real movies show. Ghost movies render in the library grid like any other entry; clicking opens the existing detail view (no file actions are exposed since `size_bytes=0` and existing paths-conditional code short-circuits cleanly). **Edge cases covered by 12 new `TestApplyRadarrWantedMovies` tests**: monitored+no-file injected, hasFile=True skipped, unmonitored skipped, tmdb_id dedup, title+year dedup, year mismatch correctly NOT deduped, pending suppression, Radarr unavailable graceful no-op, Overseerr fallback (returned by `get_download_service` when Radarr is unconfigured) detected and skipped, fetch-failure graceful, empty Radarr no-op, multiple injects with mixed conditions. Plus 3 new `TestGetRadarrMoviesList` tests for the TTL cache (cached/force-refresh/fetch-failure), 2 new `TestGetWantedCounts` cases (multiple ghosts counted, real movies without `missing` field not counted), and 1 new `TestComputeLibraryStats` test confirming `'wanted'` source is excluded from buckets. Updated 2 existing `TestGetWantedCounts` tests that were asserting the old buggy behavior — `test_movie_with_missing_episodes` renamed to `test_movie_ghost_entry_counted_as_missing` (now asserts the `missing` bool drives the count), `test_movie_with_none_missing_episodes_not_counted` replaced with `test_real_movie_without_missing_field_not_counted` (asserts the inverse: real disk entries don't count as missing regardless of fields). Full suite: 1936 passes. **What's still missing**: the "Wanted" sidebar nav badge updates on every status poll, so after deploy the count should immediately jump to include monitored-no-file Radarr movies. The library page Wanted filter pill at `/library?filter=missing` should now surface them. Detail-page UX for ghost movies is intentionally minimal in this v1 — they appear as cards with posters and a "Wanted" badge but no playable file actions; refinement (e.g. "Search Radarr now" button on detail page) can land in a follow-up if the basic surface proves useful.

- **Debrid health phase 4.1 follow-up: episode-level search restriction, retrigger-history thread safety, cooldown rollback on API failure, filename basename sanitisation**: Code-reviewer + bug-hunter pass on the phase 4.1 commit (`b950603`) surfaced four real bugs — three new, one pre-existing but amplified by the new `_remediate` caller. All fixed in one commit per the CLAUDE.md "fix every real bug a reviewer surfaces, regardless of scope" rule. **(1) Episode-level search restriction (H1, new).** `force_episodes=True` on a single-ep release name like `Show.S01E04.mkv` was correctly bypassing the `hasFile` gate, but then queueing *every* `hasFile=True` episode in the matched season — a 1-ep blocked anime release on a 200-ep show would fan out to 200 Sonarr search jobs. The new `_SINGLE_EP_RE = re.compile(r'[.\s]S\d{1,2}E(\d{1,4})(?![E\d-])', re.IGNORECASE)` module-level regex extracts the episode number when the release name contains an unambiguous `S##E##` pattern (negative lookahead `(?![E\d-])` excludes multi-ep `S01E04E05` and hyphenated `S01E04-05`/`S01E04-E05`). When matched, the TV loop adds a second filter (`if target_episode is not None and ep.get('episodeNumber') != target_episode: continue`) so only the named episode is queued. Multi-ep, season-pack, and unparseable release names fall through to the existing season-wide behavior — better extra searches than missed work in the ambiguous cases. Log line gained an `E##` suffix when an ep is targeted. **(2) `_retrigger_history` thread safety (M1, pre-existing).** The module-level cooldown dict is mutated by four daemon-thread callers (`_attempt_arr_research` from `verify_symlinks`, from `library._cleanup_broken_debrid_symlinks`, from `debrid_health._remediate`, plus `detect_stale_grabs`) with no lock — `_prune_retrigger_history` was vulnerable to `RuntimeError: dictionary changed size during iteration` and every check-then-set pair had a race window letting two threads queue the same item. New `_retrigger_history_lock = threading.Lock()` protects every read/write; the lock is held only for the in-memory ops (the for-loop building `target_eps`, the membership check + insert in the movie branch, the iterate-and-delete in `_prune_retrigger_history`, the check + set in `detect_stale_grabs`) and never across arr API calls (which can block for seconds and would serialise unrelated tasks). Pre-existing, but the new `_remediate` caller adds the fourth concurrent writer so this hurts more now. **(3) Cooldown rollback on API failure (L1, pre-existing).** Both branches of `_attempt_arr_research` reserved cooldown entries inside the build loop, then called `search_episodes`/`search_movie` AFTER releasing the loop — if the API raised (RD/arr down, transient 5xx, network blip), the cooldown entries persisted for 2 h with no actual search ever fired. Operators saw a stack trace then 2 h of silent dead time for affected eps. Now the API call is wrapped in try/except and on exception the reserved entries are popped under the lock before re-raising — next sweep retries instead. Behavior change for the existing `verify_symlinks` and `library._cleanup_broken_debrid_symlinks` callers too: an arr-API blip during their repair pass no longer silently muzzles the affected items for 2 h. **(4) Filename basename sanitisation in `_remediate` (M2, new).** RD's `/torrents` API returns the raw uploader-provided `filename` field — most are bare release dir names but uploaders can pack a path like `Show/S01/ep.mkv`. `os.path.splitext('Show/S01/ep.mkv')[0]` returns `'Show/S01/ep'`, which `parse_release_name` happily fuzzy-matches against Sonarr's library — under `force_episodes=True` that could blast the *wrong* show's episodes with searches. The one-line fix wraps with `os.path.basename()` first. **`detect_stale_grabs` scope note.** This task's existing pattern is check, API call, then set cooldown (set AFTER successful API) — so it already has the correct rollback semantics by virtue of ordering. The lock protects its dict ops but the structural pattern is unchanged. **Tests:** 5 new (season-pack still queues whole season, multi-ep release falls back to season-wide, Sonarr search failure rolls back cooldown, Radarr search failure rolls back cooldown, `_remediate` strips path from filename). 2 existing assertions updated from `[555, 556]` to `[555]` to reflect the new ep-level restriction (`test_attempt_arr_research_force_episodes_includes_hasFile_true_target_ep` renamed accordingly). Full suite: 1912 passes.

- **Debrid health: TV episodes now get Sonarr re-search after auto-remediation (plan 38 phase 4.1)**: Live validation of phase 4 on 2026-05-24 surfaced an asymmetry — auto-remediation deleted 32 filter-blocked torrents from RD and blocklisted all 32 hashes, but only 7/9 movies got a Radarr re-search and 0/23 TV episodes got a Sonarr re-search. Root cause sat in `scheduled_tasks._attempt_arr_research`'s TV branch: episodes were only queued for search when `ep.get('hasFile')` was falsy. That gate is correct for the function's two pre-existing callers (`verify_symlinks` and `library._cleanup_broken_debrid_symlinks`), both of which only run *after* a symlink has already been observed missing, so by the time they call the helper Sonarr's last scan tends to agree the episode is gone. The debrid-health remediation path is different: it calls `_attempt_arr_research` at the same instant it issues `client.delete_torrent(torrent_id)` — Zurg's WebDAV listing keeps the just-deleted file visible for ~15-30 s until its next `/torrents` poll, the FUSE stat still succeeds, Sonarr's most recent scan still reports `hasFile=True`, and every episode in the affected release got skipped. The Radarr branch never had an equivalent gate (it relies solely on the 2 h `_retrigger_history` cooldown for dedup), which is why all in-library movies fired correctly. Fix is minimal and additive: `_attempt_arr_research` now takes an optional `force_episodes=False` kwarg that bypasses the `hasFile` gate when True, and `debrid_health._remediate` is the only caller that passes `force_episodes=True`. The season filter still applies (S01-only release still queues S01 episodes only, not S02), and the 2 h cooldown still applies (multiple subsystems converging on the same episode in a window still dedupe per-episode), so the override is scoped strictly to the one true positive case where "Sonarr thinks the file is there but we just deleted it from upstream and know better." 4 new tests in `tests/test_debrid_health.py:TestForceEpisodesGate`: end-to-end debrid-health sweep with mocked Sonarr returning `hasFile=True` episodes still queues `search_episodes` (the live-validation regression), `_attempt_arr_research` without the kwarg still skips `hasFile=True` episodes (contract pin for `verify_symlinks` so a future refactor can't drop the gate for that caller), `force_episodes=True` includes `hasFile=True` episodes while still respecting season filtering, and successive `force_episodes=True` calls for the same episode within the 2 h window only fire once. Full suite: 1907 tests pass.

- **Blackhole uncached-timeout now auto-blocklists the hash and always emits a `failed` history event**: When a `.magnet`/`.torrent` Sonarr drops never caches on the debrid provider (typical for old TV / dead-swarm releases — e.g. a UK BBC show whose only WEB-DL release has 0 seeders), `_monitor_and_symlink` hits `mount_poll_timeout` and gives up. The terminal-error path (line 1932) and the disc-rip path (line 1858) both auto-blocklisted the hash via `BLOCKLIST_AUTO_ADD` so the same dead release isn't re-grabbed on the next wanted-search; the uncached-timeout path was the odd one out and let Sonarr keep re-grabbing the same release every ~6 hours forever (real-world impact: 18 grabs / 17 timeouts in 30 days for a single Broadchurch release, none ever importing). Now the timeout block extracts the hash via `_extract_hash_from_info(info)` (RD/AD/TB all populate `hash` even on uncached torrents, so this is reliable except in the pathological "every status poll raised" case where the add is silently skipped) and calls `_blocklist.add(hash, filename, reason='Uncached on debrid (timed out)', source='auto')`, then emits a `blocklist_added` history event with `cause=auto_blocklist_added`, `blocklist_reason=uncached_timeout`. The next time Sonarr drops the same `.magnet` into the watch folder, the existing pre-submit blocklist check at `_process_file` (line 2861) rejects it before it ever hits the debrid API and emits a `blocklisted` history event so the skip is visible in the activity feed. **Buddy fix surfaced while restructuring the block:** the `failed` history event was previously *inside* the `if BLACKHOLE_DELETE_UNCACHED_ON_TIMEOUT == 'true':` gate, so the default-off install never logged uncached timeouts at all — users couldn't see why their Wanted episodes weren't arriving. The `failed` event now fires unconditionally with `meta.deleted` reflecting whether the RD-cleanup actually ran, and the auto-blocklist sub-block sits outside the cleanup gate too (the two concerns — "clean RD on timeout" and "auto-blocklist failed torrents" — are orthogonal env vars and now compose independently). Five new tests in `tests/test_blackhole_debrid_dedup.py:TestBlackholeUncachedTimeoutAutoBlocklist` cover: history fires when cleanup opt-in is off, blocklist add at default `BLOCKLIST_AUTO_ADD=true` with cleanup off (records hash, source=auto, correct reason and meta), blocklist add skipped when `BLOCKLIST_AUTO_ADD=false` (history still fires), blocklist add skipped when info-hash unavailable (every status poll raised before timeout — `failed` history still fires for the audit trail), and end-to-end with both cleanup and blocklist on (records `deleted=True` on the failed event and the blocklist entry). All 33 tests in the file pass; full blackhole test suites (`test_blackhole.py`, `test_blackhole_completeness.py`, `test_activity_format.py`) green at 293 tests.

- **Library symlink cleanup: broken debrid symlinks now trigger Sonarr/Radarr re-search instead of disappearing silently**: `library.py:_cleanup_broken_debrid_symlinks` runs every library scan and deletes symlinks whose debrid targets no longer exist on the rclone mount (e.g., when a debrid release is removed from the user's RD library). Until now it deleted the symlinks without telling the arrs — Sonarr/Radarr's database still held file IDs pointing at the deleted paths, so they continued reporting the affected episodes/movies as "downloaded" until their next disk scan. The 6-hourly `verify_symlinks` task does trigger a re-search via `_attempt_arr_research` (phase 3), but the library scan runs more often and beat it to the deletion, so by the time `verify_symlinks` fired it found 0 broken symlinks and triggered 0 re-searches. Real-world impact: a user whose `Dexter S01-S08 FraMeSToR [RiCK]` pack was removed from RD lost all 12 S01 + 12 S05 .mkv symlinks (no separate per-season releases existed for those two seasons), but Sonarr still showed both seasons as "Available" because nothing told it to re-stat. The Library page correctly reported `episodes=0` for those seasons because the broken `.mkv`s no longer counted as media files; Sonarr disagreed because its DB was stale. Fix: `_cleanup_broken_debrid_symlinks` now records the unique release names of every deleted symlink (extracted via `scheduled_tasks._extract_release_info` from the symlink target — same parser `verify_symlinks` already uses), and after the deletion loop, gated on `gap_fill_enabled()` (default `true`, mirrors `verify_symlinks`' opt-out), iterates the deduped set and calls `scheduled_tasks._attempt_arr_research(release_name)` for each. That helper parses the release name, looks up the show/movie in Sonarr/Radarr by title, and triggers a search for episodes/movies that lack files — with its existing `_retrigger_history` cooldown so frequent library scans (default 30 min) can't stampede searches for the same release. Sonarr/Radarr parity is preserved because `_attempt_arr_research` already routes by `is_tv` from the parsed release name. The function now also emits a `log_event('cleanup', 'Library Symlink Cleanup', meta={'cause': 'library_symlink_cleanup', 'deleted': N, 'searched': M})` so the work is visible in the Activity feed instead of only the per-symlink debug log line; new `library_symlink_cleanup` cause registered in `history.py` plus matching Python and JS formatters in `activity_format.py` per the CLAUDE.md "add a new cause in three places" rule. Lazy imports (`from utils.scheduled_tasks import ...` inside the function) avoid a circular import since `scheduled_tasks` already imports `library.gap_fill_enabled` at the top of `verify_symlinks`. Cleanup-without-deletions short-circuits to `return` before logging an event, so noiseless scans don't create empty Activity rows.

- **Library page: parser-junk debrid titles now merge with the canonical local entry into a single library card with `source='both'`, instead of surfacing two separate posters**: The prior commit fixed the symlink-creation cascade in `_create_debrid_symlinks` so new debrid symlinks land in the canonical Radarr/Sonarr folder. But the user still saw two posters for the same movie because the matching cascade exists in two *more* places upstream of symlink creation: (1) the title-level merge step inside `_scan_read` (lines 1590-1681) where debrid + local lists get combined into the unified library data model with a `source='both'` flag when both sides have the title, and (2) the TMDB-cache enrichment step in `_enrich_with_tmdb_cache` (lines 614-647) where each item's display title gets renamed from the parsed-folder name to the canonical TMDB title. Both used direct `_normalize_title(parsed)` lookups against `local_movie_keys` / `cached`, so a parser-junk title like `"Gattaca Ethan Hawke Sci Fi"` failed to bridge to the canonical `"Gattaca"` cache entry → no merge → no rename → two cards on the Library page even though the underlying release was for the same movie. Both cascade sites now use the same `_find_canonical_tmdb_via_prefix` helper deployed in the prior commit. Merge step: when direct + TMDB-id-alias matches both miss, run the prefix resolver against the local key set; if `_normalize_title(canonical['title'])` is in `local_movie_keys`, treat that as the matched key and register the alias bidirectionally in `alias_norms_local`. Symmetric for shows via `local_show_map`. Enrichment step: when `get_cached_posters` returns no info for the parsed key, run the prefix resolver and re-query `get_cached_posters` with the canonical title — `_maybe_rename` then fires and the item's display title becomes the canonical TMDB title. Together these mean: parsed `"Gattaca Ethan Hawke Sci Fi"` (debrid folder) + `"Gattaca"` (local folder) → ONE merged item with `source='both'`, display title `"Gattaca"`, ready for the user's `prefer-debrid` preference auto-enforcement to replace the local file with a symlink in place.
