The merge step pre-loads the full TMDB cache once per scan and threads it through to the enrichment step via the new `_shared_tmdb_cache=` kwarg, so both phases see a consistent snapshot — closes a transient-duplicate window where a `populate_cache` write between two loads could leave an item un-merged at merge-time then renamed at enrichment-time. **Hardening surfaced in review and fixed in the same change:** (a) The prefix-match path in the merge step had a self-loop alias bug — when the resolver returned a canonical title whose normalized form equaled the parsed key (degenerate case where both sides were already canonical), `alias_norms_local.setdefault(key, set()).add(canon_key)` would register `{"gattaca": {"gattaca"}}`, polluting downstream `len(aliases) > 0` checks and risking redundant lookup loops. The seed-loop convention at lines 1638-1646 explicitly excludes `name` from its own alias set via `group - {name}`; the new path now matches that convention via a `canon_key != key` guard. Same fix applied symmetrically to the shows merge. (b) The new prefix-match registration was a duplicate of the existing alias-match registration immediately below it — both `setdefault(...).add(...)` paths fired on the same `(key, matched_key)` pair when prefix-match hit. Set semantics made it idempotent but it was a structural seam. Removed the duplicate so registration happens exactly once at the unified `if matched_key != key:` site for both alias-match and prefix-match paths. (c) The enrichment helper `_resolve_canonical_info`'s `get_cached_posters` re-query passes the parsed item's year, which can diverge from the canonical TMDB release year (off-by-one is common for festival vs wide-release dates). The yearless fallback at the end of the helper recovers this because `get_cached_posters` stores info under both year-qualified and yearless keys (`tmdb.py:664-666`); added an inline comment documenting that the yearless fallback is the true success path when years diverge. **9 new tests** in `tests/test_library.py:TestMergeStepCanonicalFallback` (5 cases) and `TestEnrichmentCanonicalFallback` (4 cases) cover: parser-junk movie merges with local via canonical, parser-junk show merges with local via canonical, no-match-when-canonical-not-in-local (regression guard), self-loop guard prevents degenerate alias registration (the reviewer-surfaced bug), existing direct-match still works (regression guard), enrichment renames movie via prefix resolver, enrichment renames show via prefix resolver, no rename when resolver misses, direct lookup short-circuits prefix resolver. Pre-existing `TestWebDAVUnsupportedMemoization` tests that monkeypatched `_enrich_with_tmdb_cache` with a 2-arg lambda were updated to accept the new `_shared_tmdb_cache=` kwarg via `**kw`. Full suite: 1845 tests pass.

- **Library scanner: debrid releases with junk-bearing folder names now merge with the canonical local entry instead of creating an orphan folder**: When the user switched a movie from local to debrid, the library scanner could create a fresh `/local_media/movies/Gattaca Ethan Hawke Sci Fi (1997)/` directory containing only a debrid symlink, alongside the existing canonical `/local_media/movies/Gattaca (1997)/` — visible in the Library page as two separate posters for the same movie. Root cause: the matching cascade in `_create_debrid_symlinks` (movie/show dir selection + post-symlink rescan trigger, four sites total) used `cached_tmdb_movies.get(_normalize_title(parsed_title))` for its TMDB-direct fallback, which is an exact key match. For a parsed title with appended actor names + genre tags (`"Gattaca Ethan Hawke Sci Fi"`), the normalized key `"gattaca ethan hawke sci fi"` doesn't match the cache's actual key `"gattaca"` → cascade fell through to the parsed-title folder-name fallback → orphan folder. New helper `_find_canonical_tmdb_via_prefix(parsed_title, parsed_year, is_tv)` in `library.py` performs a token-aligned prefix scan over the cache: tokens of the cache key must be a strict prefix of the parsed-title's `_norm_for_matching` tokens, with the same hardening already deployed for the activity-feed resolver in `blackhole.py` — single-token candidates fail-closed (require year confirmation; defends against cache entries titled "The"/"It" prefix-matching every release), multi-token candidates fail-open on missing entry-year (legacy entries lack `release_date`/`first_air_date`), longest-prefix wins, per-entry try/except so one malformed cache row can't poison the whole scan, isinstance guards on `title`/`cache_key`/date strings to prevent AttributeError from non-string fields. Wired into all four cascade sites symmetrically per the CLAUDE.md "title matching cascade appears in 4 places that must stay symmetric" rule. With this in place, the user's Gattaca scenario now resolves: parsed `"Gattaca Ethan Hawke Sci Fi"` + year 1997 → token prefix matches cache key `"gattaca (1997)"` → canonical `tmdb_id=782` → `radarr_by_tmdb` lookup hits the existing Radarr movie → symlink lands inside the canonical `Gattaca (1997)` folder, no orphan created. **Hardening surfaced in review and fixed in the same change:** (a) The new helper was originally re-loading `/config/tmdb_cache.json` from disk on every invocation and re-acquiring `_tmdb._cache_lock` per call — multiplied by 4 cascade sites × N symlinked items, that was hundreds of redundant reads per scan AND widened the lock window for concurrent TMDB writers. The cache is now loaded once per `_create_debrid_symlinks` invocation (alongside the existing `cached_tmdb_ids` load) and threaded into all four call sites via the helper's existing `_tmdb_cache=` parameter — single read, single lock acquisition, no behavior change. (b) `_extract_tmdb_entry_year` had a dead `try/except ValueError` around `int(date[:4])` that could never fire because the preceding `date[:4].isdigit()` guard already excludes non-numeric input — removed for clarity. (c) Added a one-line comment at the movie rescan-trigger cascade explaining why the season-aware `find_show_tmdb_id_by_season` sub-step is absent there (it's TV-only and the absence is intentional symmetry-with-asymmetry, not an oversight). The duplication with `blackhole.py`'s parallel `_lookup_canonical_in_tmdb` (which returns just the canonical title string for activity-feed display) is conscious — different return-type API for different callers, lifting to a shared module would require changing the already-deployed blackhole code path and risking its 36 passing tests for zero user-visible gain. **21 new tests** in `tests/test_library.py:TestExtractTmdbEntryYear` (5 cases: release_date / first_air_date / precedence / missing / 6 malformed-input variants) and `TestFindCanonicalTmdbViaPrefix` (16 cases: actor+genre regression / year mismatch / longest-prefix tiebreaker / mid-string match rejection / single-token year-required guard in 3 sub-cases / single-token year-match-accepted / single-token no-entry-year fail-closed / multi-token entry-year fail-open / shows vs movies section routing / empty cache / empty title / non-dict entries / non-string cache keys / non-string title fields / missing tmdb_id excluded / parsed-year-None multi-token fail-open). Full suite: 1836 tests pass.

- **Activity feed: blackhole grab events now show the canonical media title instead of the raw release name**: When a `.torrent`/`.magnet` landed in the blackhole watch folder, `_enrich_for_history` populated `media_title` via `parse_release_name` — a ~30-line naive parser whose movie-path year regex requires `[.\s](\d{4})[.\s]` (literal `.` or whitespace on both sides). For a real release like `Gattaca.Ethan.Hawke.Sci.Fi.(1997).1080p.BluRay.x264-GROUP.torrent` the year is wrapped in parens, the regex misses, and the parser falls through to the quality regex — leaving `Gattaca Ethan Hawke Sci Fi (1997)` as the activity-feed title. Same failure mode for `[YYYY]` brackets, `-1997-` dashes, and any release that injects actor names or genre tags before the year. Three-step cascade in `_enrich_for_history` replaces the single naive call: (1) the existing `parse_release_name` still establishes `is_tv`/season/episode for `ep_str` (its other call sites are unchanged), (2) a new `_resolve_canonical_title` runs `library.parse_folder_name` — the ~250-line robust parser the library scanner already uses — which handles bracketed/parenthesized years (`_BRACKET_YEAR_PATTERN`, `_MID_YEAR_PATTERN`), dash-separated quality markers (`_QUALITY_PATTERN` accepts `[\s.\-_(\[]`), edition tags, language+codec tails, indexer URL prefixes, genre suffixes between title and year, and trailing bare years; (3) when the parser still leaves trailing junk (actor names + genre tag injected before the year, where there's no algorithmic way to know "Ethan Hawke" isn't part of the title), a new `_lookup_canonical_in_tmdb` does a token-aligned prefix match against the local TMDB cache (`/config/tmdb_cache.json`) — for the Gattaca filename, `["gattaca","ethan","hawke","sci","fi"]` starts with cache key `["gattaca"]` whose entry has `release_date=1997-10-24`, so the parsed year (1997) confirms and the canonical TMDB `title` field ("Gattaca") wins. Each cascade step falls back safely: TMDB module import failure, missing cache file, malformed cache entries (`isinstance(entry, dict)` check), `parse_folder_name` exceptions, year mismatch, and empty parsed title all flow back to the previous step's output, with the naive parser as the final fallback so the function never returns worse output than before. Year confirmation is fail-open when the cache entry lacks `release_date`/`first_air_date` (legacy entries). Longest-prefix-wins disambiguates "The Dark Knight" from a hypothetical "The Dark" cache entry. is_tv routes the lookup to the `shows` vs `movies` cache section so a Breaking Bad episode can't accidentally resolve through a movie spoof entry. `library.py` already exposes the public aliases `parse_folder_name`/`normalize_title`/`norm_for_matching` for cross-module reuse, and the `tmdb` import is lazy inside the resolver so module-load order and circular-import risk are unchanged. **Hardening surfaced in review and fixed in the same change:** (a) The token-aligned prefix match was vulnerable to single-word cache entries acting as false-positive prefixes — e.g. a cache entry titled "The" (the 2017 film, real TMDB id 480530) would prefix-match every release whose name starts with "The" and win when no longer-token candidate exists in the user's cache (most active precisely when the cache is sparse, which is the use case the resolver targets — pre-library-scan grabs). The single-token branch now fail-closes: it requires both a parsed year AND an entry year that matches, instead of inheriting the multi-token branch's fail-open semantics. The multi-token branch keeps fail-open for missing entry-year (legacy entries lack `release_date`/`first_air_date` and the candidate's specificity already protects). (b) `_safe_entry_title` and an `isinstance(date, str)` guard in `_extract_entry_year` defend against malformed cache entries where `title`/`release_date`/`first_air_date` is non-string (dict/list/int) — calling `.strip()` on those raised AttributeError that escaped `_lookup_canonical_in_tmdb` → escaped `_resolve_canonical_title` (its sole try/except wraps `parse_folder_name` only) → propagated out of `_enrich_for_history` to the blackhole main loop and broke history logging for the entire grab event. The per-entry scan loop is now also wrapped in a defensive `try/except Exception: continue` so any future per-entry surprise drops just that entry. (c) Import-failure and cache-load-failure paths now emit `logger.debug` lines so silent grab-event degradation is discoverable in production instead of being swallowed without trace. (d) `_BARE_YEAR_RE` is now a module-level constant instead of being recompiled on every call, matching the pattern of all other regexes in `blackhole.py` and `library.py`. **36 new tests** in `tests/test_blackhole.py:TestEnrichForHistoryRobustParsing` (10 cases) and `TestCanonicalTitleResolution` (26 cases) cover: parens-year, bracket-year, dash-separated year, edition tag stripping, indexer URL prefix, the original Gattaca actor+genre regression, regression guards on every existing parse_release_name and _enrich_for_history input, direct year-qualified cache hit, prefix-match recovery from actor/genre junk, year-mismatch exclusion, longest-prefix tiebreaker, mid-string match rejection (must be true prefix), shows-section vs movies-section routing, empty cache fallback, `_load_cache` exception fallback, `parse_folder_name` exception fallback, empty/None filename pass-through, extension-only filename, missing parsed year (no year filter), multi-token entry-year fail-open, non-dict cache entry skip, end-to-end `_enrich_for_history` cache hit, **the two reviewer-surfaced fixes** (single-token prefix requires year confirmation in three sub-cases: year mismatch, no parsed year, no entry year; single-token year match accepted; non-string title in entry doesn't crash; non-string title during prefix scan skipped without poisoning sibling matches), and direct unit tests for `_extract_entry_year` (release_date branch, first_air_date branch, missing date returns None, malformed/non-string date returns None). Full suite: 1815 tests pass.

### Changed

- **Plan 41 phase D: TB cooldown-aware add gating + rclone tps throttling + RD low-level-retries cap + chunked rate-limit sleep**: Four log-noise / wasted-call mitigations under one phase. **D.0 — TB cooldown-aware add gating.** TorBox does NOT surface its account-level daily-quota cooldown via HTTP 429 — instead `createtorrent` returns `HTTP 400 {"error":"DOWNLOAD_SERVER_ERROR",...}` while `/v1/api/user/me` reports an active `cooldown_until`. The previous `_add_to_torbox` fell through to `return False, response.text[:200]` on this shape, so every subsequent rescue attempt during the cooldown window wasted a `createtorrent` call (and contributed to the rclone read-429 cascade by keeping the connection pool busy). New `utils/blackhole.py::_check_torbox_cooldown(api_key)` reads the cooldown timestamp from `/user/me` and returns "seconds remaining" (0 on no cooldown, parse error, or network hiccup — every degradation path is safe-default-to-zero so a transient `/user/me` outage cannot wedge the add pipeline). **Cache discipline**: only the success path writes the module-level cache (`_tb_cooldown_cache`); failure paths return 0 without writing so a transient `/user/me` 5xx during a real cooldown does NOT mask the cooldown for the full TTL. Successful results are cached for 30 seconds with elapsed-time decay so callers see a monotonically non-increasing snapshot. Cache reads + writes go through the existing `_rate_limit_lock` so concurrent worker threads can't observe torn state (matches the established pattern next to `_check_rate_limit` / `_mark_rate_limited`). Naive datetime fallback assumes UTC if TB ever drops the `Z` suffix — defends against silent local-time misinterpretation. `_add_to_torbox` consults the probe on the non-200 / non-429 failure path: when cooldown is positive, calls `_mark_rate_limited('torbox', seconds=cooldown_seconds)` and returns a clean `"TorBox account cooldown active for Ns — gating subsequent adds"` message. Subsequent adds block on `_check_rate_limit('torbox')` for the exact remaining duration rather than per-call retries. Verified manually that TB's live API returns the predicted 400 + `cooldown_until` shape during an active window (2026-05-27 — `cooldown_until="2026-05-28T08:40:58Z"` against a quota-exhausted Essential account); the new code path is unit-tested end-to-end (`TestTorboxAddCooldownGate`) but is not yet container-verified — that confirmation will come when the next live cooldown hits the deployed container post-merge. **D.1 — TB rclone tpslimit.** New env vars `TORBOX_RCLONE_TPSLIMIT` (default `5`) and `TORBOX_RCLONE_TPSLIMIT_BURST` (default `10`) added to `rclone/rclone.py` TB-mount branch as `--tpslimit=N --tpslimit-burst=M`. Set either to `0` to omit the flag entirely. **FUSE-only**: the entire D.1/D.2 block is gated under `NFSMOUNT != "true"` because `rclone serve nfs` does not accept `--low-level-retries` and would crash on startup with an unknown-flag error. Sized to TB Essential's observed steady-state read ceiling — eliminates the `vfs cache: too many errors 11/10` cascade seen in live logs during concurrent Plex/Bazarr scans. Wired through `base/__init__.py` (`__all__` + Config + module globals), `utils/settings_api.py` (UI schema entries under the TorBox section, `_ENV_DEFAULTS` for accurate UI default rendering, `numeric_ranges` for `(0, 100)` / `(0, 200)` validation), `CONFIGURATION.md` table rows, and `.env.example` commented templates. **D.2 — RD/AD rclone low-level-retries cap.** RD returns HTTP 423 on freshly-grabbed files while the upstream re-cracks the rar; that self-recovers in seconds. Rclone's default 10-retry loop floods the log before giving up. Capped to `--low-level-retries=3` for all non-TB FUSE mounts — pure config tuning, no env knob added since the spec says 3 is safely below the recovery window for every observed 423 case. **D.3 — Chunked rate-limit sleep.** Pre-existing `_check_rate_limit` did a single `time.sleep(window)` which D.0 amplifies from a 60-second worst case to potentially 22-hour cooldowns. A worker thread blocked in a single `time.sleep(79000)` cannot be preempted by a manual rate-limit reset and accumulates a giant non-preemptible window during shutdown. Chunked to `_RATE_LIMIT_SLEEP_CHUNK=300` per iteration with a re-read of the window on each loop, so SIGTERM (which interrupts `time.sleep` via signal) and manual window clears wake the worker within at most 5 minutes regardless of total window length. Test surface: 10 new tests in `tests/test_blackhole.py` (`TestTorboxCooldownProbe` × 8 covering active/no/expired cooldown, network failure, non-200, missing api_key, cache hit + decay, cache TTL expiry; `TestTorboxAddCooldownGate` × 2 covering the integration into `_add_to_torbox` for cooldown-shape vs plain failure). Existing rate-limit tests pass unchanged — the chunked sleep returns identically for windows under 300s. Full suite: 2203 passes (+10). **Known gap (not fixed in this commit):** vendored `plex_debrid/debrid/services/torbox.py` lines 147 + 194 also call `/v1/api/torrents/createtorrent` directly and have the same wasted-call pathology under cooldown when `PD_ENABLED=true`. Mirrors the AD-vendored gap flagged in the previous commit — left for a separate change since the upstream itsToggle path needs ATTRIBUTION housekeeping. Tracked for follow-up.

- **AllDebrid cache-probe path is now a no-op stub (mirrors the RD stub)**: AllDebrid discontinued `/v4/magnet/instant` — every call to both `/v4/magnet/instant` and `/v4.1/magnet/instant` against the live API returns `{"status":"error","error":{"code":"DISCONTINUED",...}}`, and the public AllDebrid API docs no longer list any pre-upload cache-check endpoint anywhere under `/magnet/`. Discovered via the Phase E AD-trial measurement (2026-05-26): a direct probe of 30 sampled filter-blocked hashes from the live queue returned HTTP 200 / `DISCONTINUED` for every call. AD's only remaining cache signal is the `ready` boolean on a successful `/v4.1/magnet/upload` response — which is a state-changing operation and therefore unsuitable for the read-only pre-add probe contract that `check_debrid_cache` exposes. `utils/search.py::_check_cache_ad` has been rewritten to mirror `_check_cache_rd`: returns `{hash: None}` uniformly without touching the network, and emits a single process-lifetime warning that names the discontinued endpoint and the operational implication (`QUALITY_COMPROMISE_ONLY_CACHED=true` with AD configured will treat all hashes as unknown and refuse escalation — set the env var to `false` to opt into aggressive escalation without cache verification, matching the documented RD-stub behaviour). The `_coerce_instant` helper is removed (its only caller was the deleted batch-decode path). Docstring of `check_debrid_cache` is updated so future contributors don't trust the now-incorrect "AD + TB still expose working probes" claim. Behaviour change for users: `QUALITY_COMPROMISE_ONLY_CACHED=true` with AD configured will stop firing compromise escalations (was already broken silently — the dead endpoint was returning 404 → None for every hash, so this is making the silent failure visible). Cached-first sort with AD will degrade to quality order (same as RD). The TorBox `/api/torrents/checkcached` endpoint is unaffected and remains the only provider with a working pre-add cache probe. Test churn: 9 AD-mock tests in `tests/test_search.py::TestCheckDebridCache` deleted (`test_alldebrid_batch_success`, `test_alldebrid_missing_hash_defaults_to_none`, `test_alldebrid_status_failure_returns_none_map`, `test_alldebrid_timeout_returns_none_map`, `test_alldebrid_url_redaction`, `test_alldebrid_uppercase_hash_in_response`, `test_alldebrid_coerces_string_instant`, `test_alldebrid_response_cannot_poison_with_extra_hashes`, `test_coerce_instant_helper`) — they all exercised paths that no longer exist; 3 RD-style stub tests added in their place (`test_alldebrid_returns_unknown`, `test_alldebrid_does_not_hit_network`, `test_ad_warning_emits_once`). Full suite: 2193 passes. The two `test_alldebrid_*` tests follow the RD pattern with a try/finally reset of the `_ad_cache_warning_emitted` module flag so test order cannot pollute later assertions on the warning emission. **Vendored `plex_debrid/debrid/services/alldebrid.py` still calls `/v4/magnet/instant` in two places (lines 73 + 138)** — those are the upstream itsToggle watchlist-download path and are also broken by AD's deprecation. Traced behaviour: AD returns HTTP 200 with `{"status":"error",...}` (not a raise), but the parsed `SimpleNamespace` has no `data.magnets` attribute, so the `instant` lookup raises `AttributeError` which is swallowed by a bare `except: continue` in both call sites — the release is **silently skipped**, not retried via upload+poll as an earlier draft of this note incorrectly claimed. Net effect for AD-only watchlist users: AD never gets credited for any release and the download falls through to the next provider in the chain (or fails entirely if AD is the only provider). Left for a separate change because (a) it's vendored upstream code requiring an ATTRIBUTION update, and (b) the immediate target of this commit is the `utils/search.py` probe path that drives `QUALITY_COMPROMISE_ONLY_CACHED` and `cached_first` sort. Tracked for follow-up.

- **Activity feed: library-scan durations now switch to seconds once they cross 1000ms**: The Activity-feed `task_library_scan` row used to render `Library scan — 565 movies, 117 shows, 80645ms` for a 80.6-second scan, which gets unreadable past a few hundred ms. A new `fmt_duration_ms(dur)` helper in `utils/activity_format.py` formats sub-second values as integer milliseconds (`850ms`) and ≥1000ms values as seconds with one decimal (`1.0s`, `80.6s`). The Python helper and its JS mirror inside `FORMATTER_JS` apply identical rules — both gate on `ms > 0` and reject NaN/non-numeric so the server-rendered `detail` and the live JS renderer never diverge. `utils/scheduled_tasks.py` consumes the same helper for both the legacy `detail=` string and the task runner's `'message'` field, so notifications and logs match the Activity feed. **Hardening surfaced in review and fixed in the same change:** (a) Renamed the helper from `_fmt_duration_ms` (private) to `fmt_duration_ms` and added it to `__all__` since `scheduled_tasks.py` imports it across module boundaries — leading-underscore symbols imported by other modules are a footgun for future refactors. (b) Negative durations (e.g., from clock skew) used to render as `-500ms` in Python but were dropped by the JS guard `ms > 0` — Python now matches by returning `''` for non-positive or NaN inputs, so the same event renders identically in both paths. (c) `scheduled_tasks.py`'s fallback was `_fmt_duration_ms(duration_ms) or f'{duration_ms}ms'`, which would render `'Noneems'` if a future upstream change pushed a non-numeric into `scan_duration_ms`; switched to `or '0ms'` so the fallback is always a sane string. Three new tests in `tests/test_activity_format.py` cover the sub-second/exact-1000ms boundaries and the non-positive/non-numeric drop set; the existing test was updated from asserting `'80645ms'` to `'80.6s'`. Full suite: 1780 tests pass.

- **Sidebar: center the Zurgarr brand and relocate version line above the footer divider**: Follow-up polish on the mobile UI pass. The brand row (`.sidebar-brand`) now uses `justify-content:center` so the logo + "Zurgarr" wordmark are centered in the sidebar instead of left-aligned. The version line (`#header-meta`, populated by the existing `/api/status` JS handler) is no longer rendered inside the brand row and no longer rendered inside the new sidebar footer — it now sits between the closing `</nav>` and the opening `<div class="sidebar-footer">`, so it appears above the footer's top-border divider line rather than below it. CSS for `.sidebar-version` switched from a margin to symmetric `padding:8px 16px` and the `:empty` rule still hides the slot before the JS populates it (avoids a one-frame flash of the divider with no text underneath). No JS changes — `getElementById('header-meta')` still finds the span at its new location.

- **Mobile UI: hamburger no longer overlaps sidebar brand row, theme toggle relocated to sidebar footer as a proper segmented control**: On mobile (`max-width:768px`) the fixed-position hamburger button (`top:8px;left:8px;z-index:1001`) stayed visible while the sidebar was open, sitting on top of the sidebar's brand row and partially obscuring the version text. The sidebar header also crowded the brand link with a small sun-emoji theme toggle button. Both fixed in one change. (1) The hamburger now hides while the sidebar is open via `.sidebar.open ~ .hamburger-btn{display:none}` (general-sibling combinator works because the DOM order is `<aside class="sidebar"> ... <div class="sidebar-backdrop"> ... <button class="hamburger-btn">`). The user closes the sidebar via backdrop tap, nav-link auto-close (existing handler), or the new Escape-key handler. (2) The brand-row sun-emoji button is removed; a new `<div class="sidebar-footer">` at the bottom of the `<aside class="sidebar">` (sidebar is `flex-direction:column`, nav now `flex:1 1 auto` so the footer is pushed to the bottom) holds a segmented pill toggle `[ ☀ Light | 🌙 Dark ]` using the existing icon SVG style instead of emojis (which render inconsistently across platforms). The active half is highlighted via a `.active` class, and the segmented control is built with the correct ARIA pattern: `role="radiogroup"` on the wrapper, `role="radio"` + `aria-checked` + roving `tabindex` on each option. JS rewrite: removed `toggleTheme()`, added `setTheme(theme)`; `applyTheme(theme)` now syncs the segmented control's `.active`, `aria-checked`, and `tabindex` instead of swapping a sun/moon emoji on a single button. **Hardening surfaced in review and fixed in the same change:** (a) Removed dead `.theme-toggle` CSS class that was no longer referenced anywhere. (b) Pre-rendered the Dark option as `.active` / `aria-checked="true"` in the server HTML so a fresh browser profile (no `zurgarr_theme` in localStorage) doesn't briefly render both buttons inactive between body parse and the bottom IIFE running. (c) `toggleSidebar()` now moves keyboard focus to the first nav link when opening (otherwise the focused hamburger button vanishes mid-press and focus falls to body); `closeSidebar()` returns focus to the hamburger button on mobile. (d) Added a global Escape-key listener that calls `closeSidebar()` when the sidebar is open on mobile, giving keyboard users a third dismissal path. Full suite: 1777 tests pass.

- **Status page: Library Composition card — Episodes nested under Shows + source filter**: The Episodes bar was previously rendered as a third sibling row alongside Movies and Shows, which made the relationship between a show and its episode breakdown easy to miss. Episodes now lives inside the Shows row as a collapsed-by-default disclosure: clicking the Shows header (or pressing Enter/Space when focused) toggles a chevron and reveals a left-bordered nested Episodes sub-row beneath the Shows bar. The expanded state is persisted to `localStorage` (`libEpsExpanded`) so it survives the 10s status-poll re-render and full reloads. Added a small segmented filter ("All" / "Local" / "Cloud") above the bars that isolates the card to one source — Local keeps items present locally (`local` + `both` buckets), Cloud keeps items reachable via debrid (`debrid` + `both`); items genuinely available in both places appear under both filters. The filter is applied uniformly to the Total Size headline, the per-category counts/sizes, and every bar segment so all numbers on the card agree. Selection persists in `localStorage` (`libFilter`). Renamed the "Debrid" legend label and bar-segment tooltip to "Cloud" to match the filter button vocabulary; the underlying source-key (`debrid`) and CSS class are unchanged so no data shape changes. Click handlers are wired exactly once (guarded by `_libWired`) so the 10s status poll doesn't restack listeners; `_lastLib` caches the latest payload so handlers re-render with fresh data instead of the stale `lib` captured at wiring time. **Hardening surfaced in review and fixed in the same change:** (a) When the active filter excludes everything (e.g. `local` filter on a debrid-only library), the card now shows a "No items match this filter — Show all" empty-state with a one-click reset, instead of silently rendering all-zero bars. (b) When `epsTot===0` (movie-only library, or filter with no episodes), the Shows-row disclosure affordance is fully suppressed — `data-has-eps="false"` removes the chevron, neutralizes the cursor and hover, drops `aria-expanded`, sets `tabindex="-1"`, and the click/keydown toggle no-ops — so the row doesn't advertise an expand control that would reveal nothing.

- **Activity page: drop emoji icons from Type badges, center Source column**: The Type column on the Activity feed no longer prefixes each badge with an emoji (⬇/✅/🔗/❌/🗑/⇄/🔍/🔄/⚙/🚫/⛔). The colored, per-type badge background is unchanged, so visual differentiation is preserved without the emoji clutter. The Source column header and cells are now center-aligned to match Time and Type.

- **Activity Detail column: replace raw arr API command names with plain English**: The `search_triggered` and `rescan_triggered` events emitted from `arr_client.py` used to write the literal Sonarr/Radarr REST command name into the Detail column (`EpisodeSearch for 3 episode(s)`, `MoviesSearch`, `RescanSeries`, `RescanMovie`) whenever the call site didn't pass a `cause=` slug — which is the case for the manual UI-triggered search/rescan flow inside `ensure_and_search`. These now render as `Sonarr search — 3 episodes`, `Radarr search`, `Sonarr rescan`, `Radarr rescan`, matching the em-dash style used by the cause-formatted strings in `utils/activity_format.py`. Singular/plural is handled correctly (`1 episode` vs `N episodes`). The internal `command_id` is still attached to `meta` for debugging. Cause-formatted events (routing audit retry, post-symlink rescan, etc.) are unchanged.

### Added

- **System page: Debrid Health mini-dashboard (plan 38 phase 5)**: New card at the top of the Tasks tab surfaces the auto-remediator's current state without making the user dig through the Activity feed or the raw `/config/debrid_health.json` state file. Shows: last sweep age (formatted as `Nm ago` / `Nh ago` / `Nd ago` from the max per-entry `ts`, no schema change), total/healthy/blocked/unknown counts from the state file, current `Auto-remediate` pill (ON/OFF, with a third "Disabled" state when `DEBRID_HEALTH_ENABLED=false`), and the count of filter-block remediation events from the last 24 h. Two action buttons: "Run sweep now" reuses the existing `POST /api/tasks/debrid_health_reconcile/run` endpoint (no new endpoint needed; the scheduler's `run_now` machinery was already wired up), and "View activity →" links to `/activity?type=debrid` which surfaces every `debrid_filtered` event. Card auto-refreshes every 30 s alongside the existing tasks-table polling. **Card is hidden when RD is not configured** — the reconciler task isn't even registered in that case (see `scheduled_tasks.py:1134`'s `rd_configured` gate), so there's nothing to surface. AllDebrid / TorBox-only users see no UI change. **One new helper** (`debrid_health.get_summary()`) and **one new endpoint** (`GET /api/debrid_health/summary`). The summary helper computes `last_sweep_ts` on the fly from the max per-entry `ts` to avoid bumping the state file schema, swallows any `history.query` failure (24 h count silently degrades to 0 instead of 500ing the response), and gates everything behind the same RD-configured check the scheduler uses so the endpoint is consistent with task registration. Blocked count renders in red bold via the new `.dh-bad` class as a soft alert when the auto-remediator hasn't caught up (e.g. between sweeps). **7 new tests** in `tests/test_debrid_health.py:TestGetSummary` cover: RD not configured → minimal `{rd_configured: false}` response; empty state → zero counts + `last_sweep_ts: None`; mixed state → correct status bucketing and max-ts; `DEBRID_HEALTH_AUTO_REMEDIATE` env reflected in the `auto_remediate` field; `DEBRID_HEALTH_ENABLED=false` still surfaces state (operators need to see the disabled-but-with-data case); `remediated_24h` correctly filters history events by `meta.cause == 'debrid_filtered'` (other-cause events with `type='debrid'` MUST NOT be counted); `history.query` exception path returns `remediated_24h: 0` without crashing the endpoint. Full suite: 1919 passes.

- **docker-compose: forward `DEBRID_HEALTH_ENABLED` and `DEBRID_HEALTH_AUTO_REMEDIATE`**: Without these in the compose `environment:` block, the values set in `.env` never reach the container — even though `base/__init__.py` reads them via `os.getenv`. Adding them here so users on the canonical compose can flip auto-remediate via `.env` + `docker compose up -d`. Defaults preserved (`true` / `false`) so behaviour is identical to the lookup-via-default path.

- **Debrid health: auto-remediate confirmed filter blocks (plan 38 phase 4)** — when `DEBRID_HEALTH_AUTO_REMEDIATE=true`, the reconciler closes the loop on detected filter blocks instead of just flagging them. For each torrent the probe classifies as `blocked`, the sweep runs a three-step pipeline: (1) `blocklist.add(hash, filename, reason='RD filter (infringing_file)', source='auto')` — idempotent; even if RD's delete fails the same release can't be re-grabbed by Sonarr/Radarr because the blackhole's pre-submit blocklist check at `blackhole.py:_process_file` rejects it; (2) `client.delete_torrent(torrent_id)` — removes the entry from RD's `/torrents`, which lets Zurg drop it from the WebDAV listing on next sync and lets pd_zurg's existing `_cleanup_broken_debrid_symlinks` reap the dangling symlinks; (3) `_attempt_arr_research(release_name)` from `scheduled_tasks.py` — triggers Sonarr/Radarr to search for a replacement, sharing the 2 h `_retrigger_history` cooldown with the broken-symlink and stale-grab paths so a single release isn't re-searched twice within the window even if multiple subsystems fire on it. Order is deliberate (blocklist before delete prevents a race where the arr re-grabs the same hash before the blocklist entry exists). Each step is independently try/except'd so a failure in one doesn't abort the others — the history event records what actually succeeded via per-step boolean flags (`blocklisted`, `deleted`, `researched`), so a partial failure (e.g. delete worked but arr re-search couldn't find the release in arr history) is auditable in the Activity feed under the new `debrid_filtered` cause vocabulary. **Safety design**: AUTO_REMEDIATE defaults OFF (mirrors `SYMLINK_REPAIR_AUTO_SEARCH`'s opt-in stance from plan 27); hard-capped at `_REMEDIATE_MAX_PER_SWEEP=100` per sweep (separate from the `_MAX_PER_SWEEP=2000` probe cap) so a first-time enable on a huge backlog can't mass-delete in one shot — remaining blocked entries stay flagged and remediate on subsequent sweeps since blocked entries always re-probe regardless of the healthy-only TTL skip. **Observability**: per-item events flow to `history.py` with the new `CAUSE_DEBRID_FILTERED = 'debrid_filtered'` slug — formatter in `activity_format._CAUSE_FORMATTERS` plus JS mirror in `FORMATTER_JS` (CLAUDE.md three-places rule satisfied) renders strings like `Filter-blocked on debrid (infringing_file, HTTP 451) — removed from debrid, hash blocklisted, arr re-search triggered`; a single per-sweep summary `notify('debrid_filtered', ...)` fires only when at least one remediation succeeded (avoids N apprise messages on a large cleanup); sweep log line adds `remediated N` whenever AUTO_REMEDIATE is on. New env var **`DEBRID_HEALTH_AUTO_REMEDIATE`** (default `false`) is the user-facing gate; settings UI section in `Debrid Health` adds the second field; CONFIGURATION.md documents the recommended rollout (run detection-only for one sweep, review `/config/debrid_health.json`, then flip ON). 12 new tests cover the full pipeline: AUTO_REMEDIATE off → zero destructive calls (the most important invariant), AUTO_REMEDIATE on with single blocked → blocklist + delete + research + history + summary notify all fire with correct args, `_REMEDIATE_MAX_PER_SWEEP` cap honored (10 blocked, cap=3 → exactly 3 deletes, all 10 persist as blocked for next sweep, 3 carry `remediated:true`), blocklist failure doesn't stop delete/research, delete returning False doesn't stop research/history, arr re-search returning False doesn't crash, no summary notify on zero-remediation sweep, healthy/unknown statuses NEVER trigger remediation (defends against the transient `http_503` case from live validation). Plus 4 new golden tests in `tests/test_activity_format.py` for the `debrid_filtered` formatter (detection-only, all-actions, partial-action, no-HTTP defensive). The existing `test_every_cause_constant_has_a_formatter` invariant test catches any cause slug that lacks a formatter — confirms the wiring across all three places. Full suite: 1903 tests pass.

- **Debrid health probe: catch HTTP 451 (live-observed) alongside 403**: Live validation against a real RD account on 2026-05-24 revealed the May 2026 keyword filter actually returns **HTTP 451 "Unavailable For Legal Reasons"** (RFC 7725), not the HTTP 403 that ElfHosted's writeup and Decypharr's repair worker assume. Body shape is unchanged (`{"error":"infringing_file","error_code":35}`) — only the status code differs. Before this fix, every actually-filter-blocked torrent on the user's library bucketed as `unknown` in the reconciler state (32 of 145 torrents on the validation account) because the 451 status went straight to the generic `http_NNN` fallback branch in `RealDebridClient.probe_file`. The fix broadens the body-inspection branch from 403-only to 403/451 (same body classifier shared by both status codes) — confirmed against the live account: 32 previously-`unknown` entries reclassified to `blocked/infringing_file` on the next sweep, all 32 filenames carrying known filter trigger keywords (`AMZN`, `WEB-DL`, `WEBRip`, `DSNP`, `NF`, `YTS.MX`, `[rartv]`, `[EZTVx.to]`, scene groups `successfulcrab`/`ETHEL`/`NTb`/`FLUX`). The `unknown` bucket continues to absorb genuinely retry-eligible failures — live sweep also surfaced 11 transient `http_503`s, all of which returned 200 on direct re-probe a minute later (RD's per-request throttling above and beyond the documented 250/min quota). The 503-bucketed entries re-probe on every sweep until they resolve, so transient throttling self-heals without operator action. Unrecognised 403/451 body shapes still log WARN with the body's top-level keys so future format drift remains visible. Three test updates: `test_blocked_infringing_by_error_code_on_403` (kept for backward compat in case RD ever reverts to the documented shape), new `test_blocked_infringing_by_error_code_on_451` (the live-observed path), and `test_blocked_infringing_by_error_key_alone` migrated to exercise the 451 path. `test_403_with_malformed_body_does_not_crash` and `test_403_with_unrecognised_body_is_unknown_not_blocked` renamed to their 451 counterparts since the shared branch makes one set of tests sufficient. Full suite: 1891 tests pass. **Note for the wider debrid-tools ecosystem**: as far as we can tell, this makes pd_zurg the only tool that correctly classifies the actual May 2026 RD filter response — Decypharr's repair worker (which only catches HTTP 404) and any tool following ElfHosted's writeup verbatim will continue to mis-classify these 451s as healthy/unknown.

- **Debrid health reconciler — scheduled sweep that detects RD filter blocks on the existing torrent set (`utils/debrid_health.py`)**: Phase 2 of plan 38 wires the probe primitive from the previous changelog entry into a periodic background sweep. Walks the user's Real-Debrid torrent list every 12 hours (configurable via `DEBRID_HEALTH_INTERVAL`, power-user-only — not surfaced in the Settings UI), probes one sample file per torrent via `RealDebridClient.probe_file`, and persists per-torrent classifications to `/config/debrid_health.json`. Detection only — auto-remediation (delete from RD + arr re-search) lands in phase 4. Architectural rationale already covered under the phase 1 entry: Zurg's WebDAV listing keeps advertising filter-blocked torrents as healthy, so without active probing the library page renders phantom 5 TB of unplayable content. **Performance posture (intentionally conservative defaults)**: probe cap of 2000 torrents per sweep (cap exists so first-time enable on a huge library doesn't run for hours; remainder picked up on the next interval), rate limit of 60 probes/minute (under RD's 250/min user quota, leaving 75% headroom for blackhole and library traffic), 7-day TTL skip for torrents already classified healthy (a 5000-torrent library re-probes only blocked + unknown each sweep, ≈minutes not hours). TTL / rate-limit / cap / persist-frequency live as `_*` module constants — env-var promotion is a one-line change if a real tuning need surfaces. **Strict-validation state loader** mirroring `library.LibraryScanner._load_webdav_capability` hardening posture: size cap via `os.fstat` on the open fd (closes the TOCTOU window between sizing and read), schema-version check, future-dated `ts` rejection (24 h tolerance for clock skew, matching the library capability cache), per-entry isinstance + status-whitelist filtering, `(OSError, ValueError)` absorbs both filesystem and JSON corruption without crashing the sweep. **Per-sweep behaviour**: blocked entries re-probe every sweep so we notice if RD reverses the filter; unknown entries re-probe ASAP (no verdict yet); incremental persist every 25 probes so an interrupted sweep doesn't lose all work; no sleep after the final probe (wasted wall-clock time on the scheduled-task "currently running" UI indicator). Public `get_blocked_hashes()` accessor returns the uppercased-hash set for library enrichment in phase 3 — does not lock during iteration, callers normalise their hash casing. New env var **`DEBRID_HEALTH_ENABLED`** (default ON) is a master kill switch — task is still *registered* whenever an RD credential is configured so it surfaces in the Scheduler UI even when disabled (preserves manual-trigger from the System page after a runtime toggle). 23 new tests in `tests/test_debrid_health.py` cover all status classifications + persistence (round-trip blocked-set, missing/corrupt/oversized/schema-mismatch/future-dated state files, TTL skip for healthy + always-re-probe for blocked/unknown, blocked→healthy state transition drops the badge, `MAX_PER_SWEEP` cap, rate-limit `time.sleep` accounting including no-sleep on single-probe runs, disabled env var no-op without touching network, missing/unconfigured RD client no-op, `list_torrents` failure preserves state file integrity, empty-list sweep still writes a valid state file, defensive skip of torrents with missing id/hash or non-dict shapes, lowercase-hash normalisation on persist). Full suite: 1890 tests pass.

- **Debrid health probe primitive (`RealDebridClient.probe_file`)** — first phase of the upcoming debrid health reconciler that closes pd_zurg's blind spot to RD's May 2026 keyword filter-gate (`infringing_file` / error 35 returned for cached torrents matching `WEB-DL`, `AMZN`, `[RARBG]`, etc., to comply with EU DSA / French law). The architectural gap: Zurg builds its WebDAV listing from RD's `/torrents` metadata, which still lists blocked torrents as healthy — the filter only fires on `/unrestrict/link`. So `os.path.exists()` on the FUSE mount succeeds for blocked files, the library scanner sees them as present, `_cleanup_broken_debrid_symlinks` never trips, and users see phantom content (in one reported case, 5 TB of library that wouldn't play). The existing `_REJECTION_CODES = {35, 30}` guard in `blackhole.py` only catches blocks at grab time on *new* torrents, never on the existing library. This phase adds the detection primitive only — no scheduling, no remediation, no UI behavior change. `probe_file(torrent_id, sample_file_link=None)` POSTs to `/unrestrict/link` with one sample link per torrent (when `sample_file_link` is omitted, `/torrents/info/{id}` is fetched and the smallest selected media file is picked to minimise probe traffic — one signal per torrent is sufficient since releases either pass or fail the filter as a whole). Returns a small classification dict: `{'status': 'healthy'}` on 200; `{'status': 'blocked', 'reason': 'infringing_file', 'http': 403}` when the body contains either `error_code: 35` OR `error: 'infringing_file'` (catching minor body-format drift Decypharr's repair worker misses by only checking 404); `{'status': 'blocked', 'reason': 'not_found', 'http': 404}` for outright removal; `{'status': 'unknown', 'error': <reason>}` for everything else (network errors, 5xx, malformed bodies, unrecognised 403 shapes, missing media files, invalid torrent IDs). Unrecognised 403 bodies log a WARN with the body's top-level keys so future RD filter-format drift surfaces in the journal rather than being silently mis-classified as healthy. The `/torrents/info` path defensively requires `len(selected_files) == len(links)` and bails to `unknown` on any mismatch — pairing a probe to the wrong file would mis-flag the wrong torrent under auto-remediate. API key never leaks into logs (existing `_sanitize_error` path). 15 new tests in `tests/test_debrid_client.py:TestProbeFile` cover all status classifications, malformed-body handling without crash, unrecognised-403 WARN visibility, `/torrents/info` smallest-media-file selection (skipping `.nfo`, unselected files), files/links length-mismatch bail, info-request failure handling, invalid torrent ID rejection before any HTTP call, and a regression test asserting the API key never appears in log records even when an exception message embeds it. No callers wire up the probe in this phase — it's strictly the primitive. Full suite: 1867 tests pass.

- **Status page: Library Composition card**: Adds a full-width card on the Status dashboard summarizing the on-disk library: total items / movies / shows / episodes / size, plus per-source breakdowns (local, debrid, both) for movies and shows with a stacked color bar and per-bucket counts + sizes. Episode counts and sizes are bucketed by episode-level `source` so a `source='both'` show that's still mostly on debrid is rendered correctly. Powered by a new `compute_library_stats(data)` helper in `utils/library.py` and a non-blocking `LibraryScanner.get_cached_stats()` accessor that reads only the in-memory cache — never triggers a scan on the /api/status poll path. Stats piggyback on the existing /api/status response so the dashboard's auto-refresh already drives it; the card stays hidden until the first scan completes (zero-item library shows nothing). **Concurrency hardening surfaced in review and fixed in the same change:** `_scan_effects._cleanup_disc_rips` does an in-place `movies[:] = [...]` slice-assignment AFTER the scan cache is published to readers (refresh() publishes before running effects), which would race with the new poll-path reader and produce double-counted or skipped items mid-cleanup. `get_cached_stats()` now shallow-copies the top-level `movies`/`shows` lists under `_lock` before releasing it, isolating the iteration in `compute_library_stats` from any subsequent in-place mutation. The outer `to_dict()` exception path also now logs at DEBUG so a future regression in the stats helper surfaces in the journal instead of silently dropping the card. The dashboard `buildBar` segments now `esc()` the count interpolated into `title` attributes for defense-in-depth even though the values are server-trusted ints. 10 new unit tests in `tests/test_library.py:TestComputeLibraryStats` cover empty data, source bucketing, episode-level source distribution, episode source falling back to show source, unknown-source fallback, invalid `size_bytes` handling, scan-metadata passthrough, `get_cached_stats()` returning `None` on empty cache, populated-cache happy path, and the concurrent-mutation isolation guarantee. Full suite: 1777 tests pass.

- **Settings → Backup & Restore: per-row Delete buttons for archives and pre-restore snapshots**: The Recent backups dropdown now shows two sections — **Archives (N)** with the existing scheduled `.tar.gz` files plus a new red Delete button on each row, and **Pre-restore snapshots (N)** which surfaces the `pre-restore-<timestamp>/` (and legacy `<timestamp>/`) directories that `restore_from_blob` writes before overwriting config. The snapshot dirs were never auto-pruned by `prune_old_backups` (it intentionally only touches files matching `BACKUP_FILENAME_RE`), so they accumulated forever — fine because they're tiny, but invisible from the UI and impossible to reclaim without shelling into the container. Each Delete confirms via the existing `inlineConfirm` modal and hits `DELETE /api/settings/backup/<filename>` (archives) or `DELETE /api/settings/snapshot/<dirname>` (snapshots). Both backend handlers are guarded by the same auth check as the rest of the Settings API, hold `_restore_lock` so a delete can't race a concurrent restore consuming the same artifact, and validate input through `resolve_backup_path` / new `resolve_snapshot_path` — strict regex match (`SNAPSHOT_DIRNAME_RE` covers both naming formats), resolved-path containment against the backup dir, parent-equality check, and explicit symlink rejection so a malicious symlink dropped into `/config/backups/` can't be used to `rmtree` an arbitrary directory. 14 new tests in `tests/test_backup.py` cover the regex (5 valid forms / 8 invalid), `list_snapshots` sorting and skip-rules (archives, random dirs, symlinked top-level), `delete_backup` happy path + 4 bad-name parametrize + missing-file 404, `delete_snapshot` happy path + legacy dirname + 4 bad-name parametrize + symlink rejection (verifies the link target survives) + missing-dir 404. Full backup suite: 62 tests pass.

- **Activity detail overhaul — events now explain *why* they fired, and repeat retries collapse in the UI**: The Activity feed and per-title detail sidebar used to render raw command names (`RescanMovie`, `MoviesSearch`) and opaque one-liners like "Debrid symlink(s) created in local library" that told you nothing about *why* an event fired or *what* file changed. Every `log_event` call site in the codebase (`utils/arr_client.py`, `utils/library.py`, `utils/blackhole.py`, `utils/scheduled_tasks.py`, `utils/search.py`, `utils/status_server.py`) now attaches a stable `meta['cause']` slug from a new vocabulary in `utils/history.py` (`library_new_import`, `library_upgrade_replaced`, `routing_audit_retry`, `stale_grab_retry`, `post_symlink_rescan`, `debrid_unavailable_marked`, `terminal_error`, etc.), plus structured fields — `file`, `quality`, `size_bytes`, `replaces`, `cycle_n`, `cycle_first_ts`, `arr_service`, `prior_event_id`, `command_id`. A new shared helper `utils/activity_format.py:format_event()` (mirrored in JS on the dashboard) converts these into human strings like *"Upgraded: Movie.720p.mkv → Movie.1080p.mkv (1080p, 4.3 GB)"*, *"Radarr rescan — new symlink available for import"*, *"Radarr search — scheduled routing audit retry — retry #14, first attempt 5d 3h ago"*, and *"Marked unavailable after 3d — retries continue in arr"*. The library scanner now detects **new-import vs upgrade** via a per-title basename set persisted to `/config/library_scan_state.json`; the first scan after a container restart is flagged `library_state_init` so pre-existing symlinks aren't mis-labeled as upgrades. A new in-memory `utils/retry_counter.py` keys by `(service, media_id)` and supplies the cycle count + first-attempt timestamp for scheduled search retries — resets when a grab/symlink lands. Symlink_created events now return an event id that the follow-up rescan_triggered event chains via `meta['prior_event_id']`, so related events on the detail page are visibly linked. The **Activity tab and detail sidebar collapse consecutive identical events** (`type+source+cause+media`) into a single summary row — *"14× over 3d — Radarr search — scheduled routing audit retry"* — whenever the run is 3+ events, replacing the 19-row spam the old 6-hour retry loop produced. The Activity page has a "Collapse repeats" toggle (default on). **Backward compatible**: legacy events in `history.jsonl` without a `cause` still render using their original `detail` string, so the 30-day retention window transitions cleanly. 51 new tests in `tests/test_activity_format.py`, `tests/test_retry_counter.py`, and `tests/test_history.py` cover golden-file formatting per cause, cycle counter semantics, and legacy-event compat. Full suite: 1690 tests pass.

- **Config backup & restore — one-click from the Settings UI**: New Settings page "Backup & Restore" bar with three actions: **Download backup** (creates a fresh tar.gz of `.env`, `settings.json`, `library_prefs.json`, `blocklist.json` and streams it to the browser), **Restore from file** (upload a previously-downloaded archive), and **Recent backups** (lists the scheduled archives under `/config/backups/` with per-entry Download and Restore buttons). Restore snapshots the current live files to `/config/backups/pre-restore-<timestamp>/` before overwriting, then reloads the affected subsystems: SIGHUP for `.env`, `blocklist.init()` for the hash cache, and a `plex_debrid` service restart for `settings.json`. Scheduled backups are rewritten to use the same tar.gz format and live under the same `/config/backups/` directory, unified with manual download. New env var `CONFIG_BACKUP_RETENTION` (default 7) controls how many scheduled archives to keep; `CONFIG_BACKUP_INTERVAL=0` disables the scheduled task entirely without touching the manual flow. Security-relevant: archive endpoints are auth-gated by the existing `STATUS_UI_AUTH`; restore uploads are capped at 10 MiB with a strict archive-member whitelist (`env`, `settings.json`, `library_prefs.json`, `blocklist.json`, `manifest.json`) that rejects symlinks, device files, path traversal, and unknown members before any mutation; saved-backup download and restore paths validate filenames against `BACKUP_FILENAME_RE` and a resolved-path containment check against the backup dir. Also **fixes two latent bugs in the old directory-based scheduled backup**: (1) the task backed up `preferences.json` but the preferences store lives at `/config/library_prefs.json`, so prefs have been silently excluded from every scheduled backup since the feature shipped; the new format uses the correct path. (2) The old task's retention prune was hardcoded to 7 and keyed on directory mtime — the new module's prune keys on archive filename + mtime and honors `CONFIG_BACKUP_RETENTION`. 27 new tests in `tests/test_backup.py` cover round-trip, snapshot/rollback, filename validation (including 6 parametrized path-traversal and bad-shape cases), per-format content validation, symlink/oversize/unknown-member rejection, same-second collision handling, and `CONFIG_BACKUP_RETENTION` clamping. All 1641 tests pass.

### Fixed

- **Manual "Add to debrid" from the search modal now appears in the show/movie detail-page Activity panel**: When the user clicked Add on a Torrentio result inside a show or movie detail page, `search.add_to_debrid()` logged the resulting `debrid_add` event with `title=<release-name>` (e.g. `"The Wayfinders S01E01 1080p WEB x265-LOL"`) and no `media_title`. The detail-page Activity sidebar fetches `/api/history/show/<title>`, which calls `query_by_show()` — that does an *exact* case-insensitive match on `title` OR `media_title` against the canonical library name. The release-name title never matches, and `media_title` was empty, so the manual-add events were invisible from the detail page (only surfacing in the main Activity feed where filtering is by substring). Three-step fix: (1) `library_page.py` adds `data-media-title` to all three Search buttons (movie-level, show-level, episode-level) holding the canonical `_detailItem.title` / `movie.title` / `show.title`; (2) `openSearchFromBtn` → `openSearchModal` thread it into module state alongside a synthesized `SxxEyy` tag derived from the season/episode ints; (3) `addSearchResult` attaches `media_title` and `episode` to the `/api/search/add` POST body, the endpoint pipes them through to `add_to_debrid(media_title=, episode=)`, and `log_event` writes them on both the success and failure events. No backfill of existing untagged events — they roll out via the 30-day history retention. Symmetric for movies and shows (same code path, no extra work needed). **Hardening surfaced in review and fixed in the same change:** (a) `/api/search/add` now type-checks `info_hash`, `title`, `media_title`, `episode` and returns a clean 400 if any is non-string instead of crashing into the bare-Exception 500 path via `.strip()` AttributeError — pre-existing for `title`, widened by this change so addressed in scope. (b) `episode` is server-side validated against `^S\d{1,4}E\d{1,4}$` so a curl client can't pollute history with garbage tags. (c) The JS guard `if (season && episode)` was changed to `if (season != null && episode != null)` so Season 0 / Episode 0 specials are tagged correctly instead of silently dropping the episode tag. (d) Zero-pad helper handles ≥100 episodes consistently. (e) `closeSearchModal` now clears `_searchMediaTitle` / `_searchEpisodeTag` so a future code path that reuses `addSearchResult` outside the modal can't mis-tag an unrelated add with stale context.

- **Library page now signals when the rendered list is stale or being refreshed in the background**: with the persisted-cache fix above, the Library page can render the last-known-good list instantly on cold start — but until now there was no visual cue distinguishing that cached view from a fresh scan, so a user looking at a 12-minute-old listing during a slow scheduler-triggered refresh had no way to tell the system was about to update the data. The toolbar already had a `<span id="scan-info">` element with a relative-time renderer (`relativeTime`) and an animated dot CSS class (`.scanning-dot`) for the manual Refresh button's "Refreshing…" state, plus a 30-second `setInterval` (`startTsRefresh`) to keep the relative-time string current — but `_scanning` was only flipped to `true` by the user-clicked Refresh path. Two-line fix: `_applyLibraryData` now syncs `_scanning = !!data.scanning` from the server (the `/api/library` handler at `utils/status_server.py:1247` already exposes `scanner.is_scanning()`) so background scans drive the same "Refreshing…" indicator the manual button does, and a new `_ensureBackgroundScanPoll()` helper picks up that state and polls `/api/library` every 3 seconds (cap 60 attempts ≈ 3 minutes) until the scan completes, then re-applies the fresh data so the grid and timestamp refresh in place — no manual reload required. The sync is gated by `if (!_refreshPollTimer)` so the manual-refresh polling path (`_pollRefresh`, which already manages `_refreshPollTimer` and `_scanning`) is never disturbed: the two paths share the same timer slot and serialize cleanly. The auto-poll's success branch clears `_refreshPollTimer` *before* calling `_applyLibraryData(data)` so the re-entrant call (which itself calls `_ensureBackgroundScanPoll`) doesn't see a stale timer reference and start a duplicate poll. **Hardening surfaced during review and fixed in the same commit:** (a) the prior implementation kept `attempts`/`maxAttempts` as locals inside `_ensureBackgroundScanPoll`, so a smart-poll tick re-entering the function during a long scan would reset the counter from zero — the cap is now a module-level `_bgScanDeadline` epoch timestamp evaluated against `Date.now()`, so the 3-minute ceiling is cumulative rather than per-invocation. (b) The auto-poll now disables the manual `#btn-refresh` button while it's running and re-enables on every exit path — without this the user could click the button mid-poll, hit `triggerRefresh`'s `if (_scanning) return` guard, and watch nothing happen. (c) A single `_bgFinish` exit handler is reached on success, cap-hit, AND repeated network failure: it resets `_scanning`, calls `updateScanInfo()` to clear the dot, and re-enables the Refresh button — without this, a flapping `/api/library` endpoint or a server-side scan that runs longer than 3 minutes would freeze the indicator on "Refreshing…" indefinitely until the user manually intervened. (d) `_applyLibraryData`'s scanning-sync now also calls `_setRefreshButtonDisabled(_scanning)` and repaints `updateScanInfo()` even on the quiet-path so a detail-view smart-poll tick that observes scan completion clears the dot immediately rather than waiting for the next 30s timestamp tick. **No backend changes** — `last_scan`, `scan_duration_ms`, and `scanning` were already in the `/api/library` response.

- **Library page renders the last-known-good list immediately after a container restart instead of waiting on a 51s cold scan**: `LibraryScanner._cache` was in-memory only, so every container restart reverted it to `None` and the Library page (and every consumer of `/api/library`) got an empty response until the first scan completed. On the user's instance that scan takes ~51 seconds because Zurg lacks recursive `PROPFIND` (verified directly: `Depth: infinity` against `/dav/movies/` returns 82 KB of folders with zero file hrefs), so the scanner correctly falls back to the FUSE walk — which is the only safe path on that Zurg build but unavoidably slow. Every other piece of derived state under `/config/` already survives a restart (`library_capabilities.json`, `library_scan_state.json`, `library_pending.json`, `library_prefs.json`, `tmdb_cache.json`, `blocklist.json`); the visible library list did not. `LibraryScanner` now atomic-writes a schema-1 envelope to `/config/library_cache.json` after every successful scan and reloads it on `__init__`, so the first HTTP request after a restart serves the persisted view and the next scheduled `library_scan` task refreshes in the background. Persisting only `_cache` would have left `_path_index`, `_local_path_index`, and `_alias_norms` empty for the same 51-second window, and those indexes are consumed by the `get_debrid_path` / `get_local_path` lookups that downstream blackhole and preference-enforcement code calls — empty indexes during the warm-start window would have scheduled wasteful duplicate grabs. All four are persisted as part of the same envelope (tuple-keyed indexes serialize as `[norm, sn, en, path]` rows, alias-norm sets serialize as sorted lists, both reconstruct on load). **Atomicity:** index snapshots are captured at the call site immediately after `_scan_read` returns and passed to `_persist_cache` as arguments, so the on-disk envelope pairs the same scan's cache and indexes — the prior implementation reading `self._path_index` inside `_persist_cache` could pair an older `cache` with newer indexes if a concurrent scan finished between the cache assignment and the index snapshot. **Inner cache strict-typing:** `last_scan` (str), `scan_duration_ms` (strict int — `True` rejected), and `preferences` (dict, when present) are validated on load, so a tampered file can't silently feed wrong types to the UI render path or scan-effects loop. **Hardening posture inherited from plan 36 phase 2 capability cache** so the same lessons aren't relearned: size cap (16 MB) is checked via `os.fstat` on the open fd — not `os.path.getsize` — to close the prior TOCTOU window between sizing and reading; schema is compared with `type(...) is int` so `True` (which equals `1` in Python) can't impersonate the version field; `ts` is rejected if more than a day in the future (clock-skew / tampering safeguard); `path_index` rows reject `bool` masquerading as `int` for season/episode numbers (`isinstance(True, int)` is `True` in Python, so the loader uses an explicit `not isinstance(x, bool)` strict-int check); persist failures (`OSError | ValueError | TypeError`) are swallowed with a warning so a read-only `/config`, quota exhaustion, or a future change adding a non-serializable field can't turn a successful scan into a failed one. **No TTL** on the persisted cache: library state is self-refreshing, the next scheduled scan replaces it; a stale persisted view is strictly better than a blank UI. **`zurgarr_version` mismatch invalidates** so an upgrade with incompatible cache fields auto-discards. **Schema-version** field gives a clean knob for future incompatible changes. **Tolerates partially-constructed instances** (unit-test scanners built via `__new__`) by no-op'ing when `_library_cache_path` is unset, so test helpers don't have to be updated en masse. Plan 37 in `.plans/37-library-cache-persistence.md` documents the design and its orthogonality to plan 36 (which is about scan strategy, not cache lifetime — the two ship without interaction). 16 new tests in `tests/test_library.py:TestLibraryCachePersistence` cover round-trip, tuple-key reconstruction, alias-set reconstruction, missing-file no-op, corrupt-JSON treated as missing, `os.fstat` size cap rejecting oversize, schema mismatch / version mismatch / future-dated `ts` rejection, persist-failure swallowed (read-only filesystem simulation), strict field-type validation (4 cases: bool-as-int in path_index, wrong row arity, non-list movies field, non-string alias values), and end-to-end "fresh scanner instantiated against the persisted file serves cached data without scanning". Full suite: 1761 tests pass.

- **Library scan: stop re-attempting the doomed `Depth: infinity` PROPFIND every cache miss when Zurg lacks recursive support**: On Zurg builds that don't honor `Depth: infinity`, the scanner correctly detects the folders-but-no-files response and falls back to the FUSE walk — but it does so on *every* scan, paying the round-trip cost of a known-doomed PROPFIND every 10 minutes (cache TTL) and on every cold-cache `/api/library` hit. `LibraryScanner` now memoizes the result on first detection in `_webdav_unsupported`; subsequent scans short-circuit before issuing any HTTP and the "using FUSE" log is demoted from INFO to DEBUG so the message doesn't spam the journal each scan. The detection itself still fires once at INFO so the capability finding is visible. A new typed exception `_WebDAVUnsupportedError` discriminates the memoized capability case from transient WebDAV failures (DNS, connection refused, timeouts) — those still log at INFO and never flip the memoization flag, so a flaky network can't permanently wedge the scanner into FUSE-only mode. **Phase 2** persists the memoization to `/config/library_capabilities.json` (atomic write via `utils/file_utils.py`) so a container restart doesn't re-pay the detection cost on every cold boot; on `__init__` the scanner loads the file and pre-sets the flag. The cache is re-validated after 7 days (so a future Zurg upgrade that adds recursive PROPFIND support gets re-probed automatically), invalidated on `ZURG_VERSION` change (so pinning to a new Zurg version re-runs detection), and rejected on size cap (>4 KB), corruption, missing fields, malformed timestamps, or a future-dated `ts` (clock skew / tampering safeguard, anything more than a day ahead is dropped). A persist failure (read-only `/config`, quota exhaustion) logs a warning and the in-memory memoization still works for the rest of the process lifetime — never raises out of the detection path. **Hardening uncovered during review and fixed in the same commit:** (a) The detection check used to fire per-category — the *first* category whose folders happened to have no files would persist `webdav_unsupported=True` for 7 days, even if a later category would have proven Zurg's recursion works. The verdict is now deferred until all categories complete: only memoize unsupported when EVERY category that returned folders returned zero files, AND the scan ran to completion (deadline-induced partial scans cannot poison the cache). When some categories work and others have folders-but-no-files, the empty ones are skipped at INFO with a "not memoizing as unsupported" message. (b) The `webdav_unsupported` field is now strict-bool — a hand-edited record containing `"yes"`, `1`, `[True]`, or any non-`True` truthy value is rejected, closing the gap where `not raw.get(...)` would have accepted truthy strings. (c) The size-cap check is now done via `os.fstat` on the open file descriptor, eliminating the prior `getsize` TOCTOU + `except OSError: pass` fallthrough that could allow an unbounded read on a transient-`getsize` race. (d) Future-dated and TTL-expired records get distinct log messages so clock skew / tampering surfaces separately from genuine cache expiry. (e) `_persist_webdav_capability` now also catches `ValueError`/`TypeError` defensively — a future change that adds a non-serializable field can't propagate out of the detection path and turn a successful FUSE fallback into a whole-scan failure. This is Phases 1+2 of `.plans/36-zurg-recursive-propfind-fallback.md`; the prior attempt at this fix (commit `9e73157`) was reverted because it introduced a partial-walk path that mis-classified shows as movies. Phases 1+2 deliberately change nothing about the scan or fallback paths — only the *re-attempt cadence* of the failing PROPFIND. 19 new tests in `tests/test_library.py:TestWebDAVUnsupportedMemoization` (4), `TestWebDAVCapabilityPersistence` (12), and `TestWebDAVCrossCategoryDetection` (3) cover flag-flip on detection, zero-HTTP short-circuit on memoized scans, INFO→DEBUG log demotion across scans, transient-failure isolation, persist round-trip across `LibraryScanner()` recreation, corrupt/oversize/stale/future-dated/version-mismatched cache invalidation, missing-file no-op, persist-failure swallowed, end-to-end "detection writes file" verification, strict-bool rejection of non-`True` truthy values, "one working category prevents unsupported lockout", "all empty categories trigger unsupported", and "partial scan does not memoize". Full suite: 1715 tests pass.

- **Library page rendered blank due to self-referencing placeholder in comment**: A comment at `utils/library_page.py:2794` referenced the template placeholder `__THEME_TOGGLE_JS__` by name inside the `_LIBRARY_HTML` string. `get_library_html()` blindly `.replace()`s that placeholder with the multi-line JS blob (`THEME_TOGGLE_JS + KEYBOARD_JS + TOAST_JS + FORMATTER_JS`), so the comment's placeholder was substituted too — the injected JS ends with `})();` plus a trailing newline, which stranded the comment's closing `).` on its own line as bare JS. The browser aborted the whole `<script>` at `Uncaught SyntaxError: Unexpected token ')'` and the Library page rendered blank. Rephrased the comment to not contain the placeholder token.

- **Settings: every blocking browser `confirm()` replaced with an in-page modal**: The Settings page was firing the native `confirm()` / `prompt()` dialogs for seven different actions — tab-switch-with-unsaved-changes, Delete profile in the Versions editor, Save & Restart plex_debrid, Reset Zurgarr form to defaults, Reset plex_debrid form to defaults, Restore config from upload, and Restore config from saved backup. The native dialogs (1) can't render rich content so prompts had to cram context into a single plain string, (2) block the entire JS event loop so any async refresh happening in the background pauses, (3) have zero visual connection to the app (different font, theme-ignoring), and (4) on mobile render at an entirely different scale than the page. Added `inlineConfirm({title, message, confirmText, cancelText, danger})` in `utils/settings_page.py` — a Promise-returning modal overlay that styles to the app's theme, supports HTML `message` content, takes Enter/Escape keyboard shortcuts, restores focus to the previously-focused element on close, and exposes a `btn-danger filled` confirm button for destructive actions. Three correctness guards surfaced in review and addressed in the same commit: **(a)** if a second `inlineConfirm` is called while a first is still open (rapid double-click), the earlier call's Promise is resolved with `false` before its DOM is removed — the old version just `.remove()`d the overlay and the earlier Promise orphaned, leaking its `{capture:true}` keydown listener forever and swallowing Enter/Escape keys site-wide until a page reload. **(b)** Enter-to-confirm is gated on `document.activeElement === confirmBtn` so a user who Tab-cycled to `document.body` or clicked in the dialog body can't accidentally confirm a destructive action; the old check was "Enter confirms unless focused on Cancel", which fired for any non-cancel focus target. **(c)** `cleanup` is idempotent via a `settled` flag so a rapid click+Enter combo can't double-resolve and leave stale state. `switchTab` additionally got a `_switchInFlight` reentrancy guard so two rapid tab clicks can't race on the final class-toggle step. All seven call sites were converted to `await inlineConfirm(...)`; `switchTab`, `deleteProfile`, `configRestoreUpload`, and `configRestoreFromSaved` were promoted to `async` functions, and `configRestoreUpload` now clears `input.value` before awaiting so a cancelled restore doesn't leave the file selector stuck on the rejected filename.

- **Settings: SHOW_MENU and "Show Menu on Startup" help text now cross-reference each other**: The two toggles live on different tabs but are bidirectionally synced by `_sync_plex_debrid_to_env` / `_sync_env_to_plex_debrid`. A user toggling one, saving, then noticing the other hadn't updated would conclude the form was broken. Both help-text strings (`utils/settings_api.py:71` for `SHOW_MENU`, `:1057` for `Show Menu on Startup`) now explicitly say "Mirrors the other toggle — changes to either propagate through the sync layer" so the relationship is visible up front instead of discovered by confusion.

- **Hardening: `/api/settings/oauth/start` and `/api/settings/oauth/poll` cap request body at 100 KB**: The two OAuth JSON POST handlers at `utils/status_server.py:2310` and `:2326` previously did `body = self.rfile.read(content_length)` with no upper bound — an attacker (or a malformed client) supplying `Content-Length: 1000000000` would have the server allocate a gigabyte-sized buffer while the Python HTTP server thread blocks. Matched the 100_000-byte cap the other settings POST handlers already use and responded with HTTP 413. The legitimate request bodies (`{"service": "trakt"}`, `{"service": "trakt", "device_code": "..."}`) are both far under this limit.

- **Hardening: `/api/settings/restore` now rejects unsupported Content-Type with 415**: The restore endpoint accepted any body up to `MAX_ARCHIVE_BYTES` regardless of header, relying entirely on the tar/gzip parser to reject non-archive content at decode time. That's still the real safety boundary (the parser also enforces the archive-member whitelist from `backup.py`), but it surfaced garbage as a confusing "Archive is not a valid tar" error instead of an up-front type error. Added a Content-Type whitelist at `utils/status_server.py:2268`: `application/gzip`, `application/x-gzip`, `application/octet-stream`, or empty (curl without an explicit `--header` would send empty). Anything else — including `application/json`, `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, `text/plain` — gets a 415 with a message that lists the expected value.

- **Settings: search now also matches help text**: `filterSettings` in `utils/settings_page.py:1545` only tested the query against `.field-label`, so searching "timeout" missed every field whose label didn't contain the word even when its help text explained the timeout behavior. The match now joins `.field-label` and `.field-help` into a single haystack. Highlighting was already rendered against both elements — only the *predicate* was too narrow. Same pass also replaced the fragile `adv.querySelectorAll('.field[style*="display"]')` visible-count inside the advanced-expand branch with an iteration that reads `f.style.display !== 'none'` directly, since browser serialization of inline `display` tokens is inconsistent and stale `style="display: ;"` attributes could skew the count after a field was toggled on.

- **Settings: init shows a "Loading settings…" placeholder and fetches env + plex_debrid in parallel**: The old `init()` ran the two fetches sequentially and left both category panels blank until the second one resolved, which on a container under load could look frozen for a second. The new init paints `<div class="loading-state"><span class="spinner"></span>Loading settings…</div>` into both category containers before awaiting, fires the two fetches in parallel via `Promise.all`, then renders both tabs. Parallelizing shaved ~half the perceived latency in manual tests.

- **Settings: secret fields get a one-click Copy button**: The show/hide toggle lived next to every API-key-style field, but copying the value required the user to reveal it, select the text, Ctrl+C, and re-hide — tedious and error-prone for long OAuth tokens. Added a second button next to Show/Hide that calls `navigator.clipboard.writeText(input.value)`, flashes "Copied" (green for 1.2s) as confirmation, and falls back to the legacy `document.execCommand('copy')` path when the Clipboard API isn't available (older browsers, insecure contexts). The fallback briefly flips the input to `type=text` so selection works, then restores masking. Also refactored `toggleSecret` to track reveal-state via `input.dataset.secretRevealed` instead of parsing the button's textContent — the old approach broke if the label was localized or if a re-render changed the button's inner HTML.

- **Settings: OAuth completion now syncs the Show/Hide button label and fires a synthetic input event**: When the Trakt/Plex OAuth device flow completed, `poll.status === 'complete'` path wrote the token into the field and flipped `input.type` to `text` to show the user what was received, but the sibling "Show" button still read "Show" — the user had no way to know whether the field was actually revealed or if the Show button would re-reveal an already-visible token.
