Also, programmatic `input.value = token` assignment doesn't fire the `input`/`change` events the dirty-state tracker listens for, so the token didn't show up as an unsaved change until the user manually touched the field. Fixed both: the OAuth-complete branch now also flips the Show button text, sets `dataset.secretRevealed = 'true'`, and dispatches a bubbling `input` event so `markDirty` fires.

- **Settings: inline OAuth name entry for list_pair OAuth (Trakt users)**: `oauthConnectPair` at `utils/settings_page.py:1288` used the browser's blocking `prompt()` dialog to ask for a username before starting the device-code flow. `prompt()` is jarring on mobile, uncustomizable, and can't display the OAuth panel's context or styling. Replaced with an inline form inside the OAuth panel — a labeled text input plus "Start OAuth" and "Cancel" buttons, Enter-key submit, auto-focus on render. Once the user commits a name, `_oauthPairStart` picks it up and kicks off the same device-code fetch path the old code did.

- **Settings: `PD_LOG_LEVEL` now includes `CRITICAL` to match the other Python-runtime log-level selects**: The plex_debrid log-level dropdown in `utils/settings_api.py:77` offered `DEBUG,INFO,WARNING,ERROR` while `ZURGARR_LOG_LEVEL` (line 221) offered `DEBUG,INFO,WARNING,ERROR,CRITICAL`. Both services run under Python's `logging` module, which exposes all five levels — there's no runtime reason to omit CRITICAL from plex_debrid. Added it so the two selects match.

- **Settings: `RCLONE_LOG_LEVEL` validator now rejects `WARNING` and `CRITICAL` — rclone's level set is distinct from Python's**: `validate_env_values` at `utils/settings_api.py:539` previously accepted `('DEBUG','INFO','WARNING','ERROR','CRITICAL')` for every log-level var and added `NOTICE` on top for rclone. But rclone doesn't implement `WARNING` or `CRITICAL` — its documented levels are `DEBUG`, `INFO`, `NOTICE`, `ERROR` (see [rclone.org/docs/#logging](https://rclone.org/docs/#logging)). The schema dropdown for `RCLONE_LOG_LEVEL` was already `DEBUG,INFO,NOTICE,ERROR`, so a user couldn't pick WARNING through the UI, but the validator would happily pass a WARNING value imported via .env file. Restructured the validator to use a per-variable allowed set (`_RCLONE_LEVELS` vs `_PY_LEVELS`) and expanded the warning message to enumerate the expected values. Two new tests — `test_rclone_log_level_warning_rejected` locks in the rejection and `test_pd_log_level_critical_ok` verifies the new PD option passes.

- **Settings: URL fields tolerate leading/trailing whitespace**: `validate_env_values` at `utils/settings_api.py:527` passed the raw user input to `_is_valid_url`, which uses `urlparse` and rejects anything with stray whitespace — so `http://sonarr:8989 ` (trailing space from a sloppy copy-paste) surfaced as an error even though `_sanitize_value` would strip the space at write time anyway. Added a `.strip()` at validation so the two paths agree: the message the user sees on Validate/Save matches what's actually going to land on disk.

- **Settings: `resetField` clears the red "invalid" outline and inline error**: The per-field ↺ revert button at `utils/settings_page.py:1722` wrote the saved value back into the input and called `updateDirtyUI()`, but left any `.invalid` class and inline `.field-error` text the last failed Validate call had pinned to the field — the input now held a valid value but still painted red. Added explicit cleanup of both states in the env branch so reset visually matches its semantics.

- **Settings: env import recognizes `export KEY=value` and tells the user what it skipped**: `envImport` at `utils/settings_page.py:1352` parsed `.env` files literally — any line starting with `export ` (common in `.env` files pasted from shell sessions) failed the `key.startsWith === '#'` guard but then failed the equals-split because the key captured included the `export ` prefix and never matched a schema key on save. Now strips `/^export\s+/i` before the split. Also, any imported keys not in `_ENV_KEY_SET` were silently filtered on the save path but quietly merged into the form, misleading the "Imported N settings" banner. Imports now split into matched vs unknown, only known keys populate the form (so the rendered state doesn't imply unknown keys will work), and the info banner lists unknown keys (up to 5 sampled, with `…` for the tail) so the user knows exactly what was dropped.

- **Settings: env-save now re-fetches canonical values so server-side sanitization doesn't leave the form phantom-dirty**: After `envSave()` succeeded, `utils/settings_page.py:486` stamped `envValues = collectEnvData()` — i.e. whatever the DOM held at the moment of save. But `_sanitize_value` in `utils/settings_api.py:354` strips trailing whitespace and null bytes from every value before writing .env, and `_sync_plex_debrid_to_env` can rewrite env keys during a subsequent PD save too. Any divergence between the DOM copy and the on-disk canonical form surfaced later as phantom "unsaved changes" the next time dirty detection ran. The success branch now does `await fetch('/api/settings/env')`, assigns the response as `envValues`, and re-renders the env categories so both sides agree. Three guards keep this from introducing new bugs: **(1)** the re-render is skipped if `document.activeElement` is inside `#tab-env` on a non-button element — a user typing into a field during the async gap between the POST completing and the GET resolving must not have their keystrokes clobbered by a full `innerHTML` rebuild. **(2)** The response body is verified to be a plain object before assignment — a reverse-proxy misconfig returning HTML or an array would otherwise get written to `envValues` and desync dirty detection indefinitely. **(3)** Any failure (non-OK status, non-object body, thrown fetch) falls back to `envValues = collectEnvData()` so the envValues snapshot always matches what's in the DOM, even if we couldn't capture server-side normalization this round.

- **Settings: `highlightErrors` regex no longer silently drops validator messages that don't wedge `=` or `:` after the key**: `utils/settings_page.py:438` matched `/^["']?([A-Z_]+)[=:'"]/`, so a validator error like `STATUS_UI_AUTH format is invalid. Must be user:pass.` (emitted by `validate_env_values` at `utils/settings_api.py:650-664`) never pulled out the `STATUS_UI_AUTH` key — the field didn't get the red `.invalid` class or the inline `.field-error` under the input. Loosened the pattern to `/^["']?([A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*)\b/` so any message that leads with an ALL_CAPS key is matched regardless of the punctuation that follows. Also allows digits inside the key (matches the real schema — e.g. `RD_API_KEY`, `BLACKHOLE_MOUNT_POLL_TIMEOUT` still match, and a hypothetical `FOO_2FA_SECRET` would too). To block false-positive captures on messages that happen to lead with a non-key ALL_CAPS word (`HTTP 500 …`, `SSL handshake …`), the captured token is cross-checked against `_ENV_KEY_SET` — a module-level Set built from every schema key — before mutating the DOM.

- **Settings: out-of-range numerics no longer paint the input red before Save**: The schema declares numeric fields as `number:MIN-MAX`, but the validator at `utils/settings_api.py:576-584` emits these as *warnings*, not errors. `renderEnvField` at `utils/settings_page.py:398` nevertheless rendered `<input type="number" min="..." max="...">`, which triggered the browser's constraint-validation UI (red outline, `:invalid` pseudoclass, native tooltip) the moment the user typed a value outside the recommended range — making a warning look indistinguishable from a hard error and undermining the backend's deliberate warn-don't-block stance. Dropped the HTML `min`/`max` attributes; kept the range hint as a `placeholder`. The Validate button's warning still surfaces on save, which is the intended UX.

- **Settings: `validate_plex_debrid_values` now validates the shape of each Versions profile**: `utils/settings_api.py:1445-1447` previously only checked that `Versions` was a list — a malformed profile (wrong length, non-string name, non-list conditions/rules, non-string language) landed in `settings.json` and crashed the plex_debrid scraper path on restart with no user-facing signal from the Save dialog. Added shape validation per profile: exactly a 4-tuple of `[name:str, conditions:list, language:str, rules:list]`, where `name` must be a non-empty string and `language` must be any string (the runtime accepts `"en"`, `"jp"`, `"all"`, and also the legacy markers `"true"`/`"both"` which `plex_debrid/releases/__init__.py` `version.setup` at line 159 rewrites to the configured `default_language` — `"en"` at line 1371 — on first load, so the validator must not gatekeep on the value). Each violation surfaces as an error prefixed `Versions entry N` so the save dialog pins the diagnostic to the profile that's wrong rather than the whole `Versions` key. Seven new tests in `tests/test_settings_api.py::TestValidatePlexDebridValues` lock down every reject path and a parametrized `test_versions_profile_accepts_language_code` that exercises `"en"`, `"jp"`, `"true"`, `"all"`, and `""` so a future tightening of the check can't regress real users' saved profiles.

- **Settings: `NOTIFICATION_DIGEST_TIME` now validates the 24-hour HH:MM format the runtime expects, and ships a UI default that matches the runtime default**: `validate_env_values` in `utils/settings_api.py:506` had no check for `NOTIFICATION_DIGEST_TIME`, so values like `8:00` (missing leading zero), `noon`, or `08:00\n` (trailing newline from a sloppy copy-paste) saved cleanly and silently broke the digest scheduler at runtime — the user got no pager, no log line, no UI signal, and a skipped daily summary until they compared their settings to the docs. Added a `re.fullmatch` check on `([01]\d|2[0-3]):[0-5]\d` (fullmatch, not match — otherwise `08:00\n` would slip through because Python's `$` matches before a final newline by default) that returns an error listing the offending value. Also added `'NOTIFICATION_DIGEST_TIME': '08:00'` to `_ENV_DEFAULTS` (`utils/settings_api.py:289`) so the help text's `default: 08:00` claim actually matches what the field renders as — previously the runtime defaulted to `08:00` via `Config.load()` at `base/__init__.py:222` but the UI rendered an empty field, so users saw conflicting information between the form and the docs. Three new tests land in `tests/test_settings_api.py::TestValidateEnvValues` — valid formats (including `00:00` and `23:59`), empty-value passthrough, and a ten-case parametrized reject list that covers missing leading zero, overflow hours/minutes, non-numeric strings, wrong separators, extra components, trailing newline, trailing space, and leading space.

- **Settings: Trakt users field promoted out of "Show advanced settings"**: The `"Connect via OAuth"` button for Trakt was tied to the `Trakt users` schema field at `utils/settings_api.py:916`, which was flagged `hidden=True` — users trying to connect a Trakt account on first setup had to discover the Advanced toggle before they could see the OAuth entry point. Set `hidden=False` so the field renders in the main Content Services category next to the Trakt configuration and expanded the help text to tell users the OAuth button below is the primary setup path. Plex users stays hidden because it's the manual-token legacy flow. Part of the same "make OAuth discoverable" cleanup that's been a pattern in recent work.

- **Settings page categories now render collapsed by default on both tabs**: `renderEnvCategories` (`utils/settings_page.py:412`) and `renderPdCategories` (line 604) both had `const openClass = i === 0 ? ' open' : ''` + `aria-expanded = i === 0 ? 'true' : 'false'`, force-opening the first category on every render. On the Zurgarr tab that meant the long Zurg section sprawled open before the user could see the category list; on the plex_debrid tab it meant Content Services. Dropped the first-category special-case in both renderers so every category starts collapsed, matching the "collapsed by default until the user opts in" pattern used for the show detail page's season list (commit 2affc0c). Search/filter still auto-expands matching categories via the existing filter path.

- **Dead `rarbg` scraper option removed from the Settings UI**: RARBG shut down in May 2023, taking the `torrentapi.org` endpoint that `plex_debrid/scraper/services/rarbg.py` calls (lines 25-38) offline permanently. The Settings page still exposed `rarbg` as a selectable scraper source and rendered a `Rarbg API Key` secret field, defaulting to the hardcoded pre-shutdown token `"r05xvbq6ul"` that has been baked into `plex_debrid_/settings-default.json:238` since the vendored copy was imported — enabling it did nothing except silently fail every search. Dropped `'rarbg'` from `_SCRAPER_SOURCES` (`utils/settings_api.py:894`) so the checkbox no longer appears, and removed the `Rarbg API Key` tuple from the `Scraper Settings` schema (`utils/settings_api.py:979`). Because the PD save flow is a full replacement (`collectPdData` → POST → `json.dump(values, f)` in `write_plex_debrid_values`), any existing user who has `"rarbg"` in their `Sources` array or a non-default `Rarbg API Key` in `settings.json` will have both dropped on their next save — which is the intended cleanup path. Also expanded the `Sources` help text to tell users up-front which backends are public Stremio addons (torrentio/mediafusion/comet), which need self-hosted infra (jackett/prowlarr/zilean), which need a paid account (orionoid/torbox), and which are brittle HTML scrapers (1337x) — so nobody else enables a source expecting it to "just work" when it needs a missing dependency.

- **Settings page `list_pairs` fields no longer flag "unsaved changes" after clicking "+" on an empty row**: `collectPdData`'s list_pairs branch at `utils/settings_page.py:1029-1039` pushed every row unconditionally, so clicking the "+" button to add a new pair but leaving both inputs blank emitted `[["", ""]]` while the saved value was `[]` — `JSON.stringify` diff → tab flagged dirty even though the user added no data, and worse, saving would persist `["", ""]` into settings.json. The list_strings branch one block up already skips blank inputs via `if (inp.value.trim())`; list_pairs now matches that behavior, skipping rows where both inputs are empty (whitespace-only). Non-blank pairs preserve the exact string (no trim) since `"Special character renaming"` intentionally uses single-space mappings like `[" ", "."]` that a trim would destroy.

- **Settings page no longer shows a phantom "unsaved changes" banner when switching between the Zurgarr and plex_debrid tabs**: `getPdChangedFields` in `utils/settings_page.py` compared DOM-collected values to `pdValues` via a `JSON.stringify` union-of-keys walk that coerced missing keys on either side to `null`. That broke two ways: (1) `list_strings` fields only put `data-pdkey` on the row `<input>`s — never on the container — so a field saved as `[]` (e.g. `Trakt lists`, `Trakt library user`, `Plex library refresh`, `Plex library check`) rendered zero inputs, `collectPdData` emitted no key at all, and the comparison became `null` vs `[]` → marked dirty. (2) Schema keys added after `settings-default.json` was last updated (`MDBList API Key`, `MDBList List IDs`, `Zilean Base URL`, every `Mediafusion *` / `Comet *`, `Torbox API Key`, `Watchlist loop interval (sec)`) render empty inputs — `collectPdData` emits `''` / `'false'` / `[]` — while `pdValues` has no key → `a = ''` vs `b = null` → again marked dirty. The state only became visible on tab switch because `init()` never calls `updateDirtyUI()` and `innerHTML` rendering doesn't fire `input`/`change` events, so the first evaluation was `switchTab`'s trailing `updateDirtyUI()` call. Replaced the union walk with a schema-driven loop that iterates `PD_SCHEMA.categories[*].fields`, skips `hidden` (the `version` field is preserved verbatim in `collectPdData`), and normalizes missing/null values on both sides to the field's type-appropriate empty default via a new `pdEmptyForField(field)` helper (`[]` for multiselect/radio/list_strings/list_pairs, `'false'` for boolean_str, `''` for string/secret/select, `[]` for the `Versions` quality-profile editor — which collectPdData always emits as an array from `_versionsData` — and `null` for any other `json` textarea) before the JSON compare. Legitimate edits still differ from the normalized saved value, so real dirty detection is unaffected. Self-healed after the first save because `pdValues = data` (line 1103) made the shapes symmetric, which is why the bug stayed hidden. All 166 `test_settings_api` tests pass.

- **Show detail page now opens with every season collapsed by default**: Previously `_shouldAutoExpand(season, showTitle)` in `utils/library_page.py` auto-expanded a season on first render when any of three conditions held — (1) a pending missing-episode search overlapped the season, (2) any episode aired within ±30 days, or (3) the season was incomplete (have some but not all aired episodes). Rule (3) in particular meant that for any show with holes (the common case for in-progress series) the relevant season popped open the moment the detail view rendered, fighting a user who just wanted to browse the season list. Replaced the ternary at the season loop (`hasPrev ? ...existing state... : _shouldAutoExpand(...)`) with `!!(hasPrev && expandedNums[String(season.number)])` so the fallback branch is now plain `false` — first visit leaves every season collapsed, and the existing `expandedNums` cache still restores whatever the user had expanded on re-render. The "Expand All" / "Collapse All" button continues to work as before (it reads `hasPrev && every(expanded)`, which now correctly reports "Expand All" on first load). Deleted `_shouldAutoExpand` entirely (no other callers) and its lone dependency on the pending-episode highlight heuristic, which was only ever used for auto-expansion and not for rendering the badge itself.

- **Show- and episode-level "Block" buttons on the Library detail page now blocklist the actual release folder instead of the show title**: Two related bugs in `utils/library_page.py`. (1) The show-header "Block this torrent file" icon at former line 2681 rendered for any show with debrid content and POSTed `{title: show.title}` to `/api/blocklist` — but a show is an umbrella over many releases, so there is no single torrent file the button could meaningfully refer to. The server handler at `utils/status_server.py:2295-2305` sees no `info_hash` in the payload and synthesizes one as `TITLE:` + sha1(show_title), which (a) can never match any real SHA-1 torrent hash, so all `is_blocked(info_hash)` call sites in `utils/search.py` / `utils/blackhole.py` are no-ops for the synthesized entry, and (b) only matches `is_blocked_title(folder_name)` when the normalized debrid folder name equals the show title — almost never true for real show releases (`Breaking.Bad.S01.1080p.BluRay-GROUP` does not normalize to `breaking bad`). Net effect: the button pretended to block a torrent, stored an entry that couldn't match real hashes, and matched a folder-name shape that show releases don't use. Removed the show-level button outright. (2) The per-episode 🚫 button at line 2521 had the same underlying bug: `_blockItem()` took no arguments and read `_detailItem.title` — which for an episode row is the *show's* title, not anything episode-specific — so clicking 🚫 on S01E03 stored the same useless show-title entry as the now-removed show-level button. Fix threads the debrid release folder name through from scan time to the blocklist POST: `_collect_episodes(folder_path)` and `LibraryScanner._collect_episodes_from_webdav(contents, folder_name)` now tag each episode dict with `folder = os.path.basename(folder_path)` (webdav path gets the folder name passed as an arg from the `folders.items()` loop at `library.py:3497`); `_build_season_data` passes the field through to the per-episode dict returned to the frontend; the per-episode button renders with `data-folder="<release folder>"` and routes its click through a new `_blockFromBtn(btn)` helper that reads the attribute and calls `_blockItem(folder)`. `_blockItem` now accepts an optional folder parameter, uses it as the blocklist target when present (falling back to `_detailItem.title` for the still-unchanged movie detail button), and stores the resolved target on the modal overlay's `dataset.blTarget` so `_confirmBlock` reads the same value the header rendered — no stale closure, no drift between what the header says and what gets POSTed. Modal header and button tooltip now display the release folder name so the user sees exactly what's being blocked (`Block: Breaking.Bad.S01.1080p.BluRay-GROUP` instead of `Block: Breaking Bad`). `is_blocked_title(release_folder)` at the two scan sites (`library.py:2865`, `library.py:3022`) now actually fires for manually-blocked episode releases — next scan drops the release from the library, Sonarr's existing gap-fill re-searches the now-missing episodes. Merge safety: for 'both'-sourced episodes, the merge at `library.py:~L1278` keeps debrid's info dict (including `folder`) so blocking targets the debrid release the user actually saw in the detail view, not the user's local show folder. Local-only episodes still get a `folder` tag for consistency but the block button is only rendered for `source in {'debrid','both'}` so a local folder name can't accidentally be blocklisted through this UI. The movie detail button (line 2287) still uses `_detailItem.title` as its block target — its existing title↔folder-name approximation is close enough for single-file movie releases that the user explicitly scoped movie-folder threading out of this fix — but its tooltip was retuned from *"Block this torrent file"* to *"Block this release"* so it doesn't imply a precision the movie button (unlike the new episode button) doesn't have, keeping the user-facing language honest across all three surfaces. `_blockItem()`, the `/api/blocklist` handler, and the modal CSS are all still used by the remaining callers and are left in place.

- **Episode source badge now centered in its column**: `.ep-source` had `text-align:right` in `utils/library_page.py`, which hugged the "Local" / "Debrid" badge against the right edge of its cell and left it visually misaligned with the narrower "Searching" / "Upcoming" / "Missing" pills on neighboring rows (those were only *approximately* centered because their badge width happened to fill the column). Switched to `text-align:center` so every badge variant — regardless of its rendered width — sits centered in the column.

- **Ghost "Season N" sections for unpopulated TMDB seasons no longer render**: A show like *Grey's Anatomy* would show a 23rd season with 0 episodes in the detail view because TMDB lists renewed-but-unscheduled next seasons in `/tv/{id}::seasons` with no episode stubs yet, and `utils/tmdb.py::get_show_metadata` stored every season it iterated regardless of whether the per-season fetch returned any episodes. The cache then carried the empty season forever and the frontend's `_mergeShowMeta` faithfully rendered it as a section with nothing in it, inflating the seasons count on the card too. Two-layer fix: (1) `get_show_metadata` now drops a season whose per-season fetch returns an empty `episodes` list, so new cache entries never carry ghost seasons; once TMDB publishes episode stubs the next cache refresh will pick the season up again. (2) Frontend `_mergeShowMeta` adds a compatibility shim for pre-fix caches — any `meta.seasons` entry with an empty `episodes` array is skipped unless the user happens to have on-disk files for that season number (rare, but a local rip of a prerelease would still render). New test `tests/test_tmdb.py::TestShowMetadata::test_get_show_metadata_skips_empty_seasons` locks down the Grey's Anatomy repro: S0 (specials, still filtered as before), S22 (five episodes, kept), S23 (renewed but no episodes, dropped) → result contains only S22. All 1614 tests pass.

- **Library grid progress bar and detail-view now agree with the monitored-aware missing count**: Follow-up to the monitored-filter fix below. Two surfaces still spoke the old TMDB-only language after the pill started showing Sonarr's monitored delta, producing a split-brain where *Grey's Anatomy* would read **"1 missing"** in the pill and simultaneously draw a **red progress bar at ~17%** for the same card. (1) The card's progress bar denominator in `utils/library_page.py::computeProgress` and the async TMDB-enrichment path in `enrichCardsWithMeta` now prefer `item.monitored_episodes` (new field) over `item.total_episodes` when set — have-count becomes `monitored_episodes - missing_episodes` so bar and pill tell the same story. The backend helper `_apply_sonarr_monitored_filter` computes `monitored_episodes = sum(statistics.episodeCount for s in seasons if s.monitored)` per matched show and omits the field when zero monitored seasons exist (so the frontend falls back to the TMDB total rather than divide-by-zero). (2) `_mergeShowMeta` now hides *fully-empty* unmonitored seasons from the detail-view merge, so S1–S15 of *Grey's Anatomy* — which render as `{episodes: [], episode_count: 0, total_episodes: 0}` with my earlier filter — no longer paint ghost "Season N" sections with nothing in them. Unmonitored seasons that *do* have on-disk files are still rendered so the user can play what they have. One new test (`test_monitored_episodes_omitted_when_all_seasons_unmonitored`) locks down the divide-by-zero guard; the existing `test_missing_is_monitored_minus_file_count` now also asserts `monitored_episodes == 18`. All 1613 tests pass.

- **Library "X missing" count now honors Sonarr's monitored flag**: A show like *Grey's Anatomy* (22 seasons, older seasons deliberately unmonitored in Sonarr) previously showed ~300+ "missing" episodes on the Library grid and in the Wanted → Missing pill, because `utils/library.py::_enrich_with_tmdb_cache` computed `missing_episodes = TMDB_total_aired - have_on_disk` without consulting Sonarr — any unmonitored aired episode was treated as a gap. Commit 6ae45fc already fixed the *search* side of this (gap-fill passes `respect_monitored=True` to `ensure_and_search` so unwanted episodes aren't re-grabbed), but the *display* still inflated with every episode the user had explicitly opted out of. New `_apply_sonarr_monitored_filter(shows)` helper runs after TMDB enrichment in `_scan_read`: one bulk `GET /api/v3/series` returns all series with per-season `statistics.episodeCount` (aired monitored episodes — Sonarr filters this by per-episode `monitored` flags internally) and `episodeFileCount` (aired episodes with a file), and the library payload's `missing_episodes` becomes `sum(max(0, episodeCount - episodeFileCount) for season in series.seasons if season.monitored)`. Shows with no Sonarr match keep the TMDB-only calculation — the conservative path for hand-imported libraries. Each show also gets a new `unmonitored_seasons: [int]` field listing wholly-unmonitored season numbers, which (1) stops `_compute_missing_episodes` from fanning out a search per unmonitored season only for `ensure_and_search` to short-circuit with "all unmonitored" (previously that round-tripped Sonarr once per season on every scan for shows like Grey's Anatomy — 15 wasted calls per scan; the gap-fill list now drops unmonitored seasons at the source), and (2) tells the Library UI in `utils/library_page.py::_mergeShowMeta` to omit TMDB-expected episodes from unmonitored seasons when building the detail-view table, so the expanded show doesn't paint red "missing" rows for seasons the user explicitly opted out of. The grid-card enrichment path in `enrichCardsWithMeta` also prefers the server-side `item.missing_episodes` over its own recomputation from TMDB totals so the badge agrees with the backend. **Shared TTL cache for Sonarr's series list** — `_scan_effects::_create_debrid_symlinks` already calls `get_all_series()` for canonical folder name lookups, so a naive implementation would fire two `/api/v3/series` requests per scan cycle. Both consumers now go through `_get_sonarr_series_list(client)` which memoizes the fetch under a 120s TTL + `threading.Lock` (scan cycle read→effects takes seconds, so one HTTP call covers both phases; back-to-back manual refreshes still get fresh data; a transient fetch failure returns `None` without caching so the next scan retries). **Title-collision-safe matching** — reboot pairs share lowercase titles (e.g. *Magnum P.I.* 1980 + 2018 without year suffixes, *One Day at a Time* 1975 + 2017) and transliteration can collapse distinct international titles to the same `_norm_for_matching` key; the earlier last-writer-wins maps silently matched a library show to an arbitrary Sonarr entry and gave plausible-looking but wrong monitored counts. The index now tracks colliding keys and treats them as ambiguous — TMDB ID remains the only reliable path for colliding titles (the ID-by-cache lookup works because `tmdb_cache` keys reboots with year suffixes, e.g. `"magnum p.i. (1980)"`). **Parsed-title asymmetry fixed** — enrichment may upgrade `item['title']` to the canonical TMDB spelling (e.g. multi-language torrent folders) and stash the original in `_parsed_title`; the TMDB cache may still be keyed under either form depending on scan order. Both candidates are now tried through every match step, not only step 1. **Matching cascade** is TMDB ID (parsed or display) → exact year-qualified title → exact plain title → `_norm_for_matching` fuzzy, mirroring the priority order CLAUDE.md documents for the four dir-selection/rescan sites (TMDB ID is first here because it's unambiguous where title-level collisions are not). Fallback behavior preserved across five unreachability modes: Sonarr not configured (→ no-op), Sonarr network error (→ no-op, cache stays empty so next scan retries), show missing from Sonarr library (→ keep TMDB count), title-only match colliding on reboots without a TMDB cache hit (→ keep TMDB count), `missing_episodes=None` pass-through for unenriched shows (→ wanted-count helper still ignores). Seventeen new tests lock the semantics — `tests/test_library.py::TestApplySonarrMonitoredFilter` (10: unmonitored-seasons-excluded, sum-monitored-gap, file-count-exceeding-monitored-clamps-to-zero, specials-season-zero-ignored, unmatched-show-keeps-count, sonarr-unreachable-no-op, sonarr-not-configured-no-op, title-collision-skipped-without-tmdb-id, title-collision-resolved-via-tmdb-id, parsed-title-matches-when-display-renamed), `tests/test_library.py::TestGetSonarrSeriesList` (3: cached-within-ttl, force-refresh-bypasses-cache, fetch-failure-returns-none-and-does-not-cache), and `tests/test_gap_fill.py::TestComputeMissingEpisodes::test_unmonitored_seasons_excluded` (Grey's Anatomy repro: S1–S15 unmonitored + S22 with a real E3 gap → only `(22, 3)` returned). All 1612 tests pass.

- **Episodes airing today no longer count as "missing"**: A show whose next episode airs today (e.g. *For All Mankind*, *Your Friends & Neighbors* on a premiere day) would appear on the Library grid with a red `"1 missing"` label and contribute to the Wanted → Missing pill count even before the episode had actually broadcast. Root cause: `utils/tmdb.py::get_cached_posters` counted any episode whose `air_date` was `<= today` toward `total_episodes`, and `library.py` derived `missing_episodes = total - have` from that — so the instant the calendar rolled over to the broadcast date, today's episode was booked as "aired" and therefore as a hole in the library. The three TMDB helpers (`get_cached_posters`, `find_show_by_season._format_entry`, `get_cached_episode_list`) now treat only strictly past air dates as aired; today's episode is excluded from the aired count. The client-side mirrors in `utils/library_page.py` (grid-card progress-bar `totalEps` accumulator and `bulkSearchMissing`'s per-episode filter) match with the same `< today` predicate, so the "Search Missing" bulk button won't queue a futile search for a release that isn't yet available on any indexer. Both JS sites now build `today` from `getFullYear()/getMonth()/getDate()` (local) rather than `.toISOString()` (UTC) so they align with the per-episode `"Airing Today"` badge at `library_page.py:2414`, which has always keyed off `_todayStr` in local time — mixing UTC at the grid-card/bulk-search layer with local time at the badge layer would have re-created the exact split-brain this fix is meant to eliminate (a flagged finding from the code-review pass on the first cut, addressed in the same commit per the "don't punt on reviewer findings" rule in CLAUDE.md). Per-episode status inside the detail view was already correct — the `"Airing Today"` yellow badge lands between `"Upcoming"` (strictly future) and `"Missing"` (strictly past) — so today's episode now shows `"Airing Today"` across all three surfaces (grid card no longer flags it, bulk-search no longer grabs for it, episode row already said the right thing) instead of the previous split where the detail view said "Airing Today" but the card said "1 missing". Matches Sonarr's convention where "missing" means aired-and-expected-on-disk. Two new tests lock down the new semantics: `tests/test_tmdb.py::TestCachedPosters::test_excludes_episodes_airing_today_from_total` (aired + today + future → total_episodes == 1) and `tests/test_tmdb_episode_list.py::TestCachedEpisodeList::test_excludes_episodes_airing_today` (same seeding shape → reconcile/search helper returns only the strictly-past episode). All 1598 tests pass.

### Added

- **Post-timeout debrid cleanup for the blackhole + cross-provider routing fix for `get_debrid_client()`**: Closes the last remaining junk-accumulation path for RD users with aggressive gap-fill on long-running shows. Without this, when Sonarr/Radarr drops a `.magnet` into the blackhole and the hash never caches on the debrid provider, Zurgarr's `_monitor_and_symlink` thread times out at `BLACKHOLE_MOUNT_POLL_TIMEOUT` (default 5 min), removes the torrent from its own pending tracking via `_remove_pending(torrent_id)`, and returns — but the torrent **stays on the debrid account** as a 0%/0-seed entry. For an RD user with `prefer-debrid` set on a long-running show + `GAP_FILL_ENABLED=true` + `BLACKHOLE_ENABLED=true`, gap-fill walks the show's aired-episode list, kicks Sonarr to search every hole, Sonarr hits Torrentio, drops a magnet into `/watch/sonarr/` per episode, and ~75% of those hashes never cache on RD in a fresh state — so after a couple of hours the user's DebridMediaManager view is dominated by stuck Grey's-Anatomy-style junk. New env var `BLACKHOLE_DELETE_UNCACHED_ON_TIMEOUT` (default **OFF**) gates the cleanup: when ON, the timeout branch at `utils/blackhole.py:_monitor_and_symlink` calls `client.delete_torrent(str(torrent_id))` via the existing disc-rip cleanup pattern (same per-service client lookup, same guards) before `_remove_pending`. Default OFF because the behaviour changes data state on the debrid account (deletes torrents) and some users tolerate long cache waits for very rare releases; users with RD's no-pre-check cache situation + the ambient uncached-grab path should flip it ON. Defensive throughout: if the debrid client can't be constructed (missing key, misconfiguration), the cleanup skips and `_remove_pending` still fires so pending state stays consistent; if `delete_torrent` raises (network error, stale torrent id, rate-limit), the exception is caught at DEBUG level with the API key redacted from the message string (RD/AD/TB URLs can carry the key in query strings — an unsanitized requests exception would leak it when the user has `ZURGARR_LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG` for troubleshooting) and `_remove_pending` still fires. The history event (`type='failed'`, `detail='Timed out uncached — removed from debrid'` vs `'Timed out uncached — debrid cleanup skipped'`, `meta={'torrent_id', 'reason': 'uncached_timeout', 'deleted': bool}`) fires regardless of whether the delete landed, so the audit trail survives a misconfigured key — matches the disc-rip cleanup pattern at lines 1671-1675. Registered in `utils/config_reload.py::SOFT_RELOAD` — reads fresh from `os.environ` each timeout so SIGHUP applies the toggle without bouncing the blackhole watcher. **Also fixes a cross-provider routing hazard** in `utils.debrid_client.get_debrid_client()` flagged during the bug-hunter review: the factory previously picked Real-Debrid > AllDebrid > TorBox in hard-coded priority order regardless of which provider the caller was actually bound to, so a blackhole watcher with `BLACKHOLE_DEBRID=alldebrid` running alongside a plex_debrid-scoped `RD_API_KEY` would route AD-format torrent IDs into an RD client — RD's `_SAFE_ID` regex accepts numeric AD IDs, so the HTTP DELETE would sail through client-side validation and fire against the wrong account. Worst case (low-probability but possible): the AD ID shape collides with a real RD torrent and an unrelated RD torrent gets silently deleted; common case, RD returns 404, wastes rate-limit budget, and looks like a silent failure from the user's perspective. The factory now accepts `service=` and `api_key=` arguments for callers that know which provider they want; `service=None` preserves the historical priority fallback for callers that don't. The blackhole timeout-delete and the pre-existing disc-rip delete both route through `get_debrid_client(service=self.debrid_service, api_key=self.debrid_api_key)` so they never cross-contaminate. Gap-fill is explicitly the workload this feature targets, and gap-fill plus multi-provider setups are a realistic combination — per the CLAUDE.md "no out-of-scope punts" rule, fixing the shared hazard in the disc-rip path in the same commit (which this change amplifies from one-shot to per-timeout exposure). Fourteen new tests across `tests/test_blackhole_debrid_dedup.py::TestBlackholeTimeoutCleanup` (9 — default-OFF, ON-removes, missing-client-still-logs-history, successful-delete-marks-history, routes-to-bound-service-not-priority, api-key-sanitized-in-DEBUG-log, realistic-status-polling-before-timeout, delete-raises-still-reaches-remove_pending, broken-debrid_client-import-swallowed) and `tests/test_debrid_client.py::TestConfiguration` (4 — explicit-service-overrides-priority, explicit-api-key-override, unknown-service-returns-none, explicit-service-but-unconfigured-returns-none). The fix pairs with `PD_ENFORCE_CACHED_VERSIONS` (the existing plex_debrid-path gate) and `BLACKHOLE_DEBRID_DEDUP_ENABLED` (the pre-add duplicate guard) to close the three-way gap-fill+prefer-debrid+RD junk-accumulation loop. For RD users the recommended combo is **both** `PD_ENFORCE_CACHED_VERSIONS=true` (if using plex_debrid) **and** `BLACKHOLE_DELETE_UNCACHED_ON_TIMEOUT=true` (if using the blackhole). All 1596 tests pass.

### Changed

- **README tagline and positioning retuned to reflect the project's actual center of gravity (integration, not packaging)**: The old tagline — *"Stream your Real-Debrid library through Plex or Jellyfin — one container, zero local storage"* — described what the upstream pd_zurg project was: packaging glue for Zurg + rclone + plex_debrid. But the capabilities Zurgarr has accumulated since the fork (source-aware Library browser, auto debrid symlinks, source preference state, `to-any` pending direction, TMDB gap-fill across both backends, quality compromise engine, season-pack fallback, routing audit self-heal, symlink verify/repair, debrid-account dedup + cache gates) aren't "streaming through Plex" features — they're all structurally about *integrating and managing local + debrid media as one library*. The new tagline — *"Local media and debrid content, managed as one library — for Plex, Jellyfin, Sonarr, and Radarr"* — and the rewritten "What is Zurgarr?" paragraph lead with the integration layer while still crediting the Zurg/rclone/plex_debrid packaging foundation underneath. The "Why this project?" feature list, previously an unstructured bullet cloud ordered by commit history, is now grouped into five intent-based sections: **Unified library** (Library browser, auto symlinks, Torrentio search, cross-machine WebDAV exposure), **Sonarr/Radarr integration** (blackhole + quality compromise + season-pack fallback + gap-fill + debrid-account dedup & cache gates), **Self-healing** (routing audit auto-tag, symlink verify+repair, blocklist auto-expiry, ffprobe recovery, process auto-restart), **Observability & control** (dashboard + settings editor with SIGHUP reload, activity history, Apprise notifications, Prometheus metrics, OAuth device-code flows, MDBList), and **Reliability & non-disruption** (atomic config writes, ordered shutdown, Docker secrets, sidecar-to-existing-arr-stack posture). Capabilities that were previously underhighlighted or missing from the bullets (Prometheus metrics endpoint, activity history log, OAuth flows for Trakt/Debrid Link/Put.io/Orionoid, MDBList subscriptions, atomic config writes across all state surfaces, non-disruptive sidecar posture alongside qBit/Usenet) are now surfaced explicitly. Net: ~60 additional lines in the README's opening section, but much higher information density per line and a framing that matches what the codebase actually is.

- **Documentation restructured: README is now lean, config reference and troubleshooting are their own docs**: The single 548-line README tried to serve four audiences at once (curious browser, pd_zurg migrant, first-time installer, existing user tuning a knob) — a structural contradiction that no amount of rewriting fixes. Split into three docs with distinct audiences. `README.md` (~338 lines) keeps the intro, the quickstart, the three workflow recipes (watchlist / blackhole / both), the docker-compose examples, the web UI overview, and the credits/licensing — but **no config tables, no troubleshooting section**, and no deep-reference prose. A new one-paragraph "Recommended settings per debrid provider" block handles the most common reason a new user googles for help (uncached junk in DMM). New `CONFIGURATION.md` (~291 lines) is the full env-var reference, grouped by feature with proper headings instead of collapsed `<details>` sections; a new "Minimum required to start" table at the top answers "what's the least I have to set?" without making users scroll the full ~80-variable list. New `TROUBLESHOOTING.md` (~172 lines) is symptom-first — entries are keyed by what the user sees ("DMM shows torrents at 0% with no seeds", "Duplicate torrents in my debrid account", "Sonarr/Radarr keeps re-grabbing the same failed torrent") rather than by setting, with a short fix and a link to the relevant CONFIGURATION.md section. The "Migrating from pd_zurg" guidance from the old README Troubleshooting section moved verbatim. The restructure also closed several gaps discovered during the audit: five new env vars from the dedup/cache-gates work (`SEARCH_DEDUP_ENABLED`, `SEARCH_REQUIRE_CACHED`, `BLACKHOLE_DEBRID_DEDUP_ENABLED`, `BLACKHOLE_REQUIRE_CACHED`, `PD_ENFORCE_CACHED_VERSIONS`) weren't documented in any user-facing doc and are now in CONFIGURATION.md, TROUBLESHOOTING.md, and `.env.example`; eight scheduled-task intervals (`ROUTING_AUDIT_INTERVAL`, `QUEUE_CLEANUP_INTERVAL`, `LIBRARY_SCAN_INTERVAL`, `SYMLINK_VERIFY_INTERVAL`, `PREFERENCE_ENFORCE_INTERVAL`, `HOUSEKEEPING_INTERVAL`, `CONFIG_BACKUP_INTERVAL`, `MOUNT_LIVENESS_INTERVAL`) that the code has been reading for releases without any prose coverage now have a table in CONFIGURATION.md's "Scheduled task intervals" section; `ROUTING_AUTO_TAG_UNTAGGED` and `PD_LOGFILE` also joined the reference; the confusing `BLACKHOLE_DEDUP_ENABLED` vs new `BLACKHOLE_DEBRID_DEDUP_ENABLED` name collision is now explicitly called out in both the config table (two distinct sub-sections: "Local-library dedup" vs "Debrid-account dedup + cache gate") and the troubleshooting entry. CLAUDE.md's Documentation Map updated to describe each doc's purpose and adds a "When adding a new env var" checklist reminding contributors to update all four surfaces (`base/__init__.py`, `utils/settings_api.py`, `CONFIGURATION.md`, `.env.example`) so future drift is less likely. No code changes — all 1582 tests continue to pass.

### Added

- **Debrid-account dedup and "require cached" gates on both the manual search "Add" button and the Sonarr/Radarr blackhole**: Addresses two long-running pain points in the interactive + blackhole add paths — uncached torrents landing in the user's RD/AD/TB account as 0%/0-seed/0-B/s entries that DebridMediaManager then surfaces as unplayable junk, and duplicate hash submissions that create parallel torrent entries for the same release after a Sonarr/Radarr re-grab. `utils/search.py::add_to_debrid` (the one-click Add on the Library detail search UI) and `utils/blackhole.py::_process_file` (the `.torrent`/`.magnet` blackhole watcher) both gained two new gates that fire *before* the provider-specific add handler is invoked: a dedup probe that queries the debrid account's current hash set and short-circuits if the incoming hash is already present, and a cache-require probe that refuses the add when the provider's `/magnet/instant` (AD) / `/api/torrents/checkcached` (TB) cache probe does not confirm the hash is cached. Real-Debrid deprecated `/torrents/instantAvailability` in Nov 2024 so `check_debrid_cache` returns `None` (unknown) for every RD hash — strict mode treats `None` as "not cached" and refuses, matching the plex_debrid compromise engine's I4 invariant; users who run RD and still want the gate on AD/TB only should leave it OFF. Four new env vars — `SEARCH_DEDUP_ENABLED` (default **ON**), `SEARCH_REQUIRE_CACHED` (default OFF), `BLACKHOLE_DEBRID_DEDUP_ENABLED` (default **ON**), `BLACKHOLE_REQUIRE_CACHED` (default OFF) — all exposed in the Settings UI under Debrid Search and Blackhole respectively, and all registered in `utils/config_reload.py::SOFT_RELOAD` so SIGHUP applies toggle changes without bouncing the blackhole watcher or status server. The dedup probe is backed by a new `_existing_hashes(service, api_key)` helper in `utils/search.py` that calls RD `/torrents?limit=2500`, AD `/v4/magnet/status`, or TB `/v1/api/torrents/mylist` and caches the resulting lowercase hash set per service for 30s — a burst of "Add" clicks issues one list call, not N — with a matching `remember_added_hash(service, hash)` hook that primes the cache on successful add so a duplicate click within TTL is caught even before the account list is re-fetched. Cache-key invariants: `_existing_hashes` returns `None` (unknown) when the service/key is missing or the API errors, which both gates treat as "cannot dedup / cannot verify — proceed" rather than silently refusing; the strict cache-require gate, by contrast, treats `None` in the *blackhole* differently from the *search* path — in the blackhole a `None` means "API unavailable, leave the file in place so the next poll retries", because silently deleting every in-flight Sonarr/Radarr drop during a transient AD/TB outage is a data-loss path; in the search UI `None` refuses the add (user can retry). Only `cached is False` (provider-confirmed uncached) deletes the drop. A stricter-than-strict guard rejects any `.torrent`/`.magnet` whose info hash could not be extracted (corrupted bencode, missing `btih:`) when `BLACKHOLE_REQUIRE_CACHED` is ON — a malformed file cannot be cache-verified so it cannot be allowed through a safety gate; dedup-only mode still falls through for malformed files because dedup is best-effort. And a bypass for `BLACKHOLE_REQUIRE_CACHED=true` + missing `debrid_api_key` refuses to delete drops and logs an error instead, so a Docker-secrets mount failure or typo in `RD_API_KEY` surfaces as "please fix your config" rather than "every drop is silently eaten". A process-wide `_inflight_adds` set (guarded by `_existing_hashes_lock`) prevents the race where two concurrent `add_to_debrid` callers both pass the account-list dedup probe before either reaches `addMagnet` — the second caller sees the first's in-flight tuple and returns `{'duplicate': True, 'error': 'Add already in progress'}` without submitting. `_existing_hashes` now returns a snapshot (`set(cached[1])`) rather than the live cached reference so a concurrent `remember_added_hash` cannot mutate the set a caller is iterating. Dedup helpers are hardened against API shape drift via an `_coerce_hash(value)` type-guard that drops non-string `hash` fields (an int or null slipping through would otherwise raise `AttributeError` on `.strip()`), plus `isinstance(data, dict)` guards in the AD/TB helpers and `AttributeError/TypeError/KeyError` added to the `_existing_hashes` exception catch so a broken API response never bubbles up into the HTTP handler. A one-time warning fires when RD returns the full 2500-entry page — heavy users get a visible "dedup may miss older entries" log line instead of a silent-degradation failure mode. The blackhole path's `from utils.search import ...` is now unguarded (it's a first-party module; a bare `ImportError` silence was hiding real bugs) and uses `cache_label` instead of `label` to avoid shadowing the `_process_file(label=...)` routing-label parameter. The `PD_ENFORCE_CACHED_VERSIONS` migration (default OFF) hooks into `plex_debrid_/setup.py::pd_setup` via a new `enforce_cached_versions(json_data)` helper that the test suite now exercises directly — the previous hand-copied migration logic in the test file would have let a regression in the real code slip through unnoticed. The helper injects `["cache status", "requirement", "cached", ""]` as the first rule of any plex_debrid content version missing it, so the vendored `plex_debrid/debrid/services/{realdebrid,alldebrid,torbox}.py` download paths — which only reject uncached grabs when the content version *explicitly* requires cached — stop falling back to uncached releases for custom versions the user added via the plex_debrid UI. Idempotent: a no-op once the rule is present, safe to leave ON on every startup. Forty-four new tests in `tests/test_search.py` (30 — TestAddToDebridDedup, TestAddToDebridRequireCached, TestExistingHashesHelpers, TestAddToDebridInFlightRace) and `tests/test_blackhole_debrid_dedup.py` (14 — TestBlackholeDebridDedup, TestBlackholeRequireCached, TestPlexDebridCacheRuleEnforcer) cover every branch of each gate, including: the "unknown account state does not block" dedup invariant, the RD-specific "None means not cached" behaviour of the strict search gate, the "None means DEFER, do not delete" behaviour of the strict blackhole gate, the malformed-torrent refusal under strict mode, the missing-API-key bypass that preserves the drop, the concurrent-add in-flight rejection, the TTL cache hit/miss and force-refresh paths, the `remember_added_hash` post-add priming, API-shape-drift robustness (non-list RD, non-dict AD/TB, non-string hash fields), the RD truncation warning, and the migration's idempotency and "malformed versions are skipped, not crashed" robustness guard. All 1538 pre-existing tests continue to pass — total suite now 1582.

- **Unconditional episode-completeness reconcile ("available anywhere" gap-fill)**: The library scan now diffs every monitored show's aired TMDB episode list against the combined debrid + local source map and triggers a Sonarr/Radarr search for every aired episode missing from BOTH backends, regardless of whether the user set a `prefer-debrid` / `prefer-local` preference. Previously `_search_for_debrid_copies` only fired for shows explicitly tagged `prefer-debrid`, and even then it only searched episodes that existed locally but not on debrid — episodes missing from both sources were structurally invisible because the scanner's `season_data` only contains episodes with files (see `library.py:3589`). The renamed `_search_for_missing_episodes` builds the missing set from the TMDB cache (new `tmdb.get_cached_episode_list(title, year)`, aired-only, season-0 excluded) via a new `_compute_missing_episodes(show)` helper; the resulting `(season, episode)` tuples feed the existing per-season search plumbing (cooldown, `_SEARCH_RETRY_HOURS`, `_SEARCH_BUDGET_SECONDS`, touch-before-search). Route selection is driven by `_route_for(norm, preferences)`: `prefer-debrid` → `prefer_debrid=True` (legacy force-grab via interactive search, merged with the new missing-anywhere set so both intents fire in one pass); `prefer-local` → `False`; unset → `None` (Sonarr's existing routing tag decides the destination). New pending direction `to-any` is introduced for the route=None case, added to `_VALID_DIRECTIONS` in `utils/library_prefs.py`; `_clear_resolved_pending` resolves `to-any` entries on ANY source (debrid, local, or both) — the user story is "playable anywhere", not "playable via a specific route" — and `_escalate_stuck_pending` leaves `to-any` entries alone (no `debrid-unavailable` promotion: the episode may still land via either backend so we keep retrying at the existing 6h cadence rather than silently giving up). `ensure_and_search` gains a `respect_monitored=False` kwarg; the library reconcile passes `True` whenever route is None or False so an explicitly-unmonitored episode in Sonarr/Radarr is never re-searched against the user's intent. The legacy prefer-debrid code path keeps `respect_monitored=False` so existing behavior is byte-identical for users who already opted in. Parity with Radarr: the movies branch drops the preference gate too; missing movies are searched under whatever route the user picked, with the same `to-any`/`to-debrid`/`to-local` direction assignment; Radarr's `ensure_and_search` gets `respect_monitored` too — short-circuits with `status='skipped'` when the movie exists in Radarr but is unmonitored. Gated by the new `GAP_FILL_ENABLED` env var (default `true`): when set to `false`, the missing-anywhere path short-circuits but the legacy prefer-debrid local-only path stays unconditional so users who explicitly opted out of gap-fill don't lose their existing prefer-debrid enforcement. 23 new tests in `tests/test_gap_fill.py` and `tests/test_tmdb_episode_list.py` cover the route selector, the TMDB diff helper (including the empty-cache safety — an unknown title returns `[]` so reconcile doesn't hallucinate searches for a show with no ground truth), unset-preference searches, `to-any` direction resolution/non-escalation, preference-preserved behavior, gap-fill-off isolation, movie parity, and the `respect_monitored` split between routes. All 11 existing `test_search_retry.py` cases pass against the renamed function — the retry/cooldown/touch-before-search semantics are preserved verbatim.

- **Post-grab release completeness audit**: New `_audit_release_completeness(filename, release_name, mount_path, info)` hook runs after successful symlink creation in the blackhole flow (`_monitor_and_symlink` Phase 3). For episode-level releases where `_parse_episodes(filename)` returns a non-empty claimed set, the method walks the mount directory, parses episode numbers from each media file via `_parse_episodes`, and compares claimed vs delivered. On short delivery it logs a `release_incomplete` history event with the claimed/delivered/missing lists, blocklists the release's info_hash via `_blocklist.add(hash, filename, reason=..., source='auto')` so the same incomplete release isn't re-grabbed (respects `BLOCKLIST_AUTO_ADD`, default true), and triggers `ensure_and_search` for only the still-missing episode numbers with `prefer_debrid=True, respect_monitored=True` — the release routed through the blackhole so force-grab is the right shape, and respecting monitored prevents re-searching episodes the user unmonitored between grab and audit. Season packs (where `_parse_episodes` returns an empty set) are deliberately skipped at this layer: there's no reliable TMDB lookup inside the blackhole watcher and the library-scan reconcile already catches pack gaps on the next cycle. Partially-delivered episodes are never un-symlinked — partial playback beats nothing — so the failure mode is "release is blocklisted and missing episodes re-searched" rather than "completed directory is rolled back". Best-effort everywhere: hash extraction failure, history/blocklist failure, or re-search failure are all swallowed at DEBUG level so an audit error never breaks the happy-path symlink-created notification that already fired. Five new tests in `tests/test_blackhole_completeness.py` cover the complete-release no-op, the short-delivery blocklist path, the re-search-only-missing path (E5, E6 missing from a claimed E4-E6 range produces a `search_episodes([5, 6])` call with the correct season), the pack-release skip, and the missing-hash-still-logs-history fallback.

- **`verify_symlinks` auto-search now on by default via `GAP_FILL_ENABLED`**: The 6h symlink-verify task's `_attempt_arr_research` call (which triggers per-episode Sonarr/Radarr re-search when a broken symlink is deleted and the target content is truly gone from Zurg) previously required the opt-in `SYMLINK_REPAIR_AUTO_SEARCH=true` flag. It now also fires when `GAP_FILL_ENABLED=true` (default), because re-searching disappeared debrid content is part of the "available anywhere" reconcile story — if E04's file vanished from Zurg (RD cache eviction, WebDAV 404), gap-fill should close the hole the same way it closes a never-delivered one. Users who want auto-search off can set `GAP_FILL_ENABLED=false`; legacy `SYMLINK_REPAIR_AUTO_SEARCH=true` is still honored as an independent opt-in for the case where gap-fill is off but re-search on repair is still wanted. Test coverage updated in `tests/test_verify_symlinks.py`: `test_auto_search_disabled_by_default` is renamed to `test_auto_search_enabled_by_default_via_gap_fill` (asserts `_attempt_arr_research` IS called under defaults), and a new `test_auto_search_disabled_when_gap_fill_off_and_legacy_flag_off` locks down the both-off opt-out path.

### Fixed

- **Gap-fill under `prefer-debrid` now honors Sonarr/Radarr's `monitored` flag**: Previously `_search_for_missing_episodes` in `utils/library.py` passed `respect_monitored=False` whenever the show/movie route was `True` (i.e. the user had set `prefer-debrid`), so gap-fill would walk the full TMDB aired-episode list and ask Sonarr/Radarr to re-grab every episode — including seasons the user had explicitly unmonitored in Sonarr. For a long-running show like Grey's Anatomy (22+ seasons, ~400 episodes) under `prefer-debrid` + `GAP_FILL_ENABLED=true`, this produced a 148-episode / 15-season search fanout in a single scan cycle, flooding the blackhole with magnets that Sonarr's indexer returned regardless of RD cache status; on Real-Debrid (no pre-add cache probe since Nov 2024 `instantAvailability` deprecation) most of those hashes never cache, time out at `BLACKHOLE_MOUNT_POLL_TIMEOUT`, and get cleaned up by `BLACKHOLE_DELETE_UNCACHED_ON_TIMEOUT` — but only after the user watches a long churn of grab/timeout/delete cycles for content they never wanted grabbed. `library.py:1903` (shows) and `library.py:2035` (movies) now pass `respect_monitored=True` for every route; the docstring for `_search_for_missing_episodes` is updated so the "never re-searched against the user's intent" invariant covers all three routes instead of only None and False. Prefer-debrid still force-grabs debrid copies — just only for the episodes/movies the user is actively monitoring. The `tests/test_gap_fill.py::TestPreferDebridPreserved::test_local_only_episode_still_searched` assertion that pinned the old `respect_monitored=False` behavior is flipped to `is True` with a comment explaining why (unmonitoring a season must suppress gap-fill for that season even under force-grab semantics). All 1596 tests pass.

## Version [2.20.0] - 2026-04-21

### Removed

- **Plan 35 Phase 6 — pd_zurg backward-compatibility surfaces fully removed**: Closes the rebrand arc that began with the 2.18.0 branding pass and the 2.19.0 dual-read / dual-emit deprecation window. Every `pd_zurg`/`PDZURG` identifier that 2.19.0 preserved behind a deprecation warning or a dual-emission helper is now gone — the legacy `PDZURG_LOG_*` env var names are no longer read (`base/__init__.py::_env_dual` and the three `os.environ.get('PDZURG_LOG_*', …)` fallbacks in `utils/logger.py::get_logger` are deleted; `utils/config_reload.py::SOFT_RELOAD` no longer lists `PDZURG_LOG_LEVEL`; `utils/config_validator.py` + `utils/settings_api.py` log-level validators no longer accept the legacy name; `utils/settings_api.py::read_env_values`'s `_LEGACY_ENV_ALIASES` fallback that surfaced a user's existing `PDZURG_LOG_*` value under the `ZURGARR_LOG_*` UI slot is deleted); the Prometheus metrics exporter no longer emits the `pd_zurg_*` prefix alongside `zurgarr_*` (`utils/metrics.py::_emit`'s two-prefix loop and the `(DEPRECATED — use zurgarr_X; removed in 2.20.0)` HELP-line suffix are gone, the `_LEGACY_PREFIX = 'pd_zurg'` constant is deleted, and the `# HELP zurgarr_X` lines are now clean — scrape payload halves back to the pre-2.19 size); the client-side `pd_zurg_theme` / `pd_zurg_log_wrap` localStorage migration is deleted (`utils/ui_common.py::LS_MIGRATION_JS`, `window._zurgarrLSGet/_zurgarrLSSet`, and the `'pd_zurg_' + key` concat path are gone — `THEME_INIT_SCRIPT`, `toggleTheme()`, `utils/system_page.py`'s `toggleLogWrap()` writer + init IIFE, and `utils/status_server.py`'s `_SETTINGS_SETUP_HTML` trailing script now read/write `zurgarr_theme` / `zurgarr_log_wrap` directly via plain `localStorage.setItem/getItem`); the `.pd_zurg_backup` atomic-swap sidecar forward-migration branch in `utils/library_prefs.py::replace_local_with_symlinks` (the `legacy_backup_path` + three-part `isfile + not islink + not lexists` guard + debug-logged OSError fallback, ~25 lines) is deleted — the function now reads and writes only `.zurgarr_backup`; and `utils/deprecation.py` is deleted entirely (the `warn_once` deduper, the `flush_pending` handler-attach flush, the `_LOGGER_NAME = 'PDZURG'` constant, the `_fired`/`_pending` module state — all gone with no remaining callers). One additional surface that Phase 5 explicitly deferred for log-stream continuity is **also** renamed this release rather than retained: `utils/logger.py::get_logger`'s default `log_name` parameter moves from `'PDZURG'` to `'ZURGARR'`, which changes the on-disk rotating-file name from `PDZURG-YYYY-MM-DD.log` to `ZURGARR-YYYY-MM-DD.log` and the `logging.getLogger()` channel name from `'PDZURG'` to `'ZURGARR'`. The default-parameter form of the call cascades cleanly — every one of the ~40 `get_logger()` call sites uses the default argument — so no call site needs editing. `utils/status_server.py::_CONFIG_PREFIXES` (the env-var filter list that drives the `/system` config viewer) drops `'PDZURG'` from its prefix set; the `'ZURG'` entry already covered `ZURGARR_LOG_*` by prefix match, so the new names stay visible in the viewer. User action required at 2.20.0 upgrade (enumerated here because 2.20.0 is a hard break for any user who didn't migrate during the 2.19.x soak — there is no longer a dual-read or dual-emit fallback to absorb the mismatch): rename any remaining `PDZURG_LOG_LEVEL` / `PDZURG_LOG_COUNT` / `PDZURG_LOG_SIZE` entries in `.env` to their `ZURGARR_LOG_*` counterparts (the in-UI save-the-form migration path that 2.19.x offered is no longer available once 2.20.0 is pulled — it was a 2.19.x-only knob-turn and the alias fallback it relied on is gone from `utils/settings_api.py::read_env_values` this release); rewrite every Grafana panel, Alertmanager rule, recording rule, and ad-hoc PromQL query that targets `pd_zurg_*` to use `zurgarr_*` (mechanical find-and-replace — sample values, label sets, and metric families are byte-identical between the two prefixes, so nothing downstream of the query needs to change); update any external log shipper, `tail -f` pipeline, log-rotation cron, or fluentd/Promtail scraper keyed on the `PDZURG-YYYY-MM-DD.log` filename pattern to `ZURGARR-YYYY-MM-DD.log` — internal `docker logs` output is unaffected because it reads stdout via the stream handler, not the rotating file; the WebUI `/system` log viewer is unaffected because it tails the current-day log by the live filename; only external tooling that hardcoded the legacy name needs updating. Stale `/log/PDZURG-*.log` files that existed on the host at upgrade are **not** rotated into or auto-cleaned by `ZURGARR_LOG_COUNT` on the new filename (backupCount is per-filename-pattern in Python's `TimedRotatingFileHandler`), so they persist on disk as orphaned artifacts — delete them manually if the disk footprint matters, or leave them in place as a before/after log-stream record of the upgrade. The WebUI `/system` log viewer's `read_log_lines()` glob pattern moves from `PDZURG-*.log` to `ZURGARR-*.log` in lockstep with the filename rename, so the viewer shows data as soon as the first ZURGARR-named log file is written (immediately on 2.20.0 start) — it will NOT surface legacy PDZURG-named files under any circumstance, so the upgrade's first-hour log history needs to be read via `docker exec zurgarr cat /log/PDZURG-<date>.log` or `docker logs zurgarr` if needed for forensics. No action is needed for localStorage (every browser that loaded a 2.19.x build migrated its `pd_zurg_theme` / `pd_zurg_log_wrap` entries on the first page load during the window — any browser that never loaded a 2.19.x build falls back to system-preference default on next visit, same failure mode as a fresh profile) or for on-disk `.pd_zurg_backup` sidecars (these existed only in the sub-second window between `os.rename` and `os.remove` inside `replace_local_with_symlinks` — anything stranded on disk today is the result of a host crash in that window; 2.19.0's forward-migration pass consumed any such file that had a prefer-debrid swap attempt on its specific path during the 2.19.x soak, and any residual files that persist past 2.20.0 are orphaned artifacts the user can `find /<local-library-root> -name '*.pd_zurg_backup' -delete` to clean). Test surface shrinks in parallel: `tests/test_deprecation.py` is deleted in full (10 tests covering `warn_once` dedupe, pre-handler buffering, and the `_env_dual` helper's four env-var combinations — all assertions were on removed code); `tests/test_library_prefs.py::TestReplaceLocalWithSymlinks` drops five migration-specific cases (stranded-legacy forward-migration, pre-existing-new-name clobber guard, directory-at-legacy-path guard, symlink-at-legacy-path guard, dangling-symlink-at-new-name-path guard) and keeps the two non-migration cases (clean-swap + rollback-restores-original-on-symlink-failure) with their assertions on `.pd_zurg_backup` removed; `tests/test_settings_api.py` drops four alias-fallback cases (`test_legacy_log_level_in_file_surfaces_under_new_key`, `test_legacy_log_level_in_env_surfaces_under_new_key`, `test_new_log_level_wins_over_legacy`, `test_save_under_new_name_drops_legacy_entry` — the save-migrates-legacy-out invariant they locked down is rendered moot by the alias fallback's removal); `tests/test_config_validator.py::test_bad_log_level_warns_legacy_pdzurg` is deleted and the new-name counterpart kept; `tests/test_config_reload.py` rewrites the three `PDZURG_LOG_LEVEL`-as-sample-soft-reload-var cases to use `ZURGARR_LOG_LEVEL` and collapses the `test_soft_reload_covers_new_zurgarr_log_names` dual-name test into the base `test_soft_reload_vars_defined`; `tests/test_metrics.py` drops the `TestDualEmission` class in full (8 tests on the two-prefix symmetry + DEPRECATED marker + clean-new-help invariants) and rewrites `TestFormatMetrics` + `TestEmitHelper` assertions from `pd_zurg_*` to `zurgarr_*`, plus adds two sweep tests (`test_no_legacy_prefix_anywhere` on `format_metrics` output, `test_no_legacy_prefix_emitted` on `_emit` output) so a future accidental revert of the dual-emission loop is caught by a failing assertion rather than silently slipping through; `tests/test_system_stats.py` rewrites 7 `pd_zurg_*` assertions on disk/fd/net metric names and their `# TYPE` annotations to `zurgarr_*`; `tests/test_status_ui_enhancements.py::TestLocalStorageMigration` is deleted in full (8 tests on the helper's presence, the copy-before-delete invariant, the fallback page's inline snippet, and the whole-surface sweep on `setItem('pd_zurg_*')`) and replaced with `TestLocalStorageKeys` — a smaller positive-only class that asserts `getItem('zurgarr_theme')` / `setItem('zurgarr_theme')` / `getItem('zurgarr_log_wrap')` / `setItem('zurgarr_log_wrap')` are present on every surface and `pd_zurg_` + `_zurgarrLS` never appear anywhere; `tests/conftest.py::clean_env` drops the three `PDZURG_LOG_*` names from its strip list (the new names were already there from Phase 1). To prevent a silent misread for users whose 2.20.0 container still has `PDZURG_*` env vars in their `.env` (the env var is no longer dual-read, so a `PDZURG_LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG` that went unrenamed would silently fall through to the `INFO` default without the user knowing their intent was ignored), `utils/config_validator.py::validate_config` now emits a one-shot startup warning listing every `PDZURG_*` env var it finds in `os.environ` with the message "Legacy env vars ignored since 2.20.0 (rename to ZURGARR_*): …". The warning lands on stdout (caught by `docker logs`) and in the rotating log file, so the signal reaches every deployment that spins 2.20.0 up with a dirty `.env`. The 2.19.0 soak window is the only migration contract — any deployment that skipped it (direct jump from pre-2.19 to 2.20.0, or from 2.19.x to 2.20.0 without ever setting the new env-var names) is on the user to reconcile before upgrade, but the startup warning ensures the user at least sees a clear diagnostic in the first few lines of the 2.20.0 container's log output. The `'PDZURG'` historical identifier now persists only in (1) the pre-2.19 CHANGELOG entries that document what shipped at the time (audit-trail integrity — do not rewrite), (2) the `LICENSE` + `README.md` "Migrating from pd_zurg" section that names the project users are migrating FROM, (3) the vendored `plex_debrid/` upstream code that is attribution-scoped by `plex_debrid/ATTRIBUTION.md`, and (4) whatever `/log/PDZURG-*.log` files persist on users' disks until they rotate off or the user deletes them — a permanent record of where the project came from and nothing more.



### Changed

- **Internal comment / docstring sweep of stale `pd_zurg` references (plan 35 Phase 5)**: Final pass of plan 35 closes the 2.19.0 rebrand work by scrubbing the last internal prose that still said `pd_zurg` where it should have said `Zurgarr`. `git grep -n pd_zurg -- ':!CHANGELOG.md' ':!plex_debrid/'` surfaced five stale hits that missed the 2.18.0 branding sweep — four test docstrings (`tests/test_blackhole.py::test_read_tier_state_returns_none_for_legacy_file` describing the v1-sidecar backward-compat invariant as "a user upgrading pd_zurg mid-retry", `tests/test_status_ui_enhancements.py::test_excludes_unrelated_vars` summarising "Non-pd_zurg env vars should be excluded", `tests/test_symlink_switch.py::test_path_translation` calling the debrid mount "pd_zurg namespace", `tests/test_verify_symlinks.py::test_keeps_symlink_when_target_base_differs_from_mount` saying "Radarr/Sonarr's container but not in pd_zurg's") plus one stale test fixture value (`tests/test_config_validator.py::test_rclone_mount_name_valid` passing `RCLONE_MOUNT_NAME='pd_zurg-RD'` into the "valid mount name" assertion — updated to `zurgarr_mount-RD` rather than the intuitive `zurgarr-RD` so the fixture still exercises BOTH character classes allowed by the validator regex `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$`; `zurgarr-RD` on its own would drop underscore coverage, leaving a silent gap if a future edit tightened the regex to `[a-zA-Z0-9-]+`). All five are non-behavioural: the docstrings describe invariants, and the fixture string is only validated for character-set acceptance so swapping the brand half leaves the underlying assertion intact. Every other `pd_zurg` hit the grep surfaced stays put by design and falls into one of four KEEP buckets documented by the plan: user-facing migration prose that needs to name the thing users are migrating FROM (README's "Migrating from pd_zurg" section, `.env.example` legacy-env-var note, `LICENSE` lineage attribution); Phase 1-4 shipping code that deliberately references the legacy name because it IS the backward-compat surface (`base/__init__.py::_env_dual` docstring, `utils/deprecation.py` module docstring, `utils/settings_api.py::read_env_values` docstring, `utils/metrics.py` dual-emitter, `utils/ui_common.py` localStorage helper, `utils/library_prefs.py` legacy-backup migration path); Phase 1-4 test suites that lock down the backward-compat behaviour and therefore assert against the legacy identifier directly (`tests/test_library_prefs.py` sidecar migration, `tests/test_metrics.py` + `tests/test_system_stats.py` dual-prefix emission invariants, `tests/test_status_ui_enhancements.py` localStorage-migration coverage); historical `CHANGELOG.md` entries under 2.18.x and earlier that correctly document what shipped under the pd_zurg name and must not be rewritten (audit-trail integrity per plan 35 §"Key Design Decisions"); and vendored `plex_debrid/` upstream code whose attribution the plan's `git grep` incantation explicitly excludes. The `utils/logger.py` internal logger name `'PDZURG'` (the default `log_name` parameter at `get_logger`) and the `PDZURG-YYYY-MM-DD.log` rotating-filename pattern it produces are also intentionally retained — renaming a live logger channel mid-deploy splits the log stream and invalidates on-disk log rotation state — and the rationale is captured inline at the `_LOGGER_NAME = 'PDZURG'` declaration in `utils/deprecation.py`. Unlike Phases 1-4 this surface has zero runtime impact so there's no deprecation helper, no new test, no soft-reload plumbing, and no version bump — it's a pure brand-consistency pass. At 2.20.0 the Phase 6 cleanup drops the backward-compat surfaces entirely (`_env_dual`, `warn_once`, dual metric emission, legacy localStorage helper, `.pd_zurg_backup` rename path), at which point the KEEP-list shrinks to just the historical CHANGELOG entries, the upstream-attribution prose, and the (optionally retained) `PDZURG` logger-channel identifier.

- **`.pd_zurg_backup` atomic-swap sidecar renamed to `.zurgarr_backup` with opportunistic migration of stranded legacy files (plan 35 Phase 4)**: The prefer-debrid preference path in `utils/library_prefs.py::replace_local_with_symlinks` performs an atomic three-step swap — rename the local file to a transient sidecar, create a symlink at the original path, delete the sidecar on success (or rename it back on symlink failure). The sidecar filename extension moves from the legacy `.pd_zurg_backup` to `.zurgarr_backup` in 2.19.0. The sidecar lives for milliseconds in the normal-completion path — rename → symlink → remove all happen inside a single function call — so it is almost never observable on disk; the only way a stranded legacy-named sidecar persists is if the container crashed (SIGKILL, host power-loss, kernel panic) in the tiny window between the `os.rename` and the subsequent `os.remove`. The plan's scanner-driven migration prose was overstated — no scanner walks the local library looking for stale backups, because the sidecar wasn't designed to persist. The real migration surface is inside `replace_local_with_symlinks` itself: before creating a fresh backup at a given path, the function now checks for a stranded `.pd_zurg_backup` at the SAME path and — only if no entry of any kind already occupies the `.zurgarr_backup` slot — renames the legacy-named sidecar forward to the new extension, so the rest of the swap logic (including the rollback rename on symlink failure) operates under the new name uniformly. The guard composes three distinct checks, each with a concrete failure mode it eliminates: `os.path.isfile(legacy_backup_path)` rejects a directory or FIFO that happens to share the legacy name (the pre-2.19 code path only ever created regular files, so anything else is hostile or spurious and renaming it forward would break the subsequent `os.rename(real_local, backup_path)`); `not os.path.islink(legacy_backup_path)` rejects a symlink at the legacy path (renaming a symlink would move the REFERENCE forward, then the atomic swap would replace the symlink itself with the user's local file and orphan whatever the symlink used to point at); `not os.path.lexists(backup_path)` rejects a pre-existing new-name entry of ANY kind — including a dangling symlink, which the naive `os.path.exists` check would report as missing and which would then be silently replaced by the rename. An `OSError` from the rename (EACCES on a read-only mount, EXDEV across filesystems, etc.) is swallowed at `DEBUG` level via `logger.debug` — the legacy file stays put, the next swap attempt on the same path retries the migration, and a persistent permission problem surfaces in the log rather than looping silently forever. No `warn_once` deprecation on this surface: the migration is file-system level, happens in a crash-recovery window the user can't meaningfully act on, and each stranded sidecar migrates exactly once when the user next triggers a prefer-debrid swap on that specific file. Seven new tests in `tests/test_library_prefs.py::TestReplaceLocalWithSymlinks` (the function had zero test coverage before Phase 4) lock down: the canonical clean-swap with the new extension (local file becomes a symlink to the translated `BLACKHOLE_SYMLINK_TARGET_BASE`-rooted path, no sidecar under either extension remains); the stranded-legacy forward-migration (pre-seeded `.pd_zurg_backup` is gone after a successful swap — renamed forward to `.zurgarr_backup`, then consumed and deleted as the swap's own transient backup); the migration's guard against clobbering a pre-existing regular-file new-name backup; the failure-path rollback (a monkeypatched `os.symlink` that raises causes the original file to be restored with byte-identical contents, no sidecar remains under either extension, and the error message to the caller starts with `"Symlink failed (restored):"` so the UI can distinguish recovered-from failures from hard failures); and three hardened-guard cases — legacy sidecar that is a DIRECTORY (untouched, along with any squatter contents inside it), legacy sidecar that is a SYMLINK to a regular file elsewhere (untouched, symlink target unmodified), and a DANGLING new-name symlink (guard sees it via `lexists`, legacy file-name sidecar is preserved rather than silently replacing the dangling link). **At 2.20.0** the migration pass is removed and the function reads/writes only the new extension; any `.pd_zurg_backup` files that haven't been encountered by a swap on their specific path by then stay on disk as orphaned artifacts the user must clean up manually — negligible in practice because they exist at all only if a crash stranded them in the sub-second swap window.

- **localStorage keys `pd_zurg_theme` / `pd_zurg_log_wrap` migrated to `zurgarr_theme` / `zurgarr_log_wrap` with a one-shot per-browser migration (plan 35 Phase 3)**: The 2.18.0 rebrand deliberately preserved the two `pd_zurg_*` localStorage keys the WebUI uses to persist the theme-toggle state (Status / Library / Activity / Settings / System pages — light vs. dark) and the System-page log-viewer's line-wrap toggle, so no user had their saved preferences reset on upgrade. 2.19.0 renames both to the `zurgarr_*` prefix while keeping existing values intact via a tiny migration helper embedded in the page head.
